i declare resumed the session of the european parliament adjourned on 17 december 2001. before moving on to the agenda, allow me, ladies and gentlemen, to take a minute, just a minute, to join in the heartfelt tribute paid by all the parliamentary groups to our outgoing president, mrs nicole fontaine, at the sitting on 17 december. as you may recall, i was one of his competitors two years ago. that is why i am particularly pleased to acknowledge, here today, by thanking her, the dignity, the detachment, the efficiency and the exceptional elegance with which she has exercised her mandate in the service of the european union and for the highest prestige of our parliament. mr president, i would like to thank and congratulate mr didier rod on the excellent work he has done on this subject and to stress that i share in substance the views expressed in his explanatory statement and the conclusions he has proposed to us and which we have approved in the committee on development and cooperation. i shall therefore confine myself to a few brief remarks. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. difficulties and obstacles which began when the partnership with the acp was being called off and which culminated in the late and unusual problem of migration, already at the final stage of the negotiations. the final outcome of these negotiations, and the establishment of a new convention for a period of 20 years, is therefore, in my view, one of the most positive elements to be taken into account. but there are other, more substantial reasons. the involvement of civil society should be strengthened, especially if it is understood broadly and if it does not contradict the cooperation and support that the central administrations of the acp states, which are usually structurally weak, deserve; the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. but it is important to say that there are also elements in this new convention which give rise to some apprehension in us. firstly, the systematic appeal to unilateral conditions and objectives which, in addition to being capable of leading to the degradation of a partnership between equals, may also call into question the choice of the acp countries themselves as to their development policies. secondly, the rapid progress towards the enshrinement of the wto's liberalizing logic, which, as we know, has proved particularly negative for the least developed countries. these are aspects which we cannot ignore and which, together with the clear turn to the east of the european union - i would remind you that none of the acp countries is already on the list of the ten countries which benefit most from european cooperation - can jeopardise a partnership which we want to be fruitful, mutually beneficial and capable of breaking the cycle of fragile development and the enormous poverty which affects those countries.
- mr president, this debate on the future of the common fisheries policy is the culmination of important work by the rapporteur, mrs rosa miguélez ramos, and the committee on fisheries, which includes a visit to portugal and a debate in lisbon with representatives of the various players in this economic sector which is strategic for the european union, in which i also had the opportunity to take part. i would also like to stress the welcome that some of the proposals i have put forward in this sector, which is essential for food supplies and vital for employment and for the economic and social cohesion of the peripheral and coastal regions of the union, have received in the course of the preparatory work. i would particularly stress the position of rejection of any proposal to introduce a system of individual quotas, or individual quotas transferable at union level, which would result in the privatisation of fisheries resources and the concentration and verticalisation of catches, with negative consequences at socioeconomic and environmental level, and i hope that the plenary will not change this decision. i also stress the rejection of the commission's intention to reduce substantially structural aid in the fisheries sector, given the socioeconomic difficulties facing the sector and considering that, in the light of past performance, there should be a fair distribution of it. i would also like to refer to the adoption of the proposal for a specific community programme to support small-scale coastal and artisanal fishing, to the defence of respect for the principle of community preference, and to the need to improve working conditions and the participation of fishermen in the management and defence of the maintenance of the current regime of exclusive use of the coastal zone of 6/12 nautical miles (which, however, i would like to see extended to 24 miles and, in the case of peripheral regions, at least to 50 miles), proposals which unfortunately did not pass, as did the compensatory allowances for the canning industry and the annual flexibility for revision of multiannual tacs. in any case, mr president, despite the contradictions and some negative aspects, i consider the report to be a positive one overall and i thank mrs rosa miguélez ramos for all her commitment to this work.
- mr president, the report on the future of the cfp should make an important contribution to the formulation of this policy after 2002. i would therefore like to stress six issues which i consider to be of priority. the first is that we must ensure the continuity of fishing for future nations, which will be achieved by strengthening the policy of protecting resources, taking seriously the recommendations of scientists, as well as the size of the nets, the protection of juveniles or the reduction of returns to sea. the second is to ensure a balanced and flexible management of quotas and tacs, allowing the transfer of quotas between member states from those which do not use them to those which need them. the successful implementation of the multiannual guidance plans, or of the instruments which will replace them, seems to be essential to this objective, with serious sanctions to be imposed on those countries which do not comply. the third issue is the need to strengthen the component of international fisheries agreements, and the european union must negotiate new opportunities for its shipowners. the fourth question concerns the need to treat fisheries with a sense of specificity, and it is in this sense that the principle of relative stability should be maintained. fifthly, it will be important to strengthen the social dimension of the cfp in order to provide fishermen with the support essential for their survival. finally, the structural policy supporting the sector must be continued with the aim of keeping the community fleet permanently modernised and adapted to new circumstances. i am grateful to the rapporteur, mrs rosa miguélez ramos, for her efforts. the final result is not ideal, but i hope it will allow the essentials of what we need. in conclusion, i would like to convey my best wishes to commissioner fischler and take this opportunity to send him a message: "there is no flu that can resist a good plate of portuguese cod and a good glass of red wine"!
in writing. - (pt) despite their contradictions, the two initial reports, which deal with the award of public supply, service and works contracts and public services not yet fully liberalised, which must remain regulated by the directive, substantially improved the european commission's proposal. it should be recalled that the commission intends to take advantage of the process of simplifying existing directives to take a further step towards the opening up of public procurement and its liberalisation, thus meeting the lisbon objective of creating an internal market for public procurement. but during the plenary vote, a significant part of the positive proposals were rejected, bringing the report closer to the commission's positions and objectives. we therefore voted for all the positive proposals which aimed to include in public tenders other objectives than simply the lowest price, but without choosing the best proposal in terms of environmental, employment, disability protection, etc. we also voted in favour of the proposals relating to the fight against or prevention of corruption. however, since several of these proposals were rejected, we had no choice but to vote against both reports.
in writing. - (pt) i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on his work, which i consider to be of the utmost relevance, given the need to state clearly that the exploration and use of space can only be done for peaceful purposes, in the interests of humanity and in compliance with international law. this is particularly important at a time when the us is stating its intention to withdraw from the anti-ballistic missile (abm) treaty and to develop its national missile defence (nmd) system. i regret that this work has been undermined by an amendment tabled by the epp which, in the name of a concept of 'peace' that is more than doubtful, seeks to open the door to the use of space for military purposes, and i therefore reiterate our opposition to this proposal and the resulting outcome for the report itself, which we had to vote against. the policy of space exploration and use should be based on non-proliferation of weapons and transparency, in order to prevent an arms race in space. we also believe that the european space agency should not be integrated into the eu, given its central role as an agency for european cooperation in the field of space.
in writing. - (pt) i congratulate our colleague, rosa miguélez ramos, on her report, which makes a positive contribution to the debate on the reform of the common fisheries policy. i would like to stress two points, however, where the rapporteur takes a different approach from the one i am advocating and which i believe are vitally important in this debate. the report calls for a new definition of areas heavily dependent on fisheries, updating the principle of relative stability and allocating available resources according to the actual degree of dependence on fisheries. however, i consider it essential to maintain the allocation keys and to establish mechanisms for correcting these keys, where justified. it should be borne in mind that certain areas are highly dependent, socially and economically, on fishing activity (this is the case of the outermost regions) and need to maintain the regime of the access limit within 50 miles, on the basis of article 299 of the treaty. both the commission and the rapporteur refrain from addressing a situation which is increasingly important in terms of overall catches, namely recreational fishing. this activity continues to be unregulated and therefore not part of the fisheries organisation.
- madam president, despite being a woman from the south and not a sailor, which is a pity because this sector is still very closed to women, i do not want to miss this opportunity to congratulate you on your new position. i would also like to start by congratulating the commission and the rapporteur, who points out some of the strategic aspects of maintaining high professional standards which are expected to meet the needs of the union if it is to maintain and implement a mechanism which it itself recently legislated on (named maritime safety), which involves a new professional approach in ports, new powers of supervision and, above all, new requirements for seafarers operating in the union's exclusive economic zone. of course, this is primarily a matter for the member states, but it has already been realised that in the field of security, if there is no strategy there will be no effectiveness. and we also know that 80% of accidents are blamed on the crews. this is an unacceptable factor, which is rooted in the fact that our shipowners, and all our maritime transport, are subordinated to a single factor which is determinant of human activity, which are reasons for competitiveness, reasons for the survival of the activity itself, but which must be regulated at the internal community level and also regulated in the international institutions, whether in the imo or the ilo. and here the member states have not had a strategy of recovery, on the contrary. employment in this sector has fallen by 40%, and wages have also fallen further, leaving these professions, especially experienced officials indispensable to navigate the coastal specificities of the union and to meet the needs i have already mentioned, to be exercised by third country nationals who do not even have the same language and the same capacity for communication. that is why, madam president, we insist that the commission should regulate, should harmonize, not only training profiles but also the conditions under which wages are paid on board, if lives are lived on board, the safety of crews; and it should also act with other economic blocs which have unilaterally enlarged the market, made it wild, or made it protectionist, as is the case in the united states. we believe that the commission is indeed in a position and on the right track to ensure that member states continue to supply quality shipping, to do justice not only to our past but also to our future.
- mr president, as this is the first time i have spoken since your election, i would like to wish you the very best of luck in the presidency of the european parliament. and i would like to congratulate my colleague mr oostlander on the quality and the effort he has put into this report, which the european people's party supports wholeheartedly. the drug problem is at the heart of the concerns of european citizens. the european parliament has been fighting drugs for years. the european dimension of the fight against drugs is now evident in actions ranging from the adoption of legal instruments against the manufacture of drugs, drug trafficking and money laundering, to cooperation with producer countries and the promotion of the prevention and fight against drug addiction, both in terms of treatment and reintegration, and the development of information and coordination networks between member states. it should be borne in mind that since the end of the 'cold war' drug trafficking has experienced unprecedented growth as a source of funding for the activities of criminal and terrorist organisations. it is estimated that drug-related crime and the size of drug trafficking may now account for approximately 8% of world trade. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the draft directive on the protection of the environment. however, as our colleague mr oostlander very well points out, this proposal for a framework decision does not propose anything new or strengthen the instruments for combating international drug trafficking. this is a necessary step, and we know that it is a step towards meeting the needs recognised in the vienna action plan, the tampere conclusions and the european union's strategy to combat drugs for 2000-04. it is a necessary but insufficient step: it merely establishes a minimum common denominator of the national provisions in force in each of the member states concerning offences relating to drug trafficking. member states should therefore take the necessary measures to ensure that those offences are punishable by effective, proportionate and dissuasive criminal penalties, including deprivation of liberty, which may be limited to a maximum of five years' imprisonment in the most serious cases. now, this is, in my view, a very low threshold, especially in view of the fact that the legislation in force in the member states already imposes heavy penalties in relation to drug trafficking, in particular when the scale and gravity of the case can be considered as aggravating circumstances. for example, in my country, portugal, the limit is 12 years and may be increased by a third. finally, i would like to mention one aspect of the greatest importance, namely the confiscation of all illicit profits derived directly or indirectly from the crime of trafficking, or from participation in a criminal organization or conspiracy whose purpose is the trafficking of drugs. i believe that it will be one of the wishes of many of the organizations which have made a considerable effort in the fight against drugs that part of the proceeds from the confiscation of these goods may be used for prevention policy.
in writing. - (pt) the result achieved by the conciliation delegation is very positive and represents a very significant achievement for the european parliament. the adoption of this directive on the organisation of working time for persons engaged in mobile road transport activities as a profession eliminates distortions of competition in this sector and at the same time promotes road safety. i am particularly pleased with the agreement reached on the central issue of this directive: the inclusion of the selfemployed. the inclusion of selfemployed drivers in the scope of the directive, seven years after its entry into force, which allows the commission to draw up a study on the consequences of the exclusion of selfemployed drivers, to be carried out two years before the end of this seven-year period, has enabled the resistance of certain member states to be overcome in a skilled and balanced way and thus to obtain the agreement necessary for the adoption of this new directive, which is of crucial importance for the common transport policy. the definition of the selfemployed driver envisaged in the directive is also an important contribution to preventing new disguised forms of selfemployment during the temporary period of exclusion of selfemployed workers. as regards night work, the competitiveness of peripheral countries whose hauliers have to travel long distances to reach the major production and distribution centres is maintained by driving at night when traffic is less dense. the result of the agreement between parliament and the council is therefore a good balance between economic aspects, road safety and workers' health.
in writing. - (pt) this proposal for a directive deserves to be voted on because it is an important step and a contribution to the creation of a more coherent community policy on immigration. the question here is to define the status of long-term resident, since we all recognise the need for the european union to reach a compromise on the harmonisation of the situation of third-country nationals legally and permanently resident in a member state, which must guarantee them legal, economic and social rights comparable to those of union citizens. this status can be obtained after five years of uninterrupted legal residence, at the request of the person concerned, who must have economic resources and health insurance (so as not to be a burden on the member state) and not to be a threat to public policy or national security. in return, once this status is acquired, the national would be guaranteed a set of rights, such as the right to work, education, social security, health care, access to goods and services, etc., which constitutes a real instrument of integration into the host community. once again, it is important to stress here the importance for us of developing a more vigorous integration policy, which is fundamental to promoting greater economic and social cohesion, to avoid discrimination and racism. it should be noted, however, that genuine integration requires efforts from both sides. i also welcome the set of procedural safeguards provided for in this proposal for immigrants seeking this status, which make it possible to avoid possible abuse by the competent authorities of the member states.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. it is true that the absence of the united states makes the kyoto protocol less comprehensive than desirable. it is also true that the protocol is less ambitious than the one originally drawn up. however, the alternative to this small step was incomparably worse: the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we are therefore pleased to approve the kyoto protocol tomorrow and hope that it can come into force later this year. there is, however, one point about the council's decision which concerns me. and i am referring to denmark's alleged willingness to reopen the discussions on the burden-sharing agreement agreed in 1998. such renegotiation is unjustifiable, unacceptable and would make the international position of the european union look ridiculous. we have to remember that we have just recently challenged russia for wanting to do exactly the same thing. but beyond this environmental message calling for action, the adoption of the kyoto protocol also sends other political signals that should be highlighted. first, it has proven that globalization can be regulated. secondly, the european union has shown leadership, and in this particular case i would like to congratulate commissioner margot wallström on her outstanding leadership. thirdly, it has given rise to a new economic paradigm, the carbon economy. the tonne of carbon will now have a market price and carbon dioxide emissions will be a cost to be reflected in the price of all products. whoever is able to produce the same using cleaner technologies will win. so, with that out of the way, let's talk about the future. it is true that the european union has already reduced its greenhouse gas emissions by 4% between 1990 and 1999. however, the performance of the vast majority of member states is still very poor and there are sectors for which the situation is very worrying, such as transport, where emissions are expected to increase by up to 50% over the next ten years. in this context, the community instruments being presented by the commission, in particular the european climate change programme and the emissions trading directive, on which the european parliament will be voting later this semester, are to be welcomed. but given what the community strategy is, what role should the member states play? i think that the member states should not fall into the easy temptation of simply implementing reduction measures which represent both low economic costs and low political costs. the problem is to be solved by cutting emissions in industry and the energy sector, not by cutting emissions in transport or building. this option would be environmentally untrue and economically irrational. by avoiding a policy for some sectors, member states would not only lose the opportunity for technological transformation, but would later have much higher reduction costs. the strategy should therefore be to reorient economic policies by internalising the environmental costs of the 'greenhouse effect' in all economic sectors. now, this will only be possible if we introduce some measures considered to be consensual such as the production of renewable energy, such as energy efficiency, such as public transport, alongside clearly unpopular measures such as the end of subsidies to the fossil fuel industry and the taxation of energy and carbon dioxide emissions in the transport sectors. i would like to thank the shadow rapporteurs from all the political groups for their cooperation and commitment.
- mr president, the situation of poverty in the world and the deep chasm that separates the two hemispheres of the planet are well known. but despite the many declarations of good intentions, the solemn commitment in 1974 to achieve the objective of 0.7% of gnp in official development aid and the fact that the world bank itself recently considered it necessary to double the resources allocated to official aid, the truth is that it is no more than 0.22% in the oecd countries, the lowest ever, having fallen by about 25% in the last decade and currently not exceeding 0.32% in the member states of the european union. the monterrey conference is of vital importance in this context and in the context of johannesburg. it is time to reverse negative trends and facts, to look at the problem of developing poor countries with new eyes of solidarity and to take concrete and binding measures. we appreciate the council's decision of 8 november, but it is essential that the spanish presidency and the commission spare no effort to ensure that monterrey produces objective and timetabled commitments which guarantee significant progress, even if we are aware of the backward and unacceptable positions of the american administration in this area, which cannot determine positions of conformity, or even the weaknesses of the so-called monterrey consensus. and it is also necessary that guidelines should emerge from monterrey that put an end to the devastating effect of external debt on the development of developing countries, advocating mechanisms that do not adhere to the initiative on the heavily indebted poor countries, but rather are based on a global recognition of the principle of debt cancellation. the terrible situation in which the developing countries find themselves cannot continue, and the european union and its member states cannot be satisfied with being the main donor, but must take a clear stand at this conference to enable the developing countries to get out of this situation.
in writing. - (pt) this state aid report follows closely the line taken by the previous report, arguing that there has been a reduction in state aid over the period 1997 to 1999, notably due to reductions in germany and italy, the two countries which grant the largest volume of state aid. the report's rationale is based on the defence of reducing state aid and increasing the measures included in this context, in particular fiscal measures. in line with the conclusions of the stockholm summit, the objective is to gradually reduce state aid by 2003, allowing only such aid as member states would require in exceptional circumstances. the commission has already made a number of proposals. however, with the creation of the state aid review panel, pressure on member states to reduce state aid will increase, always under the pretext of defending competition, forgetting the problems of the weaker economies and ignoring us policy, which continues to grant state aid to different sectors of the economy, despite wto rules. that is why we voted against.
the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environmentally friendly use of energy. - i know . mr president, as mr napolitano has pointed out, the question of relations between national parliaments and the european parliament is one of the central issues on the agenda of the convention which is about to begin its work. this is the occasion for the debate on this report by the committee on constitutional affairs, which includes the opinion of the committee on external affairs, for which i had the honour of being rapporteur. in my personal view, mr napolitano's report has many merits, and i congratulate him on that, but it also defends some options that are open to political criticism. as a speaker, however, i must confine myself to stating the objectives pursued in my committee's opinion, which have been partially echoed in the motions for resolutions under discussion, particularly in paragraphs 13, 14 and 15. as regards the common foreign and security policy, including defence, which, as is well known, remains essentially in the intergovernmental sphere, the wording of the question is relatively simple: on the one hand, the european parliament exercises its supervisory function on the basis of the rights of information and consultation conferred on it by article 21 of the treaty, together with the budgetary power to approve operational expenditure in the field of civilian crisis management and the associated administrative burden; on the other hand, national parliaments do not really participate in the formulation of such a policy, although they exercise decisive powers in the field of military expenditure in their respective countries, which then have a strong impact on the operational means made available to the cfsp. the type v provisions of the treaty on european union require the member states to respect the principles of coordination and mutual solidarity in defining and implementing the cfsp, and this democratic and parliamentary deficit must therefore be corrected. i would stress the significance and importance of the proposal for the european parliament and the parliament of the member state holding the presidency to convene a regular joint parliamentary conference on the cfsp, composed of the european parliament and the national parliaments, and aimed precisely at intensifying the indispensable exchange of information in this field. in the present institutional framework, this conference would enable the various parliaments to carry out their work more effectively in accordance with their respective competences. but not only: in this proposal we also give substance to the principle that both the european parliament and the national parliaments represent the people within the european union, since both are directly elected by the citizens and are full guarantors of the exercise of democracy.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, first of all i would like to congratulate president napolitano on his report. the subject of relations between national parliaments and the european parliament is one of the central themes of the european debate, it will be one of the key points of the convention's work and it was absolutely necessary that, beyond the possible quarrels and the normal diversity of opinions, this house should confront it calmly and intelligently. it is not worthwhile to ignore the difficulties facing the construction of a democratic europe, especially at a historic moment when, even at the national level, it is urgent to reinvent and strengthen representative democracy. at this stage of the journey we have been making together for many years and which we want to continue to make with the same commitment in many others, we must understand, assume and claim the special responsibility that comes from being the representatives directly elected by the citizens, both national and european members. it is therefore essential to make progress towards strengthening the participation and control of national parliaments and the european parliament, both in the formulation of legislative decisions and in the political guidelines of the european union. as this report clearly acknowledges, none of these parliamentary institutions has the exclusive right to represent the european citizens. this is why their collaboration must be complementary, organised and, if possible, accomplice, while respecting their autonomy and their specific responsibilities. and for that we do not need to complicate the decision-making process or overlap competencies or continue to breed mistrust. what is required of each institution is that it should do its part better and better. we too want to see national parliaments more closely involved in the political and legislative process of the european union, and we are therefore committed to helping to create the conditions for effective national monitoring and control of their governments in their capacity as members of the council of the union. i conclude by saying that the diagnosis and the paths indicated in this report offer good prospects for the joint work we are going to carry out in the convention to make this new europe more democratic, more understandable and closer to all of us, european citizens.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, this owninitiative report by the european parliament which we are now considering is fully justified: it makes perfect sense for the european parliament to analyse, debate and propose ways of improving the practical implementation and management of the structural funds with a view to achieving increased effectiveness. although the reform of the structural funds culminated in the 1999 berlin summit, which introduced substantial improvements in their functioning, the experience of the last two and a half years tells us that there is room for further improvement. the importance of the objectives of economic and social cohesion policy, of which the structural funds are a fundamental instrument, obliges us to make an effort to continuously improve. we are all obliged to do this: the european institutions, in particular the commission, but also the member states and the regions which benefit. in fact, efforts to simplify and de-bureaucratize the operation of the structural funds must be continued, and management methods must be improved in areas such as financing, monitoring, monitoring and implementation of the supported programmes, just as the member states and regions must make a more careful choice of the projects to be supported and make the best use of the appropriations. the mechanisms such as the 'efficiency reserve' should therefore be strengthened in order to encourage and reward regions which have made the best use of the structural funds. on the other hand, national economic policies must never lose sight of the objectives of economic and social cohesion at home, and they must be consistent with and not contradictory to those objectives. the same applies to the other community policies, in particular the common agricultural policy, competition policy and state aid, which, given the importance of the legal and financial instruments they are endowed with and if better oriented towards other cohesion objectives, could be of enormous value. finally, we stress the need for greater decentralization or for more effective application of the principle of subsidiarity in the context of the european union's economic and social cohesion policy. obviously, there is still room for member states and regions to assume more responsibility in this area. the principles of decentralization and subsidiarity must be carried to their logical conclusion. but watch out! we should not confuse the application of the principle of subsidiarity in the context of the structural funds with a hypothetical and absurd renationalisation of the economic and social cohesion policy of the european union. the principle of subsidiarity can never be used as an instrument for attempts to drain the european union's economic and social cohesion policy, particularly of financial resources. on the contrary, such a principle requires an effective policy of economic and social cohesion at european level, and it also justifies, in the face of the challenge of enlargement and the drastic increase in regional disparities resulting from it, a strengthening of the resources allocated to this true pillar of the european construction. let us not forget that countries like the united states of america allocate more resources than the european union to their redistributive policies. in short, mr president, yes to more decentralization in the management of the structural funds. the european council also called for the european union to continue to play a leading role in the development of the union's external relations, but also for the maintenance and strengthening of the essential functions of the european union's economic and social cohesion policy, namely the redistribution of resources and the promotion of development in favour of its most disadvantaged regions.
the council. - the decision to which the honourable member refers was not taken at the laeken european council, which did not deal with the situation in colombia, but is contained in the presidency statement on behalf of the european union on the peace process in colombia of 7 december. in it, the european union states that until further notice, the member states of the union will not issue new visas or residence permits to representatives of armed groups, not only to those of the farc, but to all armed groups, without prejudice to the continuation of the efforts of the member states of the union participating in the dialogue between the colombian parties. the european union also expressed its support for the efforts of the member states of the union which, at the request of the colombian government and the parties to the conflict, are participating in the talks which the government of president andrés pastrana has opened with the eln and the farc in order to reach a negotiated solution to the conflict. this decision has nothing to do with the framework decision on the fight against terrorism to which the honourable member refers, but is part of the firm condemnation by the entire international community of the practices of armed groups, which engage in activities such as kidnapping, extortion and other types of crime that constitute a violation of human rights and international humanitarian law, and endanger the peace process that is not yet firmly established in that country. the european union insists that its position on armed groups in colombia will be considered and reviewed in the light of respect for human rights, international humanitarian law and diplomatic immunity. the european union fully supports efforts to ensure that all parties concerned respect human rights and international humanitarian law and calls on all parties to condemn them. it also calls on the armed groups to release all hostages, foreigners and colombians, and to immediately abandon these practices. the european union also urged the farc to return to the negotiating table to, inter alia, address the issues agreed at san francisco de la sombra in october 2001. e eu welcomes the resumption of dialogue between the government and the eln and hopes that both movements will show a firm determination to seek concrete results towards a peaceful and lasting solution to the conflict. in its statement, the european union also urged the colombian government to continue and intensify its efforts to disarm the paramilitaries and bring those responsible for the crimes committed, whoever they may be, to the full extent of justice, to continue - both at local and regional level, and with respect for biodiversity and the environment - the efforts already begun to combat illicit cultivation, production and trafficking of narcotic drugs and to draw up and implement urgently, and independently of the peace process, a programme of decisive socioeconomic reforms. as this parliament knows, the peace process in colombia is currently going through uncertain times. the european union continues to actively support the peace process and the laudable efforts of president pastrana and considers that there is no alternative to a negotiated solution to the internal conflict. the european union will endeavour to implement the autonomous commitments made under the european programme in support of the peace process in colombia, provided that security concerns do not prevent it from doing so.

the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. - mr president, the committee on industry considers this report to be very positive, believes that indicators in euro are a more reliable picture than the present measurement and eligibility criteria and also believes that unemployment indicators are important because there are regions where only 44% of the active population is in employment, but productivity disparities are equally important. it also considered that the centralisation of the union's economy in a triangle with two to four times the productivity of the peripheries was one of the problems that accentuated and made it difficult to combat these inequalities, and felt that future programming should include a qualitative assessment of the various indicators. as regards enlargement, the orientation of the funds should be flexible, taking account of the specificities of each member state. the committee on industry supports the increase in the union's financial effort for cohesion policy, in particular through competitive rigour and a budgetary adjustment of community policies, believes that no policy whose financial effort is greater than cohesion can neutralize it and that the most developed countries pay production aid which represents almost the entire community budget. the lack of competitive rigour does not promote cohesion or integration and is a recurrent cause for criticism within the wto. the committee on industry calls on the commission to ensure that a european energy and communications market is built up which respects universal service, gives priority to energy efficiency and research, energy recovery and the use of renewable energies. it considers it essential to complete the accessibility and transport infrastructures in order to reduce the impact on the competitiveness of the present peripheral regions, without which the cohesion effort already made will be compromised. the committee on industry draws attention to the effects of energy prices and taxation in the peripheral and overseas regions. it recommends that indigenous energies and clean and safe technologies for the exploitation of coal, nuclear energy and hydrocarbons be developed.
- mr president, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, in the impossibility of dealing in two minutes with the numerous cohesion issues which are also dealt with in the report for the consideration of mr musotto, whom i congratulate on the excellent work he has done, allow me to express only a few ideas on the implications of enlargement for the economic and social cohesion policy of the european union. the first idea is that if we want a viable and future-oriented enlarged european union, it will be inevitable to strengthen the policy of economic and social cohesion. eastern europe must feel the same level of european solidarity that countries such as portugal, spain, greece and ireland have experienced since their accession to the european union. but the strengthening of cohesion policy is necessary not only to combat the enormous regional imbalances resulting from the entry of dozens of disadvantaged regions from the current candidate countries, but also to continue to meet the still existing backlogs of the present europe of 15 countries, particularly in the outermost regions. the european union is not a single country, but a single continent, and the european union is not a single continent, but a single continent. the european union is not a single continent, but a single continent. this is to say that after 2006 we will need a cohesion policy which does not merely look to the east but also takes account of the cohesion deficit still existing in the present europe of the fifteen and the new problems which enlargement will create for it. reading the second cohesion report tells us that the commission is aware of this problem. we are confident that the third report will provide the right and fair solutions.
the report by mr de gucht, which i am sure will be of great interest to the house, is a report which is not only a good report but also a good one. the commission's proposals to reduce the excise duty on certain alcoholic beverages produced in madeira, the azores and the french overseas departments are another measure to give effect to the principle of the outermost regions, enshrined in article 299 (2) of the treaties. it should be borne in mind that this legal basis, approved by the treaty of amsterdam, provides for the adoption of specific measures in various fields, including taxation, in accordance with the geographical, social, economic and cultural realities peculiar to these european regions. in short, special treatment by the european union in accordance with the very specific situation of these territories, which should be limited only by the integrity and coherence of the community legal order, including the internal market and common policies. only by giving these regions this special treatment will we be able to ensure that they are able to integrate into the dynamics of the internal market, economic and monetary union, the forthcoming enlargement of the european union and globalisation. only by this special treatment will we be able to guarantee the conditions for effective equality of opportunity for citizens living in the outermost regions. the measures to reduce excise duty on rum and liqueurs produced and consumed in madeira, on spirits produced and consumed in the azores and on rum produced in the french overseas departments are essential to compensate for the extra costs of production and thus to ensure the survival of traditional activities of significant economic, social, historical and cultural importance for these regions. 140 jobs are at stake in madeira and the azores and 40 000 in the dom. elements linked to the very specific identity of these regions are also at stake, particularly in the dom, where activities linked to the planting of sugarcane and the production of sugar and rum are an important part of the economic and social history of these territories. all these activities linked to the sugar cane, sugar and rum sector are thus dependent on this tax measure, in a context marked by the revision of the common organisation of the market in sugar and the abolition of customs protection for exports of spirit drinks to the community market from third countries. indeed, if the proposed tax measure is not adopted, how can rum production in the dom be viable when it will have to face production costs that are much higher than those of rum produced in the neighbouring caribbean countries when it enters the community market freely from 2003 onwards? it should be noted that community rum producers pay four to six times more for sugar cane and three times more for labour. in addition, there are costs for adapting rum production units to environmental standards in accordance with community rules. finally, it should be stressed that, despite the permanent nature of the specific features justifying the special treatment granted to the outermost regions by article 299 (2), the present tax measures are not only limited in time, but are valid for seven years, as are also the measures in quantitative terms. they are also subject to an interim evaluation to verify whether the reasons for granting them are still valid. we are therefore faced with a well-formulated and balanced proposal from the commission which should deserve our full support. it is in line with the need for special treatment for the outermost regions and safeguards the integrity of the community's internal market. it is also through such measures that european citizenship is achieved and promoted in territories far from the european centre, such as the outermost regions.
- mr president, commissioner, rapporteur, ladies and gentlemen, this debate is taking place on a specific subject which in the azores has a limited scope and significance, without any comparison with what is happening in the dom or even in the autonomous region of madeira, just two days after the summit of the presidents of the outermost regions in las palmas, with the presence of representatives of the member states and the commission. i therefore welcome the conclusions of that summit and call on the council and the commission to take them into account. this year is particularly important for the azores in the fisheries sector, with important initiatives such as the revision of the common fisheries policy, the revision of poseima-pescas and a quota policy for bottom fish to be adopted. these are areas where it is essential to know the reality well and to take account of the specific situation of the azores. the commission proposal on fishing quotas for deep-sea fish raises our greatest concerns in this respect.

in writing. - (pt) i was obliged to vote against the napolitano report, even though i recognize that the resolution reflects a set of positive concerns about the role of national parliaments both in the exercise of control over their respective governments, especially when they act to define and implement european policy, and in the construction of european policy. i also acknowledge, as i mentioned in my speech, that it is positive that the document partially takes up some of the proposals in the afet opinion, for which i was the rapporteur. i cannot, however, agree with certain conclusions which i consider to be politically decisive. i would like to emphasize, among other things, paragraph 21 on the emergence - through the convention - of a constituent power, even if it is exercised jointly by the european parliament and the national parliaments, the commission and the governments of the member states, and the prior rejection of a future senate (paragraph 17), which in my view could respect the principle of parity between the states of the union. in this declaration of vote, beyond my objections, which led me to vote against the report, i would like to make it clear that i respect mr napolitano's work and his efforts to make the eu more parliamentary, by rejecting the simplistic scheme of making the ep the exclusive depository of the representation of citizens and the guarantee of democracy in its relations with the other institutions of the union.
in writing. - (pt) this report has the merit of making an approximate diagnosis of the current situation concerning economic and social cohesion within the european union. we particularly agree with the emphasis on the fact that cohesion is, according to the treaties, one of the community's pillars. i also agree with the explicit reference to the delays in the fight against development and living standards asymmetries, or even the regression observed in the regions of some countries. the express need to ensure the continuity and strengthening of regional policy in the future is worth sharing, as is the opposition to the possible withdrawal of certain objective 1 regions for purely statistical reasons. however, as regards the causes of these backward and regressive situations, the report does not go as far as would be desirable. in mentioning the insufficiency of the financial resources made available for the cohesion objective, he omits, however, essential aspects, such as the absence of a redistributive nature in the community budget and the absolute need to revise the financial perspective, moreover on the eve of enlargement. it also omits certain determining factors for the less developed regions, in particular the negative effects and, therefore, the need to review macroeconomic and financial policies, sectoral policies which often prove to be deeply inadequate.
in writing. - (pt) i voted in favour of this report, which is an excellent initiative to create a coherent, clear and effective policy at european union level on the problem of illiteracy. this problem has not received the attention it deserves, probably because until recently it was thought that the most developed countries did not have a literacy problem due to compulsory schooling. the last community measures specifically dealing with this problem were approved in 1987. however, the oecd's literacy report, published in june 2000, clearly shows the opposite, namely that illiteracy is a reality in our countries and that it entails significant economic and social costs. the oecd defines literacy as the reading and writing skills that adults use in their daily lives to achieve their goals, acquire knowledge and increase their potential. thus, the existence of illiteracy is the denial of one of the most basic human rights: the right to education. it also constitutes an obstacle to access to other rights, because it prevents the improvement of living conditions, restricts access to the labour market, limits the freedom of expression of individuals and, therefore, their participation in society. it also deprives them of their civil rights. the figures we have on the scale of this phenomenon at community level cannot be underestimated, they concern a considerable percentage of the population of the european union aged 15-65. it is no surprise, but always with a lot of non-conformity, that i see my country, portugal, as the most affected. if nothing is done, this problem will only get worse. - mr president, what is striking in the commission document is, on the one hand, the recognition that the lisbon strategy had as its scenario economic growth of 3%, which was not achieved in 2000 and 2001 with the economic slowdown that was felt, and, on the other hand, the document insists on two fundamental objectives which the bullmann report also mentions: meeting the objectives of the stability pact, namely the 'zero deficit' strategy in 2004, and accelerating the processes that are lagging behind. the commission is also proposing to increase the pressure on public markets to be liberalised, the creation of a single market for venture capital in 2003 and for financial services in 2005, the opening of the electricity market in 2003 and the gas market in 2004, progress with a second liberalisation package in the railway sector and a focus on labour market reform. while pointing to some positive measures in the social area with the reduction of the poverty rate by half by 2010 and the approval of social inclusion indicators, the truth is that accelerating privatisations, the attempt to make the labour market more flexible and the insistence on applying the rigid criteria of the stability pact without taking into account the diversity of the member states and the serious social problems of some will lead to more poverty and unemployment. the confidence which the big employers place in this barcelona summit is symptomatic. that is why we are firmly opposed to this commission document. the bullmann report, which was initially fairly balanced, was subsequently distorted and came very close to the commission's positions, and that is why we must oppose it.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, unfortunately sustainable development is still a cause to be pursued and not so much a reality to be improved. ten years after the rio summit, the signs of unsustainability have worsened and our global response remains insufficient. in this context, it was very important that the gothenburg council adopted a european strategy for sustainable development and added the environmental dimension to the lisbon process. this decision, like the work to rescue the kyoto protocol, has made clear the european union's leadership in the environmental field. as for the barcelona council, i think it is essential that three objectives are met. first, it must be action-oriented, setting targets, timetables and precise actions to correct the unsustainability in the european union. secondly, it must indicate when and how the 'environmentalisation' of the european union's common policies will take place. third, it should adopt an ambitious set of environmental indicators to serve as a reference for the annual assessment. a final word on amendment no 10, which condemns the spanish water plan: the credibility of the european parliament and the balance of the institutions are at stake. parliament cannot become an instance of the court of justice of the european communities, at the risk of setting a very serious precedent. european citizens, like members of the european parliament, have the means to challenge the legal conformity of certain projects in the context of community litigation. this amendment is unacceptable, it threatens the very adoption of the resolution, and i hope that the common sense of the proposers will prevail.
- mr president, i belong to a country which has already completed the process of ratifying the rome statute, confirming the humanist tradition of attachment to justice which characterizes the portuguese, that of a country which abolished the death penalty 450 years ago and life imprisonment 118 years ago. but more importantly, we can see that the values of justice and the fight against impunity are becoming more and more widespread on all five continents. slowly, yes, but go. we also welcome the fact that the forthcoming inauguration of the icc will contribute to the rejection of the death penalty. furthermore, justice is only fair if it is applied to all equally. we know that the icc is not and will never be perfect. it will often be dressed as a 'victory court', but in addition to its normal judicial function of investigating and prosecuting particularly serious crimes, it will also have two other very important tasks: preventing new crimes by deterring them and providing reparation and compensation to victims. it should also be noted that the international court of justice only takes action if the national criminal authorities fail to investigate and prosecute crimes, either by legal omission or because they are not in a position to do so. we believe that this is a satisfactory solution, provided it is applied properly to the difficult problem of the conflict between the jurisdiction of the international criminal court and the sovereignty of national criminal systems. mr president, the immunity and independence of the icc are essential values and are part of the defence of what is the greatest heritage of the european union: respect for human rights and fundamental freedoms, and the rule of law. every occasion is therefore suitable to highlight the objectives which led to the creation of the court, which cannot be undermined under any circumstances.
- mr president, with the discharge procedure for 1999, the commission and parliament have opened a new road marked by greater institutional cooperation. it is a result to which the commission, but also our rapporteur, freddy blak, who has managed this process with determination, common sense and intelligence, can be attributed. in the agricultural sector, there has certainly been progress, particularly through the implementation of the integrated administration and control system, but there has been no progress in the control of food counterfeiting. this situation is paradoxical: a deviation of half a metre in the route of a tractor ploughing the land or the replacement of a dairy cow on a date considered non-regulatory can lead to the detection of irregularities and financial corrections by statistical projection. however, food counterfeiting for export subsidies is rarely controlled and, when it is, fraud often gets lost in the cracks of national justice systems, with no community sanctions or public clarification of the facts. it is a state of affairs with which we cannot settle.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. it was worked out quickly and, in my opinion, well. in view of the need to ensure in good time all the conditions which can contribute to the effectiveness of the convention's work, the budgetary framework for the convention has already been defined, in accordance with the rules of transparency and institutional balance. given the particular nature of the convention, the specificity of its legal and budgetary framework is understandable, reflected from the outset by the very existence of the fund and the fact that it is subject to exceptional financial rules. the new entity's temporary nature is therefore taken into account and the requirement for increased flexibility is met. the conditions referred to are the basis of a specific interinstitutional agreement, which defines in particular the amount of the contributions of the funding institutions, parliament, the council and the commission, as my colleague kathalijne buitenweg has already mentioned. the full involvement of parliament in all stages of the life of the fund (created by an intergovernmental decision) is therefore ensured in accordance with the terms of the trilogue of 18 february - it should be emphasized here - particularly as regards the procedures for transferring funds and discharge, and the exceptional nature of the initiative and, consequently, the applicable rules are perfectly clear. i would like to thank all the members for their cooperation, particularly the work of those who took part in the trilogue, reimer böge and kathalijne buitenweg, and the contribution of the committees on constitutional affairs and budgetary control, which helped to give more direct attention to certain aspects. i hope that through this budgetary procedure we will also be associated with a convention which may make history.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i have tried, along with my colleagues, to follow the various aspects of the programme and the methodology or methodologies adopted for its implementation, and to listen to our constituents and to european cultural agents and operators in general, so that we can know half its planned duration. the commission will shortly have to review it, particularly in view of the financial shortcomings which have been noted from the outset, which are becoming increasingly apparent and which the president has just mentioned. it is also necessary that we all start thinking about the post-culture 2000 programme, that is, the programme that will succeed the current programme and which should take into account its successes and failures. the report i am presenting reflects the deep and broad opinion of all the members who voted for it in the committee. the virtually all the amendments tabled have been incorporated into the text. it therefore corresponds to the many sensitivities that are reflected in the parliamentary committee and it expresses very clearly the reactions of our electors. a report of this kind cannot fail to reaffirm the major objectives of culture 2000 and its role in building european citizenship and in defending cultural diversity and linguistic pluralism. it must also reaffirm the great inspirational principles which have guided the programme since its conception and which cannot be abandoned in the implementation phase under any pretext. these principles and objectives must be regarded as fundamental elements of european construction and the development of democratic citizenship for the benefit of all citizens of the union. as such, they have been carefully considered, approved and endorsed not only by this house but also by the council and the commission, and there is no reason why these positions should possible for culture 2000 to be succeeded by an even better programme. the vote in this house will thus mark an important date in the cooperation between parliament and the commission. the culture
