in writing. - (pt) the economic cycle's entry into a recessionary period in 2001 was not only aggravated by the fall in world demand and the sectoral consequences of the attacks in the united states, but above all by a policy of strict application of the stability pact and an obsession with nominal convergence. in practice, issues affecting the quality and standard of living of the population are left out, such as productive investment, especially public investment, the maintenance of compatible and productivity-enhancing wage growth, the profound inequalities in income distribution, high unemployment and poverty. these are objectives that are hampered by the rigidity of monetary policy on which the euro is based and which will eventually lead to greater difficulties or an undesirable increase in flexibility in other areas, particularly in the labour relations and in work itself. it is not surprising that a report which was supposed to focus on ways of supporting the sectors directly affected by the attacks of 11 september has become a reaffirmation of a fashionable dogma: in barcelona, we must accelerate the liberalization and reform of the labour market and blindly achieve the objectives defined by the stability pact without taking into account the problems linked to the phase of the cycle in which we find ourselves. the rapporteur nevertheless raises two positive points: that companies should not use 9/11 as an excuse for laying off staff and that the european central bank should be pressed to reduce its key interest rates. however, in view of the foregoing considerations, we cannot but vote against this report.
in writing. - (pt) this is a thorny question which requires the most serious analysis. an international judicial body dealing with crimes against humanity must be completely independent, which is not guaranteed in the case of the present icc. it is extremely dependent on the un security council. the risk that it will be used as a political instrument by the "winners" against the "losers", as an instrument of the law of the "strongest", is therefore not excluded. remember the intolerable example of the tribunal for the former yugoslavia. on the other hand, the sanctions envisaged must not mean a retreat from what we have achieved in terms of civilization, such as the abolition of life imprisonment, as is the case in portugal. it should also be borne in mind that the icc should be the most appropriate way of guaranteeing the non-impunity of the crimes provided for in its statute. it will also be necessary to ensure that this court complies with the appropriate rules as regards the principles of criminal law and the prerogatives of national courts in relation to these same crimes. we believe that this is not the case at present. finally, we cannot but criticize the proposal in the resolution in question concerning the revision of the geneva conventions on the status of prisoners of war, at a time when we are witnessing their total violation by the united states. that is why we voted against this resolution.
in writing. - (pt) despite its enormous shortcomings, we are voting for this joint resolution, if only to assure our support for all that can be done to fight for democracy in turkey, for the recognition of the rights of the kurdish people, especially their language, and to prevent the continuation of the attacks on political parties and the defenseless people. but we had to go further, as was the case in the declaration of our group, which i endorsed. i have no longer supported the joint resolution because of its enormous shortcomings. the resolution does not speak of the need for a general amnesty for all political prisoners, although it calls for the release of all students, and it removes the possibility of sending a delegation to turkey on 1 march 2002 in order to obtain objective information on the hadep judgment. nor does it stress the need to release leyla zana and the former kurdish deputies who were sentenced to 15 years in prison, when the secretary-general of the council of europe has asked turkey to retry these prisoners and the european court of human rights has issued decisions to that effect.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the european parliament's position at first reading on the draft directive on the opening up of postal services to competition was decisive. the council has in fact followed four of the five fundamental principles we have advocated. in essence, we have missed the point of setting the minimum weight limit for normal mail from 150 grams to 100 grams, but we must not forget that the minimum limit proposed by the commission was 50 grams. what is essential in this discussion? in my view, it is to safeguard postal services as a universal service to which all european citizens are entitled, regardless of where they live. it is also essential that postal services continue to fulfil the indispensable social function they have so far fulfilled, with the possibility of being competitive and of being able to provide increasingly up-to-date services. what is at issue, then? the point at issue is that by the end of this year the monopoly of postal services, which has been possible so far, will be abolished, and if we cannot reach a common position between the council, parliament and the commission in 2003, the commission will be able to deal with this problem as a matter of competition in the internal market, which is within its own purview, on another legal basis. the proposal we are now discussing is therefore the safest under the present circumstances. not only is the common position reasonable and accepted by the companies responsible for the postal services in our countries, but it still leaves any further liberalization subject to co-decision by the council and parliament. we therefore argue that we should vote in favour of the ferber report with the amendments we have approved in the committee on transport and tourism.
- mr president, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, we must stress that the protection of personal data is essential to ensure that every individual can have his or her fundamental rights and freedoms respected, particularly his or her right to privacy, throughout the european community. as has already been said, this proposal is intended to answer two essential questions: the working conditions of the european authority responsible for data protection, which remained unresolved in regulation 45/2001, namely the question of the remuneration of the european authority and the assistant authority and the question of their location. as regards the location, i agree with those who have said that it makes no sense to go anywhere but brussels for the reasons already mentioned and which i shall not repeat. however, there are some other questions which, perhaps because of the highly technical and abstract nature of the regulation, are still open and should be answered. provisions should be included here, for example, on procedural aspects and on how the authority should exercise its powers of investigation. it is also regrettable that there is no data protection instrument applicable to all the community pillars. the functions of this authority will be limited to the first pillar, with europol and schengen - for which data protection instruments are also provided - currently excluded from its scope, and a joint secretariat established in 2000. this means that few of the most sensitive data will fall within the remit of this european authority, which justifies the criticism of those who say that the pay scale is likely to be excessive for such a narrow set of functions. i sincerely hope that in the near future the current structure and organisation of this authority can be adapted so that it also integrates, under the responsibility of a single european authority, the data protection supervisory authorities operating in the european bodies established on the basis of titles v and vi of the treaty in order to avoid unnecessary duplication of functions and resources. at the same time, it would be good to be aware of the risk of some duopoly between the ombudsman and this european authority in order to optimise the use of community resources.
- mr president, the basis of this report is the decision to liberalise the internal energy market by privatising the public electricity and gas sectors that still exist in the european union. forgetting the negative experiences of similar processes, such as the one in california, the opening up, liberalization and competition of these strategic sectors is insisted upon in order to satisfy the interests of economic groups, even though it is known that this is putting at risk fundamental aspects of a universal public service: the right to a supply of energy of good quality at reasonable prices and the commitment to the development of alternative and renewable energies. while these reports express some concern about universal access, minimum environmental rules, consumer information and choice and guaranteed supply in each country, the truth is that the move towards full liberalisation and market opening will challenge these concerns. hence, of course, our vote against.
in writing. - (pt) in view of the guidelines contained in the goran färm report, the 2003 budget should not be very different from previous ones, which means that we will again have a tight budget, in the name of the stability pact. despite the emergence of new priorities which have resulted in increased needs, the policy is to take community money for old priorities and redistribute it to new priorities and vice versa. the aim is to extend the european union to more than 10 countries without changing the budgetary envelope or even making savings, which is unacceptable. the revision of the financial perspective is an essential condition for guaranteeing the principle of economic and social cohesion, a truly distributive budget and adequate financing of development and cooperation policy. this year the rapporteur gives priority to enlargement, but he is lost in administrative considerations without questioning the limited proposals for financing enlargement put forward by the commission, without guaranteeing transfers of appropriations to prepare for accession in 2002 and 2003, insisting on the priority of financing the communitarianisation of the third pillar - justice and home affairs - supporting the strengthening of the amounts allocated to europol, eurojust and the external borders, but forgetting fundamental social areas, in particular employment and the improvement of living conditions. it is concerned with budgetary implementation, especially of the structural funds, but it does not put forward any solution, i.e. no clear commitment to increase payments to levels that guarantee proper implementation. it ignores the reform of the common fisheries policy, whereas 2003 will be the first year of application (hence the proposal we are making) and it opens the door to co-financing of the common agricultural policy. the financial needs of category 4 - external policies - remain unclear at a time when priorities are piling up (palestine, timor, afghanistan, argentina, the balkans, the mediterranean and humanitarian aid) without additional resources. the rapporteur is balancing between redistribution and increase of the amounts, without ever expressing himself clearly. that is why we are not in favour of this report.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, it is very important that the european parliament should discuss and vote in favour of a report on women and fundamentalism. this is a very topical and sensitive political issue which must be dealt with carefully but with unquestionable firmness. we consider that in the european union, the equal rights of women and men, the right of all of us to non-violence in public and domestic life, in the exercise of the functions for which we have the capacity and the taste and interest to perform, is one of the greatest assets we have. we want to share these assets with all the women of the world. the violence we see in the world, especially the situation of afghan women under the taliban regime, unfortunately exists in other countries where women's dignity and will are trampled on for religious or ideological reasons. we must denounce and effectively combat the fundamentalisms that exist in the world and that allow such situations to exist. no one will understand why the european parliament does not take a strong stand on this matter. many compromise amendments have now been tabled. it is important that we make an effort to understand each other, that we have an open and tolerant spirit on this matter. there can be no fundamentalism on our part on this issue of intransigence in the face of the extremely serious situations in the world and in some cases, especially with regard to the decision on reproductive rights even within the european union. the aim of this report is to vote against all forms of fundamentalism. if the european parliament does not approve it, it would be extremely serious from a political point of view, it would be telling the world that the european parliament does not understand how to combat fundamentalism in the world.
- mr president, i too would like to thank commissioner fischler for the clarifications he has given us here, which i think are very useful. but i must confess, commissioner, that i am a little disappointed by the lack of vigour i see in your words in relation to this commitment, which i consider absolutely essential, to a gradual reduction in agricultural export subsidies. i note from the press that these refunds in the meat sector have increased very substantially since last december. i should also note that during 2001 this reduction had much to do with the rise of the dollar and not with any reform of agricultural policy; i would also like to point out that the subsidy volumes for all the products not listed in annex i have increased substantially. this is a situation which concerns me and i would like to ask commissioner fischler whether he thinks it realistic that these reforms of the cap which he has mentioned will be made in time to allow these export subsidies to be phased out.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, this owninitiative report has the extraordinary merit of placing the european parliament at the centre of the debate on the broad lines of european economic policy for the coming years. the rapporteur, on the basis of a correct and accurate diagnosis, rationally lists the real causes of the current european recession and, accordingly, defines an appropriate therapy. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives to improve the quality of the information it receives from the member states. the commission has also taken the view that the budgetary procedure should be simplified and that the budgetary procedure should be simplified. it recognises, however, and recommends appropriate flexibility, necessary for the use of public policies as instruments for economic recovery, without making the zero deficit in 2004 a fetish out of step with current reality. the same applies at structural level, by suggesting and supporting the application of instruments to improve the general economic environment, in particular in training, access to public services, the creation of the knowledge society and the adaptation of labour markets. finally, by taking up the objective of the extraordinary summit in lisbon and pursuing that objective at the extraordinary summit in barcelona.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. it is urgent to achieve a more effective cooperation practice and to achieve more success in the objective of sustainable development of various areas of the globe as a basic condition for a future with more security and more peace. it is to this strategy that the european union must devote its foreign policy capabilities, particularly because it is the only area in which europe can emerge victorious without producing losers. decentralized cooperation is one of those ideas which, emerging in a discreet way, forms part of a line of thought and action which is consistent with what i have just mentioned. as a concept, it lacks even greater precision. it is therefore appropriate to link it to the commission communication on civil society and development which commissioner poul nielson has promised us for next autumn. the european parliament, for its part, has always considered decentralized cooperation to be an approach to development which deserves all the support it can muster. however, the cotonou agreement also stresses the importance of the contribution of civil society to development processes and suggests 'the strengthening of the role of community and non-governmental organisations in all areas of cooperation'. the initiatives and actions covered by the support line referred to in this regulation are intended to promote a more participatory development and a greater diversification and strengthening of civil society, as well as various structures at local administrative level in a perspective of democratic construction. these are some of the main requirements for achieving effective poverty reduction. the commission now points to a new stage in which 'it is a question of moving from an experimental phase to a phase of consolidating the concept on a larger scale in the framework of official cooperation'. we are in complete agreement with this objective. it is true that the subject should be discussed more intensively among us, particularly because it is a codecision procedure, but the commission has sent us its proposal close to the expiry of the regulation, and the fact is that failure to approve it at first reading would create a legal vacuum that would harm precisely those whom we intend to support. in view of this concern, intensive work was carried out in a spirit of mutual collaboration involving the various ngos, the commission, the council and the spanish presidency. our contribution as the european parliament has been to bring greater coherence and cohesion to the regulation. some amendments have been made, and in the explanatory statement i describe the work on the search for an identity, so to speak, for decentralized cooperation. if the proposals on the table are adopted, we will have a regulation on decentralised cooperation which will guarantee us the following: first, an instrument extending until 2003 with a financial framework of eur 24 million; secondly, a commitment to consolidate decentralised cooperation as a vector of cooperation policy, which gives more prominence to local actors by encouraging their own responsibility in the development process of the societies in which they live and in their relations with european partners; and thirdly, a commitment to the future existence of decentralised cooperation itself, while reinforcing the objectives stated by the commission itself, namely 'the ownership by partner countries of their development strategies through a wider participation of all segments of society'.
the commission has already proposed that the basic regulation on the common organisation of the market in tobacco, regulation 2075/92, be amended by fixing the premiums and guarantee thresholds for leaf tobacco by group of varieties and by member state for the 2002, 2003 and 2004 harvests. on this basis, it could be assumed that the commission's proposal would be of a fundamentally technical nature and would simply adapt the main instruments of support for tobacco production in the light of the evolution of its supply and demand on the market. the commission has also been asked to make a number of adjustments, which are required by the current system itself, since the quotas must be notified to producers before 15 april and the corresponding contracts must be concluded before 30 may. however, under the pretext of the technical updating of regulation 2075/92, the commission's proposal goes much further. in fact, it introduces in a veiled form a reform of much wider political scope, even going so far as to indicate as its ultimate objective the progressive elimination of tobacco subsidies (see recital 5). the commission's decision to adopt the procedure is in fact a condition for the scope of the decisions which, in due course and on the basis of the studies and proposals submitted, will be taken by the community institutions at the appropriate time. this is for two reasons: firstly, because the commission has launched a study to evaluate the cmo for tobacco in the branch, a study which should allow an assessment of the effects of the current community policy on the sector and the conclusions of which will not be available until the end of this year; secondly, because the commission itself has undertaken to present proposals on the new tobacco policy at the beginning of next year, but only on the basis of the studies it is currently carrying out. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment and the environment from the adverse effects of pollution of the atmosphere by nitrates and their salts, which is to be adopted by the council on 1 january 1993, is a very important one. your rapporteur is of the opinion that the european parliament cannot accept that the commission anticipates and assumes the decisions that both the european parliament and the council will take in the coming months or next year, on the basis of the evaluation studies and the latest data recorded in the sector and which have been mentioned above. the rapporteur's amendments are limited to improving the commission's proposals in a realistic way, with an essentially technical approach, and refusing to engage in a substantive political debate on the future tobacco support scheme, which will have to be reviewed only in 2003 in the light of the studies mentioned above. it must be noted, however, that the rapporteur's position does not amount to a mere acceptance of the status quo, which seems to him to be unsustainable in the future. the rapporteur has therefore tabled amendment no 23 to restore the spirit of the initial commitment on the community tobacco fund. this amendment has two implications: firstly, to accept the fund's financing not only of tobacco prevention measures and experimental measures for conversion to other activities, but also to maintain the financing of agronomic research on tobacco varieties which are less harmful to the environment and health; and secondly, to raise the rate of withholding of aid from 2% to 3%, thus rejecting both the 2% status quo and the commission's proposal of 5%. and to ensure consistency with this view of compromise, the rapporteur proposes that amendments nos 9 and 13 should also be rejected. the approximately 130 000 tobacco growers in the european union, most of whom are located in disadvantaged areas in the southern regions of europe, deserve that the relevant community institutions should take a responsible interest in their future. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
the commission's proposal for a regulation on the compensation of the additional costs of ultraperipheral fishing for certain fishery products from the azores, madeira, the canary islands, french guiana and réunion, like the previous regulations, has proved to be an instrument of fisheries policy of enormous and indisputable economic, social and even cultural value. however, in accordance with article 6 of the same regulation, the commission should have submitted to the european parliament and the council, by 1 june 2001 at the latest, an evaluation report on the measures adopted in the fisheries sector in favour of the outermost regions, which, as is well known, are entitled to special support under the treaty of amsterdam, which has conferred a special status on them. the commission failed to achieve this objective by not complying with the deadline and by citing the need for a more in-depth analysis, using external consultants, against the background of the overall review of the common fisheries policy on the basis of the green paper, which has already been put to the public's attention. the results of these studies are a condition for the submission of the report, and the commission proposes to postpone its submission until 1 june 2002 and, as a logical consequence, to extend the current scheme for one year, until 31 december 2002, thus ensuring the continuity and stability of the current system of compensation for additional costs. in short, nothing to worry about. it is actually a pause for reflection. i therefore proposed to the committee on fisheries that it should give a favourable opinion, accompanied by certain amendments to the regulation which i consider appropriate. it is clear from the outset that the system of compensation for additional costs must be permanent, and that the time limits must be abolished, since the disadvantages of the outermost regions, as a result of their distance from the centres of consumption, are not factors which time and development will have to mitigate, but are rather of a structural nature, unless it is hoped that in the long run the fishing industry in these outermost regions will simply disappear. let me turn, to break the rigidity of these debates a little, to the powerful metaphor that josé saramago, nobel prize winner for literature, inserted in his novel "jangada de piedra" where he imagines a fracture between the iberian peninsula and the european continent and the consequent drift of that from the continent. i think that no one will dare, even ironically, to imagine that the peripheral islands will, even in the distant future, be drawn closer to the european continent, and that the current concept of ultraperiphery will therefore be obsolete. the system of support for the additional costs of marketing certain fishery products is therefore proposed as a matter of urgency, with a view to its gradual adaptation. the future regulation should therefore address this requirement. in my view, it is perfectly possible in the future to move towards a model of income support for fishermen in the outermost regions, based on the model which is being adopted and developed for income support for farmers. in any case, mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, the fishing activities in the azores, which provide some 8 000 jobs and have a major socioeconomic impact on the archipelago, the 1 500 fishermen in madeira, the fishing sector in the canary islands, which represents some 13 000 jobs, and the thousands of fishermen in french guiana and réunion cannot be left to uncertainties or the vagaries of chance. they must have security and stability, all the more so since they have been subject to severe challenges, as was the case recently with the failure of the agreement with morocco, which dealt a severe blow to the canary islands' fisheries. i therefore believe that it is imperative to increase support for fishing activities in the outermost regions, and that support should be even more generous than at present.
(parliament adopted the legislative resolution) the specific measures in force for the outermost regions are insufficient and exist only in the context of agriculture and fisheries - a framework much more modest than that established ten years ago, when the posei programmes were launched, and below the possibilities offered by the new article 299 (2) of the treaty on european union, which is incomprehensible and unacceptable. even in the areas covered, the respective 'financial envelopes' have remained constant or are decreasing today, in relative terms, compared to when the measures were launched. there have been limited successes under the measures in agriculture and the aid for the disposal of certain products has been extended for fisheries, as has just been proposed again, for an additional year. permanent schemes, periodically adapted, backed up by appropriate 'financial envelopes' are needed, not only for agriculture and fisheries, but also for energy, the environment, communications, new technologies, and aid to small and medium-sized enterprises.
in writing. - (pt) the community scheme for aid for the disposal of certain fishery products from the outermost regions, including the autonomous region of madeira, which was set up in 1992 as a result of the posei programmes, has been subject to successive extensions and revisions. parliament has consistently reiterated the need to adopt specific measures for the outermost regions, based on article 299 (2) of the treaty on european union. there is therefore no doubt that the instruments for supporting the outermost regions should be maintained as provided for in the proposal for a council regulation presented by the commission. i fully support this proposal for a regulation, which aims to extend the current scheme for one year, until 31 december 2002, thus ensuring the continuity of the legal framework for the compensation scheme for the extra costs incurred by the ultraperiphery of fishery products. the one-year extension will allow the commission to design a new aid scheme beyond 1 january 2003 in the light of the ongoing assessment of the impact of these aid measures on the fisheries sector in the outermost regions.
- mr president, the barcelona european council raised a number of reservations and doubts in advance, some of whom feared a setback in the lisbon strategy. while we were aware of the enormous ambition of the objectives set out in lisbon and of the different and more adverse economic circumstances at present, we never doubted that progress in social policy would, as it has been, be honoured by the member states and, in particular, by the spanish presidency of the european union, which i take the opportunity to congratulate. in this respect, the emphasis on the pursuit of european union policies to promote competitiveness and improve employment, to increase the occupational and geographical mobility of workers, which requires greater attention to labour training and better qualifications at the level of the knowledge society, as well as the recognition that the ageing population requires new and creative responses, should be emphasised. the proposal to create legislation providing for partial retirement as a means of voluntary permanence of older workers in the labour market, without prejudice to the need to accelerate pension reforms, is very welcome. one of the groups most affected by unemployment in the european union is women. we must promote their participation in the labour market, which, without active measures to reconcile family and working life, is a utopia. a joint effort is therefore urgently needed at community, intergovernmental, regional and local level to create childcare and support infrastructure for the elderly, in particular by making available and implementing european social fund funds, alongside the adoption of fiscal and social measures. we are convinced of the need for coordination and synchronisation between the social and economic dimensions. in fact, only with greater wealth creation distributed fairly and equitably can there be greater inclusion and more social justice.
in writing. - (pt) as i said in the resolution of the group of the european united left, to which we are attached, the barcelona summit addressed very important issues, particularly as regards the continuation and acceleration of privatisation, liberalisation in the energy, transport and telecommunications sectors and important aspects relating to workers' rights, also insisting on strict adherence to the stability pact. unfortunately, once again the european council is putting the emphasis on structural labour market reforms based on flexibilization, job precariousness and wage moderation. it is also important that the council recommends lowering the retirement age by five years. this dangerous path is a threat to social protection, which must be maintained and strengthened to support the fight against poverty and social exclusion. as the grandiose demonstrations of the trade union and association movement which took place in parallel with the barcelona summit have shown, despite the reprehensible attempts of demobilization and repression by the spanish government and police, a change in policy is necessary. it is essential to defend public services, to stop liberalising and precariousising the labour market, to fight against neoliberalism, to defend another world which is more united and more just. that is why we are voting against the joint motion for a resolution tabled by the other political groups, which we do not support.
the commission has already taken the initiative in the area of the environment, and i am sure that the commission will take the initiative in the area of the environment. we have often rightly criticised the commission; the commission's proposal for a regulation on the establishment of a european agency for the evaluation of medicinal products for human use is a good one. as we all know, under article 29 of the treaty, the union must pursue its objective of providing citizens with a high level of protection in an area of freedom, security and justice by establishing joint action between the member states in the field of police and judicial cooperation, including in criminal matters. it was with this in mind that the five biannual funding programmes under title vi, which are due to expire in december 2002, were created. these were - and still are - grotius ii, for legal professionals, oisin ii, for the competent law enforcement authorities of the member states, stop ii, for those responsible for action against trafficking in human beings and the sexual exploitation of children, hippocrates, in the field of crime prevention, and falcone, for those responsible for action against organised crime. the proposal under discussion today creates a single framework programme which aims to bring together and merge in a harmonized legislative and operational context all the actions covered by title vi of the treaty on european union, reflecting the evolution of community programmes in general and the experiences of five years in the field. we believe that this will ensure a more coherent package and an even greater impact of the actions supported, make it possible to use financial resources more efficiently, avoid any overlapping between the different programmes and close the existing gaps. although i regard the commission's approach as quite positive, i thought it appropriate and wise to introduce some amendments with the following objectives: - first, to give greater clarity to the programme's line of action and precise wording (even as the projects submitted will have to be examined in the light of their conformity with the objectives of the programme), thus rejecting vague or imprecise notions; - secondly, we support the inclusion in the programme of crossborder projects in the field of assistance to victims of criminal activities, along with the promotion of police and judicial cooperation. we are thus promoting the effective protection of the interests of victims in criminal proceedings in the member states, in particular through the creation of a network of contact points. it could also, for example, be appropriate to arrange for an independent study on the state of implementation of the framework decision, accompanied by proposals for improvement for each country; - thirdly, to recognise that it is important and appropriate to involve the applicant countries, but to make it clear that their participation should be at both the drafting and the implementation level. a mere participation in the project as a mere consumer cannot be considered sufficient; - fourth, to specify that the power of initiative for the formulation of proposals cannot be restricted to the public sector of the member states and the applicant countries alone, but must be extended to non-governmental organisations, universities and other interested entities; - fifthly, as regards financing, a maximum co-financing of 70% is provided for, which in some cases may reach 100%. these are specific projects and complementary measures. 100% community funding is only justified for projects which are of the greatest interest to the european union itself and which cannot be carried out on the basis of lower financial support. but it is worth pointing out here, and i am sure that this is also the approach of commissioner antónio vitorino, that the underlying idea is to try to finance as many projects as possible with the available resources; - i know . the commission's proposal is consistent with parliament's wish to increase support for projects to promote the creation of an area of freedom, security and justice, but it is a little difficult to accept a proposal that goes beyond the current financial perspective. the commission has therefore proposed that this framework programme should run from 2003 to 2006, after which it should be renewed in line with the new financial perspective. and finally, i regret that the european parliament's position is not sufficiently cautious. i therefore propose that parliament be given the opportunity to be involved, which will certainly enable it to exercise its responsibility as budgetary authority in the best possible way. the construction of an area of freedom, security and justice is one of the fundamental objectives of this parliament, and its association is therefore of the utmost importance.
- mr president, i would first like to congratulate mr di lello finuoli and mr oomen-ruijten on the excellent work they have done in converging their efforts to create instruments to combat the increasing scale and frequent cross-border effects of environmental crime. i cannot help but recall one of the latest eurobarometer surveys, which confirms that the environment, together with safety, health and unemployment, is one of the major concerns of the citizens of the european union. we therefore have an obligation to help find solutions that can address these concerns and that can ensure protection of the environment and the health of citizens, while seeking to raise the quality of life for all. on the one hand, we have an initiative put forward by denmark (in 2000) for the adoption of a council framework decision on the fight against serious environmental crime, which proposes that the member states adopt concerted measures to protect the environment in the framework of criminal law, including effective criminal investigations and prosecutions, as well as effective cooperation in police, criminal and administrative matters, that is, to establish an acquis on criminal sanctions against environmental crimes. on the other hand, we have an initiative by the commission (in 2001) to create a directive on the protection of the environment through criminal law on the basis of article 175 of the ec treaty. this proposal is intended to apply to activities which are in breach of community environmental legislation and/or rules adopted by the member states to enforce it. i think we should commend the strategy of both rapporteurs, di lello finuoli and oomen-ruijten, of making both reports as close as possible to each other, so that the european parliament's position on the two initiatives is clear and consistent. the aim is to give clear indications of parliament's unequivocal preference for the commission's approach, i.e. the adoption of a first pillar instrument, since legislation on environmental matters is a community competence. it should be noted, however, that criminal law is not the exclusive competence of the european union, i.e. the present system of penalties is not sufficient to ensure the application of community law, and as such it is essential that minimum rules be guaranteed which imply the existence of effective, proportionate and dissuasive penalties, although it is up to the discretion of the member states to adopt stricter rules and penalties in this area.
the rapporteur. - mr president, when someone knocks on the door of europe fleeing some violence, some war, some civil war, he is a refugee in the light of the geneva convention and he is seeking asylum in the name of fundamental human rights. you cannot tell him frankly that he has got the wrong street or the wrong number and that it is not there that the matter is, but somewhere else, somewhere in another country, that someone will tell him which, if he insists, if he insists and asks himself... it is to avoid these and other situations which we often encounter in europe that the commission has drawn up this proposal for a regulation at the right time, a kind of road map for the right of asylum, which tells us, for each situation and specific case, who and where is obliged to give an answer, which country should examine an asylum application; after all, that door opens up to the anguish of a refugee who is only waiting for someone to talk to. the nature of the problem shows the extent of the initiative. we will not be solving the whole asylum issue, but we are certainly taking a step. as rapporteur, i am proud to have done everything possible to get this initiative approved, and i have a clear conscience about it, giving the commission the strength it needs in the fight it will have to fight with some - i hope not many - member states. i would like to thank the members and colleagues of the committee on civil liberties and justice and particularly the rapporteur, mrs eva klampt, without whose efforts and understanding we would hardly have been able to vote for this initiative of the commission. thank you very much, mrs klampt. it is that, mr president, we have been living lawlessly in a sea of good intentions. norms, as they come, ratified by whoever, are valid here and not there, doors are opened and others are closed, and it is in this swampy area, where little law is mixed with no law, that the expedients, the tricks, the skills, the escape from the authorities are created. and so the clandestine networks are born, the mafias proliferate, crime, of course inexcusable for those who commit it, but only possible because the states, good people, have never agreed on minimum rules to objectively hold one of them responsible for the analysis of an asylum application. the commission's proposal is a bold one. it ensures that an application is actually examined, prevents the confusion of multiple applications and the existence of refugees in orbit, and subjects these rules to its supervision and to the control of the court of justice. it is based on the principle that each member state is responsible for the irregular entry or stay of an asylum seeker, and requires each state to improve its mechanisms for combating illegal immigration. this responsibility, based on objective facts, only gives way to additional criteria having to do with the values of family unity. the minor is therefore accompanied by the adult's asylum application and the unaccompanied minor is sent, in the form i propose, to the state where any relative who wants to take them or can take them is present. similarly, entry or residence criteria may be waived when the unity of the family group needs to be ensured where one of the members has already applied for asylum in a certain member state. finally, mr president, the broadest possible concept of the family is on the table, by virtue of the initial proposal and the feasible amendments, and whatever the result of the vote on these: it covers marriage and de facto unions where any of the states has granted legal recognition to them. the elimination of discrimination on grounds of sex, and the protection of kinship for parents and minor children, extended to other children and other relatives when cohabitation and dependence on them in the country of origin are proven. and also, for humanitarian reasons, health reasons or otherwise, the asylum seeker may, in the formulation approved in my report, approach any other relative provided that the requested state accepts him. the essential thing, mr president, is to ensure that the asylum application and the refugee behind it are answered and that the european legal framework serves the rights of the applicants, the obligations of the states and prevents the absence of law from leading to a resurgence of racism and xenophobia. this initiative guarantees us these three objectives and i therefore believe that it will naturally be approved.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the question of asylum has been high on the political agenda of the european union and its member states, especially since the entry into force of the treaty of amsterdam, which gave the community new powers and opened up new possibilities for action at european level. the proposal we are discussing today is part of the process of establishing a common european asylum system based on the full and comprehensive application of the geneva convention, which aims to create a clear, simpler and more operational method for determining the state responsible for examining an asylum application lodged in one of the member states by a third-country national in the context of an asylum procedure which is intended to be fair and effective. the proposal contains innovative provisions which aim to end the process which has proved to be very time-consuming by providing for shorter procedural time limits, and to increase its effectiveness by facilitating cooperation between member states, thus creating a system which is fair and humane for asylum seekers, which is of the utmost importance not only for asylum seekers but also for the member states themselves, by creating uniform rules directly applicable throughout the european union, ensuring that each and every asylum application is de facto examined, while avoiding the possibility of multiple applications being lodged in several states. i welcome the efforts of the rapporteur, mr luís marinho, and of mrs eva klamt, who have tried to achieve a balanced text that is in line with the intended objective. it is now urgent that we all work together to prevent the adoption of unnecessary amendments which could make unworkable the balance of the text which has been obtained in the committee on civil liberties.
- mr president, commissioner, i would like to congratulate all our rapporteurs on their excellent work and to thank the commission sincerely for their cooperation. i would like to emphasize the constructive and european spirit of our colleague mr mccartin and the support provided by the commission's agriculture directorate-general. the mccartin report is an important element in the process of reforming the common policy. i hope that the other european institutions will take this into account by preparing the necessary measures for the phasing out of agricultural export subsidies and for these subsidies to be used sooner for a purpose laid down in the treaty to ensure a fair standard of living for the agricultural population. i would like to say that in the field of food fraud, however, we need to go much further. as regards dairy products - a key sector in the azores - i cannot accept that farmers should live in the constant anguish of paying fines for overproduction while european criminal organizations counterfeit tens of thousands of tonnes of butter with the complicity of large european dairy groups, while the dairy industries receive subsidies for milk powder, which is, after all, dried cheese whey, while large dairy companies sell, as dairy farmers, the equivalent of hundreds of millions of litres of milk from counterfeit products which are not made from milk. and the commission has little or nothing to say about this! and if you look at other domains, like olive oil, wine or even meat, the picture is not much different. food falsification, often done to obtain export or marketing subsidies, is now one of the main problems in budgetary control of agriculture. that is why we urge the commission to draw up a plan of action in this area as soon as possible to put an end to this state of affairs.
- mr president, in this debate, and after hearing the statements by the council and the commission, which i thank for their quality and timeliness, it seems appropriate to recall that the european union still has no provision in its treaties for mutual military assistance, such as exists in nato or the weu, nor for specific provisions on cooperation in the field of armaments. as far as europe is concerned, these matters are, as is well known, regulated in the revised brussels treaty in the context of the so-called residual tasks of the western european union. in addition, since 11 september, we all have a clearer and more conscious notion that a new definition of defence is needed, combining internal and external security. we must now be able to deal with enemies who, although they have no army, endanger the security of countries and citizens without the traditional armies being able to give adequate answers in terms of their defence. in fact, in the globalised world we live in, politics, diplomacy, information, internal security and defence complement each other to respond to these new types of threats. security has become a global and indivisible concept, different from traditional conceptions. the european union and its member states are thus obliged to rationalise their military and arms production capabilities and to strengthen the means of diplomatic cooperation, the intelligence services and judicial and police cooperation, in a genuine synergy of national and transnational policies. as regards relations with the atlantic alliance, we believe cooperation with nato and not in the temptation, dangerous and useless, of any competition with this organization. finally, a word on parliamentary control of the esdp, which, as is well known, is carried out at two levels: the european level, which is ensured by this parliament in relation to the prevention and civilian management of crises, and the national level in relation to the military management of crises, this type of control being exercised by the national parliaments. this matter has been recently discussed and decided upon in this house. it is therefore absolutely appropriate to call for closer relations and greater exchange of information between the european parliament and national parliaments on esdp issues in order to enable the various parliamentary bodies to carry out their democratic control in a more appropriate and appropriate way.

mr president, this parliament is debating today a matter which concerns the world. the second united nations world assembly on ageing is currently taking place in madrid. i therefore congratulate mr fatuzzo on the preparation of this report which highlights the urgency of addressing the problem of the sustainability of pension systems in the future. the european union faces a major challenge for the coming decades due to the increase in population ageing linked to the decline in the birth rate and the increase in life expectancy. the ageing of the population and the decline in the number of people in the labour force require the formulation and implementation of innovative and effective policies. in the european union, there is an urgent need for member states to reform pension systems at an accelerated pace. and this implies changes in the organization of work, the creation of more and better jobs, especially for the elderly, the reformulation of demographic and birth rate policies, as well as of emigration policies. if these reforms are delayed, as the rapporteur says, the european social model, growth and economic stability of the european union will suffer serious consequences. one of the most important measures in this context is the promotion of policies which promote the idea of active ageing, i.e. every citizen should be able to choose to retire or to remain in the labour market on a full-time or part-time basis. this requires incentives for retirement at an advanced age, the promotion of lifelong learning and improved health systems. the legislation governing social security is, as is well known, the exclusive responsibility of the member states, which explains the considerable differences between them. however, the importance of this issue and its socioeconomic implications require a global approach and strategies. the european union should therefore play a leading role in supporting national strategies through an open method of coordination, agreeing common objectives and working methods, and encouraging the exchange of experience and good practice alongside the continuous and consistent assessment of progress made. another aspect to be highlighted is the considerable proportion of older people in the member states who are victims of poverty and social exclusion. pension schemes should contribute to eliminating such problems and to maintaining standards of living worthy of old age. finally, i cannot fail to mention that the current pension systems are particularly unfavourable to women, and that member states should give priority to gender equality and promote european legislation which effectively guarantees the principle of non-discrimination on grounds of sex in pension systems.

- doc. a5-0091/2002 by mrs damião, on behalf of the committee on employment and social affairs, on the proposal for a european parliament and council directive amending council directive 83/477/eec on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work (com (2001) 417 - c5-0347/2001 - 2001/0165 (cod)).
the commission has already taken a number of initiatives in this area, and i am sure that the commission will take them up. however, it has the advantage of focusing on the groups of workers currently at greatest risk and also of including sectors which in fact had no reason to be excluded, such as the shipping and aircraft industries. the commission rightly states the need to extend the protection others, and we therefore hope that the commission will support this revision in the direction of better legislative practice in this area.
- mr president, it has long been recognized that asbestos can cause serious diseases, including various types of cancer. such an agent poses a danger not only to workers exposed to it but to public health in general. the commission's proposal, which is being welcomed and which aims to amend this directive for the third time, arises from the need to strengthen the existing requirements in order to safeguard the health and safety of workers, especially in sectors where the risks of exposure are now recognized to be the most dangerous. it also aims to provide greater clarity in the definition of the various types of fibres, as many doubts remain about them and the risks involved in the minimum exposure dosage. the rapporteur, elisa damião, has taken a balanced position on a sensitive and very technical subject and i congratulate her for that. emphasis should be placed on the prevention and minimisation of exposure of workers, particularly in the removal, maintenance, remediation or demolition of buildings. however, the measures envisaged for the exposure limit values appear excessive for asbestos-cement, a substance which contains only 10% fibre and therefore poses a lower degree of risk. the vocational training provided by employers, improving the financing arrangements for small and mediumsized enterprises and selfemployed workers, is another of the measures that is being imposed and welcomed. as well as the maintenance of medical records and medical records for a period of 40 years. the amendment of this directive not only provides greater protection and safety for workers but also avoids unnecessary conditions for employers.
mr president, without any parliamentary obligation, i would first like to congratulate and commend mr peter skinner on the quality and depth of the report he has presented to us, which, moreover, does not require me to make any specific comments on the subject since he has dealt with them very well in his report. the economic recovery of europe, which is absolutely essential if all the political objectives of the union are to be achieved, must depend more on the quality of its own policies than on the result of the favourable impulses it receives from the recovery of other economic areas. the union set itself the clear and imperative objective in lisbon in 2000 of making the european union the most dynamic and competitive economy in the world by 2010. achieving this objective requires, on the one hand, making full use of the potential resulting from the generalization of the information and knowledge society and the liberalisation of certain markets, particularly in the energy sector, and, on the other hand, defining and implementing public competition and competitiveness policies that create an environment conducive to sustained economic development. the community's europe is an economy based on an entrepreneurial fabric in which private enterprise is dominant and the culture of innovation and risk is an inescapable reality. what is required, therefore, of national policy regulators and the appropriate community institutions is to promote, not hinder, all these innovation efforts. the risk capital action plan which the lisbon european council welcomed and called for its full implementation by 2003 is one of these instruments and certainly one of the most effective instruments available. it is therefore important to take stock of them and monitor their application, not only to record the progress made, but above all to motivate and appeal to all the relevant forces in the member states for the need to progressively and continuously pursue a healthy entrepreneurial spirit based on a culture where risk is a factor permanently under control. the use of risk capital as a means of promoting investment in new enterprises, especially in sectors where there is high uncertainty at the beginning of the production process, should be encouraged and defended, since the enormous potential it generates and multiplies is not yet exhausted. the commission mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the proposal for a common organization of the market in ethyl alcohol of agricultural origin put forward by the commission is based on good intentions. the aim is to create conditions for greater market regulation and greater transparency and fairness in trade within and outside the european union. it should also make an important contribution to the functioning of the single market in this field. its use in sectors such as the spirits, chemical, cosmetic and pharmaceutical industries gives it strategic importance in the european context. but agricultural alcohol accounts for only about half of the alcohol consumed in the european union, the rest being synthetic. this type of alcohol, with good conditions of competitiveness and substitutability in all uses except for alcoholic beverages, has been increasingly widespread in the european union. however, the commission proposal covers only alcohol of agricultural origin and does not include synthetic alcohol, as mrs ayuso gonzález has said. as a consequence, it is not possible to achieve the slightest degree of effective regulation of the market by excluding the latter type of alcohol, either without rules or with different rules. it should also be noted that the commission proposal did not solve the problem of small and very small distillers which has so far been solved through national schemes and national aid. for the reasons i have given, it seems reasonable to reject the commission's proposal now, as the rapporteur proposes. parliament therefore rightly expressed its dissatisfaction with the commission's intentions and asked it to submit a new proposal to regulate the whole market in alcohol, agricultural and non-agricultural, and to resolve the outstanding problems with the various national schemes in force. the issues of bioethanol and enlargement should also be considered in the future proposal.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the strategy for international action of the united nations world assembly is based on the commitment to achieve a society for all ages, devoting political and financial resources to this end. it is essential that the european union and its member states make this commitment by mainstreaming ageing issues in community policies and by including older people in social, economic and development programmes, paying particular attention to older people at risk of isolation and developing the principle of active ageing by promoting the possibilities for older all these aspects into account in an integrated approach and after hearing the organisations of pensioners and the elderly. the european union should also do more to provide international aid to developing countries to enable them to develop policies on ageing, in particular in favour of women and the fight against aids.
in writing. - (pt) once again - this is the fourth time - the commission is asking the european parliament to vote on restrictive measures targeting certain categories of persons and entities in order to combat international terrorism, without any prior debate on this subject, either in plenary or in the appropriate committee. it is a method we reject and that is why we voted against it. reaffirming our strongest and clearest condemnation of the terrorist acts perpetrated in the usa, and the need to develop a sustained response in the context of joint efforts and international cooperation, within the framework of the un and in full respect of its charter, we consider it unacceptable to submit to parliament's approval a list of persons and organisations which is not based on any foundation or evidence, and this is the second reason why we vote against. we also believe that the necessary fight against terrorism cannot be used as a pretext to touch upon essential aspects of the sovereignty of the member states of the european union.
in writing. - (pt) with the proposal for a directive which it is submitting to us, the commission wants, albeit in a softer way: to reorient the measures for the protection of the most exposed persons; review exposure levels and review the method for assessing the asbestos fibre content in the atmosphere; to insist on measures to prevent or minimise exposure and to consider the assessment of the risks associated with work in relation to which exposure to asbestos is intrinsic or incidental. through our work in committee and in plenary, we have helped to improve the report with the help of proposals adopted by rapporteur elisa damião and the plenary, including: - the need to pay particular attention to respect for labour law, which is of paramount importance in this sector, in particular the fight against precariousness of work and the respect for health and safety standards at work; - the need for member states to pay particular attention to workers' rights, in particular health, in situations of closure of mines or production units; - the abolition of exceptions provided that it is proven that the cases envisaged do not pose a risk to workers. we now expect the commission and the council to accept the european parliament's proposals.
in writing. - (pt) we support the deepening of relations between the member states of the european union and the mediterranean countries and we consider that action must be taken on the basis of the deepening of cooperation and economic and cultural relations between these countries, with respect for the interests of all and of each, in favour of the development and improvement of the living conditions of each people. we therefore criticise the european commission's proposals, supported by the council, to use the 'barcelona process' to lead, in the context of the process of association agreements with these countries and the creation of a 'free trade area', to the practice of liberalising services and agriculture and, in general, to the neoliberal orientations advanced at the wto conference in doha. we are also concerned about the objective of making the fight against terrorism a central political priority, without considering its root causes (such as the resolution of the conflict in the middle east or western sahara, in the framework of un resolutions), or the response to the most basic needs and shortcomings affecting many populations, ensuring economic development and the improvement of social conditions in the mediterranean countries.
in writing. - (pt) the mediterranean region, by virtue of its strategic dimension, mainly in the context of enlargement to the west and the new international political context following 9/11, justifies further strengthening and deepening of euro-mediterranean relations and, therefore, of the barcelona process. the main objective of the barcelona process is the development of a euro-mediterranean area of peace and shared prosperity. there is therefore no doubt about the importance and need to continue, revitalize and deepen existing cooperation instruments. i therefore support the action plan proposed by the commission for the euro-mediterranean interministerial conference to be held in valencia. it considers that this plan reflects the concerns and challenges currently facing the euro-mediterranean partnership. however, i would like to end by highlighting three points in the action plan which i consider to be priorities: strengthening the mediterranean countries' sense of belonging and partnership through a reorganisation of dialogue structures and working methods; support for the private sector; and strengthening the links between cultures and civilisations.
in writing. - (pt) in general, this report is part of the action plan on financial services and capital markets integration, as agreed at the lisbon summit and reiterated in stockholm, with the deadline of 2003 for the integration of securities markets and 2005 for the full integration of financial markets. the myth of private equity as a way of solving smes' financing and start-up capital needs, as if this integration were the solution to their problems, is being replaced by the myth of a further financialization of the economy through the private equity market and the participation of smes in volatile markets, whose main funders are the large pension funds. the rapporteur's request for the liberalization of pension funds' investment in private equity, the tax exemption for capital gains from smes, the defence of the community patent and tax advantages for investments by private equity funds is therefore appropriate. for all these reasons, we voted against.
- mr president, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, once again angola has the attention of the european parliament. today for better reasons than those which led us to discuss angola in this house last time. the whole international community, and the european union in particular, must make it clear that we expect all parties to do everything possible to make this peace process final and irreversible. the angolan government was right when it stopped all offensive military measures and the political leaders and military leaders of unita were right. all of you have shown a capacity for dialogue which should be underlined and applauded. it is now urgent to lay the foundations of a democratic life in common. we must demilitarize our forces and extend state administration to the entire territory. unita must be integrated into democratic life. the conditions must be created for the democratic rule of law to take root, respecting the rights of the opposition, freedom of the press, strengthening the transparency of public authorities, fighting corruption and arbitrariness, combating poverty and social exclusion and promoting the development of the country in a peaceful environment. it is urgent to reintegrate into normal civilian life those who formerly engaged in war and to re-settle all those who have been driven from their lands by the violence of the civil war. as soon as the necessary conditions are created, elections must be held. i would also like to appeal to all parties to work towards a speedy clarification of the situation of portuguese children who have been missing for months. our word is thus one of encouragement. the european union is not only a political force, but also a political force. even from thousands of kilometres away, our voice can be heard in luanda! good luck to you, angola!
- mr president, mr commissioner, there is every reason to look at our debate today on the situation in angola with a much greater degree of optimism and hope than in previous debates. the rapid development and the positive prospects now opening up for the country and the angolan people allow a different approach to the situation in the country, now fundamentally oriented towards the implementation of measures that will enable the consolidation of peace and the reconstruction, development and progress of a country and a people martyred by decades of war. and on this occasion it is important to underline and applaud the enormous steps that have been taken in this short period of time since the death of jonas savimbi. we particularly welcome the significance and scope of the memorandum of understanding signed on 30 march in the framework of and as a complement to the lusaka protocol, as well as the amnesty proposed by the government and approved on 3 april by the national assembly, and the formal signing of the ceasefire on 4 april by the angolan armed forces and unita. these measures, together with the emergency programme presented by the government and aimed at the return of four million displaced persons to their regions of origin, the reintegration of 150 000 demobilized and former combatants and the integration of 100 000 maimed and 50 000 war orphans, constitute the fundamental framework for launching the country into a new era. but for this new era to be a reality without return, the cooperation and commitment of all angolan political and social forces is now more necessary than ever to ensure the mobilization of resources and the capable and indispensable will of the new country that seems to want to be reborn. in this context, and at the same time as the essential and definitive cessation of unita's military activity and its total demilitarisation, it is also important to ensure and encourage its full integration into normal angolan political life as an unarmed political party. the active and positive contribution of the international community, including the european union, is also crucial, with a view to providing urgent humanitarian aid to the angolan people and, in general, to the reconstruction of the country. as regards the european union, and in view of the weakness and destruction of that country and the enormous effort of reconstruction and rehabilitation that must be made, and also the importance that this country assumes in the regional context, special attention and support and perhaps even the adoption of a specific programme of aid for the consolidation of peace and the reconstruction of angola are well justified. the situation is developing in an encouraging way. the measures and instruments already adopted are seen as being in the right direction. the very prospect of elections, which has already been announced, indicates the will to pursue the path of political normalisation, even if the path is not without difficulties and choices. the time is optimistic and all our action must be determined by unlimited support for the realization of the hope that angolans are imbued with today.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, by awarding the sakharov prize to the angolan archbishop zacarias camuenho, the european parliament wanted to support the struggle of all those in angola who were working for peace against the relentless logic of violence and war. we could hardly have imagined then that events would have precipitated events in such a way that today great hopes of peace and reconciliation are being raised in that martyred country. the award of that prize has undoubtedly helped to strengthen the spirit and the demands for peace that have been imposed on all following the disappearance of the unita chief. angola is now leaving this tragic category of countries that have been called "of endless wars" and entering a period of building a lasting peace. this is something that all of humanity should welcome, especially all those who love angola and feel infinite pity for the untold sufferings of its people, who have been the victims for decades of a bitter struggle between the two factions vying for power. in angola there are only heroes and martyrs among your anonymous people, nothing more! this extraordinary people now have the right to peace, freedom, democracy and progress. you have the right to bread and freedom, you have the right to the future. only then can the great hopes raised by decolonization and independence finally be realized. the international community now owes more than ever moral and material solidarity to the angolan people. there are thousands and thousands of abandoned and missing children. and at this point we cannot but also demand the truth about the lives of two young portuguese men who have recently disappeared in a brutal ambush and about whom a great mystery hangs. there are thousands and thousands of maimed people in angola and millions of displaced people. everyone is waiting for a solution to their dramas. the restoration of peace and the reconstruction of angola will have to respond to this situation of a real human catastrophe. the conditions for peace and democracy will only be truly achieved in angola when the monopoly of power is over and the diversity of political currents can express themselves without hesitation or fear, when power is truly democratized and when there is a fair and equitable distribution of resources. we must be optimistic and cautious at the same time. optimistic because angola is now on the right track, prudent and vigilant so that there are no distortions and setbacks. we are particularly surprised that the voices of leading intellectuals, religious figures and independent politicians have become too quiet. in fact, without the full emergence of civil society in angola, everything can be more fragile, limited and uncertain. in any case, it is time to say: long live angola!

- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, once again we are discussing proposals for the establishment of a common european asylum system. in this area too, the aim is to pursue the objective of an area of freedom, security and justice open to persons who, for various reasons, legitimately seek protection in the community. in accordance with the tampere conclusions, a european asylum system should be established, which should include, in the short term, common minimum conditions for the reception of asylum seekers. such conditions should allow for a decent standard of living and respect for their fundamental rights. the aim is to limit secondary movements of asylum seekers affected by disparities in reception conditions between member states, by ensuring that applicants have comparable living conditions in all member states and that there is a fair distribution of efforts between the member states of the union. i recognise that integration into local communities is complex and difficult, but it is the path that must be followed, with repercussions on the right to health, education and work. as regards access to work, freedom of access should be allowed, although it is right that it should be conditional for a period of six months. our rapporteur, mr hernández mollar, is right when he suggests that we must approach this matter seriously and without demagogy. too much voluntarism in these matters only helps those whose discourse is based on intolerance, racism and xenophobia. that is why, mr president, ladies and gentlemen, it is right to send a message to the left-wing benches of this parliament: those who go too far in the matter of asylum, as in other matters, become the greatest ally of the extremism which they say they want to combat but which they end up encouraging.
