- madam president, like other members, i too believe that there is no reason for excessive euphoria about the results of the monterrey conference. it is true that some small steps were taken during the conference, especially if we consider the current situation of development finance and if we take into account the very limited prospects that were already emerging before the conference. these steps naturally include both the european union's offers to increase its public aid by $20 billion by 2006 and the $5 billion that the united states has proposed to increase annually. and i also take account of the fact that the european union has set for the first time, and with binding force for the member states, a precise and targeted target of 0.39% of gdp in 2006 as a community average and as an intermediate step towards the 0.7% target. but we must recognize that these are too short steps, far short of the needs and possibilities of the present. and another conclusion we cannot help drawing if we take into account the steady declines in official development aid over the last decade, which only a limited number of member states of the european union have steal away from, as if to confirm the rule. and that conclusion is equally unequivocal if we take into account the decisions and recommendations that have been repeatedly made by the united nations, including the millennium development goals to reduce extreme poverty by 2015. or even the very hopes created by the commitment made at the gothenburg council to reach the 0.7% target as soon as possible. in addition, however, no action has been taken to resolve the problem of external debt, although it remains one of the fundamental parameters of the economic development of the poorest countries, as our colleague mr mantovani states in the explanatory statement to his report, which we welcome. and in this context, given the importance of the financial aspect, i believe that the outcome of the world summit on sustainable development next september is somewhat limited in its desirable scope. we hope, however, that in this and other areas more promising steps will be taken in johannesburg.
in writing. - (pt) the common text that has been adopted is clearly insufficient, despite the positive aspects that deserve to be highlighted. nevertheless, its practical application can help to involve social organisations and movements, as well as local and regional authorities, more closely in the implementation and evaluation of national action plans for employment. it is equally important to achieve, as the adopted text states, a quantitative and qualitative evaluation of the effects of the european employment strategy, including an analysis of its coherence with general economic policy, as well as with other policy areas, and support through the european employment observatory. but, most important of all, it is about changing political priorities within the european union and pursuing a genuine policy of quality employment, with rights and aiming at full employment, which is far from the case.
in writing. - (pt) the report which has been voted on improves certain aspects of the proposal put forward by the commission; it is regrettable, however, that after having decided to establish a common asylum policy, the european union now limits itself to establishing a set of minimum standards, as if it did not know that the men and women for whom they are intended deserve full support, given the conditions that have generally led them to seek refuge in one of the member states. furthermore, we cannot but reject the commission's objective of making these 'minimum standards' a mere contribution to limiting the secondary movements of asylum seekers, which arise from national disparities in terms of reception conditions.
in writing. - (pt) the adoption of this report is a positive development. it begins by stating that one of the main objectives of the european union must be to maintain the universality and indivisibility of human rights, including civil, political, economic and cultural rights, while recognising the fundamental responsibility of the united nations in the maintenance of international peace and security, and that the european union must actively work to further strengthen its cooperation with the united nations and other international organisations in the areas of conflict prevention, crisis management, humanitarian aid, post-conflict rehabilitation and development at the global level. another important point is that the report stresses that the fight against terrorism cannot compromise the protection of fundamental human rights and must be based on international human rights standards and international humanitarian law, as well as the call to the council and the member states to include state terrorism in the definition of terrorism and reaffirming that all forms of forced labour, trafficking in human beings and sexual exploitation of children are human rights violations that amount to a form of slavery, stressing the call to the united states of america and somalia to ratify the un convention on the rights of the child. however, the report contains aspects with which we do not agree, particularly references to the international criminal court.
in writing. - (pt) while it is important to adopt a five-year action plan (2001-2006) to put good political intentions into practice in the context of equality policy and community development cooperation policy, thus ensuring the continuity of the 1995 beijing declaration and the respective platform for action, it is regrettable that this proposal comes so late. given that the elimination of inequality between men and women and the empowerment of women are crucial for social justice and effective development efforts, it is important to emphasise that mainstreaming equality promotion should not be seen as a means of promoting the effectiveness of development cooperation interventions alone, but as part of a much broader struggle for human rights and the role of women and men, whose differences must be recognised, but whose rights must be equal. the emphasis should also be placed on strengthening financial and human resources to support the planned actions, given that only €2.02 million was spent in 2001 on mainstreaming gender equality in development cooperation, compared with €5 million in 1998.
in writing. - (pt) during the first four years of the map iv (1997-2001), the community fleet was reduced by 2.5% in terms of tonnage and by 5.9% in terms of engine power. however, despite the relatively modest targets set by the map iv, some member states have not achieved them and others have even increased their capacity. this means that this report shows that the problem is always the same: some member states, like mine, have achieved the goals set while others have not. worse still, these states have not been penalised for their failure. at a time when the european commission is about to make its proposals for the future of the common fisheries policy, it is essential to rethink the pops because the issue of conservation of resources will remain one of the fundamental dimensions of the cfp, because without fish, fishermen have no future. it is therefore necessary to say that all member states - not just some - will have to contribute to this objective. it is on this basis that pops - or other measures with equivalent effect - continue to have a place in the cfp, provided that there is a minimum effective sanction regime. if this is not the case, this system, or any other system to adapt the size of the fleet to existing resources, will be doomed to failure.
in writing. - (pt) as a general rule, the report deals with the question of the application of the pops in a positive manner, stressing the degree of compliance which varies greatly depending on the country and considering that the maintenance of the current aid for the modernisation and renewal of fleets is essential to make the multiannual guidance programmes operational and thus to achieve the priority objective of a sustainable balance between the resources available and their exploitation. this is particularly important at a time when the commission intends to reduce substantially or even to end support for fleet modernisation and renewal. unfortunately, the report does not identify which countries are not meeting the targets. as you know, portugal has already exceeded the planned reduction by more than 40%, which is all the more regrettable as some countries have increased their fishing capacity instead of reducing it. it is therefore essential to demand that not all countries are treated equally in the future. we do not entirely subscribe to the policy of ship-wrecking, because we support aid for refurbishment, not for ship-wrecking.
the rapporteur. - mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, tourism is one of the main economic and social sectors of the european union because of the wealth it generates, the jobs it creates, and its essential contribution to the preservation of europe's cultural and historical heritage. it is also a key sector for achieving full employment in the european union, as envisaged at the lisbon summit, and is central to regional policy by boosting growth and employment in less developed areas, especially in europe's periphery and outermost regions. finally, it is a sector in which europe is still the region that receives the most tourists in the world and in which the potential, especially after the planned enlargement of the european union, is immense. and yet, it is rare for this parliament to have a major debate on tourism. i therefore congratulate commissioner erkki likanen and his team on giving us this opportunity. i would also like to thank the rapporteurs for their contributions, which were overwhelmingly voted in favour by the committee on regional policy, transport and tourism, thus adding value to this report. europe has a rich historical and cultural heritage and a diversity and density of tourist attractions which make it the most visited region in the world, which means that the value of european tourism can be expected to double in the next twenty to twenty-five years, with clear effects in terms of revenue of the order of 3% per annum. employment will grow by about 15% over the next ten years. this very rich cultural and historical heritage of europe is a key factor in attracting tourists, who have the advantage, unlike other industries, of not being able to be relocated. another important factor in the growth of tourism in the coming years will be the evolution of the demographic structure of the population. in 20 years, the population over 65 will number more than 17 million. people in this age group, who will have better health, longer life expectancy and more resources, will feel much better prepared to travel than in the past. demand for tourism will also evolve: cultural tourism and nature tourism will experience significant growth. but the main point is that for workers tourism has become a social right and for young people the european union has become their territory. other factors that have boosted tourism, such as the liberalization of transport, the development of networks, the impetus given by the single market - in particular the single currency - the major advances and the emergence of the information society will continue to promote mobility and the internationalisation of tourist flows. many measures taken by the commission have been essential for the development of tourism. i am referring in particular to the free movement of people, since land borders have been abolished, and the creation of the single currency. a very important new step will be taken on 1 july this year, when the euro area will become a domestic market and electronic cards will be treated as if we were in our own country. i think it is a very important step in terms of european citizenship. as tourism is so important, we ask commissioner erkki likanen that when he presents the package of measures to be proposed or in progress for next year, he should integrate all the measures relating to tourism, because one of the fundamental problems is that there are so many sectors which have an impact on tourism that if we do not look at them together, much of the impact, the importance and the coherence of these proposals will be lost. so your proposal to set up a european tourism forum, which would bring together every year the industry, the european parliament and the commission, is a very good and very important measure, as is the assumption that we have to listen to the industry, what they need, and what we can respond to. another idea which i think is very important is that of a community-wide programme to promote tourism in europe. one of the factors that has been asked of us most, and which i think is of the utmost importance, is that the tax policy of the european union should consider the labour-intensive sectors, which are basically the tourism sectors, as being liable to be subject to the lowest rate of vat. finally, i would ask the house to support the strengthening of the legal basis in the convention on the future of europe now under way so that europe can really have a strong tourism policy, as it needs to.
- mr president, as a member elected by an ultraperipheral region - madeira - and in the absence of the time allowed to deal with the various issues raised in this report, which have been well dealt with by the rapporteur, my colleague helena torres marques, whom i would like to congratulate on the excellent work she has done, i would like to focus my speech on three points which directly concern the ultraperipheral regions. firstly, i would like to stress the importance of tourism as a driver of economic activity in the rups, and as a means of combating development delays. in these regions, the tourism sector is the main source of wealth creation and makes a major contribution to the effort to bring the regions closer to the more developed regions of the european union. it is therefore essential to make the most of the role of tourism and the potential for economic development it represents. it is crucial, however, to remember that these regions cannot become solely dependent on tourism, an activity whose vulnerability is well known. it is therefore necessary to support the effort of economic diversification by making use of the possibilities provided for in article 299 (2). secondly, i would like to stress the need to develop efficient and sustainable transport infrastructure, with a view to facilitating access to remote regions such as island regions. the increase in tourist flows and the consequent development of the sector depend on measures to facilitate accessibility, which, combined with a correct policy of promoting and exploiting environmental, historical and cultural resources, can be a crucial instrument in the development of these regions. thirdly, i would stress the particular sensitivity and environmental richness of territories such as the outermost regions, which, while being a determining factor and a tourist attraction, require sustainable tourism. otherwise, the economic and tourist development of these regions will be irreparably compromised in the medium term.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, our group naturally supports the sixth framework programme in general and the objective of more financial resources for european research policy which is being achieved here. we are aware of the enormous importance of this programme for scientific research within the union and that it is a decisive step towards the establishment of the european research area. but the reason i am speaking - just for a minute - is to draw attention once again to the ethical issues that must not be left out. in our group's view, the wording of article 3 is not satisfactory. and that is why we are maintaining amendment no 89, which proposes a solution which we believe is the right one: the principle of legislative unanimity. this is a highly sensitive ethical issue and we must trust the decisions taken at the highest level in the various member states on this issue. and to that extent we think that european research money should only be able to be used for those purposes and according to those techniques and means which are not considered illegal in any member state.

in writing. - (pt) the treaty of amsterdam enshrined the concept of openness and transparency in the treaties, so that all citizens of the union, both natural and legal persons residing or having their registered office in a member state, have the right of access to european parliament, council and commission documents. it is with this in mind that parliament has provided in its rules of procedure that the categories of documents which are directly (and fully) accessible will be included in a list which parliament will have to approve and which will be annexed to these rules. this report deals with this procedure and this list. we have fully supported this report because it represents a positive contribution to the implementation at european level of the principle of transparency and public access to documents, here in the specific framework of our parliament.
in writing. - (pt) it is regrettable that the council rejected at first reading some of the proposals we had made and which parliament had accepted with a view to better protecting workers in the event of the insolvency of their employer, amending the 1980 directive to adapt it to the frequent cases of insolvency in the european union. although the council's position already improves the previous situation, it was necessary to insist on amendments to include, within the framework of the directive, new forms and new employment relationships and the extension to termination benefits, as provided for in article 6 of the ilo convention 173. the emphasis was limited to cases provided for by national legislation. while we voted for the amendments proposed in the report, we can only regret that we did not go further and that we adopted a compromise solution with the commission, which will study the situation of workers in the face of new forms of work organisation and will organise a public hearing with the ep on this subject again this year.
in writing. - (pt) it is essential to continue to insist by all means on a better use of the values of sport to improve education programmes (in particular so-called 'parallel education'), the consolidation and expansion, in this context, of a close partnership between educational organisations and sports organisations, thus giving full expression to the educational value and enormous educational potential of sport. we therefore support the initiative of organising a 'european year of education through sport' in 2004, launched by the commission on the occasion of the euro 2004 (football) and the olympic and paralympic games in athens in the same year. that is why we voted in favour of the valuable report by our colleague, mrs doris pack. the objectives of the european year - among which we highlight the awareness of educational and sports organisations about working together, the consideration due to volunteering and the encouragement of sports activities in school curricula - as well as the financial means to be mobilised - a financial package of € 11,500,000 - clearly show the importance of this new line of action to raise awareness. but we want to give clear priority to the greatest effectiveness on the ground and it is to this extent that we support the amendments in favour of a determined increase in funding for local, national and transnational actions, as well as for initiatives of european organisations working in the education and sport sectors. indeed, many of the objectives of the european year would be frustrated if, in the end, we were to find that these resources were largely swallowed up by new 'research and studies'.
in writing. - (pt) we consider it very important to propose that the tempus iii programme incorporate the objective of 'promoting understanding and rapprochement between cultures and developing free and flourishing civil societies', exactly in line with the barcelona declaration of 1995, which established the euro-mediterranean partnership. this is particularly important at a time when the tempus iii programme is to be extended from next year to countries and territories also covered by the meda regulation (morocco, algeria, tunisia, egypt, israel, jordan, the palestinian authority, syria and lebanon) and not just to the countries of central and eastern europe - currently covered by the cards and tacis programmes. the evidence from the experience of the tempus i and ii programmes, through transeuropean cooperation in the field of university studies, is clearly positive, which is why we look with confidence (but subject to a prudent and objective evaluation, ultimately) at the innovations now proposed under tempus iii. we trust the various evaluations of the tempus programme, which show that it is a valuable instrument for promoting mutual understanding, and we consider that it is precisely in this spirit that the entire respective programming of activities should be concentrated.
in writing. - (pt) we know the important role of tourism for the european economy and its great potential in terms of growth and employment development. it is also known that tourism has already been addressed on various occasions, at ministerial summits and in the conclusions of the european councils, which underlines its importance as a driver of economic growth in the union, especially in the less developed regions. that is why, on behalf of the cds/portuguese people's party, we welcome the commission's proposal, while largely following the criticism of the rapporteur - our colleague mr torres marques - who considers it to be ineffective and unambitious. we also believe, to be sure, that the introduction of the single currency is the ideal springboard for giving a new impetus to the development of tourism in the european context and we also take a positive view of the proposal to organize an annual european forum with the leading representatives of the sector. this forum would help to increase the european visibility of the sector and its integration into the various community policies. but we are not entirely convinced that the proposed revision of the treaties in isolation, adding tourism to the european competences, will be carried out on this occasion. the unfortunate experience of many other sectors, where community competence has served to inspire excessive uniform regulations, continuous intrusions into the very sphere of national (or even regional) decision-making and constant attacks on the principle of subsidiarity, calls for caution on this occasion. at least until the issue of the distribution of competences in the european union is generally settled properly, in the context of the overall debate currently under way.
in writing. - (pt) the rapporteur's analysis of the white paper is positive, although it does make some controversial and unnecessary proposals. however, there are several important aspects to this process which are highlighted in the report, including the fact that it does not correspond to the consultations carried out, both in terms of quantity and quality, since despite the opposition of the participating organisations during the consultation process, the proposals which were not taken into account, even those considered, and which are visible in annex 1, were not retained by the white paper (e.g. issues related to education or employment). in addition, the white paper takes the views of young people in a direction opposite to that which has been conveyed, such as the use of the expression 'adaptability' of young people to a complex economic and social context which appears as an 'advantage', whereas young people are dissatisfied with the difficulties they face. expectations for this process have also been distorted by the fact that the white paper is very limited in its proposals, including on participation, information and funding, and the youth programme is not required to cover the expenditure for the actions proposed in the white paper.
in writing. - (pt) as regards the white paper presented by the european commission on 'a new impetus for european youth', we join our colleagues in the committee on culture, youth, education, the media and sport in criticising the european commission for having started the process with preconceived ideas and then leading it in such a way that these ideas are transformed into results. we also believe that it is also essential to ensure that in the future, greater attention is paid to the needs and interests of young people in a transversal way in the various union policies. finally, we also regret the absence in the respective chapter of the white paper of an explicit reference to the many community programmes - from the structural funds to programmes such as urban, equal or even meda - which have long since become central instruments of community policies and for which it is increasingly important to ensure that they promote a sufficient number of projects which benefit young people. we are therefore happy to support mrs lissy gröner's report.

- mr president, with the second european union-latin america and the caribbean summit taking place in madrid at the end of this week, it is entirely justified that the european parliament's position on the relationship between these two regions should be updated on this occasion. latin america continues to face political, economic and social challenges which require europeans and latin americans to work together in concert and with mutual respect. the summit is therefore an excellent opportunity to define the strategic framework for the european union's external policy to promote and develop more balanced relations of association and cooperation with the countries of the region. in this context, a common european union vision for the southern cone of the american continent is not only strategically important but must be politically stimulated. the starting point of this common strategy and of the european union's policy towards latin america must be based on the principle of association and must have the support of both parties. its implementation implies the adoption of a global approach which constantly and in an orderly manner stimulates relations between the various actors. this does not mean that the european union should not encourage ever closer cooperation with the individual countries of that region of the americas, especially when some of them, such as argentina and venezuela, are going through a particularly difficult situation for different reasons. in this context, we also see the bilateral partnerships, both of the european union and of the member states, with the various latin american countries as very positive. these bilateral cooperation agreements are the prelude to the successful implementation of the common european strategy. the aim of sharing prosperity by creating a free trade area by 2010 is very important and cannot fail to take account of the different weight of the various partners. in the framework of this bi-regional political dialogue, both the european union and the latin american countries must direct their efforts towards identifying concrete initiatives and actions that will give their relations a strategic framework with real and structural content. for example, a comprehensive plan to combat drug trafficking is needed, as is the intensification of security arrangements, which have become more urgent after the terrorist attacks of 11 september. it is also essential to increase trade and stimulate investment in both regions, which presupposes support for private enterprise in latin american countries as a crucial element of this mutual development strategy. finally, we must not lose sight of the defence and strengthening of human rights and the promotion of democracy in all the countries of the region without exception. i therefore support the joint motion for a resolution, although i recognize that compromise texts such as the one we are discussing sometimes lose in clarity what they are trying to gain in parliamentary support.
in writing. - (pt) unfortunately, the amendments i tabled on this report on potato starch were rejected by the committee on agriculture. i would remind you that these amendments were aimed at creating a common organization of the market in potatoes for consumption, which is particularly important for portugal. the report now only considers the commission's proposal on starch and continues to ignore the position we have advocated. the truth is that the mechanisms to support potato starch production are costly to the community budget and only benefit certain countries. this is why this support is important in order to ensure that potato aid is more equitable and to benefit all producers, especially small producers, and family farmers. we have therefore not voted in favour of this report.
in writing. - (pt) our vote against this resolution is basically to protest against the inclusion of the amendment tabled by the epp and the pse asking the council to complete the list of organisations comprising all groups and organisations which use assassination, kidnapping, blackmail, extortion and any other type of terrorist action. it does not explain what other types of terrorist acts it refers to and, secondly, it does not distinguish between the actions of organisations fighting for democracy in their country and the real terrorist acts. this is unacceptable. however, the resolution contains several positive aspects, especially as regards the intensification of relations of cooperation and solidarity with the countries of latin america, in order to promote their economic development and to guarantee social services and the fight against poverty, reiterating the need for the european union to have sufficient resources to implement this policy. we of course consider it essential to develop a genuine cooperation policy and not just trade agreements which are fundamentally beneficial to multinationals, as has sometimes happened.
in writing. - (pt) therapeutic cloning, which opens up new opportunities for treating certain diseases, involves the destruction of embryos. although it is the most widely used (and therefore the most accessible) technique, it is not the only one and certainly not the ideal one. this is why i am advocating a legal ban at european level on the creation of human embryos for cloning (for therapeutic and reproductive purposes). however, this prohibition must allow for two exceptions: surplus embryos and the 64 available cell lines. however, as regards research funding, and in particular under the sixth framework programme (the subject of this caudron report), i believe that priority should be given to community funding of research projects involving the use of adult germ cells for therapeutic purposes.
in writing. - (pt) after many months of work and extensive debate at interinstitutional, public and parliamentary level, it is very regrettable that the majority of the house insisted on rejecting all the amendments which were inspired by basic ethical and humanist imperatives. the majority of the parliament has thus shown a complete lack of sensitivity to basic human values and a harmful spirit in which unlimited technicality prevails. i am referring to the rejection of amendments nos 89, 91, 92 and 95, which tarnishes in a negative way such an important milestone for the european scientific community and research policies within the union. for my part, i regret in particular the rejection of amendment no 89, which i had proposed, defending what is known as the 'principle of legislative unanimity', which in my view represents the only sound and unavoidable solution, from a legal point of view, to these problems, in full respect of the democratic legislative decisions of the member states. so, after this stubborn blindness of the majority, we risk that, throughout the eu, the money of the taxpayers of a member state, which considers a specific object, method or technique to be illegal, is used to finance what is prohibited and illegal in their country. it is clear that this result is not only an affront to democracy in the strict sense and to national legal systems, but also represents a total absurdity from a legal point of view and a violation of the substantive hierarchy of norms.
in writing. - (pt) as regards the first of the two schnellhardt reports on the proposal for a regulation on food hygiene, we are pleased to note that the committee responsible has adopted an amendment which will allow member states to take specific measures applying to local markets and the food businesses supplying them and to protect traditional production methods and food ingredients, provided that they do not pose a risk to food hygiene. this is an aspect which cannot really be ignored and for which efforts must be channelled towards improving the formulation of these specialties, given that certain concepts may not yet be formulated in the most satisfactory and comprehensible way. food hygiene is undoubtedly a fundamental requirement, but we must resist exaggerated fundamentalist views which, on the one hand, subject local markets to disproportionate demands and incompatible bureaucracies and, on the other hand, wipe out countless traditional productions, to please industrial lobbies, weakening the diversity of the food of our peoples and dealing a new blow to european agriculture and our own national and regional cultures.
in writing. - (pt) this initiative, which aims to unify the various directives in force in this field into a single text, is very important. in fact, there are currently seven different directives governing specific hygiene standards for intra-community trade in products of animal origin and imports from third countries with the aim of preventing the spread of epizootic diseases through marketing, a framework which obviously makes the overall european regime opaque and has allowed for very negative treatment discrimination. i also agree - cautiously - with the new model regulation adopted by the commission to achieve this unification and simplification of european legislation, since its direct applicability will make it possible to overcome another current weakness in this area: that of the disparity in the concrete law enforcement framework applied in the various member states. it is in this spirit that we voted and it is in this spirit that we will continue to follow up this matter in the future.
in writing. - (pt) our vote against the marinos report is due to the fact that it essentially supports the commitment to respect the stability pact, as the commission has just done through the intervention of commissioner pedro solbes, by insisting on stability and the continuation of a budgetary situation close to balance or in surplus by 2004 at the latest. what emerges from many of the points in the report is the importance attached to neoliberal revenues, which consist in shifting the burden of budgetary and economic problems back to the same people - the workers. it advocates a moderate wage policy combined with a budget deficit of 3% and 'the implementation of structural reforms in sectors of the economy, in particular in the goods and capital markets, including the pension sector, as well as in transport, energy, communications and labour markets'. i mean, the same ones. as regards portugal and germany, the report even criticises the lack of rigorous application of the stability pact, which is unacceptable and deeply unfair, because it compares completely different situations and does not take into account the socioeconomic reality of portugal, which has one of the highest poverty rates in the european union. as we have argued, the stability pact must be suspended and not the measures which the portuguese government has just taken through the amending budget.
in writing. - (pt) for a portuguese member of the cds/partido popular, a party which has distinguished itself by its opposition and criticism of the very negative way in which the portuguese socialist government, which lost the elections of 17 march, has managed the economy and public finances of our country over the last few years - and therefore during the reference period - it was particularly difficult to take a position on the important report by our colleague mr marinos. on the one hand, we follow him in many of his denunciations and criticisms, drawing attention to worrying situations in different countries. but on the other hand we do not follow him when he says that the council should have adopted stricter measures. we regret, however, that it has come to this. even if, for portugal, the report considers that we must not complain any more, but concentrate the attention and the will of all those who follow these issues on the vigorous support for the redoubled effort which the new portuguese government has already undertaken, in very difficult conditions, to put things back quickly in a positive frame of development, as commissioner pedro solbes expressly acknowledged yesterday at the end of the debate, in response to one of my speeches. i am sure that this effort by the new portuguese authorities will be understood and duly supported politically by all the european institutions and that it will merit the solidarity of the majority of the members of this parliament.
in writing. - (pt) there are many aspects of this resolution adopted today by the european parliament with which we are critical and in deep disagreement. the first aspect concerns the choice of nato as the central framework for defining the relationship between the countries of the european union and the usa, aiming in particular at the realisation of a partnership for world domination, which places the realisation of the 'european army' and the development of the military industry at the centre of its priorities. despite the timid references to the us's non-compliance with various fundamental international protocols, conventions and treaties, the report does not criticize or condemn the us for its responsibility for the current deterioration of the international situation, for the war, for the growing military interventionism, for the arms race, for its responsibilities in supporting the criminal policy of the israeli government towards the palestinian people. we also note the reference in the report to the eu's timid reaction to the us's non-compliance with trade rules, particularly in the case of steel and agricultural export aid, and we note the absence of any us condemnation of the embargo on cuba, or the so-called 'plan colombia', among many other examples.
in writing. - (pt) in general, the elles report is a good report. its primary objective is to enrich the debate on strengthening relations in a new global context by announcing an official parliament position on this matter. the strategy used to draft this report is, in our opinion, sufficiently effective. the report insists here and there, with some regularity, on the idea of 'new global challenges/interests'. the european union is also committed to the development of a common foreign and security policy, both in the security and economic fields, thus justifying the current need to strengthen transatlantic relations. however, the report and, therefore, the resolution contain a clear call for the communitization of the esdp (paragraph 9), with which i do not agree, just as i consider inappropriate some heightened criticism of us foreign policy, which does not contribute to the climate of confidence necessary for the settlement of open questions, nor to the strengthening of the undisputed common heritage, which unites and must continue to unite europe and the usa. that's why i abstained.
- mr president, i would also like to begin by thanking mr javier solana, the secretary-general of the council, for his presence in this debate and by congratulating him on the document he produced in march and which was already under preliminary discussion in barcelona. i have also found it difficult to understand the criticisms of some members when it is found that this document is largely consistent with the positions of this parliament. i am also not concerned by the criticism i have seen in some diplomatic circles that the document produced by the secretary-general of the council would not have many new ideas but would be a repository of ideas long debated in the council. i do not think that this is a bad thing, but a good thing, especially if effective decisions are taken in seville, vigorous decisions, along paths that have long been sought but which the heads of state and government have been reluctant or resistant to take. in politics, there are sometimes matters on which we agree on everything, and others on which we differ on everything, and these matters of european construction and institutional reform are not of one quality or another. we don't agree on everything and we don't disagree on everything. these are matters on which we agree in part. i would also like to make a point: sometimes we agree on the objectives but not on the way, sometimes we agree on the way but not on the objectives. here we agree on the proposed paths, but not on the objectives. we are clearly moving away from the motion for a resolution tabled by the committee on constitutional affairs when it points out that the proposed reform should give priority to the community method or the intergovernmental method. we do not think that is the way to go. but we agree with the concrete measures proposed, particularly with regard to the essential thing to be achieved in the short term, namely the so-called transparency of the council's functioning in its legislative capacity. i think it would be better to call this transparency by another name, because the transparency we are talking about here is not the same as transparency in access to documents. the publicity of the council's work in its legislative capacity, its work in open session, is in fact the decisive reform of its work in this area and a way of combating the democratic deficit of the european union at its heart. i am one of those who are calling for greater involvement of national parliaments in the construction of europe. i believe that this can only happen when the council opens its doors to legislative discussions. our colleagues in the national parliaments will follow, will react differently to the legislative work within the european union at council level, if they can follow directly what our governments, what the governments of each member state are discussing and the positions they are taking within the council. so in this framework of secrecy there is a serious contradiction, a radical contradiction between the democratic basis of the council and the non-democratic method of the council's functioning. and if we believe that this must be corrected, we do so not because we want to weaken the intergovernmental axis of the european union, but, on the contrary, because we want to protect it, because we want to affirm it, because we want to give it more visibility and more democracy and participation. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done in this area.
- mr president, mr secretary-general, mr commissioner, there are gestures which alone deserve our applause. this is the case with the intention to reform the council. some of the very complex political issues raised by the secretary-general call for a revision of the treaties. it would therefore be very useful if the council were to submit these proposals to the convention on the future of europe in time for them to be discussed and incorporated, if that is the option, in the outcome of its work to be presented to the intergovernmental conference next year. but i think we can all agree that many, perhaps most, of the reforms needed for the council to function properly are not of this kind. it is almost universally acknowledged that the current reality of one of the most important bodies of the european union has moved away from the model laid down in the treaties. the so-called intergovernmental drift has in practice removed from the commission and the european parliament some of the powers which they have under the treaties, but it has also removed from the national parliaments themselves the possibility of effectively monitoring their governments. i therefore agree with mr poos, co-rapporteur of the report by the european parliament on the reform of the council, when he says that what is expected of the european council is a political vision, a vision of the future, strategic options, guidelines and not a coordination or decision-making power to be taken by the general affairs council. it is also essential that the council, as a co-legislator, deliberates in the open, taking on its political responsibilities. it is important to be able to identify who is deciding and who is not at european level. the council is very much responsible for this lack of closeness between the community institutions and the citizens. that's why your mission is so important. i wish you the greatest happiness!
- mr president, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, although we cannot support the lamassoure report in the final vote, i would also like to congratulate the rapporteur on the outstanding quality of his work. i would like to welcome this report as one of the most important documents under discussion in this parliament. it is important in the timing and timing of its presentation, it is important because of its subject matter, which deals with one of the most controversial, most sensitive and most important issues for the future of the union, it is important not only because of the final quality of the report, but also because of the work, the debates that preceded it, and because of the many amendments, even those that were left in the way, and therefore because of the enormous wealth of theoretical content; finally, it is important for the understanding and the systematizing of the work that results from this work with regard to the european union and also in the light of the parallel debates taking place in the convention. and we regret that we did not vote in favour of the final vote because we agree with the first two paragraphs, we agree that the time has come to update the division of powers between the union and its member states in the light of historical experience, respecting subsidiarity and proportionality, and in the light of an experience which has unfortunately been characterized by so many excesses and violations of the principle of subsidiarity. and we also agree that a better distribution of powers is needed and that this will lead to a strengthening of democracy in europe, which for us includes a reassessment of national democracies and the role of national parliaments and a new interparliamentary line in building the future of europe. but we are already irreversibly moving away from the third paragraph and we believe that the report is deviating from its original objective by going along with this wave in favour of a european constitution, a constitution of the union, which we cannot support. and we believe, because we are talking about competence, that it is not the competence of any european institution to draw up a european constitution. it is the competence of the states to make and revise treaties, and this is a contractual mechanism which is completely different from the typical mechanisms of constitutions, which are based on the existence of a sovereign people, a people who regulate a sovereign entity, and this is completely different from our mission in the european union. and so there we have a difference of principle which is essential to us. moreover, when mr méndez de vigo spoke a moment ago about the powers of delegation, he touched on the essential point: the powers of delegation, the fact that we recognise them does not only mean what mr méndez de vigo said, but also that the states have their own powers of common law, no one needs to delegate them, whereas the european union only has them in so far as the states delegate and delegate them, and can also withdraw them, as the report now provides in the new vision of the mechanism that would replace the current article 308. and this is a very important aspect which has consequences for the institutional design and the way we understand the competences of the european union. there are a few more observations we'd like to make. for example, the criteria identified in recital (g) indicate the synergy criterion, but this criterion can only be considered in a very limited framework. the criteria of synergy and effectiveness are extremely dangerous in themselves, because that is where all attacks on the principle of subsidiarity can be directed. it is easy to argue that any action in isolation represents more efficiency - and efficiency is a dangerous word, it is good for everything - and economies of scale, and we would be laying the foundations for continuous violations of the principle of subsidiarity. but in the end, we welcome the report, we would like to vote in favour of it, but what is keeping us away from it is this constitutional vision, this vision of a european union which is increasingly taking on a state profile and design, which we consider to be in breach of the deep nature of europe and of the vision of the european union we have for the future, which respects the nations.
