- mr president, next monday, 20 may, the independence of timor will be declared. it is a historic day for that people, who over the years, in the worst conditions, fought for a free and independent homeland. it is therefore important that we welcome the victory of the people of east timor and that you, mr president, on behalf of the european parliament, send our greetings to president xanana gusmão and to the entire timorese people. but it is equally important that we take into account that their problems are not over, it is essential that the european union maintains strong cooperation with this new independent state and that the necessary financial support for timor is approved in the next community budget to contribute to the development that the occupation has impeded. the people of timor deserve our full solidarity and i thank you, mr president, for expressing that solidarity and congratulating us on the victory of independence.
- mr president, commissioner, we cannot allow the johannesburg summit to be just an event to mark the 10th anniversary of rio de janeiro. there is little to celebrate, given that in the last ten years the signs of unsustainability have become more pronounced. on the contrary, the johannesburg conference gives us an opportunity to start anew, by making a global commitment to a development model that combines the economic, social and environmental dimensions. there is, however, an additional difficulty with the rio summit. this time we'll be more alone in leading a global agreement. the political agenda that the european union sets will therefore be decisive for the success of the conference. i think that both the commission's communication and the reports by mr papayannakis and mr lannoye contribute to this good agenda. there are, however, some paragraphs and amendments which, in our view, take away some of the objectivity and serenity of parliament's resolution - and i am not saying that the commission is not prepared to accept them. i am talking about the tobyn tax and the debt relief for developing countries. the epp will nevertheless vote in favour of the final resolution. we look forward to the following objectives at the johannesburg conference: the first is the entry into force of the kyoto protocol. to do this, some countries must speed up the ratification of the same protocol - i am thinking of russia, japan, canada and australia. secondly, that the political conditions be created for the united states to return to a common environmental effort; thirdly, that a greening of the economy be launched by internalizing environmental costs, decoupling economic growth from resource intensive use and focusing on cleaner technologies; fourthly, we hope that the conference will provide common answers to global problems and that, as in rio de janeiro, when the foundations were laid for the climate change conference, johannesburg will also lay the foundations for protocols and new institutions in other environmental fields such as water. fifthly, we hope that there will be a boost to international governance and institutional reform. you cannot create a sustainable internet if the nodes in that network are obsolete. finally, mr president, it is hoped that this conference will not be based on vague intentions but rather will set timetables, targets and policies.
- mr president, i would like to thank and congratulate mr papayannakis and mr lannoye on their important reports. the johannesburg world summit is another important opportunity for them to face up to the enormous and complex problems facing humanity, especially those living in the poorest countries. we look to it with the necessary expectation and attach the importance due to an event which can or should lead the international community to establish an agenda effectively geared to the sustainable development of the planet, to the improvement of the quality of life and to the eradication of poverty, based on the agreements reached at the earth summit in rio. we should like to be optimistic, even if there are sufficient reasons to adopt a different or even opposite position, reasons which are based on the enormous delays in the implementation of the rio agreement, the deepening of the north/south divide, the perpetuation of poverty and of debt itself, the insistence on inadequate and unfair economic recipes, the lack of political will on the part of the rich countries - and in particular on the part of the united states - to change the guidelines they themselves determine and to provide the resources necessary for a world turnaround which would ultimately benefit everyone. monterrey, which is still so young and despite the last-minute decisions, is in line with these debilitating reasons for the necessary optimism. it is now time to urge the member states and the commission to take a firm and decisive stand against the scourges that have struck the world and to promote guidelines and measures and to find ways of achieving equitable and sustainable development, combining environmental protection with the eradication of poverty. and, crucially, to commit themselves to an ambitious agenda for the next decade that will avoid the unacceptable failure that some already take for granted for this world summit.
in writing. - (pt) our vote against reflects our opposition both to the guidelines for economic policies based on the stability pact and to the amendments contained in the report, which also go in the same direction. unfortunately, the community institutions, in particular the ep, insist on maintaining the macroeconomic policy stance despite all the evidence and the need for growth and jobs in the european union. the path should be the opposite, with more investment, including public investment, given the period of economic slowdown, the risk factors that remain at international level and the growth of unemployment. the trentin report calls for respect for the barcelona strategy, which confirms the liberal leanings of lisbon, calls for a relaxation of the labour market, accepts wage moderation, although it introduces the possibility of growth in line with productivity, forgetting that the transfer of wages to profits has not translated into an increase in investment and employment. it also calls for flexibility and mobility of labour and for more quality of employment, which is a contradiction. there will be more jobs, with more investment and better quality, only if the jobs are not temporary, precarious and poorly paid, which the report unfortunately does not say. some of the positive aspects he was addressing were even removed during the vote in plenary.
in writing. - (pt) the resolution adopted today by the european parliament follows the federalist line, as shown by the attempt to bring the functioning of the council, in its legislative function, closer to that of a parliament, by bringing it closer to a second parliamentary chamber at the level of the european union. but, fundamentally, it should be stressed that it misses the central issues that the council is already discussing today, in particular the model of rotation of presidencies or working languages, among others. in his speech yesterday in plenary, mr javier solana said that it was not possible to maintain the current system of rotating the presidency of the council every six months on the false grounds of eu enlargement, which would open the door to the introduction of other models, including elections, which would strengthen the influence and power of the 'big' at the expense of full and effective equality of rights between states, a situation which we firmly reject. the same can be said of working languages, an area where the principle of equality between states is in danger, and we also reject this situation. the absence of dissemination (and we might ask ourselves why) of the proposal presented to the barcelona council by mr javier solana concerning the reform of the council is also significant. this was on the agenda of the seville summit in june.
in writing. - (pt) the proposals in this report follow and deepen the dynamic initiated at maastricht, which has set the current 'european integration' on the federalist course. the report contains proposals which are detrimental to the national sovereignty of eu member countries, which we reject, in particular the proposal that foreign policy and the definition of the legal basis for a 'common area of freedom and security' become the exclusive competence of the eu, maximalist proposals compared to others which have defended shared competences. the proposal also stresses that the financing of the community budget must be the exclusive responsibility of the eu, with the consequence of the creation of 'european tax' instruments, which we also reject. in general, the report focuses on the constitutionalization of the treaties, superimposing them on national constitutions in the areas of fundamental competence. even in the case of shared competences between the eu and the member states, the eu 'makes the rules', 'coordinates' or 'intervenes in a complementary way'. the only remaining option for the national states would be to transpose the rules or follow the guidelines using the budgetary and fiscal resources at their disposal, as in the significant example of the attempt to renationalize the costs of the common agricultural policy.
in writing. - (pt) our abstention on this report on the situation in iraq is due to the contradictions in the report, for two main reasons. the first concerns some positive aspects of the report, in particular support for the decision to lift sanctions against iraq, except for the arms embargo, albeit in a mixed formulation. but the dramatic situation in that country means that all will must be united to end the economic and trade sanctions, for humanitarian reasons. the second concerns the negative aspects, revealing the difficulty for the european union to free itself from dependence on american interests, as shown by the rejection in plenary of the amendments we had tabled, in particular the proposal for an international inquiry into the health and environmental consequences of the american and british bombings of 1991, in particular as a result of the use of depleted uranium weapons. it is equally regrettable that we have not clearly condemned the bombing of iraq, which continues to be the victim from time to time.
the rapporteur. - mr president, we may not be many, but i'm sure we're good! commissioner poul nielson, i think we have a deep consensus on the need to forge an effective partnership between the european union and the united nations, starting with humanitarian and development action. this was evident in the debate and vote we had in the committee on development and cooperation, and i hope it will be so now. and it would be incomprehensible to do otherwise if we take into account the universally proclaimed objectives in these areas, especially the challenges and objectives of the millennium declaration, and at the same time the notorious lack of coordination to make them a reality and the evident shortage of resources that has been observed so far. it is regrettable, however, that the commission has not already opted for a comprehensive and integrated approach to this partnership and has left for later the issues of security, peacekeeping, conflict prevention and crisis management, all the more so as the interlinkage of these aspects with those which motivate this communication is known. i would like to ask the commissioner whether he is aware that the united nations is a major player in the development of the community's policies in this field. however, it is important to stress the need to overcome the contradictions that are now evident, on the one hand, from the important role played by the member states in the united nations, especially in terms of financial contributions to the various actions and to its budget, or even from the important contribution of the member states themselves and the european union to the various united nations funds, and on the other hand, from the obviously low level of political influence that both have, particularly with regard to the specialised agencies. this situation must be changed, and this must be underpinned by greater coordination between the member states themselves and greater involvement of the member states and the european union in the various stages of the united nations programmes. and above all, greater capacity to be involved in defining development strategies and in planning and implementing the actions to be carried out in this field. this will also ensure greater coherence and optimise the impact of the two. the commission communication stresses the need for an administrative framework to achieve the stated objectives of this partnership, either by the rapid and successful conclusion of the ongoing negotiations on the so-called 'verification clause' which will ensure sufficient information on the use of community funds made available, or by the renegotiation of the 1999 framework agreement between the community and the un in order to ensure a more focused approach to cofinancing operations, performance-based budgeting and programme funding. we support these concerns and positions, but we also stress that the pursuit of an effective partnership and the harmonization of structures and procedures must be promoted on three levels: by strengthening the strategic political dialogue with a view to defining policies and programmes, by strengthening cooperation on the ground at the level of the developing countries in order to ensure complementarity and coherence at the operational level, and by creating a stable financial framework with consistent rules and principles between the two institutions, as well as, of course, the new framework agreement. however, as the commission's approach indicates, it is essential that the first step in this cooperation should be the selection of the most appropriate partners within the united nations system. it is essential that the processes of decentralization and decentralisation of management and decision-making be brought to a speedy conclusion at community level and that the democratic involvement of social movements and, in general, of so-called civil society be ensured. without cleaning up our own house we can't get much further! the decisive implementation of the reform under way is therefore essential in this area too. finally, i would like to say that our commitment to this partnership and our desire to follow its implementation process leads us to ask the commission for adequate and timely information on the development of the partnership, and in particular to suggest that the commission include specific information on this development in its annual report on cooperation policy.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, once again the european parliament is looking at the situation in indonesia. we are concerned about the continuing expression of violence against defenseless civilians in the name of regional and religious disputes. every life is important and sacred to us, but the situation in indonesia has already killed thousands. we deplore the incitement to the slaughter of christians by radical muslim leaders and offer the example of tolerance and democratic spirit that east timor provides us. in a territory where more than 95% of the population identify with the catholic church, the current prime minister is, without any problems and after democratic elections, a muslim. we welcome the initiatives of the present indonesian government in the normalization of relations with east timor and portugal. the symbolic gesture of their representatives at the inauguration of the last cultural event held in indonesia with the support of the portuguese embassy was not overstated. we call on the indonesian government to continue its efforts to protect the population and to identify and punish those responsible for human rights violations. impunity cannot be the rule, especially, as has been proved in some cases, when military or police officers are involved. and it is in this context that it is desirable and advisable for the international community to be involved in independent investigations to establish the truth in some of the cases reported. we call on the indonesian government to continue to try to find political solutions to the differences that exist. we respect the intention to preserve the unity of the territory, but we insist on the need to find other solutions than the mere use of force to respond to the ethnic, religious and regional differences that are agitating life in that state. we call on the un commission on human rights not to remain in inexplicable silence in the face of what is happening in indonesia.
- mr president, i should like to begin by saying that i do not think that this emergency resolution is the best way of helping to overcome the still worrying situation in gujarat, particularly because the violence which has occurred in that state in recent months is a manifestation of a phenomenon which affects the whole region, not just india, and which is extremely complex. it is a situation where social factors converge, such as poverty, urban ghettos, some interference by organised crime whose international links are unknown, some inability of the political elites to mobilise energies in accordance with political ideals and the diversion to the religious sphere, particularly through fundamentalist extremism. it is nevertheless legitimate for the european parliament to be concerned about a case which has already cost the lives of about a thousand people, or even more, depending on the sources. it is not the time to analyse the events that gave rise to this violence, but it should be stressed that everything has happened since the fire on 27 february last in two carriages of a train, in which 58 people died, half of them women and children. there is therefore no reason to justify such an act. the retaliation and revenge that followed cannot be justified either. it should be noted that godhra is an urban periphery type area of high population density with a level of latent social conflict, where law enforcement action is difficult in general, apart from the possible involvement of local police members in one religious faction and another, according to findings at the time. what is certain is that initially the intervention of law enforcement agencies failed to stem the violence. the question was, however, immediately taken up by the democratic institutions of the indian union, namely the government, the opposition parties and the parliament. i had the opportunity to visit the indian parliament on the very day when this matter was being discussed. urgent measures were taken not only by the will of the government but also by the strong pressure of the opposition and the indian media. i would also point out that sometimes positions taken outside of indian democracy itself, although well-intentioned, end up having counterproductive effects, that is, they feed nationalist and fundamentalist positions of various origins, whether of islamic or hindu origin. in any case, on behalf of my group, i recommend that this resolution be adopted without amendment, because we think it is quite balanced.
mr president, the proposal that commissioner franz fischler has just made here is a real declaration of war on the fishing sector, with particularly serious implications for countries with weaker and outdated fleets, such as portugal. the proposal, under the pretext of protecting the environment, is moving towards a sharp reduction in fishing effort and measures to reduce the volume of catches and reward the killing of vessels, without taking due account of the specificities and needs of each country. for example, it is forgotten that portugal is the member state which has already far exceeded its objectives in terms of reducing fishing effort, which maintains an ageing fleet - some 40% of the boats are over 25 years old - and which has the lowest average incomes in the european union. however, what the commission is promising is more incentives to cut vessels (287 in portugal), i.e. a reduction of about 12 000 tonnes of gross tonnage, when what was needed in the portuguese case was to increase support for the modernisation of the fleet in order to supply a population which is the largest consumer of fish per capita in the european union, which also makes portugal an attractive market for multinational interests. but that is not in the interest of the population or of those who work in the strategic fisheries sector, which is now so severely threatened. we therefore reject the proposal and propose a global review of its content.
mr president, no one in his right mind can deny or question the absolute necessity of preserving and protecting fisheries resources, that is to say endangered marine species. in fact, we cannot allow a purely predatory attitude as if we were in prehistory but with 21st century technologies. the fishing industry as a predatory activity must be stopped, it has no reason to be. however, the reform started badly, mr fischler, it was mired in controversy, it started as a farce, i might add! it is therefore in danger of death, it is mortally wounded. that's too bad! you're turning the recipients who are in revolt against you. the reform needs objectivity, serenity and pedagogy, not the guerrilla atmosphere that has developed. on the other hand, it is too brutal a reform which pits the affected against it. it is an unbalanced reform which keeps the industrial fisheries of certain countries intact. it is an unfair reform because it does not take account of those who have been wronged and those who have respected the policies that have been put in place so far. it is a reform with a very loose scientific basis. everybody says that! finally, it is a technocratic reform: it does not take into account the survival of millenary cultures and living patterns which must be protected. here are our reservations and our protests!
- mr president, commissioner, i represent a country where fishing and the sea are the basis of its history and where there are still some 50 000 jobs in the entire fisheries sector. the first criticism i make of the commission's proposal is that it does not take account of the diversity of the composition of the fishing fleet in the european union: there are countries where the fleet is mainly made up of large vessels engaged in industrial fishing and others where the majority is made up of small boats. in portugal, 90% of the existing vessels are less than 12 metres long and are engaged in small-scale coastal fishing. as some 40% of these vessels are over 25 years old, without community financial aid the overwhelming majority of our fishermen will be unable to modernise and make their activity viable. the second major criticism i make is that there is a fundamental question that poisons all these proposals and that has to do with the way in which the different countries have met the targets set out in the multiannual guidance plans, the so-called pops: i would like to thank the commissioner for his report and for the work he has done in the past. i should also mention that since portugal joined the european union in 1986, the portuguese fleet has been reduced by 45% and the total catch by 50%. given these figures, i do not think it is fair to ask us to make any more sacrifices when there are countries that have increased their fleets. by failing to take account of this recent historical past, the commission's proposals not only make the just pay for the sinner, but, even worse, compensate those who have wronged in the past. in conclusion, commissioner, i would like to say that it is wrong to want to rush through reform because it will always be bad. the argument for the sustainability of resources is a very serious one, but it cannot be used to do things in a careless way and to underestimate the human and social dimension of the problem and, above all, to impose an unfair balance. nothing is said about the question of fish meal, the outermost regions and illegal fishing. i hope that the reactions to the commission's proposals will serve to re-examine what has not been considered and will lead us to a more balanced reform which takes account of all the dimensions of the problem. although that's gonna have to wait a little longer.
- mr president, the commission's proposal for the car market introduces clear consumer benefits into community legislation. it is therefore worth supporting, irrespective of the adjustments which may still be made and which are geared towards a more comprehensive sense of balance of all the interests involved, while recognizing that we are faced with a process of consultation with parliament. in particular, actions to improve road safety through the correct use of the car should be recommended. the new regulation represents a significant advance in the whole motor trade sector, since it also puts an end to the excessive power of trade marks, which in some cases even foreshadows an abuse of a dominant position. the draft regulation takes in and balances the importance of the motor trade, wholesale dealers and retail dealers, for example. but the basic philosophy of the new regulation is - i repeat - essentially that of defending the consumer's interest, not so much immediately, since there is no certainty that there will be any significant changes in the price of vehicles that can benefit him, but above all in the medium and short term, when the rules of active competition enshrined in the regulation are fully absorbed. it is therefore essential that this regulation be brought forward quickly so that economic operators can prepare for the full liberalisation of the sector from 2010. however, the postponement of the entry into force of the location clause for a reasonable period of time can and should be accepted as positive discrimination in favour of small and mediumsized enterprises only. the public debate on this legislative initiative has sometimes been guided by the argument that significant falls in car prices could be expected immediately. it is not true that this can happen automatically. there may even be immediate perverse movements towards higher prices. the objective announced by commissioner mario monti on behalf of the commission will only be fully achieved if we move towards fiscal harmonization. i hope that this new regulation will bring about this change.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to welcome the commitment made in madrid at the second european union-latin america and the caribbean summit and welcome the recognition of common values between the two continents. i am referring in particular to the protection of fundamental rights, economic cooperation, the fight against terrorism and the fight against drugs. if this summit had not produced other results, these would have been enough to give us reason to believe that cooperation between the two sides of the atlantic is a politically credible process - as commissioner antónio vitorino has said - which will continue at a good pace, despite the difficulties faced by some of the latin american countries. there is, however, a note of realism which i must bear in mind. the fact is that, as at all summits, the objectives set are one thing and the path to be followed to achieve them is another. in this context, i believe it is urgent to put into practice concrete initiatives and actions which give real substance to the objectives that have been agreed. it is also essential to increase trade and stimulate investment in both regions through a prudent but determined policy of opening up markets and resisting unjustified protectionist temptations, as president romano prodi also said. this implies support for private enterprise in latin american countries as a crucial element of this strategy of economic development on the one hand and of combating poverty on the other. finally, we must not lose sight of the defence and strengthening of human rights and the promotion of democracy in all the countries of the region without exception, and this expression 'without exception' must be emphasized so that the dictatorship of cuba is not forgotten here once again, except by my colleague malmström. despite the shortcomings, i support the joint motion for a resolution, which is also supported by my group, in the certainty that the european parliament will continue to contribute in the coming period with its constant attention to the development of the conditions for the success of the next summit to be held in mexico in 2004.
the rapporteur. - mr president, i will go beyond your best expectations in terms of time saving by saying that this report is aimed at the creation of a european institute of police studies, which would essentially consist, at an initial stage, in the creation of a network bringing together the different national institutes of police training and research and which would then have to evolve in a direction not explained by the authors with a view to offering an added value to the union in terms of coordination of the different police authorities of the member states. in our view, however, the initiative suffers from a certain infantile disease of community construction, a certain tendency towards the proliferation of bodies with more or less overlapping purposes. in this area we already have europol, eurojust, the european police college, the chief of police operational unit, the european crime prevention network and the european criminal law network on the ground. the proposal, as presented to us, therefore suggests an increase in irrationality and incoherence in a system which is, moreover, a relatively new area of community action. perhaps some of the worthy concerns expressed by the presidency, which deserve attention and reflection, concerning coordination, police coordination and the sharing of experience in this field, could be better served by an amendment to the statute and regulations of the european police college, and spain's unreserved accession to this mechanism would also be an important step towards strengthening this coordination within the police bodies. what is important is to discern what innovations really bring added value and what really represent efficiency gains for the system, introducing coherence into it, putting aside the changes that translate into a useless multiplication of bodies, officials and expenditure, in line with a certain bureaucratizing tradition that does not interest us and without useful benefit relevant to the action of the union in the field of combating crime. for these reasons, the committee on civil liberties has decided to give this house a negative opinion on the adoption of this initiative.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the european parliament has been consistent in adopting a number of initiatives to extend europol's mandate: in addition to drug trafficking and initial competences, we have seen the allocation of competences to human trafficking, terrorism, money laundering, counterfeiting of currency and means of payment, and all serious forms of international crime. we want to help make europol a more effective organisation in the fight against organised crime. the initiative under discussion today is intended to go further, to amend the convention itself, which, in its current form, limits europol's functions to the collection, analysis and exchange of data. we welcome the introduction of operational powers within europol, which is in the treaty of amsterdam itself as we interpret it. but we stress the need for this to be accompanied by measures which guarantee both democratic control and judicial control. the procedure adopted for amending the convention is, however, strictly intergovernmental. we therefore support the proposal by the rapporteur, mr gérard deprez, to withdraw this initiative and to put forward a new proposal to replace the convention with a council decision based on article 34, which will make it possible to integrate europol into the third pillar and thus into the institutional system of the european union, as has been repeatedly requested by the european parliament. in this area of freedom, security and justice, we want concrete, thoughtful but firm steps. we reject initiatives which are intended to give the impression that something is being done, but without any concrete content. that is why i support the sousa pinto report and why i propose that the spanish initiative to create a european institute of police studies be rejected, since it would lead to an overlap with the existing structure and, in particular, the european police academy. we also support the report by mr deprez on the increase in the basic salaries of europol staff: if the european parliament is not consulted on europol's general budget, there is no point in consulting it on a matter of detail. i agree with the rapporteur that it is important to integrate europol's budget into the community budget.
in writing. - (pt) i congratulate our colleague mr bradbourn on the excellent report he has presented to us, which i fully support. this is a work which shows great technical knowledge in the approach to such a vital topic as the development of the trans-european transport network and in relation to which i would like to emphasise the following points: - the need for a policy of infrastructure of the trans-european networks as a means of integrating the economies of the european market; - the trans-european networks as a vital instrument of the european policy of growth, competitiveness and employment, and as drivers of an operational cross-border transport market, allowing the creation of a genuine internal transport market and a further advance of europe.
in writing. - (pt) until now, there has been a set of vertical agreements in the automotive sector between industry, distribution and repair. a block exemption was granted in order to avoid the application of the principles of article 81 of the competition act. this exemption regulation is now coming to an end and a liberalisation is being prepared with a transitional period of only one year. thus, the industry has hitherto enjoyed a great deal of control over its market through exclusive (dealer) agreements, the exclusive distribution of original parts and the guarantee of repairs. with the opening up of the sector to competition, large retailers will be able to sell cars, dealers will be able to sell different brands and manufacturers will have to provide technical information to other parts producers and independent repairers to enable them to produce and repair in a competitive way. the european sales market is also to be liberalised, despite the car tax issues, since restrictions and national agreements on a territory no longer have any effect. thus, a consumer may benefit from lower prices, as some dealers and some distributors, but smaller dealers without exclusivity may not have business prospects. there will also be a tendency towards greater concentration of dealers. it is estimated that 20% of dealerships could disappear, resulting in job losses. that is why we do not support this report.
in the case of the report, the commission has not yet taken a decision on the matter. it is positive that this mid-term review is re-launching the debate on the reform of the cap and the need to be able to make some adjustments in various aspects, including the defence of the creation of a permanent system of aid for dried fruit, the protection of the origin of oils, the cmo for bananas, and others, recalling the principle of food sovereignty and food security and the need to review the trend towards exports of the cap, but it is negative that it is insisting on the principle of co-financing, which would call into question the application of the cap and would affect farmers in the least developed areas in particular, which we reject. it is also positive to refer to the need to approve the compulsory nature of the modulation of aid under the first pillar of the cap, but negative to insist on the need to decouple aid from production. that's why we abstained.
in writing. - (pt) the report is generally positive, although it retains some confusing aspects. in any event, it should be stressed that the increase in the amounts for rural development must involve appropriate changes, in order to take account of the specificities of the different agricultural sectors in the european union. as we proposed in the debate, and as the rapporteur accepted in his report, it is important to support the marketing of quality products and to provide greater support for the development of local and regional markets, in particular by strengthening the dialogue between producers and other local development actors. here too it is important to defend the mandatory progressive modulation of cap aid to defend rural development, in particular compensatory compensation, but it is important that this modulation be community-wide, with the establishment of maximum aid values and with the possibility of increasing aid for small and mediumsized farmers and family farming, because the rebalancing of cap aid between countries, producers and productions requires this, which is fundamental to combat injustices, especially in portugal.
in writing. - (pt) the european parliament has paid particular attention to shipbuilding in the european union, given the problems it has faced since the 1994 oecd agreement and the seriousness of the situation following the closure of many industrial plants and the large number of redundancies. it is clear that not everyone respects this agreement in the same way, particularly the usa and south korea, which use unfair practices and unequal conditions of competition at world level, especially south korea, whose share of the world market has increased from 10% to 50% in ten years. the negotiations initiated by the commission with south korea have not been able to restore fair and transparent conditions of competition on the world market, leading to a critical situation characterised by very low prices and the existence of considerable excess capacity, which could lead to the closure of the remaining companies. we therefore advocate the revision of the oecd agreement and the introduction of community preference in shipbuilding, so as to encourage member states to purchase ships built in european shipyards. urgent action by the commission and the council is needed, which is why we support the resolution adopted by parliament.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the social partners are able to take part in the negotiations. - i know . mr president, i would first like to congratulate mr corbett on the merit of his report. in this report on the general revision of the rules of procedure, the rapporteur makes proposals to allow simplification of procedures and the concentration of parliamentary work on current and important legislation and political debates. in the opinion of the committee on employment and social affairs, i have highlighted a number of points which would in some way optimise the dynamics of this institution. they are as follows: as regards question time, it would be important to give the president the possibility of deciding to refer a question to question time in the committee responsible, which would have the advantage of providing more time for debate in plenary. it would also be necessary that, in the context of the open method of coordination, the european parliament should not run the risk of being excluded from the debate on topics of the greatest importance. the proposal in my opinion was therefore to refer the matter to the committee responsible for the subject-matter for a decision on the procedure to be followed and to propose that a report be drawn up. another aspect which should not have been ignored - but was - is that the committee responsible for the subject matter could, on the basis of reports and annual reports from the council and the commission, decide to invite members of the council to attend its meetings and to answer questions. the aim would be to reinforce the european parliament's influence once again within the framework of the open method of coordination, making it more effective. finally, i would like to stress the close link between lifelong learning and the subjects which fall within the remit of the committee on employment and social affairs, and it would be logical to include it in the remit of that parliamentary committee.
the report by mrs salisch, which i am sure you will agree with, is a very good report, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support it. i thank all of you for your contributions, which i hope the majority of parliament will approve. the report is based on the commission communication on the first 15 national plans for social inclusion presented by the member states in june last year, following the decisions taken at the lisbon european council in march 2000 on the strategy to combat poverty and social exclusion and taking into account the four common objectives defined in nice, namely: promoting participation in employment and access for all to the resources, rights, goods and services essential for living in dignity; preventing the risks of exclusion; working with the most vulnerable groups; mobilising all the actors involved. despite the limitations and diversity of approaches adopted by the different member states, the joint report by the commission and the council adopted during the belgian presidency points to important risks and challenges that need to be taken into account in order to move more rapidly towards a thorough and comprehensive analysis of the causes of social exclusion and towards concrete measures that need to be put into practice more quickly and with greater commitment, if the declared intentions of the need for greater economic and social cohesion are not to go unfulfilled. i would like to recall the importance of the overlap between the central challenges identified in the joint report, namely: developing an inclusive labour market and promoting employment as a right and an opportunity for all; ensuring adequate levels of income and resources and decent living conditions; tackling educational disadvantages; preserving solidarity and protecting the rights of all children with disabilities; ensuring decent access to housing; ensuring equal access to health, social care, recreational, cultural, transport and social services; improving the quality of services provided by the affected areas; ensuring equal access to health, recreational, cultural and social services. in order to meet these challenges, it is essential that member states make significant improvements in the distribution of resources and opportunities in society in order to ensure the participation and social inclusion of all people and the exercise of their fundamental rights. equally important is an integrated and multidimensional strategy which, based on the objectives set out in nice and the implementation of the community programme to promote the fight against social exclusion, in particular the first round table to be held in october during the danish presidency, seeks to add value to the fight against poverty and social exclusion and encourages the drawing up of the second series of national social inclusion plans to include clear priorities, specific targets and objectives, appropriate policies and measures, together with their respective amounts and budgetary sources. the common challenge for community policies and for the various national governments is therefore to ensure that the main mechanisms for redistributing opportunities and resources - the labour market for all, including women in quality employment, tax systems, social protection systems, education, housing, health and other public services - become sufficiently universal to meet the needs of those most at risk of poverty and social exclusion. this is also why it is important that economic and monetary policies, including competition, agriculture, fisheries and the stability pact, form a coherent triangle with social policies, each of which must be assessed for its effects in the social field, with a view to their revision and adaptation to social objectives, the need for greater economic and social cohesion and the significant reduction of the scandal of keeping some 18% of the population of the european union, or more than 60 million people, living in poverty. equally important is the existence of credible and relevant social indicators, also in terms of gender, which not only measure changes in people's daily lives, but also serve as indicators for alerting and monitoring the evolution of the social situation and, in particular, poverty and exclusion. it is therefore important not to be blind to reality and to take into account that, despite the difficulty of analysing the correlation between public expenditure, social protection and the risk of poverty, without social transfers, in particular old age pensions, 41% of the population of the european union would be at risk of poverty and that this percentage would still remain at 26% if we included old age pensions but excluded other social transfers from income. i therefore regret most of the proposals of the european people's party, which are designed to hide reality and to make it more difficult to implement measures which are essential to achieving social inclusion. i call on them to withdraw these proposals so that the work done is not called into question and there is a positive contribution to the implementation of an effective strategy to combat poverty and social exclusion.
- mr president, the risk of poverty and social exclusion in the european union is one of the most serious and urgent economic and ethical challenges of the 21st century, and i therefore congratulate the rapporteur on her useful and timely contribution. because poverty is a multidimensional problem, impacting every aspect of the human being, there is currently a shift in the perception of this scourge. this has repercussions not only on human dignity and quality of life but also on macroeconomic policies, social security, freedom and security. it is essential that member states provide for long-term strategies aimed in particular at groups at the highest risk of social exclusion and most affected by poverty, in particular the long-term unemployed, the disabled, the elderly, women and migrants. the new knowledge society offers opportunities to reduce social exclusion by creating economic conditions that favour growth and employment and by opening up new forms of participation in society. the key idea is that employment is the best safeguard against social exclusion and poverty. it is up to the member states to ensure open access to education, including computer skills and language skills, and to promote lifelong learning. the new open method of coordination should include greater involvement of local and regional authorities and social partners in order to allow for an effective exchange of best practice. finally, i would like to stress the need for the candidate countries to participate as soon as possible in the social inclusion strategy and to draw up their own national action plans to strengthen it.

- the report (a5-0175/2002) by mr morillon and mr cohn-bendit, on behalf of the committee on foreign affairs, human rights, common security and defence policy, on relations between the european union and the arab maghreb union: implementation of a privileged partnership (2001/2027 (ini)).

- the next item is the recommendation for second reading (a5-0180/2002) on behalf of the committee on the environment, public health and consumer policy, on the common position adopted by the council with a view to adopting a directive of the european parliament and of the council amending council directive 76/768/eec on the approximation of the laws of the member states relating to cosmetic products (15073/1/2001 - c5-0072/2002 - 2000/0077 (cod)) (rapporteur: mrs dagmar roth-behrendt).
- mr president, mr commissioner, my first words are to congratulate the rapporteur on her excellent work, both at first and second reading. this report made proposals in areas where the commission proposal was not sufficiently comprehensive or ambitious, and the truth is that some of these proposals were not accepted in the common position. i therefore believe that there are three matters to which this parliament should draw the attention of both the commission and the council. firstly, the need to ban all potentially carcinogenic or toxic substances in cosmetic products; secondly, the prohibition of the use of fragrances in products intended especially for children, given the risks of allergic reaction which may be associated with them; and thirdly, the most hot issue in this debate is animal testing. i think that both the council's position and the commission's position are unacceptable. on the one hand, the commission is taking a step back from the legislation that was in force, that is to say, in view of the non-compliance with the legislation that was in force, the commission prefers to stop talking about a marketing ban. the council is taking a step forward, because it is throwing the oecd a wrench in the works on the regulation of a matter which it is for the european union to define. i do not know whether this appeal to the oecd is another symptom of the intergovernmental drift which has been affecting the council recently, and i therefore think that the rapporteur's position is the most correct: we must ban animal testing in europe, we must move towards a ban on marketing and the promotion of alternative methods.
- mr president, commissioner, i would like to congratulate mr nisticò on the excellent work he has done and to say that throughout europe the issue is to resolve an extremely difficult situation where blood is concerned. the feeling that citizens have is that the discrepancies between the laws of the member states do not allow them to have security in the face of the increasing circulation and import of blood products, on the one hand; the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament on the subject. and so what we are dealing with here is not so much the question of price, whether blood donation is paid or not, but rather ensuring the level of safety and not forgetting the importance of the question of blood for the citizens. i think there has been a leakage of the debate in recent times and i regret that the question of whether or not gifts are remunerated has been raised as a central issue in this debate. on this point, i think the rapporteur is right and we should follow the common position, because on the one hand it does not ban remuneration, which is essential in some cases to fill the shortage, but on the other hand it encourages voluntary donations and even allows some member states to go further in defining stricter rules which may even prevent the importation of blood products which do not follow the same rules. but again, mr president, what is at stake is safety and the solution to the blood shortage problem.
in writing. - (pt) the purpose of this report is to revise article 6 of parliament's rules of procedure as regards: (a) the concept of the competent authority of a member state to request the waiver of immunity; (b) the appropriate procedure for verifying the competence of the authority from which the request for waiver of the immunity of a member subject to criminal proceedings originates; (c) the procedure to be followed by parliament when examining a request for waiver of the immunity of a member; (d) the procedure to be followed when it is for parliament to confirm the parliamentary immunity of a member. i have given my unconditional support to this initiative, particularly to that part - which i consider the most relevant - which seems to me to fill a glaring gap in the rules of procedure which is well illustrated by the cases presented by mr maccormick in his report, which was debated and voted on simultaneously, namely the cases in which it is not the authorities of a member state who must request the waiver of immunity but the member who must request confirmation of his immunity in the event of non-compliance by a member state (or, as in the particular case of the italian regime, when it is the duty of the member himself and not the authorities to raise the question of immunity).
in writing. - (pt) the preparation of this report has its origins in proceedings brought by italian courts against certain members of the european parliament. in the context of these proceedings, certain provisions relating to immunities were not respected, since it was not the italian authorities who asked the european parliament for an opinion on whether or not to waive immunity, but the persons concerned themselves. the difficulty therefore lay in whether the ep could consider that it had indeed been consulted on the matter, since article 6 of the rules of procedure requires that a request be made to the president of the [ep] by the competent authorities of a member state. it should be noted that in the proceedings in question it was the members concerned who brought these requests to parliament in accordance with italian practice, according to which, when the question of the waiver of the immunity of a member arises in the context of legal proceedings, it is for that member to refer the matter to the chamber to which he belongs. it is in this context that the rapporteur has proposed a specific procedure which is not intended to replace the request for waiver of immunity lodged by the competent national authority as provided for in article 6 of the regulation but to avoid that, in the absence of such a formal request, the national courts may disregard the protection granted to members of the european parliament. the procedure suggested by the rapporteur deserves our unconditional support because it is really the best way of dealing with the shortcomings of the text currently in force. only a substantive reform, such as that partly proposed in the duff report - which was debated and voted on the same day - can provide a complete and structured answer to such problems.
in writing. - (pt) the vote took place in the context of the committee on the environment's opinion on the aspects which have proved to be the most politically sensitive, namely: first, the immediate ban on the sale of new cosmetic products tested on animals, where there are valid alternative methods of testing; secondly, a total ban on the sale of all new cosmetic products tested on animals five years later; thirdly, the ban on animal testing by the end of 2004. it is well known that if the plenary follows the path traced by the committee on the environment, we would be in serious dispute with the council, which would in turn abandon the idea of banning both experimentation and marketing. in other words, we would be faced with a heated and lively conciliation procedure. my position is exactly that, disappointed by a certain conformism which i perceived in the positions which dominated in the council, because i am firmly convinced that it is absolutely essential to advance the positions of industry in this matter, in the name of a balanced balance of forces.
in writing. - (pt) the proposals presented constitute amendments to the existing regulation which clarify the concepts; establish criteria for the allocation of slots, their legal nature and the possibility or not of exchanging them; adopt international concepts for time-facilitated and coordinated airports - which replace the previous designations of coordinated and fully coordinated airports. however, i cannot help but point out that by replacing the facilitator and coordinator roles, the former has an advisory rather than a regulatory aspect, which is why we would be entitled to ask whether the operation of these scheduled airports will be effective, specifically as regards the allocation of slots. as, with this proposal, only genuinely congested airports, known as 'coordinated airports', will have clear legal regulation, the standards will only apply to a limited number of situations, which could be a step backwards in the system of allocating slots.
in writing. - (pt) the amendments tabled during the vote on my report on social inclusion weaken its content on important points. indeed, of the 19 amendments tabled by the epp, 12 were adopted and are aimed at subtracting important aspects from the real strategy that needs to be developed to effectively promote social inclusion and include the negative aspect of defending temporary work, which goes against the promise and the concept of full employment. the deletions of the items relating to the following themes are particularly significant: - the importance of social transfers, especially pensions, without which 41% of the population of the european union would be at risk of falling into poverty; and other social transfers without which, even if pensions were maintained, 26% of the population would be at risk of falling into poverty, instead of the current 18%; - evaluation of the impact of monetary policies, including the stability pact, on social issues (employment, poverty and social exclusion, equal rights and opportunities, etc.). finally, it is important to note that other epp amendments were rejected, which preserved important aspects of the report. that is why i am voting against.
- madam president, madam commissioner, mr minister, the outcome of the preparatory meeting held in bali was disappointing. the fact that the nations have proved incapable of overcoming their unilateral selfishness leads us to regard the outcome of the johannesburg conference with great apprehension. it really is worth saying that there is a certain sense of eventual failure in the air. the european union is not only a political union, but also a political union of the peoples of europe. failure is something that would be more than unacceptable, it would be revolting. it is important to build bridges to save this conference, but let us be clear: on this journey to johannesburg there are many countries which are blocking, others are passive, and it is not too much to say that, objectively, only the european union is going to johannesburg with the will to build an ambitious global commitment to sustainable development. in this context, and because we cannot afford to lose an opportunity which did not arise ten years ago and which is unlikely to be repeated in the next ten, the european union must take the lead. it was the same with the kyoto protocol, it will be the same with the johannesburg summit. leadership that aims to set concrete policies, timetables and targets in areas such as biodiversity, greening the economy, financing for developing countries, energy, water and trade. but this leadership cannot be based on an institutional imbalance, with the commission and the council on one side and the european parliament on the other. we must learn from our mistakes but also from the successes of the past. the role of the european parliament's delegations, for example at the crucial conferences for the kyoto protocol in the hague, bonn and marrakesh, a role which has been emphasized by the commission and the council, makes us think that the european parliament should be given a more appropriate role at this summit in johannesburg. so, out of respect for the citizens we represent here, it seems to me essential that the european parliament should have the right not only to send a delegation of 25 members of this house, but also and above all the right to participate with its representatives in the daily coordination meetings of the european union. the european parliament does not want to negotiate, we know the treaties. it is not the european parliament's job to negotiate, but if we want parliament, the commission and the council to play a unifying role in johannesburg and if we want the conference to be a success, it is essential that parliament should have the right to participate in these daily european union coordination meetings.

- mr president, it is indeed with astonishment that i hear the commission say that there is no cartel situation on the sugar market. commissioner, there is no need for studies, surveys or inspections. i would ask the commissioner to pick up the phone, identify himself as the owner of a supermarket in his country, call another member state, france, germany or the united kingdom, and say that he wants to buy sugar. do that and see what the answer is. the answer is that there is none, because the market is totally cartelised, which is done with the blessing of the commission, which takes it upon itself to defend the cartel when there are cases of resistance, as in the autonomous region of the azores. these are the facts. none of its justifications appeared in the court of auditors' report when it said this, and on the contrary the commission merely said that this was due to the nature of the sector.
- mr president of the european institutions, ladies and gentlemen, we must immediately express our support for the intensive work which the european union has been carrying out, together with the united states, russia and the united nations, to prevent a war between india and pakistan. i had the opportunity to visit the area a few weeks ago and from the contacts i have had with the parties concerned in the framework of the presidency of the parliament's delegation for south asia, i can say that, in addition to the obvious tensions, there is also a real desire for peace. it is true that these two great countries have been at war three times already: twice over kashmir and once over bangladesh. but now it would not be, so to speak, another war, for both countries have nuclear weapons and the capacity to carry them. as minister ramón de miguel has stressed, in a military conflict the possibility of escalation cannot be excluded. so it is with great hope that we have seen some positive developments in the last few days. on the one hand, the commitment of the president of pakistan to stop infiltrations through the line of control in kashmir; on the other hand, india's decision to reopen its airspace to pakistani civil aviation, to resume diplomatic representation and to withdraw vessels from its military fleet in the sea of oman. however, the news from the united states on monday linking the terrorist who was planning to build a nuclear device to connections in pakistan forces us to maintain a high level of vigilance over what is happening in this area. the world can only benefit from a more active european union on the international scene. but this requires, among other things, better coordination of our efforts in foreign and defence policy and concrete progress towards common diplomacy. only then will the european union be able to play a real role in conflict prevention.
in writing. - (pt) the final agreement on the revision of directive 76/207/eec is positive, although insufficient in relation to the initial proposals and the existing problems. however, with the amendments introduced and the inclusion of the definition of sexual harassment, direct and indirect discrimination, and the prohibition of discrimination against women on the basis of maternity leave, the directive is more in line with the evolution of society in terms of working conditions and family situations. among the amendments adopted, i would also like to highlight: - the possibility for member states to grant specific advantages to the underrepresented sex in an occupational activity; - the creation of equality bodies, which will have competence in the promotion, analysis, monitoring and support of the principle of equality between men and women; - effective measures to ensure that effective penalties are applied in the event of non-compliance with the directive; - the protection of persons who help victims of discrimination and sexual harassment; - the promotion of planned and systematic equal treatment at the workplace. that is why we voted in favour. we now hope that national governments will implement and promote this directive.
in writing. - (pt) as you know, the proposal for a regulation establishing the european maritime safety agency is part of the second package of measures proposed by the commission following the sinking of the oil tanker erika. the planned creation of the agency is in fact accompanied by other measures by which the commission aims at more effective protection of european shipping against the risks of accidents and protection of the seas against pollution. the new agency is very important in our view because it will provide the member states and the commission with the technical and scientific support needed to enable them to apply community legislation effectively in the field of maritime safety and to prevent pollution from ships. it shall monitor the implementation of the legislation; it will assess the effectiveness of the measures adopted. we hope that portugal will be chosen to host the seat of the future european agency, thanks to its unique geographical position and the extent of its maritime area.
in writing. - (pt) we share the concern to adopt stricter requirements for the eligibility of donors and to establish a quality system at community level for blood establishments. the same applies to the establishment of common standards for the training of staff and the traceability of blood from donor to patient. we also welcome the amendments adopted by the committee on the environment and public health concerning the basic principle of 'voluntary unpaid blood donation', the principle of subsidiarity and the professional qualifications of those working in this sector. the question of the gratuitousness and voluntariness of blood donation is indeed of crucial importance. we therefore welcome the fact that the committee has taken up its original definition, formulated at first reading (making a very clear distinction between payment for blood itself and reimbursement of the costs borne by the donor) and we support this line in the final vote in plenary. in addition, member states should be able to set their own stricter requirements for voluntary unpaid donations and to prohibit or restrict imports of blood and blood components that do not meet their own requirements.
in writing. - (pt) i congratulate mr gil-robles on his report on the election of the representatives of the european parliament by direct universal suffrage. the participation of eu citizens in the european elections is far from satisfactory. the participation rate has risen from 63% (1979) to 49.7% (1999), while the powers of the european parliament have been substantially increased. the european parliament gains in power and loses in legitimacy. we are concerned about the steady decline in the turnout in the european elections. for the next european elections in 2004, the community institutions and the member states must adopt the necessary measures to enable the electorate to be more aware of the importance of the ballot. member states should agree on the date of the next european elections and ensure that it respects national traditions or statutory holidays, in order to optimise the conditions for voting. member states must take all measures to allow greater participation in european elections, including procedures for registration in the electoral register and for the exercise of the right to vote. i agree with the definition of incompatibility between the mandate of a member of the european parliament and that of a member of the national assembly. this was already provided for in portuguese law and is now becoming a community provision. i note that the council has not accepted parliament's proposal to reserve 10% of the seats for the european constituency. she did the right thing. this would not have helped to bring citizens closer to the voters. this would have only served to remove national representation from the scene in favour of a more distant group of so-called european personalities... in order to increase voter participation, it is essential to close the information gap and to work on the development of an information and communication policy of the european union geared to the target population.
in writing. - (pt) we regret the clear federalist content of the report, as well as its timid nonconformism in not approving the creation of a new single european electoral constituency. it is absolutely inappropriate to take advantage of this opportunity to try to insinuate changes in the system and its balance in areas or aspects which can only be changed by a revision of the treaties. on the contrary, we directly support a council decision which, in its simplicity, should be limited to updates consistent with the revisions of the treaties which have taken place in the meantime, as well as other practical improvements arising from past experience. for example, the period for holding direct elections to the european parliament should be extended - allowing some member states to hold them in may. portugal, for example, could solve its chronic problem of the european elections coinciding with the month of june; this, according to analysts, is inflating the level of abstention.

the rapporteur. - mr president, this report represents a significant political boost for the enlargement process from the european parliament. it is also an encouraging sign at this crucial moment when the commission, the council and the member states, as well as the applicant countries, are preparing to enter into negotiations on the most complex chapters with the greatest budgetary implications. we cannot ignore the fact that the agricultural, regional policy and financial and budgetary provisions alone account for about 80% of the community budget. it is certainly for this reason that the commission has prudently proposed a system of gradual implementation for direct aid paid to farmers under the cap and for the allocation of structural funds to the new member states. we have no doubt that these were reasons of the same nature as those which led some of the present member states to call for a thorough restructuring of the cap as a condition for obtaining a common starting position of the european union for the negotiations on the agricultural chapter. if we add to this the obvious need to continue to preserve the policy of economic and social cohesion among elections last april. the socialist party, in coalition with the liberal party, won the election, taking 198 seats in parliament, compared with 188 for the previous government. it seems certain that opinion between the previous government and the winners of the elections on a number of issues (e.g. the statute law, social dialogue, public procurement), it cannot be ruled out that hungary's position in the accession process may change, but i do not think that this will affect the process. the hungarian economy remains a viable market economy and its economic performance is among the best in the region. in addition, hungary has so far closed 24 chapters and is among the candidate countries most advanced in the negotiations. in the chapter on culture and audiovisual, i am pleased to measure the hungarian authorities have shown the consistency of their initiatives against corruption and money laundering. it is these progresses, summarised here, and the dynamics they reveal, that presence of its 22 members in this house from the next parliamentary term in 2004.
