- madam president, what is happening with the accession negotiations on the agricultural dossier is a clear example of the insistence on pursuing the enlargement process on the basis of a community financial framework which does not guarantee the principle of economic and social cohesion, given that the financial framework of agenda 2000 was intended for only six candidates and now it is ten countries with an income per head of about 40% of the european union average. this is particularly true in the case of agriculture, given the known weaknesses of a sector important for employment and the economy of several candidate countries. this implies major socioeconomic impacts and increased costs, requiring a review of the financial perspective to avoid the costs being borne by those most affected or least developed. the division of aid between second and first-tier countries and access to quotas far below their capacities and needs is therefore unacceptable. the consequences of this would be more unemployment and more poverty, which is unacceptable. rural development and the defence of quality agricultural production require a review of the cap and not the renationalisation of its costs, which would harm farmers not only in these countries but also farmers in countries with weaker agriculture, such as portugal.
- mr president, i should like to express my agreement with the report by the turkish member. regional development programmes aim to promote employment competitiveness and the creation of conditions for sustainable development in the most disadvantaged regions of europe in an enlarged union. efficiency in community aid is needed, which implies an evaluation not only of the cost-benefit analysis but also of quantitative and qualitative indicators. the priority of the funds is to enhance the quality of programmes, their interlinkage with the resolution of regional disparities in the union and their influence on the quality of life of the population. the programmes of the common agricultural policy, the common fisheries policy and the information and research society must be incorporated into economic and social cohesion policy so that disparities do not worsen in relation to the richer regions. the application of the cohesion fund and the structural funds to the development of the transeuropean networks, and in particular the european high-speed rail network, is necessary if it is to extend to the peripheral continental territories in the enlarged european union.
- mr president, as an inevitable reality, enlargement of the european union must also be an opportunity to take stock of its own policies, bearing in mind that there are still many inequalities within it and that enlargement cannot be at the expense of those who are the poorest in the present european union. the commission's proposal for agricultural negotiations seems to me to be reasonable as a whole. the most controversial issue is the application of the current direct aids, created by the cap reforms of 1992 and 1999 to compensate for the price reductions that were then decided. objectively speaking, these countries are not entitled to this kind of aid. they were created for the sole purpose of compensating for price reductions. in the new member states, not only have prices not been reduced, but they are likely to increase. what is wrong with the commission proposal is that it sends a signal that the current direct aid figurehead will be maintained beyond 2006. the current system of direct aid under the cap has proved justified in the medium term in order to avoid a fall in farmers' incomes. but it is not sustainable in the long term because, not being extended to all agricultural production, it is highly discriminatory. we therefore need to create in 2006 a new system of aid for agriculture justified by the logic of the multiple roles that agriculture plays in society. but does this mean that aid should not be given to the new countries in the east? no, i'm not going. i think that the commission's proposal to introduce 25% of the aid already will be acceptable if such aid is paid on an objective horizontal basis - for example on the basis of holdings or areas - without a link to specific crops or productions. if so, then the proposal will already have greater technical consistency and will be fairer, both in the present and in the future.
the commission has already proposed that the council should adopt a decision on the question of indirect taxation in the canary islands. in fact, in the context of the introduction of a tax called 'arbitrary on the imports and deliveries of goods in the canary islands-aiem', it is intended to authorize spain to apply reduced rates to certain products manufactured in this outermost region. it should be noted at the outset that the community authorization does not concern the creation of aiem, since the spanish authorities are free to do so, since the community directives on vat and excise duties do not apply in the canary islands. the council's decision is therefore necessary, but in order to validate tax derogations and their extension to canary regional products, a point which is not properly clarified in the commission proposal. most of the amendments i tabled, which were approved by the committee on regional policy, transport and tourism, were aimed at making this clearer. the central issue in the commission's proposal is therefore the introduction of tax derogations from articles 23, 25 and 90 of the treaty, in so far as there is favourable discrimination between imported industrial goods and certain industrial goods produced in the canary islands, since the latter may be taxed at reduced rates of 5, 15 or 25 percentage points, depending on the type of product. the tax exemptions are justified and have a legal basis in article 299 (2) of the treaty. this article recognises the very specific reality of the so-called outermost regions and the corresponding need for special treatment by the european union towards these regions. the proposal under consideration deserves my full support. it is intended to enable a region such as the canary islands, which has serious competitive disadvantages in the industrial sector as a result of its ultraperipheral situation, to better integrate and take advantage of the highly competitive dynamics of the european single market, economic and monetary union and globalisation itself. this type of specific measures is crucial to ensure the survival and modernisation of industrial activities in the canary islands, which are currently reduced to just over 5% of regional gdp, whereas historically they have always taken on a very significant expression. but the integration of the canary islands in 1991 into the customs territory of the european union and the gradual dismantling, by 1999, of the tax then in force, which also contained a logic of defence of regional productions, accentuated the sensitivity and vulnerability of the canary islands industrial sector to external competition. in fact, as the situation in the tobacco industry clearly shows, the decline has been steady. between 1985 and 2000, the tobacco industry lost more than 3,300 jobs, or about 67% of its workforce. so it is imperative that we ask ourselves the following question: the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the canary islands are fully integrated. should the canary islands rely almost exclusively on tourism-related activities for job and wealth creation? is this not too heavy a price to pay for the canary islands to be fully integrated into the european union? the commission proposal under consideration thus represents a legitimate and appropriate instrument to ensure, in a region with the specific characteristics of the canary islands, a minimum of economic diversification. but the commission's proposal is also justified by other considerations, in particular the competitive inequality suffered by industrial enterprises in the outermost regions. it is well known that the situation in the outermost regions imposes additional costs on the pursuit of industrial activities in these regions and that, in the specific case of the canary islands, these costs are estimated at around 8 percentage points for large companies, 9% for smes, and even reaching 29% in the case of tobacco production. this situation requires us to draw a most important conclusion: rather than being harmful to free competition, the tax measures proposed by the commission are intended to level the playing field. only in this way will effective equality of opportunity be guaranteed for canarian industrialists, or at least for some of them, since the proposed tax measures are not of a general and systematic nature. i understand that we are discussing an appropriate instrument to meet the specific needs of the canary islands as an outermost region. the same understanding was expressed by the committee on regional policy, transport and tourism in approving my report by an overwhelming majority. the european parliament, in keeping with its various previous positions of clear solidarity and support for the outermost regions, cannot fail to give a favourable opinion on the commission proposal under discussion.
- mr president, i too would like to congratulate our colleague, mr sérgio marques, on the excellent work he has done here, particularly with regard to the clear separation between the existence of a tax system of its own, which the canary islands enjoy in their own right, and the possibility of discriminating against certain local products for their economic development. i would also like to greet our colleagues from the canary islands who are here and to tell them, speaking on behalf of the azores, that we are fully with them and that we fully support them in their objectives of the most balanced development possible for the canary islands. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i think it is essential that we understand one thing: we are not dealing here with proposals for tax exemptions from some rich countries in central europe, such as luxembourg, which seriously jeopardise the balance of the internal market, which create economic imbalances, which even create a concentration of economic activity which is of no interest to anyone. we are dealing with specific measures which are intended to support industries, small businesses which would otherwise not survive. we cannot compare the survival of small rum manufacturers in the canary islands with what is happening to sugar in the european union: a cartel that gives fabulous profits to an overprotected industry and which the commission is not able - nor does it want - to prosecute. quite the opposite! we had proof of that here yesterday. i would therefore like to call on all members to support without any doubt the proposal put forward by mr marques.
- mr president, evasion and fraud are unfortunately common elements in the tax systems of all the countries of europe. tax evasion and tax fraud have a country-specific national dimension, which is tax-related to its culture and dependent on its level of citizenship, but also have a supranational dimension based on the use of sophisticated techniques and schemes to circumvent the law. it is clearly up to each country to develop the appropriate measures to eliminate tax evasion, but part of this stimulus must also come from union policies. in portugal, for example, it is known from studies of unquestionable scientific merit that more than 25% of the potential tax revenue is lost every year. it would therefore be sufficient to recover around one third (eur 4 billion) of this tax revenue to avoid any problem in meeting the objectives set out in the stability and growth pact and, in particular, in balancing public finances in 2004. this, of course, without taking into account the social damage resulting from the injustices and inequalities - that is, the inequity - that such a permissive tax system allows and enhances. in this context, all the community measures taken to support this effort, and in particular the continuation of the fiscalis programme, updated and strengthened in the framework of the proposals presented by the european parliament, also applicable to the applicant countries, are correct, welcome and supportive. however, tax evasion and tax fraud also have a supranational dimension and, although important, it is not enough to combat it by harmonizing technical behaviour or by standardizing administrative procedures. it is essential to move decisively towards legislative harmonization itself. economic and monetary union implies, sooner or later, a similar and harmonised taxation model in all countries. although steps and progress in this area (sufficiently supported by common policies, which imply various concessions) must be careful and progressive, this policy cannot fail to be implemented in compliance with the objectives set for europe at the lisbon summit. in accepting the renewal of the fiscalis programme as an indispensable instrument for the effective functioning of the internal market, the european parliament must also give a clear signal in this regard, which will be nothing more than the other side of the requirements of economic competitiveness and financial consolidation which we are so often - and rightly - prepared to demand.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, fishing exploits renewable natural resources, it is true, but they are fragile and destructible. for centuries, with limited means of capture, the idea was that the resources available were inexhaustible. this sentiment is still echoed today by some reactions against conservation policies. however, faced with a public opinion sensitive to the protection of nature and the economic risks generated by the collapse of some fisheries species, fishermen are aware that they must be allies of marine life and not its enemies. it is in this perspective that any reform of fisheries policy, whether general or targeted at specific situations, as is the case, must be balanced. the legislation in question, which is the subject of the excellent stihler report, is intended to prevent the catastrophe of the extinction of such prized fish as cod and haddock. the gradualness of the measures proposed and the effectiveness of the mechanism for implementing them can be debated, the accuracy of the scientific data on which they are based can be debated, but what cannot be underestimated is the danger threatening cod stocks and, to a lesser extent, fish stocks and the need to reverse the situation and recover these stocks. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the fisheries sector is not left behind. this is why the committee on fisheries has chosen to make the proposals on fisheries more flexible. i wonder if we're on the right track. i have my doubts. for these reasons, i call on the commission to set up a scientifically and technically credible research system on which to base our choices. it is imperative that you do so. as for cod, it is one of the elements of portuguese cuisine, everyone knows that. but it's not just food that man lives on. the cod fishery in newfoundland left deep marks on the memory of the fishermen and indelible traces in portuguese literature. so it is fitting to say: let's save the cod!
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, we see the internet as an opportunity for everyone and not as a new source of inequality, exclusion or isolation. this is why we will always stand with movements that are dedicated not only to combating 'info-exclusion', but also and above all, in a positive and proactive way, to leveraging the extraordinary resources offered by information technologies to respond to the needs and aspirations of vulnerable and traditionally marginalised social groups, such as people with disabilities and the elderly. we must therefore give all our energy to this drive. it was during my country's last european presidency, in june 2000, at the santa maria da feira summit, that the eeurope 2002 action plan was officially adopted, including among its objectives the making of the information society accessible to all citizens of the european union, and thus special attention should be paid to people with disabilities and to the fight against 'info-exclusion' in general. we're keeping active on that front. the adoption in 2001 by the eib and the member states of the guidelines for websites was very important in this respect. in this context, we welcome the commission's communication and the extraordinary work done by the rapporteur. and while we doubt the willingness of the public to see them fully implemented by the end of 2003, we believe that we should insist on this objective and make a particular demand on those public websites which, because they have a specific social function or are of particular relevance to people with disabilities and the elderly, should be the first to fully meet this objective. then no delay would be understandable.
in writing. - (pt) although we largely agree with the report and follow it in its entirety, our votes reflect the great caution we always exercise in these matters and the constant vigilance which must be maintained with regard to any change in the status quo which explicitly or implicitly tends towards the communitization of tax policy, which we do not favour.
in writing. - (pt) for hake, the current exploitation model, irrespective of the reliability and limitations of the basic information, means that the level of fishing is too high. therefore, temporary reductions in the level of fishing are beneficial and a reduction for the period 2002-2006 seems essential for the proper exploitation of this stock. the commission's concern to reduce fishing effort is therefore acceptable, but it is completely unacceptable that this reduction should be achieved by tearing up vessels, which implies the irreparable loss of fishing licences. there are other equally effective methods of managing this fishery to ensure an appropriate level of fishing, such as reducing the number of fishing days per year until the stock returns to acceptable levels of abundance. this would significantly reduce the socio-economic problems of the companies that currently hold a fishing right. the situation of cod stocks appears to be more serious and requires somewhat more stringent measures than those proposed for hake, although these may vary according to species. however, these measures must also be temporary and must never involve the permanent loss of fishing licences. we regret that the two cases, that of hake and cod, were not separated, and that is why we voted against.
in writing. - (pt) our vote against the joint resolution is due to the adoption of unacceptable positions, including the continuing pressure on the irish people to ratify the treaty of nice, which establishes a close and undue link between its ratification and enlargement, or the insistence on an eu-nato agreement on the use of resources and the operability of the esdp. nor is it acceptable to claim to make faster progress with more restrictive community policies than the existing policies in the various member states in the field of immigration and asylum law. as proposed in the gue/ngl resolution, which was not adopted, the main focus was on giving greater attention to economic and social cohesion in the member states, by reviewing economic and monetary policies, including those of the stability pact, by halting the process of liberalisation and deregulation, by paying particular attention to the social dimension of the enlargement of the european union and by giving greater firmness and determination in implementing initiatives in favour of developing countries and promoting peace, with emphasis on defending the palestinian people.
in writing. - (pt) we are used to hearing each other speak in the name of europe, but the use of this common ground is clearly an abuse: we are only 15 countries in the european union, and europe has many more states, peoples and nations. we mention it to put our essential perspective on this debate: enlargement is indispensable; enlargement is a major political issue from which we cannot turn away. of course, enlargement brings challenges and difficulties, but we must face them and overcome them - for that is the purpose of the european project, from which the union was born, to make sense. it is essential for us that europeans are united, all other agendas are secondary. that is also why we reject the obstinate institutional fantasies of our colleagues who make a religion of the federalist creed and who worship the power of brussels as a deity, which intervenes in everything and wants to govern everything for the future and even for the past. they only create unnecessary resistance and dangerous tensions. the union - it is time to remember this - was not created so that we could meddle in each other's affairs; it was created so that we could cooperate, with mutual respect, and strengthen our common energies.
in writing. - (pt) the problem of border regions is probably one of the problems that will arise in the future with enlargement. clearly, the financial allocation for this initiative is not sufficient - it never will be. but even in the face of these limitations, it demonstrates the attention that is rightly due to the regions which will suffer from 'geographical remoteness' within the future european union and which may suffer from the harmful effects of competition from the new neighbouring regions of the eu. we therefore hope that it will be possible to establish - in some way, also through the best possible use of the resources available - a new dynamism in these regions and that this new dynamism also has positive effects for the regions immediately beyond the current eu borders.
in writing. - (pt) i congratulate our colleague maurizio turco on his report, which makes a positive contribution to the debate on the importance of cohesion policy, its maintenance and its strengthening. however, i would like to emphasize two points which seem to me important for this discussion: - the need to continue the effort to simplify and accelerate the implementation procedures for structural actions, which are essential for their sound and effective implementation, guaranteed by appropriate evaluation and control systems; - respect for the principle of additionality, which guarantees a coherent structural policy, reflecting the added value of the community's structural policy in national regional policies.
in writing. - (pt) this report should balance the need for testing in order to put an end to the tragedy of some 2 000 deaths and more than 18 000 serious injuries in accidents that occur every year in the eu. after more than 20 years of debate, the commission is satisfied with the voluntary agreement of the european automobile industry - by refraining from presenting a legislative proposal - which is unacceptable in our view. the consequences of this inactivity are serious. and the reduction of the phase a tests, which go from four to two, contrary to the proposal of the european committee for experimental vehicles, will mean a 75% reduction in the lives saved; moreover, the objectives proposed by the agency will not be achieved for another 10 years. only a framework directive can guarantee the objective of effective protection of pedestrians in the eu. the report we have just adopted, which insists on this voluntary agreement, will therefore be the subject of an abstention on our part.
in writing. - (pt) our vote reflects our view that the compromise reached in the committee on regional policy, transport and tourism is acceptable. in this very sensitive and often forgotten and undervalued area - basically structured pedestrian protection - we agree with the preparation of a framework directive clearly setting out the timetable and the terms of a negotiated agreement with the automotive industry, particularly with regard to the control tests indicated. this approach preserves, in a way, the commission's approach, which has the merit of speeding up a process which might otherwise be stuck indefinitely and not reach its objective, except at the end of an extremely long phase of consultation. of course, it will be necessary to analyse the framework directive in which the commission will have to implement the general conditions of the agreement negotiated with the automobile industry, as indicated. only then can we make a careful and informed assessment of its contents.
in writing. - (pt) we welcome this commission communication and the excellent report by our colleague bas belder, both of which are intended to give substance and further development to a fundamental proposal put forward at the feira summit and reaffirmed at the nice summit, concerning the fight against all forms of exclusion, particularly those linked to disability and age. the new opportunities offered by new technologies cannot be squandered, nor paradoxically act in the opposite direction through a perverse info-exclusion arising from our insensitivity. we consider the adoption in 2001 by the eu and the member states of 'directives' on websites to be very important, as we consider it essential to continue the work and efforts in this area of exchanging information, evaluating, reviewing the relevant legislation, stimulating best practice and creating and articulating centres of excellence in this field.

in writing. - (pt) with this proposal, which the rapporteur has accepted, the commission intends above all to guarantee the financing of 12 priority projects in the energy sector, while ensuring that the barcelona conclusions on the liberalisation of the energy sector are put into practice. it is with these objectives in mind that the community co-financing is increased from 10% to 20%. beyond a change of priorities in the field of transeuropean networks, the proposal again emphasizes a shift of focus towards eastern europe, to the detriment of the current cohesion countries, such as portugal, which are only marginally included in this proposal. the proposal suggests an additional need of €150 million for the financial perspective for the period 2000-06. however, the commission intends to finance eur 100 million through the use of amounts included in the current internal policy ceiling, which will lead to greater risks for other objectives under this budget line, by requesting new programming for the remaining eur 50 million from the current amounts of the trans-european networks. in these circumstances, this proposal does not deserve our support.

- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to congratulate mrs anne van lancker on her report. it is an excellent work, which follows closely the final declarations of the cairo and beijing conferences promoted by the united nations and aims to exchange best practice in the field of reproductive health and the defence of women's rights and dignity. as was clear in portugal only recently, when 17 women were subjected to the vexation of a trial in a court, with the complete deprivation of their private life, on the charge of clandestine abortion - which was not proved, except in one case - and in which an inadmissible young woman was convicted, this is not the way to combat clandestine abortion. even because those with the financial means can always resort to a country where this practice is free. portuguese women do it in neighboring spain, for example. while it is true that abortion, as mentioned in the report, should not be promoted as a method of family planning, it is essential that there be a health and social policy that allows a reduction in the use of abortion, in particular by promoting sex education and family planning properly targeted at young people in order to avoid unwanted pregnancies, especially of adolescents. but it is also essential that voluntary termination of pregnancy is legal, safe and universally accessible in order to safeguard women's reproductive health and rights, as the report says, and that it can be accessible to all women, regardless of their economic and financial situation, and not just to those who can do it in a good clinic in the neighbouring country. this is hypocrisy and it must end as soon as possible!
- mr president, any of the matters in the report are not the responsibility of the community but of the national political bodies. so we shouldn't be debating it or voting on it tomorrow. this abuse of external interference against self-determination and the free conscience of peoples reaches the extreme of absurdity in paragraph 28, by seeking to force our taxpayers to finance actions in third countries in the service of a militant agenda of promoting abortion. this is the report's greatest weakness: it is obviously confusing and misleading. it mixes isolated positive things, such as the rejection of abortion as a method of family planning, the need to achieve a decrease in the use of abortion, the consideration of sex education in a global and positive way, based on mutual respect and responsibility, with others that either correspond exactly to the opposite doctrine or lead demonstrably to the opposite result. and so the overall application of this set of guidelines, which has been dominant in many countries, as it is amalgamated in the report, cannot but lead to disastrous results, some of which are acknowledged in the report, whether in the number of cases of teenage pregnancy or in the alarming spread of sexually transmitted diseases despite all the information that has been widely distributed. according to the press, my country would be one of the direct targets of this report. portugal held a referendum four years ago on the liberalization of abortion, and won "the right to life". i therefore regret to see some occasional federalists looking to the community as a forum for appeal and supporting a little pressure trick against the free expression of the people's will. and of course, the debate continues. but this debate, which is a serious debate, is not about women's rights, it is about the rights of the child, the unborn child. that's what divides us, not the body of the one who chooses, but the life of the one who doesn't. the report takes the position of the strongest against the weakest. the weakest is also often the woman, abandoned by her husband or partner, or pressured by the social circle to have an abortion that she does not want in her heart. and the weakest is always and in every case the child in a vulnerable and helpless stage of growth. it is these weaker ones who need the protection of society and the law and who look to us, asking for it. in my country we chose to do this, we wanted to take the side of the weakest. and it is our right to do so as long as the law is so and the will of the people so.
- mr president, this owninitiative report is plagued by several grave flaws. the first concerns the principle of subsidiarity which underlies community law. the present motion for a resolution violates this principle, which requires not only respect for but also encouragement of the exercise of the autonomy of the member states in matters within their competence and the exclusion of unlawful interference in the internal affairs of each state. the european union should refrain from intervening in matters which fall within the competence of the member states. it is notorious and obvious that this report deals with issues which exceed the sphere of competence of the european union, inter alia, in relation to health and justice policies. another of the capital sins is the seriousness of this proposal, which deliberately and blatantly disregards the popular will expressed in a free and democratic way in the referenda on the decriminalisation of abortion held in some member states, such as portugal. the aim of its adoption is to provide a means of lobbying to bring the discussion on voluntary termination of pregnancy back onto the political agenda in some member states . and this will have the perverse consequence of creating mistrust among the citizens of the member states and also of the candidate countries, and this report does a disservice to the european union. the european union is an original political model based on cultural diversity and respect for the historical and ethical heritage of each of its member nations. the european construction must give priority to these values. the deepening of the european spirit must be achieved by respecting differences and by reinforcing the principle that decisions should be taken as closely as possible to the citizens of the union. these two requirements are lacking in the report we are debating today. for these reasons, we do not agree with this proposal and we will have no choice but to vote against it. a final word to congratulate the efforts of our colleague emilia müller, the shadow rapporteur on this controversial report, who has spared no effort in trying to improve it.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, we welcome a proposal from the commission to establish the rules for the participation of companies, research centres and universities, and the rules for the dissemination of research results in the framework of the implementation of the 2002-06 framework programme. i congratulate my colleague the rapporteur on the excellent report she has presented to us and i give her my full support. like the rapporteur, i agree with the commission's approach to facilitating access to finance through simplified legislation and greater operational flexibility. i therefore welcome the new objectives proposed, namely the introduction of simple and understandable rules for participation, faster and simpler management and the protection of the community's financial interests. let me now concentrate the final part of this speech, as the member elected by an outermost region, the autonomous region of madeira, on a point which directly concerns the outermost regions. recital 9 of the amendment to the commitment refers to the need for the activities of the sixth framework programme to promote the participation of the most remote regions of the community. the previous framework programme also referred to the need to take account of the specificities of these regions. i have, however, argued in an amendment that we should go further, even as a follow-up to that recital, because i believe that there should be an increase in the criterion of community added value for projects in an outermost region. such an increase would be of great importance, as it would avoid penalising the outermost regions, particularly because of the difficulty in finding external partners willing to partner with institutions based in those regions despite the quality of the projects to be applied for. however, whilst full equality of participants and a general opening up of projects to new participants is advocated, the increase in the criterion of community added value would be indispensable to guarantee access to the sixth framework programme for projects from these regions.
in writing. - (pt) the right of consumers to be informed about the type of products they buy cannot be jeopardised by the interests of multinational companies which want to conceal the use of gmos in agricultural and food production. the commission's proposed 1% threshold for the absence of labelling should therefore be lowered or even eliminated, as the proposed threshold will result in a large number of processed and derived products from gmos not including this information on the label. the report is, however, a positive step, thanks to the protections it provides, which justify the opposition of the industry itself and the usa and make its adoption strategically important not only for the european union but also for the contribution it makes to the non-proliferation of gmos in developing and least developed countries. the epp's proposals are regrettable. it is clear that the main aim at the moment is to avoid the worst, since the main thing was to maintain the moratorium on new authorizations in the field of gmos, because, as you know, they prevent a future option in that they constitute a point of no return with unforeseeable consequences in the fields of the environment, the economy and human and animal health.
in writing. - (pt) in the absence of strong and integrated legislation on genetically modified organisms (gmos) that can provide guarantees for consumers and industry, the procedures for granting marketing authorisations at eu level have been stalled for three years. i welcome the text adopted today (traceability and labelling of gmos) because it allows the movement of gmos on the market to be recorded and monitored, as well as the movement of foodstuffs for human and animal consumption produced from gmos. this will give consumers a choice and make it easier to withdraw a product from the market if necessary. however, it would have been preferable if these rules had been contained in a single regulation rather than two, as was the case on a proposal from the european commission. so there is an unnecessary fragmentation of legislation on gmos. the proposal for a directive also contains provisions which, if over-zealous, can open the way to falsification, consumer deception and distortion of competition. rules should be clear and simple. i therefore support the amendments tabled by the rapporteur to ensure that traceability and the resulting labelling apply to gmos and products containing genetically modified dna or proteins. the extension of labelling to other products, which do not contain any traces of genetically modified dna or genetically modified proteins, would be totally illusory for the consumer. moreover, such a provision would be contrary to the objectives of the regulation and would be costly for both companies and consumers.
in writing. - (pt) i congratulate mrs quisthoudt-rowohl on the excellent report she has presented to us, which i wholeheartedly support. i agree with the rapporteur on the commission's approach of facilitating access to finance through simplification of legislation and greater operational flexibility for beneficiaries. i would like to stress one point, however, which concerns the outermost regions and on which i tabled an amendment because of its major importance: it is the need for programmes relating to an outermost region to increase the value-added criterion. the preamble to the sixth framework programme mentions the need to take account of the specificities of the ors as regards access to the programme. however, an increase in the criterion relating to community added value avoids penalising the ors, in particular because of the difficulty in finding external partners willing to associate themselves with institutions established in the ors despite the quality of their projects.
in writing. - (pt) our vote against this report is clear from its statements, in particular: the reaffirmation of the 'primordial importance of the primary objective of maintaining price stability', independent of growth and employment; defending the irrationality of the stability pact; the call for 'constant wage moderation' and the insistence on the old capitalist recipe of making workers pay for the crisis, while the productivity gains transferred from wages to profits have not produced more investment or more employment, but more enrichment of the usual small group. this report ignores the current economic climate and again calls for more liberalization, privatization, integration of capital markets and, of course, more flexibility in labour; he considers that criticism of the ecb's behaviour towards the federal reserve is unfair, forgetting that the disinflationary policy followed contributed to unemployment as the restrictive monetary policy did not allow the full benefit of the period of economic growth in the second half of the 1990s, a criticism shared not only by various internationally renowned economists, such as solow, but also by the united nations economic commission for europe.

- mr president, first of all i would like to thank the rapporteur, mr medina ortega, for his kind words, but i must say that the report has lost nothing by my replacement. mr president, the sharing of opportunities that is provided by the timeshare use of real estate has not exactly brought happiness to all users. the problems which these products have had in the past and which still persist are public knowledge and it was because of these problems that directive 94/47 was adopted at one time with the aim of providing protection against flagrant breaches of trust. there are, however, problems that remain. the directive follows the principle of minimum harmonization, setting a low level of consumer protection measures which member states could then extend if they so wished. unfortunately, this was not the general practice. as a result, consumers are now faced with a patchwork of laws which offer greater protection in some states than in others. since the amendment of the directive, the number of complaints received from european consumers has increased rather than decreased, as is acknowledged by the commission in its implementation report. there are also increasingly sophisticated ways of offering timeshare products, contracts of less than three years, usually 35 months, thus not allowing the minimum duration covered by the directive and escaping community discipline. similarly, the directive covers only real estate which is used for at least seven days a year, which has led to an increase in timeshare contracts for periods of less than seven days. it is therefore clear that the directive needs, as mr medina ortega rightly says, urgent revision. consumers need an effective measure to protect their transactions, in particular those which take place in more than one member state. the industry needs assurances that fair practices will be encouraged and supported through a revised directive that will eradicate the outrageous practices we have seen. mr president, either we adopt rules which, without any regulatory exaggeration, of course, allow this activity to develop freely, or we leave everything as it is and subject ourselves, in an open and information society such as the one in which we live, to all the well-intentioned and ill-intentioned attacks on this industry, which want to destroy it in the competitive way in which it should operate. our choice must be in favour of consumers, tourism, leisure, the opportunity for everyone to take a holiday, respect for responsible and honest promoters, and it is in this spirit that i would like to congratulate the rapporteur, manuel medina ortega, who clearly sets out in this text the shortcomings to be overcome in a future legislative text. the responsibility and the say is now with the commission.

- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, in greeting mr ozan ceyhun, we regret the increase in the number of racist demonstrations in european countries. according to the european monitoring centre on racism and xenophobia, there was an increase in hostilities and attacks in 2000 (compared with the previous year), with particular expression in several countries of the union. with the tragic events of 11 september 2001, the situation has worsened, with an increase in tensions and violence, including anti-islamic violence. it should be stressed once again that racism and xenophobia are a clear violation of the principles of freedom, democracy, respect for human rights and fundamental freedoms and the rule of law - - doc. a5-0115/2002 by mrs mckenna, on behalf of the committee on fisheries, on the proposal for a council decision on the conclusion by the european community of the convention on the conservation and management of fishery resources in the south-east atlantic (com (2001) 679 - c5-0666/2001 - 2001/0280 (cns)).
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, protecting, preserving and conserving fisheries resources means respecting nature, considering the seas as precious and irreplaceable means of life, and defending life and biodiversity. but it is also an imperative for the survival of human activities that, if the seas are exploited wildly, are depleted and with them those activities. no marine environment should be left to predatory activities, and it is therefore surprising that a marine area as vast as the south-east atlantic should not be subject to the discipline and management of any kind of organisation. the convention establishing a regional fisheries organization for this part of the high atlantic, which was signed in april 2001 by the main coastal states and other interested states, is therefore to be welcomed. we therefore fully support the proposal for a decision on the conclusion by the european community of the convention on the conservation and management of fishery resources in the south-east atlantic. it is also the case that the species concerned are, by their biological constitution and the environment in which they live, highly vulnerable. the rapporteur, mrs mckenna, was right to make only one amendment. and, while making specific criticisms, i consider the convention to be positive. we're with her and we appreciate her work.

- the following reports are requested for joint debate: - a5-0176/2002 by mr busk, on behalf of the committee on fisheries, on the commission report on monitoring the application of the common fisheries policy (com (2001) 526 - c5-0008/2002 - 2002/2001 (cos)); - a5-0228/2002 by mrs attwooll, on behalf of the committee on fisheries, on the communication from the commission to the council and the european parliament on the behaviour of serious infringements of the rules of the common fisheries policy detected in 2000 (com (2001) 650 - c5-0197/2002 - 2002/2093 (cos)).
- mr president, with the growing signs of the weakening of many fish stocks, the common fisheries policy (cfp) has progressively incorporated and strengthened a component of protection and conservation of resources. this is a matter of civic and political responsibility, since such measures are essential to safeguard the future of the fisheries themselves and of the fishermen, even though they may not be very popular at first. to ensure the effective implementation of this important aspect of the cfp, a set of community control, inspection and surveillance arrangements has long existed, the implementation of which is essentially a matter for the member states. what the commission has now come to present is an evaluation report of the way in which these provisions are applied in the various member states. the report shows that there are profound inequalities in the interpretation and application of these measures, in particular in the control and surveillance equipment, in the training of inspectors, in the frequency of control actions, in the sanctioning procedures. mrs attwooll's report also focuses on some of these same problems. it is therefore clear that such disparities create an objective inequality of treatment of fishermen in the various member states. the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat this problem. this is what happened, for example, with the famous multiannual fisheries guidance plans, the pops, in which member states which failed to meet the objectives set did not suffer any sanctions, and those who did not comply ended up benefiting. what the commission now suggests in this report is the need to substantially strengthen the control and monitoring mechanisms for fisheries, including the possibility for the commission's inspectorate to be able to act autonomously vis-à-vis the national administrations. we agree with this intention and this debate should now be incorporated into the cfp debate after 2002. i would just like to conclude by thanking mr niels busk for his support for the committee on fisheries and for his report, the content of which we agree with in the main.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, the busk and attwool reports, and the commission documents on which they deal, are timely and of the greatest use. we are indeed at a pivotal moment, with the common fisheries policy undergoing a review in all its chapters. the reports shed light on and help to clarify a key element of fisheries policy: control and sanctions. the conservation of scarce but renewable resources is a vital issue, it is not too much to repeat. the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat the spread of the disease, including the introduction of a new system of controls on the spread of the disease. the farming model may also be regulated by technical measures, nets, minimum fish sizes, closed or restricted areas. however, and this is the point, control systems are essential because they are designed to ensure that fishing activities respect and comply with the conservation measures adopted. unfortunately, the reality today is that the effectiveness and quality of fisheries control is uneven and leaves much to be desired. some activities are effectively controlled and others are completely uncontrolled. there has been progress, yes, but not enough, everyone says! the report says that control and the penalties that go with it vary so much between member states that the end result is an unequal treatment of fishermen in different countries, which is bad for the reliability, acceptance and respect of community rules. in these circumstances, it is urgent to fine-tune the instruments for monitoring community fisheries in the areas of surveillance, control and enforcement. finally, let me encourage the commissioner to make compromises and adjustments which will make his proposal for reform - which is so controversial - acceptable to the member states. we sincerely hope that an agreement can be reached by the end of this year and that we do not fall into a quagmire of ambiguity.
- thank you very much, commissioner. the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow at 12 noon. the next item is the report (doc. (the sitting was closed at 11.35 p.m.)
the rapporteur. - mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, despite the positive steps that have been taken in the legislative field to promote equality between men and women, there are still many shortcomings, discrimination and inequalities in the various practices of the member states that need to be addressed. following the adoption in 2000 of the european union's framework strategy for equality between men and women and its programme of community action for 2001 to 2005, there is an effort to involve all community policies and all commission services in this strategy, with some positive practical results. but you have to go further. it is not acceptable, for example, that in the community institutions themselves, including the european convention which is now in operation, there are less than 20% women, that is to say, a percentage even lower than in the previous convention which drew up the charter of fundamental rights. it is particularly important to closely monitor the implementation by member states of guidelines, directives, recommendations and resolutions aimed at promoting equality so that decisions taken in the context of equality, rights and opportunities policy are implemented more correctly and are also effectively translated into equal rights and opportunities policy. for example, with regard to the lisbon council's objective of achieving 60% of women in the labour force by 2010, it is reaffirmed that this target will only contribute to equal opportunities for men and women if all have quality and well-paid jobs, and the importance of training in various forms and modalities is emphasised, as well as the creation of a sufficient supply of childcare services for children and dependent persons and a diversification of women's career options. this is why it is important to implement the council recommendation of 31 march 1992. it is not enough for the commission to say that it adopted a report on this application on 4 february 1998. however, four years have passed and it is important to know where we are today, stressing the importance of establishing community indicators on childcare facilities and on the difference between men's and women's wages and other incomes by sector, including atypical and part-time work. it is also important to insist on the completion of the investigations into the causes and the measures taken and to be taken by the various member states to combat the existing wage gap (in the range of 15 to 20%) despite the existing directive - and already from 1975 - on equal pay. the commission is also proposing to introduce precise quantitative and time-bound targets in the employment guidelines, which are currently being revised, and to submit a report to the council on this subject, including measures to be taken. it is also important to set targets to be achieved through realistic and measurable milestones in the areas of intervention provided for in the framework strategy, in order to ensure effective practical progress in all areas, taking into account that the number of breaches of legislation on equal treatment between men and women remains high. the new directive on equal treatment for men and women in sectors other than employment opportunities, which was due to be proposed in june, must be presented without further delay. the committee also stressed the need to update and improve other existing directives, in particular those on the safety and health of pregnant workers, workers who have recently given birth or are breastfeeding, and on equal treatment for men and women in the legal and occupational social security systems. a key issue is to strive for the integration of the gender perspective in the economic guidelines, including in the accession process, in order to avoid negative effects generated by the processes of privatisation, liberalisation and cuts in public expenditure in social sectors, reaffirming that quality public services are essential and calling for the strengthening of budgetary funds in social areas in order to prevent social exclusion and combat trafficking in women. finally, i would like to thank all those who have contributed to the preparation of this report, convinced that the success of the fight for equal opportunities for men and women, including in political life and in decision-making bodies, also depends on greater involvement and political will on the part of all of us.

in writing. - (pt) i am voting for this report because it is an important step in the realisation of the close link between globalisation and the promotion of basic labour standards. there is a particular need to develop a strategy that sees globalisation as a contribution to social development with respect for fundamental rights. globalisation brings with it challenges and opportunities and should improve living and working conditions and increase development aid for the most disadvantaged. the establishment of a single european code of conduct governing the operations of european companies at world level is an essential instrument for ensuring the application of fundamental labour standards. in this respect, the european union must work in collaboration with the governments of developing countries and include respect for basic labour standards in all its cooperation agreements with third countries. the importance of gender equality must be emphasized once again. women's organisations need to be given greater attention when addressing the issue of the social partners involved in policy-making for sustainable development and social cohesion.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, a few days ago a delegation from the committee on development and cooperation of our parliament, led by its chairman and including among others our colleague teresa almeida garrett, visited angola. they had the opportunity to see firsthand the gravity and extent of the humanitarian situation which requires urgent european and international aid. it is not just a question of improving the standard of living of a people, but of the last chance to save many lives, especially children, who die every day of hunger and disease in the most scandalous silence, without the energy or the courage to even cry. this aid must be effective and swift. often, in this huge bureaucracy that we've become, when we decide to act, it's too late. angola needs emergency humanitarian aid, which translates, for example, into such practical realities as the urgent need to send seeds and agricultural tools before the rainy season in october to avoid another year of famine. our priorities are clear in the motion for a resolution which we support and endorse: the union must provide emergency humanitarian aid, contribute to peacebuilding, including the conversion of unita into a political party and the integration of its cadres into national life, support democratic reconciliation by seeking legitimacy through free and democratic elections with respect for essential freedoms, including freedom of information, assembly and expression, and pursue economic consolidation and social reorganisation. as we have said many times in this house, angola is a large country with the potential to establish itself as a regional, influential and important power and thus to contribute to the democratic stabilisation of that african region and to its economic and social progress. the war was the limit of that path. everything is now open: it depends on the will of the angolans, but also on our support. may ours not fail so that i do not weaken their will!
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, almost thirty years ago everything seemed to favour the emergence of a new nation in angola: an immense territory, fabulous natural resources, a small population, a network of small towns spread throughout the country. everything could have been different, but for the blindness of the portuguese dictatorship and the brutality of the struggle for power that followed decolonization. without the mercy of the gods and a victim of the madness of men, angola has plunged into one of the great tragedies of our time: a third of the population displaced, hundreds of thousands of abandoned children, destruction of cities, dismantling of the incipient economic infrastructure existing in the territory. all the resources obtained by the contenders were devoted to war and cruelty, with total indifference to the population. amidst the general misery and suffering, it is well known that there are numerous individuals connected to the state apparatus who have managed to accumulate, by illegitimate means, incalculable wealth. now, at last, the time of peace and hope has arrived. the international community, and the european union in particular, must do everything in its power to consolidate peace, promote democracy and help millions of people who are facing hunger, disease and poverty. the motion for a resolution which we are going to adopt today shows that the european parliament does not forget angola. in fact, it has made many moral commitments to angola. we must redouble our efforts to provide emergency aid to the threatened population, particularly those in the areas affected by unita who are in the most dire poverty. but the angolan authorities - it should be stressed - have a moral obligation to give up much of the accumulated wealth and help their own people. they also have an obligation to help to channel emergency aid, to ensure that it reaches its intended recipients and is not lost along the way. that is why, mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, we in the european parliament are attentive to what is happening in angola. the european parliament wants angola to be a land of peace, a land of progress, a land and a country that is part of the international community. angola has immense resources, it has immense talents and it has immense hope. and here in the european parliament we must stand with the angolan people, even if we often have to be tough on the powers that are in power in luanda.
mr president, i shall confine myself to a few essential points, because the motion for a resolution which we have approved and supported is sufficiently comprehensive and also because we shall have the opportunity to discuss in the committee on development and cooperation a report on our recent mission to angola. i would like to refer first of all to the new and auspicious political situation in this country, where peace finally seems irreversible after decades of war. we warmly welcome this extremely positive development because of the expectations it creates in terms of stability, governance and improved living conditions for angolans, because of the doors it opens in terms of consolidation, strengthening and democratic experience, and because of the positive effects it may have throughout the region. however, the difficulties remain and may affect the development of the peace process itself. the expectations created by the signing of the memorandum of understanding are enormous and it is important that they are not disappointed. in addition to the inevitable traumas, the most pressing problems are humanitarian, but also concern the necessary reconstruction of the country, whose economy and infrastructure have been completely destroyed by the war. all the figures are worrying and show that it is impossible to have an adequate and timely response at internal level alone. i would therefore like to emphasize some guidelines which i consider to be fundamental at this time: the need for the international community and therefore the european union to give priority to humanitarian aid to angola, especially the delivery of food, clothing and medicines, and the urgency of making this aid more rapid and effective; the urgency of providing tools and seeds to start crops and to reduce in the short term the current and complete dependence on food aid; the determined support for the resettlement of displaced populations, in particular by helping to rebuild basic infrastructure; and, as a corollary, the clear and committed adherence to the two phases of the donors' conference advocated by the angolan authorities. angola expects a lot from us, and we should not disappoint those same expectations.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your reply. i was one of the members of the mission chaired by my colleague, joaquim miranda, who was recently in angola. i can therefore have a very clear idea of the word which should mark our debate and resolution today: absolute priority. we must recognize the priority value of the angolan question. of course, on the humanitarian front, that is a huge emergency that we still have ahead of us. and then, on the general political level, because the peace that is opening up for angola can transform this important portuguese-speaking country into a huge factor of stability in a very troubled region, from central africa to south africa. we must all work for this in the european union: the union's institutions and the member states. we must act to strengthen the confidence factors. but we have a very clear humanitarian emergency at this time. we cannot allow the war to continue to kill in angola because of our indifference or our slowness, when the guns have been silenced for some months by the efforts of the angolans to bring the country back together. i would also like to ask you, in the short time i have, to pay a little attention to the particular tragedy of two portuguese children who have disappeared and to justify the amendment we have tabled in this connection. we have had occasion to raise the matter with the angolan authorities; the difficulty of the situation was made clear to us, which we understand, given the war situation that the country has been experiencing for some time; but we cannot let this issue fall into oblivion. we have been told that official investigations are under way and we express our confidence that these same investigations will finally bring to light what happened in that barbaric attack last november, what is happening to the portuguese children and, if they are alive, as the families believe, that they can be returned to their families. i would also ask the house to support this point which particularly affects portuguese public opinion.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the purpose of this report is to express the european parliament's position on the commission communication on the options to be taken in the oleoprotein sector in view of the worsening of the two million tonne deficit resulting from the ban on the use of animal flour in the european union. the debate on this subject at the present time in the european union gives rise to a diagnostic comment and a suggestion for the solution of this problem. the diagnosis is unfortunately easy to make, since it has to do with the extremely vulnerable situation in which the european union finds itself, with a self-sufficiency rate of only 23%, as the report says, and it is not just the fact that one of the most powerful blocs in the world - the european union - has a livestock production so dependent on external supplies of raw materials. the fact that these imports are concentrated in only three countries, the united states, argentina and brazil, which are our main competitors on the world agri-food markets, is also a factor in addition to the high degree of dependence on raw materials for meat and milk production. it is therefore easy to see the high risk we are running in the european union in terms of competitiveness, because such important sectors as meat and milk are, after all, dependent on our main competitors. this doesn't make any sense. the solution to this problem must involve three main measures, in line with what the rapporteur says. the first is to end the area limitation imposed by blair house. it is clear that with the current wto agreements coming to an end and with the alignment of aid for oilseeds with that for cereals from the beginning of the current marketing year, i do not think there are any more reasons for self-restraint. the second reason, or second measure, is that, because of the excessive deficit in the specific protein sector, the current increase in aid should be maintained or, if possible, increased. with this aid differential and without the area limit (the semi-area imposed by blair house), we can then improve our situation within a few years. and the third reason, also mentioned in the report, is that the ban on the use of fishmeal in the feeding of ruminants needs to be reviewed following a scientific study that needs to be done. i would like to conclude by saying that, apart from the importance of oleoprotein crops in the european union's food system, it is important to stress the strategic importance of these crops in the mediterranean regions of the european union, where there are not many crop options, and especially not many crops that give farmers options, but where these crops in particular are very well adapted to the weak, poor, thin, marginal soils that are prevalent in these areas. the rapporteur also says that it is unbelievable and unacceptable that the main protein crop varieties in the mediterranean regions are not currently eligible for aid. that needs to be reviewed. finally, i would add that we must also see how this commission communication relates to the new proposals for the mid-term review of the cap, which envisage a system of production aid that is not linked to production aid.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, i congratulate mr struan stevenson on his report, which deserves our support. as mentioned in the document under discussion, the european union's current self-sufficiency rate in vegetable protein has fallen below 23%, as has already been said by other members, and this is all the more serious when we know that worldwide production of protein crops has increased by 60% in the last 15 years and is concentrated mainly in three countries. the commission's position of insisting on almost exclusive access to the world market, with the added complication of concentrating supply on three countries, one of which is the united states of america, is thus not understandable. i would also like to mention your protectionist agricultural policy, which is also competitive with the european union. this puts the european union in a very vulnerable position and does not provide a lasting answer to its needs in terms of plant proteins, as our fellow rapporteur points out in his document. it is therefore necessary to encourage the development of protein crop production in the european union, to help reduce dependence on imports and to ensure the multifunctionality of community agriculture by introducing other species and varieties of grain legumes which have inexplicably been excluded from community support and improvement plans. i would like to mention the scallop and other species which are traditionally used for animal feed, particularly in the mediterranean countries, and which have not benefited from community support. that is why it is important to give support to farmers for rotation, encouraging the cultivation of protein crops in the grain rotation cycles, using land lying fallow, as we have argued in other reports here, and also the need to rescind the blair house agreements, or at least to renegotiate them. it is not clear why no attempt has yet been made to renegotiate them with a view to ending them and thus making it possible to provide more direct aid for protecting proteins in the european union. we also consider it important that the commission should approve supplementary aid for protein crops - beans, peas, radishes - which are not covered by the blair house agreements, and we support the terms of the report by our colleague the rapporteur. we therefore hope that the commission will take these proposals into account, in accordance with the principle of control of production and the positive effects of these proposals, both in the socioeconomic and environmental fields. i would remind you that this report and the proposals which will certainly be adopted here in the european parliament must not be forgotten in the review of the cap.

- mr president, commissioner, the fact that we are the most frequent users of air transport in this parliament requires us to pay particular attention to all initiatives designed to improve the organization, punctuality and safety of air navigation and air traffic control services. we certainly do not doubt that the proposal to set up a community body to regulate airspace, the so-called 'single european sky', and the development of related regulations are aimed at increasing the efficiency and safety of this means of transport and responding to the challenges of its foreseeable increase in the future. simply put, there are questions in this area that cannot go unanswered. the smallness of my speaking time leads me to put only the main ones. firstly, is parliament aware that the legal basis for the four regulations, article 80 (2) of the treaty, may not be sufficient to justify the measures advocated by the commission, which have the effect of making national airspace community-wide, leaving the member states to exercise their sovereign powers in concert? furthermore, are the honourable members aware that the regulations mentioned above dilute the distinction between the civil and the military users of the common airspace? thirdly, is it not obvious that the assumption of these new powers by the union, to be exercised in accordance with the rules of comitology, implies a rule of express competence for the future, which does not exist in the present treaty, and even the definition of a common defence policy, which is still far from being achieved? secondly, is it appropriate to introduce economic competition mechanisms into air traffic control activities in order to pursue the objective of air traffic safety, i.e. the safety of persons and goods carried and of the population flown over, given the need to maintain the high safety standards in place? finally, has there been an impartial and objective analysis of the real causes of delays on european flights throughout this process? are these only attributable to malfunctions in the air traffic control system, which are now being corrected? are the responsibilities of, for example, airports or airlines, which refuse to delay their flights, or the weather conditions, properly accounted for? we do not discount in these new regulations proposals for measures to improve the practices of the other components of this complex system. we have not uncovered them and so we remain concerned.
- mr president, we would have nothing against a single sky aimed, as it is called, at increasing safety levels in european airspace by coordinating guidelines and actions and harmonizing rules. but that is not the real objective rationale behind the commission's present proposal. moreover, safety issues or even delays are not at the forefront of airspace control. and the proposal adds little to the security aspect. on the contrary, some of the guidance it contains may even jeopardise the high safety standards it claims to aim at: the commission has already taken a number of measures to improve the safety of the community's railways, and it is therefore proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the railways in the community's railway network. but it is at other levels that we are most concerned about this proposal and, in particular, some of the amendments which have been tabled in the committee responsible. i am referring specifically to the intention to transfer the definition of 'functional blocks' from the member states to the community framework. it is a matter of territorial management, and according to the treaties and the constitutions of some countries, including my own, it is a matter for the member states alone. there is therefore no legal basis for proposing what is proposed, for more in the terms adopted in the parliamentary committee, in which the role of the commission as the ultimate authority is strengthened. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. it is therefore unacceptable to seek to subordinate control of military airspace and, in general, to define new 'functional blocks' without the competent decision of the member states. we therefore believe that the council's recent approach to this question is appropriate. these concerns - as well as others, such as those concerning the mobility of controllers - have been given our full attention and have prompted the submission of a number of amendments which, if adopted, could be added to some other amendments already adopted in committee and which have improved the commission's initial proposal in some respects: the air navigation service is considered to be in the general interest; the participation of the social partners in the process is also something which was previously omitted. the possibility is now admitted that the operation and management of air navigation services may continue in the public sphere; the commission is also committed to strengthening cooperation with eurocontrol. but i repeat: the questions i raised at the beginning are decisive for the final evaluation of this proposal!
- mr president, commissioner, rapporteurs, the need for regulation to make european airspace an integrated airspace is recognized. the present situation is characterised by the existence of several service delivery systems run by half a hundred air traffic control centres, with two dozen different farms and 70 computer programming languages. this is absurd! it is really necessary to amend and regulate, allowing for the necessary harmonisation, which can also be extended to the training of air traffic controllers. and let me make an aside here: i was an air traffic controller in my military service and i know well the difficulties of the lack and absence of coordination between military controlled airspace and civilian controlled airspace. the issue of regulation is of the utmost importance and therefore the power and independence of eurocontrol, understood in its transcendent role as a public service for the safety of airspace, is unavoidable in this proposal for a package of four regulations for the single european sky. however, the question of creating functional crossborder airspace blocks does arise. it is also appropriate to clarify transparently what they are and what they are for. they must be created with the agreement of the countries concerned, otherwise there would certainly be unconstitutionality in some of the member states. finally, safety should be the first concern of all those involved in the implementation of the single european sky, followed by environmental issues and efficiency.
in writing. - (pt) it is now generally recognised that cabin crew have a huge responsibility and an increasing importance for the safety of passengers, given the current civil aviation environment. the harmonisation of flight times, duty times and rest periods for cabin crew is urgently needed, not only in view of future enlargement, but above all in view of the fact that there is an urgent need to protect these workers against the expansion of low-cost airlines within the european union. that is why we support this report, which is intended to fill a gap in the commission's proposal for a regulation, which we regret has not been included in this area, especially since several member states do not yet have legislation in this area. we would like to stress, however, that existing legislation which is more favourable to workers than the proposal which has just been approved will have to be maintained at national level.
in writing. - (pt) this is a proposal for a highly technical and detailed regulation, the purpose of which is to harmonise, in the name of aviation safety, the rules governing flight and crew rest times, in order to guarantee all operators identical conditions of exercise and common safety rules. despite the massive support from airlines, pilot unions did not support the rapporteur's proposals, considering that flight times are exaggerated. the rapporteur's views, who said he was satisfied that the long-overdue negotiations had been concluded, were supported by almost all his colleagues in the committee on regional policy, transport and tourism. the solution which has been found is therefore the best and perhaps the only possible solution. i therefore support the rapporteur's conclusions.
in writing. - (pt) in a sensitive and topical matter such as the definition and harmonization of operational rules to make air navigation safer, we welcome the fact that a great deal of consultation has been achieved between the agents involved, resulting in an agreement between the various operators on the limitation of flight and duty time, as well as crew rest periods. we therefore follow the rapporteur's conclusions, confident that they reflect the need and urgency for the gradual adoption of technical rules which guarantee ever higher safety standards and also aim to give maximum confidence to passengers in european air transport.
