in writing. - (pt) as the rapporteur says, the monitoring and enforcement of community environmental law is currently very unsatisfactory. in an area of increasing importance to european citizens, who are already aware of the importance of managing, rationalising and protecting the environment, there is a continuing cycle of gross attacks on the environment of all, which shows an inadequate legislative framework and an unsatisfactory coordination between operators and the community authorities. to avoid this, greater control and concrete and firm action are needed following the triennial reports sent by the member states to the european commission. moreover, there is a worrying delay in the infringement procedures. the commission must therefore be able to 'consistently assess the overall situation in a policy area, for example waste management', in response to the ever-growing and persistent concerns of european citizens. that is why i voted for this report.
in writing. - (pt) the deva report recognises an overall positive aspect of globalisation, which contributes to the economic, social and cultural enrichment of the whole planet. the report stresses, however, that the poorest do not share in the cake that comes from technological progress and the development of international trade. the rapporteur suggests that in order to break free from blind globalisation, the concept of 'maximising the benefits of the most disadvantaged group in the global community' should be emphasised. the report therefore seeks to reconcile the overall principle of the positive aspect of trade liberalisation with the imperative of subjecting transnational companies to the same types of regulation, stabilisation and legitimisation as those imposed in the national framework, which is why i voted in favour of the report.
in writing. - (pt) the frequency of air accidents varies from one region to another, with aircraft built in western europe being among the safest in the world. eastern european airlines have a high accident rate, along with africa, asia and latin and central america. the convention on international civil aviation has assigned responsibility for aviation security to states. the european civil aviation conference applies the safa (safety assessment of foreign aircraft) procedure to inspect aircraft and thereby increase aviation safety. that is why i support the european commission's proposal for a directive which provides for the formalisation of the safa procedure in the community. the aim is to ensure coordinated action, coupled with an exchange of information which allows for early identification of possible deficiencies, which in my view reflects the concern to ensure aviation safety. however, i would like to conclude by stressing the importance of the proposal under consideration for the restoration of the safety image of the aviation sector, which has been and continues to be severely shaken in the context of the post-11 september 2001 events. this recovery is vital for the survival of the sector.
- mr president, commissioners, members of the european parliament, my speech is naturally motivated by the various disasters which have hit the member states of the european union in recent years, such as floods, droughts and devastating fires. this summer, torrential rains in germany, austria, the czech republic and romania caused untold damage. human beings first, with dozens of human lives lost, thousands injured, missing and families displaced. and then there is the material damage, with extensive damage to infrastructure, destroyed roads and bridges, flooded farmland, and the destruction of secular cultural heritage. first of all, and i am sure i speak for all my colleagues, i want to take this opportunity to commend the total commitment and mobilization of the rescue teams, firefighters, police, military and volunteers. on the eve of the gloomy conclusions of the johannesburg summit, the european union is already facing the consequences of climate change and the failure to comply with the kyoto protocol. for me and my country, the european union means solidarity, both with the member states and the applicant countries. the european union must therefore activate all the financial mechanisms and means at its disposal to provide relief to the affected areas. aid must be direct and immediate, because victims do not sympathize with bureaucracy and administrative delays. i therefore want to express my unequivocal support for the establishment of an exceptional budget line in the short term to respond to such disasters, for the reorientation of structural funds already allocated in the light of the new reality and for the unblocking of appropriations under the pre-accession programmes. in the medium term, i am also in favour of the commission's proposal to create a special fund for natural, technological and environmental disasters worth at least one billion euro. this fund would allow for a rapid response in the event of disasters in the broad sense of the term. i suggest that the amounts of this fund which were not spent during the current budgetary year could be accumulated over the following years in order to strengthen it. year by year there would be greater responsiveness. in addition, the size of the loans released should take into account not only the scale of the disaster but also the prosperity of the affected regions.
- mr president, two words: first, a word of profound solidarity which i wish to bring from the autonomous region of the azores, which is absolutely sincere on our part; second, a word of praise for the words and action, in particular, of commissioner barnier, who has devoted enormous attention and great effort to the problem of natural disasters for several years now. the aim is now to transform this commitment into a draft regulation which ensures that these phenomena are dealt with objectively and impartially, so that all citizens can be treated equally wherever they are, whether on the borders of the atlantic or in the heart of europe. and all this must be done in accordance with the principle of subsidiarity and in the spirit that has been shown here in this house, the spirit that european solidarity is built precisely in these moments, which are the most difficult for us.
mr president, the commission's valuable study on the promotion of sales in the internal market, the legislative approach adopted and the solutions generally found for the regulation under consideration must be regarded positively. however, there is no good intention to strengthen the functioning of the internal market and to enhance the rules of competition - as the commission wants, and rightly so - that can hide the irrationality of allowing sales below cost price. as has been mentioned in this debate, nobody gives anybody anything, there are no free lunches. and so, selling below cost is a marketing ploy that sooner or later will be borne by consumers. the integrity of business practices is an indispensable and absolute value for the construction and development of a healthier, fairer and more cohesive economy. the predatory practice of below-cost pricing is incompatible with these values. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full, and i would like to ask the commissioner to take this opportunity to explain why he has not taken the necessary steps to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. this practice is also and moreover responsible for the promotion of impulse sales which obviously do not constitute any advantages for consumers, but rather affect them in their interests by changing their scale of priorities. the prohibition of sales at a loss should therefore remain a fundamental principle of fair and equitable trading. moreover, it is established that the predatory pricing strategy cannot be controlled by the mere application of general unfair competition rules. it is therefore the duty of the european parliament to amend the commission's regulation in accordance with the principles i have outlined.
- mr president, violence against women and children, although condemned in successive un and eu documents and resolutions as an unacceptable and intolerable violation of their fundamental rights, continues to be a reality in our society, including in portugal. in particular, domestic violence, one of the most common forms of violence, although one of the least visible, entails immense human, social and economic costs for the whole of society and is a threat to women's safety, physical integrity, freedom and dignity. this is why an integrated approach is important, with the reinforcement of the measures, actions and financial means which the daphne programme itself provides for, but which are insufficient, as has already been stressed here. on the other hand, as the report says - and i congratulate the rapporteur - about 500 000 people, mostly women and children, are trafficked for sexual exploitation in the european union every year, which also requires more effective measures to combat all forms of violence and to support victims, including a strengthening of the financial means currently available. but i would also like to draw attention to the area of prevention, whether in creating more quality jobs, better benefits and social support, or paying more attention to education against violence, more information and better promotion of the rights of women and children. i therefore call on the commission, the council and the member states to take account of the proposals put forward by parliament, by capitalising on the experience gained since 1997 and by presenting a new action programme with an adequate financial budget, also taking into account the full participation of the candidate countries and the creation of a common database on the elimination of violence.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the social policy agenda is a dynamic programme and therefore subject to adjustment. this is why the annual evaluation panel is so important, as it enables the european parliament to monitor and control the implementation of the social agenda, analysing the past and planning for the future. the rapporteur, mrs smet, has done a magnificent job of articulating the criticisms and weaknesses of the commission's communication, together with clear and lucid contributions to the future implementation of the agenda. employment is the european union's top priority. in 2000, the employment rate stood at 63.2%. despite the creation of some 3 million new jobs, it is still 7% below the lisbon summit target for 2010 and a number of unemployed people, 14.5 million european citizens, still remain. low employment rates of older workers, gender disparities in both employment rates and pay (18% and 14% respectively), high levels of unemployment - some 8% of the labour force - and regional differences in employment are structural weaknesses that require attention and active measures. the active involvement of the social partners is essential. poverty and social exclusion are another issue that deserves increased attention. more than 60 million people are at risk of poverty. this phenomenon varies, as we know, between the member states, but we are not wrong to consider the fight against poverty and exclusion and their eradication as the greatest challenge we face. the commission should therefore speed up its ongoing work to understand and assess the phenomenon, with particular attention to women. as regards the promotion of equal treatment between men and women, the approach of an overall strategy is still missing from this commission scoreboard. the commission should therefore propose measures to improve the situation of women and, in parallel, take initiatives to promote their full participation in decision-making at the workplace.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i have enthusiastically and 100 per cent supported this excellent report by mrs avilés perea. in fact, the daphne programme, which aims to protect women and children against violence and which is now also open to the applicant countries and the efta countries of the european economic area, has had an exceptional response rate and, as has already been said, is a victim of its own success. it is therefore not too much to make every effort to draw attention to the fact that the appropriations for this are clearly inadequate, which is a matter of great concern, particularly when we think of the forthcoming enlargement of the union. to date, the participation of the candidate countries represents only 6% of the total resources of the programme. and it's projected to grow. we also support the rapporteur's proposals for the rehabilitation and treatment of offenders, studies on legislation and penalties, approaches to the problem of prostitution, educational programmes demonstrating the unacceptability of violence against women and children and the creation of a common database on the repression of violence against women. however, in the spirit of our conservative colleague who has just spoken, we would consider some excessive progress to be dispensable, which could be interpreted as favouring a common european code, which we consider to be in breach of the principle of subsidiarity.
in writing. - (pt) physical, sexual or psychological violence exists in all eu countries. the statistics for the various member states are rather high, ranging from 14.5% in germany to 56.3% in portugal. 52.8% of women are victims of physical violence by their partners or spouses. since 1991, there has been a law in portugal to protect women who are victims of domestic violence. this law was adopted as a result of international pressure and not after awareness, which means that the measures to enforce it are practically non-existent. violence against children, adolescents and women must be condemned as a violation of their fundamental rights, as it has serious consequences for society as a whole and as an obstacle to equality between men and women. the daphne programme to combat violence involves the implementation of policies to prevent, suppress and support victims. in order to meet the objectives set, this programme must receive sufficient financial allocation; this is why it is urgent to increase the budget for the coming years.
in writing. - (pt) there is a big difference between the declarations of good intentions proclaimed in the social agenda and their application in practice, as the report itself shows, despite certain fragility and certain negative aspects with which we do not agree, which is why we abstained in the final vote. however, it is positive that the report criticises the commission both for the formal aspects and the lack of information provided to parliament and the social partners, and for delays in meeting commitments, especially in the following areas: - amendment of directive 92/85/eec on the application of measures to encourage improvements in the safety and health of pregnant women, women who have recently given birth or women who are breastfeeding at the workplace, on the basis of the proposals set out in the resolution adopted by the european parliament on 6 july 2000; - definition of criteria for the recognition of disability and presentation of an action plan to prevent musculoskeletal injuries at the workplace effectively; - increased attention to quality of work, the development of the social economy, illiteracy, the fight against illegal work and discrimination against disabled people in access to employment, education and vocational training.
in writing. - (pt) the report on the proposal for a regulation on sales promotion in the internal market, which was voted by a majority of the european parliament, improves the european commission's proposal, although it retains some of the contradictions and unfair aspects against which we voted. we have voted for the proposals which aim to give greater protection to consumers and very small, small and mediumsized enterprises by combating the usual and repetitive dumping by certain economic groups using an aggressive pricing strategy which is in fact aimed at ruining the weakest competitors and eliminating them from the market. we also support proposals to maintain the most positive national legislation in defence of subsidiarity and the sovereignty of each member state, in particular the rules to protect consumers and minors, those to support micro, small and mediumsized enterprises, as is the case in portugal, in particular with the prohibition of sales at reduced prices outside sales periods.
in writing. - (pt) i supported the rapporteur's proposals, which significantly improve the commission's text and seek to respond satisfactorily to the points which could be the most controversial. but i abstained in the vote on the legislative resolution because i believe that it is still necessary to observe carefully the follow-up to this report by the legislative process. i agree with the proposals to ensure price transparency and legal certainty, to respond to the urgent need for uniform rules with the introduction of the euro and to match the community's 'new approach' to more appropriate and coherent regulation at community level. this proposal can help to clarify contractual positions in areas which are sensitive to consumers, such as sales promotions of the type covered by the regulation: discounts, free offers, gifts or opportunities to participate in promotional competitions or games, in connection with the sale of a product. i particularly welcome the continuing emphasis on the need to provide all information in sales promotion, as a feature of the new agreement on commercial communications policy, which i hope will be reflected in future regulations in this area, as the rapporteur has advocated.
in writing. - (pt) it is worrying that the report insists that 'improving employment in the european union necessarily requires promoting labour market flexibility through structural reform'. it thus forgets all the unemployment problems caused by restrictive monetary policies and by constant privatisation, including in the basic sectors of the economy and in the social fields. it is also unacceptable to regard state aid for employment as a last resort, thereby condemning disadvantaged regions and sectors in difficulty to failure and poverty. the negative impact for countries such as portugal could be of particular concern; very small, small and medium-sized enterprises are experiencing serious difficulties, as are sectors such as shipbuilding, which are affected by unfair international competition policies. that is why we are voting against this report.
in writing. - (pt) we consider it quite understandable that the european commission considers that the time has come to formulate more binding criteria in the field of active employment policy and to make them public in a single legal framework. in fact, this rationalization is likely to pursue a twofold objective: first, to simplify and clarify the system of regulation of employment aids, which is supposed to ensure greater transparency in the commission's action and to facilitate its understanding and monitoring; and second, to contribute to promoting employment within the european union, in accordance with the european strategy for employment, especially for workers belonging to the most disadvantaged categories. i therefore endorsed the rapporteur's position, which largely supports this commission proposal, considering that this is the way forward to achieve the lisbon strategy, and particularly welcoming the references to the need to 'support the economic development of the less favoured regions of the european union', with a particular focus on the outermost regions.
- mr president-in-office, thank you for your information. i should like to say that this incident, which is connected with the seville summit, has caused a minor political storm in portugal and has been used by many opponents of the european union against the european union itself. because we live in a free movement area, the majority of the public in portugal could not understand how this free movement area, these rights of citizens could be violated by a border control carried out with some arrogance and arbitrariness by the spanish authorities. we don't want to turn this into a portuguese case. we are asking the president-in-office of the council to put this matter on the table when he has the opportunity to do so, in order to establish, i do not say legislative amendments, but common and consensual procedures so that these episodes do not happen again.
the rapporteur. - mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, we are now discussing european citizenship on the basis of the report which i had the honour to draw up and which was approved almost unanimously by the committee on civil liberties and internal affairs of our parliament. i should start by emphasizing what is essential and what we do not want. it is essential to promote awareness of a common heritage, based on a set of common traditions and fundamental values and, at the same time, on the recognition of a number of rights. what we do not want is a concept of european citizenship understood as the artificial creation of a supranational identity which is intended to replace national feelings. in fact, european citizenship as we understand it is a superposition, it is added to national citizenship, completing and extending it. there is no independent way of acquiring it other than by being a national of a member state. although our community has had an essentially economic career, it is now much more than an economic community: the european union is home to more than 375 million people who are not only consumers in a vast single market, but also citizens of the european union, who have the right to move freely within it, to settle anywhere in its territory, whether for personal or professional reasons, and who expect to be able to live without fear of persecution, discrimination or violence, which underlines the need for intensified cooperation between police forces, customs services and judicial systems. however, the recognition of this european citizenship has been a long process which still faces too many obstacles. there are eight areas in which i am putting forward proposals for the full application of the concept of european citizenship in its political, administrative, judicial, social and economic dimensions. firstly, it is essential to put into practice a strategy for promoting european citizenship, both at institutional and legal level and from the point of view of education and information. secondly, the lack of information. there is a need to better inform european citizens about the existence and scope of their rights, in particular as regards the right to petition the european parliament and to apply to the ombudsman. it is regrettable that most petitions to the european parliament and complaints to the ombudsman have been declared inadmissible, which confirms the ignorance of the petitioners as regards the union's competences. it is essential to strengthen by all available means an information and communication policy of the european union which is geared to and adapted to the target population. it is essential to make the union's documentary base accessible to the public in the various official languages and free of charge and to encourage the development of interactive portals. thirdly, the steady decline in the participation rate of the citizens of the union in the european elections, which undermines the legitimacy of our parliament. it is essential to take the necessary measures to enable the electorate to be made more aware of the importance of such a ballot and to agree a date for the next european elections which does not conflict with national traditions or legal holidays, in order to optimise the conditions for electoral participation. it is absurd, for example, that in portugal the day of the european elections often coincides with a national holiday, seriously undermining the electoral participation of the citizens of my country. fourthly, diplomatic and consular protection. it is important that member states which have not yet done so adopt in their national legal systems the necessary measures to guarantee diplomatic and consular protection to citizens of the european union in countries where their home state is not represented. fifthly, the protection of citizens. it is essential that member states take all necessary measures to ensure respect for fundamental rights, the protection of citizens against terrorism and organised crime, support for and compensation for victims, and effective and fair access to justice for european citizens. let me make a special point with regard to the implementation of the anti-discrimination directives, the fight against racism and xenophobia and equality between men and women. sixthly, the obstacles still preventing the free movement and residence of community citizens. to overcome these obstacles, the proposal for a directive of 23 may 2001 should be adopted as soon as possible, which, by recasting the existing texts, introduces the necessary simplifications and, in particular, enshrines the right of permanent residence after four years of uninterrupted residence. it is also essential that the member states overcome the many legislative differences which still exist and which hinder the full expression of european citizenship, both as regards the status of persons and justice and the conditions of movement and residence of migrant workers. seventh: obstacles to the free movement of students and trainers. in view of the remaining obstacles, it is important that the mobility action plan be adopted as a matter of urgency in order to facilitate and increase the mobility of students, volunteers, teachers and trainers. the member states and the competent authorities should also promote a better knowledge of europe in their educational programmes, particularly through the teaching of history and languages. finally, i would like to invite the convention on the future of the european union to decide on the binding legal value to be given to the charter of fundamental rights and to integrate into the community framework the rules which belong to the third pillar, as well as to seek and ensure that the texts which enshrine the policies of the european union are legible and accessible to the general public.
the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment. - i know . mr president, commissioner, mr carlos coelho, ladies and gentlemen, the committee on women's rights regrets that the commission did not include the dimension of equal opportunities for men and women in its third report on citizenship of the union and, in particular, that it did not present guidelines and indicators specifically geared to gender issues and taking account of the inequalities between men and women and that it did not use data directly related to gender issues at all levels. it welcomed some of the measures which had been adopted in the meantime but which it felt were urgently needed. first of all, the charter of fundamental rights. based on the principles of human dignity and equality between men and women, the charter should serve as a framework for the defence of the fundamental rights of women and men in the union. the daphne programme, which was set up to protect the rights of children, adolescents and women as citizens, must also continue to be used as an instrument for strengthening european citizenship. the action plan for mobility and the commission's proposal for a directive to bring together in a single text the various legislative instruments on the freedom of movement and residence of union citizens are of particular importance for women and the problems they face in the context of european integration. we therefore ask the commission to present in its next report an appropriate analysis based on comparable statistics which will allow an assessment of the impact of the gender perspective in areas linked to the electoral process, such as the percentages of women who vote, who are candidates and, above all, who are elected. we also recall that the strengthening of citizenship of the union will be achieved through full equality between men and women in all sectors of economic and political life.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by congratulating the rapporteur on the work he has done. it is a balanced and clear report which identifies the shortcomings, acknowledges the progress made and points out the way forward to overcome these shortcomings, with a view to building a genuine and effective european citizenship. the concept of european citizenship was one of the main legacies of the maastricht treaty and has helped to involve citizens of the member states more and more in the common project of european integration. more than a decade on, the balance is clearly positive. the free movement of persons, the right to vote and stand as a candidate in municipal and european elections, the right to petition and the right to diplomatic and consular protection are already irreversible developments with practical effects, and the citizens of europe are actually benefiting from them. however, the development of genuine european citizenship requires more, both in terms of these rights already enshrined in the treaties, but whose full realization still faces too many obstacles, and in terms of the construction of the future institutional model of the union, which is undoubtedly the great challenge facing our community of peoples and countries today. a full european citizenship cannot ignore the social and economic rights of our fellow citizens, lest the european project be understood by them as a regression in relation to the social contract which the national constitutions express. as regards the freedom of movement and residence of community citizens, allow me, mr president, to congratulate the commission on the reform introduced in this area, which is embodied in the directive of 23 may 2001. the aim of this commission initiative is to ensure that citizens of the european union can move and reside in the same conditions as nationals of the member states in their own country. it has made the conditions for movement and residence more flexible, simplified the legislation in force in this field and introduced an innovative rule whereby after four years residence in the territory of the european union can be extended without any formality. the european union cannot ignore the plight of those who, not being nationals of member states, have their lives settled on european territory, thus making an extremely important contribution to the cultural, social and economic enrichment of the union. we therefore welcome the commission's proposal for a directive of 5 july 2001 on the legal status of longterm residents who are nationals of third countries. we urge the council to adopt this proposal swiftly so that the existing discrimination will end and these people can enjoy rights in employment, education and social security similar to those of union citizens. the report gives a detailed description of the present situation with regard to voting rights and eligibility, which is characterised mainly by the low participation of community citizens in european and local elections in their member states of residence. the lack of information and the short census periods provided for by the member states undoubtedly help to explain this relative failure. however, this phenomenon is part of a more general trend of steady decline in voter participation, which we believe can be effectively counteracted by clarifying the institutional framework of the european union and drawing up a genuine constitutional text that allows citizens to become more closely identified with the decision-making process at european level.
- mr president, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i cannot begin without highlighting the excellent work done by mr carlos coelho and thanking him again for the openness and availability with which he accepted two proposals from our group in the committee, making it possible to adopt them in accordance with the compromise versions that were agreed. the report by mr carlos coelho is a necessary and qualified complement to the third report by the commission, giving it the indispensable political meaning which is well expressed in the motion for a resolution which we shall be voting on tomorrow. the report and the motion for a resolution deal sufficiently with the subject, dividing it into different aspects in terms of the political, administrative and judicial dimensions and, finally, in terms of promoting european citizenship, where various practical actions are proposed. i do not want to add anything here, either to the analysis plan or to the proposal plan. but we have something to add to the table of reflection, especially now that the convention is in progress and so much debate is taking place on the future of europe and institutional reform. we must work to promote european citizenship and its full knowledge by our fellow citizens. but the fact that, ten years after maastricht, so many direct elections to the european parliament have already taken place and so many actions have been carried out to promote and disseminate the european framework on which vast sums are spent every year, after all this, the state of european citizenship being that which the commission report depicts and which justifies some of the rapporteur's complaints forces us to have our feet on the ground; to build the future with our eyes on the ground, but with our feet on the ground; and to build the future with our eyes on the ground, but not so much on what the visionaries wanted, but on what the realities show. the real state of european citizenship - for example, the emblematic low voter turnout - shows, as the motion for a resolution recalls and underlines in the treaty line, that 'european citizenship does not replace national citizenship, but complements and extends it'. in other words, let us always bear in mind that for the overwhelming majority of our fellow citizens, european citizenship is a complementary citizenship, the central place being spontaneously occupied by the most intense relationship of belonging and identity proper to national citizenship. many profound reasons converge on this inexorable fact. the future of europe will only be seriously considered in the light of this present reality. whenever we do so, we will be building - with the solidarity of nations and the true democracy of citizens. we're building something with a future, because something with roots. but where we stray from that, because we want to follow light dreams, we are creating nightmares and fuelling sources of conflict, tension and discord that undermine the european project. because we are yielding to the pressure of a new kind of collectivism - a multinational collectivism - and thus turning away from democracy, offending real citizenship and replacing the europe of all that we have dreamed of with a bureaucratic artifice. the european construction can be built behind the backs of the citizens' feelings, and it will be safer. it should never be done in front of them and preferably on a par with the citizens. the convention must not forget that either.
in writing. - (pt) despite its positive aspects, especially in terms of its intentions, the report presents ill-conceived proposals which are the result of an overly economic view of the system. for example: the defence of a single level of initial training being positive, it is necessary to ensure that it is not excessively reduced under the pretext of meeting the needs of rapid "employability" of training. these courses would be cheaper, but once again it would be the students and families who would finance the system by paying for the specialisations. indeed, with regard to the so-called bologna process, in which this proposal and others are based on the 1999 bologna declaration, repeated and developed in prague in 2001, a distinction must be made between the official discourse and the practices that are put into practice on the basis of the objectives underlying the whole process. despite claims to the contrary, the set of measures already known, some of which are already being applied, are aimed at reducing expenditure by member states on higher education, as is the case in portugal. we therefore emphasise our opposition to attempts to commercialise knowledge, to the reduction of the role of public higher education, to the structural change of the social function of higher education and its organisational models and to the ideological instrumentalisation, by the ruling classes, of the contents and objectives of education.
in writing. - (pt) the serious floods which have recently devastated various countries in central europe deserve our full attention and solidarity, especially for the families of the victims and for those who have lost their homes and property. we are well aware that the consequences of natural disasters of this kind, largely due to climate change, take on a transnational dimension and require the support and solidarity of the european union as a whole. our abstention is not therefore due to any divergence with the principles and proposals of the immediate aid decided by the joint resolution which has been approved, but only to our doubts about the creation of a permanent financial reserve for emergency measures following disasters of this type through the mobilisation of part of the structural funds. in our view, it is not necessary to mobilise the financial resources of the structural funds on a permanent basis for emergency measures which are unpredictable in nature, whereas, as we have just seen in the present case, it is possible to find more appropriate alternatives to deal with such tragedies without affecting the permanent objectives pursued by the application of these funds.
in writing. - (pt) our vote against this joint resolution is justified in particular by the adoption of parts of the resolution which purport to justify the war and the american intervention in afghanistan, a situation which is all the more serious in view of all the pressure from the usa for a new war against iraq. in fact, we are totally opposed to the use of war to solve the problems of terrorism. as we have said on many occasions, there are other ways of combating terrorism, including by addressing its direct and indirect causes. we cannot accept that fundamental rights and freedoms are being called into question or that state terrorism is being practised under the pretext of terrorism. however, we voted on this point because we consider it positive to approve the request, under the auspices of the united nations, for a detailed investigation into the allegations of massacres and war crimes, and our group had the opportunity to denounce it, in particular by inviting the director jamie doran to screen his film in the european parliament on 12 june. we also support the call for increased financial resources for the economic and social reconstruction of the country and for emergency humanitarian aid.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the laaken european council explicitly called for greater confidence among those involved in judicial cooperation, with a view to the rapid establishment of a european network to promote the training of judges. the proposal under consideration is therefore intended to ensure cooperation between the judicial authorities of the member states by creating a european network for judicial training, the aim of which is to encourage the coherence and effectiveness of training measures for members of the judicial bodies of the member states by establishing links between national schools and institutions specifically devoted to the training of judges and prosecutors. we agree with the idea of establishing stronger links between judges and the institutional mechanisms of european judicial cooperation in order to facilitate the monitoring by those professionals of the various stages of the process of building a judicial europe and thus contributing to the consolidation of the area of freedom, security and justice and to the creation of a european judicial culture. i would ask mrs berger to convey to mrs gebhardt our congratulations on her excellent work and our agreement, as expressed by mr hernández mollar, that this network should cover all areas of judicial cooperation in the european union, that is to say both criminal and civil matters. in this way we will make a valuable contribution to improving knowledge and understanding of cooperation instruments and to fostering the development of a climate of trust among judges and magistrates in the various national legal issues. this will benefit the mutual recognition of judgments in both civil and criminal cases, which is, after all, the cornerstone of judicial cooperation.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, asylum policy is one of the most pressing and sensitive issues on the community's agenda. the last elections have been full of sad examples of political exploitation of the situation in relation to asylum and immigration. the fact is that the development of faulty asylum and immigration policies has contributed to the frightening growth of xenophobic and racist reactions which we cannot but condemn. the need for common immigration and asylum powers is therefore more urgent than ever, as was confirmed once again at the laaken summit, which reiterated the need to adopt a common asylum and immigration policy by 2004. it is the european citizens themselves who are expecting and demanding action to add european value to national policies, whose limitations are becoming increasingly evident, both in terms of security policies and in terms of policies to safeguard fundamental rights and freedoms. the proposal under discussion today supports the introduction of an open coordination mechanism, whereby the pursuit of a policy of coordination should act as an incentive and a means of approximating national legislation and practice. we agree with the comments that commissioner antónio vitorino has just made before the house, stressing the need for legislative action at community level. we are with him not only when he says that this legislation is necessary and that the path of com munisation, which he has mentioned, must be strengthened, but also because this mechanism, rather than com munisation, is a process which denies the european parliament an active role in this process. it is essential that a fair and effective asylum procedure is developed, particularly as this procedure has often been the easiest way of gaining access to the territory of the union. and while we are able to distinguish between what should be different, namely asylum and immigration, it must nevertheless be stressed that it is equally urgent to establish a common immigration policy which is rational and credible and which necessarily involves a policy of integration of these immigrants as well as an effective fight against illegal immigration and human trafficking. not just because of a human requirement, but because we are constantly aware that the reduction in pressure that has been felt on the asylum system also goes there.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, we welcome two reports which form part of a package of measures designed to promote the creation of an area of security, freedom and justice in order to provide a useful response to the concerns of the daily lives of european citizens. i congratulate my colleagues roberta angelilli and giacomo santini, because it is clear from both reports that the great challenge facing us is to create a genuine area of justice, where european citizens can see their integrity protected, have access to justice and see their rights guaranteed wherever they are and as easily as if they were in their own country. the angelilli report is a response to citizens who have been victims of crime. it is essential that we make every effort to avoid secondary victimisation. it is unacceptable that someone who has been the victim of a crime should become a victim again because the system provides him with inadequate or no protection. mrs klamt has already given some examples of paradigmatic situations. it is essential that there be immediate, full and effective reparation for the loss and damage caused. and to do this, member states must promote the appropriate approximation of their laws and regulations, because it is not fair that, because of the profound disparities that exist, there should be unjustifiable differences in the compensation to which european citizens are entitled simply because they have been the victims of an offence in one place of the european union rather than another. it is equally important that citizens have accessible and complete information on these rights in the various community languages. often the right is not exercised not because these rights do not exist or are not regulated, but because the citizen does not have the right to such information. the santini report is based on the observation that the exercise of freedom of movement implies a trend towards an increase in crossborder disputes which may involve individuals with incomes so low that they cannot access justice or benefit from adequate legal assistance. the charter of fundamental rights, in particular article 47 thereof, provides that legal aid is to be granted to persons who lack sufficient resources in so far as such aid is necessary to ensure effective access to justice. we therefore agree that, as already provided for in the tampere conclusions, minimum standards should be established to ensure an adequate level of judicial assistance in crossborder cases throughout the union, stressing, however, that member states always have the possibility to provide for more favourable provisions in accordance with their respective traditions.
mr president, i would like to ask you to take this opportunity to express my thanks to the rapporteur, mr pimenta, for the work he has done and for the work he has done on this report. the commission proposes that a number of compulsory checks on commercial and industrial undertakings which receive community funds for agricultural export subsidies, the disposal of agricultural surpluses on the internal market or other purposes be reduced. we have nothing against reducing that number, provided, of course, that the quality, the community character, the selection process and the transparency of the process are significantly improved. we have been following with great concern the high level of fraud and irregularities affecting the operations controlled by this regulation, in particular exports of live animals and the subsidization of the disposal of dairy products, and we have repeatedly made this point. as was shown in the european case of butter counterfeiting, fraud against the community's financial interests is inseparable from fraud against the financial interests of the member states, public health and farmers, in whose name these considerable sums of community money are spent but who are often harmed by them. according to recently published press reports, the number of seizures of counterfeit food products at customs in the member states rose from 2 340 in 2000 to 4 106 in 2001, and commission administrators have said that organised crime is now more concerned with food counterfeiting than with the counterfeiting of luxury goods. we are also hearing reports of clear shortcomings in the fight against food counterfeiting in the member states, particularly in key areas such as wine, olive oil, meat and dairy products. in these circumstances, we again insist, as we have done for the past three years, that the commission significantly step up its direct action to control fraud and counterfeiting in the food trade and industry. we naturally welcome the proposals for reform of the cap, which call into question the continuity of these intervention mechanisms, which have proved to be open invitations to fraud and counterfeiting, and are of doubtful effectiveness in supporting farmers' incomes, opting instead for direct support based on agricultural area and rural work using sustainable development criteria. there is of course no possibility of effective control without transparency of procedures and without democratic monitoring by the parliamentary institution. several years after the scandal of the more than 35 thousand tonnes of butter falsified by the camorra and disposed of in europe and abroad by dairy companies in various european countries was reported, we still do not know which companies are involved and what measures have been taken by the european institutions against them and to prevent such actions. when will the final settlement of this case be made? what progress has been made in the physical controls of these high-risk products that we can reasonably expect that scandals of this magnitude will not be repeated? i should like to stress that it is unacceptable to consider that only the falsification of milk products directly subsidized by community funds should be considered as affecting the financial interests of the community. if this logic is accepted, while the farmer who exceeds his quota of cow's milk production, in the interests of the community's financial interests, is heavily penalized, the manufacturer who puts on the market dairy products which are not made from milk would not be penalized, although he would do much more damage to the community's financial interests. it seems to me also important in the context of this regulation to disseminate the list of commercial and industrial undertakings and the amounts of aid they receive from the agricultural fund to dispose of surpluses. i cannot understand why any tendering procedure for the award of a commercial contract or even for the award of a scientific grant must necessarily be public, the amounts involved and the participants known, but when it comes to multinationals which receive sometimes very large sums for the disposal of food products the public has no right to know who they are, what they receive and for what purposes they do so. the democratization of europe and the increase in citizens' confidence in its institutions inevitably require a more transparent attitude and fairer procedures. we hope that this amendment to the regulation will be taken into account by both the council and the commission as a unique opportunity to overcome the obvious shortcomings in this area of the protection of the community's financial interests.
- mr president, i congratulate the rapporteur, mr paulo casaca, on the quality of the work he has put before us, but above all on the intense work he has done in this parliament as a member of the european parliament in favour of the transparency of community accounts and procedures. i agree with the rapporteur that the commission's proposal is acceptable, provided that it clearly implies a significant improvement in the quality of community controls and procedures and that the commission is already committed to this procedure. the control of expenditure and of all community financing processes is inextricably linked to the strengthening of transparency and fairness. it is therefore in these areas that the commission and parliament should focus their main concerns. we all know the number and the scale of fraud that has been perpetrated - despite such a system of supervision - and so nothing we can do to improve the system will be free or expendable. the commission in particular and the european union in general can only benefit from the dissemination of information, all information, to the public and from the public's knowledge of the relationship between the main beneficiaries of the common financing policies. we look forward to the promises made by the commissioner responsible for agriculture in this area being fulfilled in the near future. i would also like to support the rapporteur's proposal to change the legal basis from article 37 to article 280 of the treaty. this is a significant, important change, which has placed the protection of the community's financial interests at the top of the agenda. finally, i would like to remind you that the loyalty of international trade is an invaluable asset in the medium term and, above all, that there are no static and independent economic sectors. the damage that may be caused to third countries by protectionist policies or the lack of clarity in procedures and rules will sooner or later be borne by those who make this behaviour a general rule of intervention. it seems to us absolutely essential that the announced reform of the cap should incorporate these and other principles.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we have all had a view which is essentially common on the need to protect financial interests and we have developed a work which is essentially collective. i would also like to thank the commissioner for her attention to this report, but i must say, commissioner, that her speech raised some questions which i think it is absolutely essential to clarify. and with the permission of the president, i would like to ask you four very specific questions which are highlighted by this report: - firstly, the commissioner tells us that article 280 is only about fraud and that this regulation is about control. commissioner, this is not the first time i have heard this from the commission, but i am tempted to say that the commission may need to read article 280 more carefully. because, to give a free translation of the english version, the very first paragraph of the same article says that 'the community and the member states shall combat fraud and all other illegal activities affecting the financial interests of the community'. it is therefore very clear in the wording of article 280 that we are not only against fraud but against any illegal activity which affects the financial interests of the community. it is therefore very clear that this regulation falls entirely within the scope of this protection, and i must say that the overwhelming majority of financial recovery procedures carried out by the community are carried out under this regulation. i believe that perhaps about three quarters of these cases and three quarters of the funds involved in recovery are under this regulation. how can the commission say something like this, that this has nothing to do with article 280 when this regulation is the plain transcription of what we have in article 280? secondly, the commissioner says that this regulation has worked satisfactorily. i believe that we cannot look at this regulation - as i have said - in isolation from the other regulations and the whole legal architecture which is intended to protect the financial interests of the community. and from this point of view i would just like to remind you once again of the scandal i mentioned in my speech, the scandal of the fake butter from the neapolitan camorra, which involved the collaboration of large european dairy companies in almost all the major countries of europe. this is a scandal that has been developing since 1995, involving at least over 35,000 tons of counterfeit butter, which was only charged in this case in the year 2000, that is, there were five years of operation on 35,000 tons of butter involving large companies in france, germany, belgium and probably other countries. and through this regulation the commission has never been able to find out anything about the counterfeiting of butter. it took a local investigation, by the italian police, from naples, to begin to unravel this scandal. and it is 2002 and we still do not know which companies are involved and what the commission intends to do about these companies. and the commissioner thinks that this is satisfactory. i'm so sorry, i don't think this is satisfactory. i think it is not satisfactory that the number of food counterfeits has almost doubled from 2000 to 2001, which is another fact that has been reported in the press and has not been refuted by the commission so far. this, as far as i am concerned, is not satisfactory; - i know . thirdly, commissioner, there is the question of transparency. i have heard the commissioner advocate transparency - and very well - in a variety of circumstances. i cannot understand why the commission, and therefore the commissioner, is no longer in favour of transparency at this point. how is it possible that we can go on the internet and see who has applied for grants funded by the community science programme, for example, and who has received these grants, but we cannot know which multinationals are receiving millions and millions of euros to export european food surpluses or to dispose of them on the internal market? why is this a state secret? why does the commission not say which companies are involved? how much do they get? for what purpose? what's there to hide from this view? and here, as our colleagues have already said, and rightly so, our problem is not only with the commission, but it is indeed with our colleagues from the epp and the liberals, who have also so far refused to accept that this rule which seems to me to be a rule of basic transparency should be introduced into the regulation; - and lastly, commissioner, i have a question to put to you on the definition of the protection of the community's financial interests. because this definition has been made as follows: the commission has already taken the initiative in the area of the protection of the environment. in other words, if they do not receive this subsidy, it is no longer a problem for the community. this is absolutely absurd, commissioner, because you will note the following: if a milk producer exceeds his reference quantity - that is, he is producing milk and that milk is not subsidised - community law allows the industry which buys his milk to make the withholding immediately, that is, even before an excess of quota is established, money is withheld to pay a fine on the quota. i mean, he hasn't even committed a crime, he hasn't even increased milk production, and he's already being taken out of his money. this is what happens to a dairy farmer. if we have an industrialist who hammers milk products, that is to say, makes butter without milk, makes butter with all kinds of things that should not be used in any food product, with totally artificial things, then the community tells me the following: i am not saying that the commission should be concerned about the fact that the council has not yet taken a decision on the matter. this is a pointless thing! but this is the doctrine of the community and it has been the doctrine defended before cocobu, year after year, and it continues to be so. i can't accept this, madam commissioner! i would like you to give us a clear explanation of why the commission has taken this approach, because i cannot explain this to anyone! i cannot go to the farmers in the region i represent here and explain this to them, because there is no possible explanation for this, and i would therefore like the commission to explain clearly to us the reason for this inconsistency.
- mr president, with this point of order i would like to draw attention to this highly irregular procedure which only shows bad luck after a vote. this intervention should have been made at the very least at the time of the explanations of vote and not at this time. it was a very irregular procedure, in my opinion.
in writing. - (pt) as portugal, and more specifically the algarve coast, has already suffered from unprecedented marine pollution, i can only agree with the recommendation of the rapporteur, mr bakopoulos, and with all the initiatives of the european commission aimed at strengthening the legislative standards applicable to maritime safety and the prevention of pollution from ships, and at increasing the efficiency of the implementation of european policy in this field. i therefore wholeheartedly support the proposal to set up a single committee for maritime safety to replace the national committees. this new structure will involve a qualitative leap in the level of safety of european coasts and the fight against pollution caused by ships.
in writing. - (pt) i would like to agree with this recommendation for second reading and with the adoption of the council's common position, which seems to me to be of the utmost importance for the promotion of road safety. as our colleague mr hatzidakis has said, and i congratulate him on his excellent work, we should welcome the improvement in road safety through the limitation of the speed of vehicles used for both passenger and freight transport. the commission's proposal to extend the scope of the existing directive 92/6/eec and to gradually introduce speed limitation devices reflects a reiterated concern of parliament, namely the promotion of road safety in the european union, with which i have always agreed and which i continue to defend. i therefore agree with the rapporteur that the council's common position should be adopted as soon as possible and without amendments, since many of the amendments proposed by parliament have been taken into account, and new elements have been introduced which have contributed to greater clarity in the text under consideration.
in writing. - (pt) the main message of the kyoto protocol is that the environmental costs of the greenhouse effect should be internalized in the economy. this economic internalisation, which is already taking place in the industrial and energy sectors, notably through the european directive on emissions trading (the first reading of which will soon be voted by this parliament), must be extended to all economic sectors and activities. i therefore welcome this proposal for a directive on motor vehicles of category n1 and mr goodwill's report.
in writing. - (pt) in view of the significant development of tourism, the vat system applicable to european travel agencies is inadequate, outdated and highly anti-competitive. that is why i support the european commission's initiative, and the amendments by the rapporteur, mr torres marques, which make the system simpler and more competitive. the tourist industry, which is very active in the algarve, can only benefit from this change. travel agencies and operators established outside the eu will have to pay vat when providing services to european customers and, conversely, european agencies will be exempt from vat when providing services to customers residing outside the eu, thus restoring fair and healthy competition in the european internal market.
in writing. - (pt) the tourist industry and the operation of travel agencies have been transformed over the last 25 years, with the adoption of the special vat regime for this sector. in the meantime, the business of travel agencies has changed profoundly. the special scheme laid down by the sixth directive is finding it increasingly difficult to adapt to the current needs of the sector. the multiple exemptions enjoyed by several eu countries and the privileged position of third country travel agencies with regard to vat have led to unfair competition or the practice of the establishment of subsidiaries in third countries - for example switzerland - by the most powerful companies, with a view to avoiding tax obligations in the european community. the commission's proposal deserves our vote in favour. i am proposing a concrete method of collecting vat to be imposed on travel agencies from third countries who sell products in the european union or to community nationals, the same method that parliament has adopted for e-commerce. i also propose a method to improve the competitiveness of travel agencies marketing tourist products within the union to third country nationals.
in writing. - (pt) i voted in favour of these two proposals because they form part of a coherent set of horizontal measures which ensure the correct and effective implementation of the measures already approved by the ep on the fight against terrorism (the framework decision on the fight against terrorism and the framework decision on the european arrest warrant). the threat of terrorism must be taken seriously on both sides of the atlantic, because it is not only one of the greatest threats to democracy, the free exercise of human rights and free economic and social development, but above all it is an attack on the most fundamental of human rights: the right to life. in this respect, i am pleased to note that the european union has made considerable progress since the terrible events of 11 september 2001, both in terms of the exchange of information and police and judicial coordination in the fight against terrorism, and in terms of its legislative framework. i hope that the next step for us will be to reach agreement on the priority to be given to issues relating to the financing of the fight against terrorism, as well as to strengthen the dialogue with the united states (and the priority to be given to the negotiations that started this summer so that they lead to the conclusion of a judicial cooperation agreement with the united states). the fight against terrorism must always be guided by the values of the democratic rule of law, respect for citizens' rights and common judicial standards. a fight which forgets these priorities would be an unjustified concession to barbarism and an unacceptable retreat from our civilization.
in writing. - (pt) this initiative by spain, in the council, is part of the strengthening of supranational police and judicial mechanisms - particularly at eu level - in the name of the so-called 'fight against terrorism'. the centralisation, structuring and strengthening of entities (europol and eurojust) which are beyond democratic and judicial control, exceeding the guarantees of rights and freedoms provided for in national legislative and constitutional frameworks. the cooperation between national states is being overtaken by the creation of supranational mechanisms for their control. it should be stressed that the proposal to create a 'mechanism for evaluating legal provisions on the fight against terrorism and their implementation at national level' goes beyond and violates the legal basis currently existing in the treaties. among other things, such initiatives are part of a security drift that increasingly threatens fundamental rights, freedoms and guarantees. although the rapporteur puts forward amendments which attempt to introduce concerns and protections for fundamental rights, freedoms and guarantees - for example, the transfer and management of information and personal data (it should be noted that europol has signed agreements with third countries, including the united states) - this does not change the general meaning of the proposed initiatives. that is why we are voting against this report.
in writing. - (pt) my vote in favour expresses my agreement with the two initiatives of spain which aim to create certain measures to facilitate cooperation between police and judicial authorities in the fight against terrorism. it also reflects my support for the main amendments proposed by the rapporteur: specify that the information transmitted under this decision is to be limited to criminal investigations relating to terrorist activities; request the designation by each member state of a eurojust correspondent responsible for terrorism-related matters, instead of giving member states the option of designating another competent authority; to ask member states to set up joint investigation teams to combat terrorism where greater collaboration is needed and, in particular, for complex cases with transnational implications.
in writing. - (pt) i voted in favour of this report because i believe that, thanks to the french initiative, knowledge of the legal and judicial situation in the member states and the community will improve, and consequently the involvement of all those who, within the framework of the european union and its member states, belong to the judiciary - that is to say, judges, prosecutors, lawyers and, possibly, police officers with functions identical to those of a magistrate. this network, composed of the national schools and institutions of the member states responsible for supplementary judicial training, has already existed at an informal level since october 2000, but it does not have legal personality. this restriction prevents it from having the means to finance the budgets of the community or other member states, which is set to change with the adoption of this french initiative, since it will be possible to finance it from the general eu budget. in summary, it is a new impetus for improving knowledge of the judicial and legal systems of the member states and of the legal instruments underlying judicial cooperation in the eu.
in writing. - (pt) we voted in favour of this report because of the improvements it makes to the original council proposal. for example, it is important to recognise the fundamental right of citizens to free choice in relation to gmos and to take account of the precautionary principle, beyond the protection of the environment and human health. equally important is the recognition at the first session of the conference of the parties of the need to support the implementation of article 27 of the protocol. this article creates a framework for the development of an internationally acceptable system of civil liability and compensation for losses and damage arising from gmos, and introduces special precautions in all matters relating to the production of food and feed. however, the report does not address vital issues such as the problem of the eu's dependence on imported oleoproteins, given the risks of gmos. it should also be noted that the council wishes to lift the existing moratorium and that we are opposed to this, as the vote on the previous report clearly showed.
in writing. - (pt) in may 2000, the european union and its member states signed the cartagena protocol on the prevention of biotechnological risks in the transfer, handling and use of gmos. this proposal for a european commission regulation is extremely important because it gives impetus to the practical implementation of the cartagena protocol in the european union by avoiding the distortions of competition that could arise if such rules were defined at national level. the rapporteur has improved the proposal for a regulation and we particularly welcome his proposal to ban the export of gmos which have not been authorised in the european union itself.
in writing. - (pt) continuous work is essential to ensure, also in the context of transboundary movements, an appropriate level of protection against the transfer, handling and safe use of genetically modified organisms (gmos) resulting from modern biotechnology, which may have adverse effects on the conservation and sustainable use of biological diversity, and thus to prevent risks to human health. this is the concern of this regulation, which we are now hearing at first reading and which aims to establish the provisions necessary for the implementation of the cartagena protocol. i fully support it, since in this highly sensitive area the community regulation aims to avoid disparities and overlapping of the legislative, regulatory and administrative provisions of the member states and thus to bring about greater legal certainty, while contributing to the proper functioning of the internal market.
in writing. - (pt) in voting against this report, i am aware that the laeken summit in december 2001 reaffirmed the need for the eu member states to adopt a common policy on asylum and immigration by 2004. in fact, i am sensitive to the need to establish coordination procedures in the field of asylum policy and even, in respect of the sovereignty and specific competences of the member states, to the permanent introduction of a method of coordination in the field of asylum in order to promote the convergence of national systems. however, i will not go into too much detail - especially in an extremely delicate area such as this - and i regret that both the rapporteur and the committee have taken a maximalist line, frankly excessive, which goes far beyond what is necessary. that is why i voted against it. this is also why i am in the end condemning the report by issuing a warning for the future: better is the enemy of good.
in writing. - (pt) my vote in favour reflects first of all my feeling towards the green paper on 'compensation for victims of crime' presented by the commission last may, which has given a strong impetus to this sensitive area by attempting to establish common minimum standards for compensation for victims, to be respected throughout the european union and, if possible, on a reciprocal basis with regard to third countries. i also vote in favour of mrs angelilli's excellent report. the report states in particular that the minimum standards must include a clear definition, shared by all member states, of the types of infringements for which compensation may be provided, providing in advance certain objective characteristics. i also agree with the rapporteur when she recommends the creation of a european green telephone number for victim assistance, which would be operational 24 hours a day in all the official languages of the union.
- mr president, commissioner, when we see the commission putting forward a proposal which we are fighting for in this parliament, such as the establishment of a solidarity fund for people affected by natural disasters, we must of course congratulate it with all our heart. some points in this proposal, however, raise some questions which i would like to see the commissioner clarify. firstly, why has the commission decided to extend the coverage of this type of disaster to environmental and technological disasters in its proposal? secondly, the structural funds must not be confused with this solidarity fund. it is therefore not clear why the structural funds are included in this regulation. would a general rule not be sufficient to ensure that the solidarity fund is not used twice over for measures already financed by any other instrument? thirdly, the reference to absolute figures for the cost of disasters is meaningless, since it naturally favours the large economies, and on the other hand it is also not clear why there is no reference to the financial capacity of each state as a criterion for the support to be granted by the communities. finally, and most importantly, we cannot understand why the regulation does not provide a clear typology for the situation of the outermost regions, which have been the most affected by natural disasters, and i would ask the commissioner to explain to us how they can in fact be covered by this proposal.
