- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, when implementing the european employment strategy, the european union intends to set medium-term full employment objectives for each member state, integrated in a context of political coordination. the rapporteur, mr herman schmid, whom i congratulate, highlights the positive effects of the european employment strategy over the last five years, as well as some less successful points. one of the problems to be solved is the poor transposition of the strategy into the level at which employment decisions are taken. the open method of coordination is an important instrument for the implementation of this strategy. as you know, it is designed for sectors in which the member states retain their autonomy. however, differences between national and european interests still exist, and it is suggested that each government should integrate the national action plan into the development of common employment policies. the active support of the social partners and their contribution to the implementation and mainstreaming of the priorities set out in the strategy into their annual programmes is particularly relevant in areas such as lifelong learning, active ageing, gender equality and modernisation of the organisation of work. the consequences of enlargement for the current labour market in the european union must also be taken into account. to improve the current situation between the common overall policy at european level and the adoption of policies at national level, adjustments in economic development are needed, including the mobilisation of human resources and the creation of new institutional structures, but also the strengthening of the entrepreneurial spirit. in portugal, the national employment plan transposes the content of the strategy into portuguese reality, trying to respond to the new challenges. in recent years, the portuguese labour market has shown an overall positive behaviour. however, structural weaknesses remain and only a coordinated medium- and long-term strategy with the active involvement of the social partners will allow them to be overcome. among these weaknesses, i would highlight:- a low level of training,- a significant burden of long and very long-term unemployment,- the existence of a group with special problems of reintegration into the labour market, the case of young people, older workers, women, ethnic minorities and people with disabilities,- a sectoral and business structure with serious vulnerabilities,- a low level of productivity,- and significant regional inequalities. finally, i would like to stress that in order to ensure effective coordination and coherence of national economic and employment policies and to ensure the effective implementation of the european employment strategy, the open method of coordination must be improved and its virtualities exploited, without losing sight of the requirement that this method must always give priority to transparency.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the balance sheet of the five years of the european employment strategy shows that there are still major shortcomings in the creation of quality jobs with rights. most of the jobs created during these years were precarious, parttime or very short-term and without guarantees of equal rights and opportunities. as our colleague herman schmid, whom i commend for his work, has said in his report, unemployment remains high, and in particular longterm unemployment is one of the main causes of social exclusion in europe, together with precarious and low-paid work. women continue to be the most affected with average wages remaining well below those of men and great difficulties in promotion to senior and managerial positions. the maintenance of the restrictive monetary policies of the stability pact, the cutback in public investment, the insistence on privatisation, particularly of public services, puts social policies and job creation in the background, as is currently the case. priority is also given to community policies in the fields of competition, fisheries and agriculture, which do not take account of small and mediumsized enterprises, family farms and small-scale and coastal fishing, which contribute to increasing unemployment and prevent the preservation of quality jobs, and to the restructuring and relocation of companies, particularly multinational ones, with total disregard for workers' rights. it is particularly significant that in most member states there is a lack of awareness of the european employment strategy and that in their national plans there are no quantitative objectives, in particular as regards employment rates for women - as defined at the lisbon summit - nor are these plans presented in national parliaments and there is a debate on their contents. these issues therefore need to be taken into account in order to achieve positive results in creating jobs with rights.
- mr president, commissioner, although the european institutions have stressed in various documents that there must be a more balanced and proportionate representation of women in the various political and social structures and bodies, including at the level of the social partners, the truth is that deep imbalances and enormous inequalities remain, as our colleague, mrs smet, has pointed out in her report and as commissioner diamantopoulos has just confirmed. and even if the information on the social partners is not very complete, the limited data available on women's participation show that, at the level of management positions, the percentage of women is low, and they are clearly under-represented at the top of the organisations, which weakens democracy and makes it difficult to achieve the effective equality of rights and opportunities, which we have enshrined in the treaties and in various directives that we complain about every day. we therefore support the initiatives proposed in this report, both those aimed at gaining a deeper understanding of the current situation (and we have already spoken here about the need for data collection) and those aimed at a more balanced representation of men and women at the level of the social partners. we call for the establishment of timetabled targets on the quantitative and qualitative representation of women, both in the internal structure and in collective bargaining, including the forthcoming tripartite social summit - and i would like to draw the attention of the commissioner to this point - and also for the preparation of reports on the progress achieved and the impact of the measures taken. but it is necessary to create the objective conditions for women's participation in social and political bodies to become a reality. it is also important that the commission establishes a database and that good practice is disseminated, including in the joint employment report, by disseminating information on women's participation, in particular in collective bargaining, and also on progress made in the field of equal opportunities. in the various reports on the social and employment fields, this information, analysis and approach must be reflected.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, in this excellent report, mrs miet smet, who i commend for her excellent work, stresses the insufficient participation of women in the social partners in the european union and its consequences for the implementation of equal opportunities policy. in order to fill the very clear gap in the representation of women in the decision-making - mr president, i'm going to talk about the johannesburg summit. as regards the hulthén report, my colleague peter liese will be speaking on this subject. the european parliament has been able to take part in the european council in brussels, and has been able to take part in the european council in brussels. we have not aligned ourselves with the cynical exercise of those who tried to lower expectations before they left so that they could then turn small victories into big ones. and we did that for two reasons: first, because the planet demanded it. the planet is in a state of emergency, both in the environmental and social fields, and making the summit a reasonable outcome was not acceptable to this parliament. secondly, because it was an opportunity that did not exist ten years ago and that will not exist in the next ten years. so it would be tragic not to take this opportunity. we therefore set out with high ambition, with a great spirit of unity and solidarity between all the political groups. and at the end of the summit, i have to say that, while we certainly have moved in the positive direction and have gone beyond rio, we have fallen short of what is needed. it is true that some results have been achieved, but these results are by no means sufficient to reverse the signs of unsustainability that the planet suffers from. on the positive side, in addition to what the danish presidency and the commission have highlighted in terms of timetables and targets, particularly for poverty, water and sanitation, the positive side would be the change in the character of the summit. in rio we stood for the concept of sustainable development, johannesburg was associated with action. that was a qualitative leap in the character of the summit. secondly, as regards the concept: sustainable development has linked the environment and poverty in a very intimate way. and i think that was also a qualitative leap: eradicating poverty while at the same time stopping environmental degradation. thirdly, the actors: reversing unsustainability is no longer just an obligation of nations, of states. with a huge number of partnerships approved, the participation of civil society was ensured. public and private are associated with this sustainable development goal. on the negative side, of course, it was disappointing that there was no quantified target for renewable energy as well as for reversing biodiversity loss. but i would add one point which was not mentioned by either the danish presidency or the commission, and which i consider negative: there has been no push for institutional reform in the area of the environment. this summit could have been used to green existing institutions, the world bank, the world trade organization, but also to launch new institutions that would put globalization at the service of sustainable development. i am thinking, for example, of a world environmental organization. this issue was not raised, it was not discussed, it was not part of the summit, and that was unfortunate. i would like to say one last thing, also on the negative side, about kyoto. many have put kyoto on the positive side of the balance sheet of this summit. i'll put it on the negative side. it is true that russia, canada, india and china have made very sympathetic statements about the future. but what was expected from this summit was not statements or intentions regarding kyoto: the summit was expected to give way to the practical implementation of the kyoto protocol. and ten years after the rio summit, kyoto has not yet begun to exist in practice. a final word on the role of parliament and its relationship with the commission and the council. we are accustomed to praising the commission and criticizing the council at these summits. this time it was the other way around: the relationship with the danish presidency has been excellent, transparent, very cooperative and unfortunately this time we have not seen the same level of collaboration with the commission as we were used to in the conferences with commissioner wallström on climate change. we had excellent cooperation at the climate change summits. unfortunately, this time there was not enough transparency, cooperation or collaboration. - mr president, the secretary-general of the united nations, kofi annan, said at the end of the johannesburg summit that one cannot expect miracles from conferences of this kind. i would add that the results of previous conferences, notably rome and monterrey, were not so favorable. but let us agree that it is legitimate to expect more than has been achieved. and fundamentally, much more was needed. we are not, of course, minimizing some of the objectives set and some of the time-bound targets adopted, particularly in terms of access to drinking water and basic sanitation, the use and production of chemicals or the recovery of fisheries resources. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. but at the same time we cannot forget or ignore the clear failures in many other areas, particularly in the field of renewable energy, on which the european union has invested so much, but where the results have been far below the desirable. it is also important to bear in mind that the means and even the will to achieve the objectives set have not yet been defined, especially in view of the deadlines. although some new objectives have been set, as has been said, nothing substantial and concrete has been achieved beyond the insipid commitments made in monterrey in the field of financial resources, both in terms of official development aid and in terms of debt cancellation. we are aware that it has been decided to set up a solidarity fund to eradicate poverty. but we must also bear in mind that it is purely voluntary and therefore not binding. and we shouldn't expect great results from him just for that. however, this fund shows a clear failure on the part of the developed countries to meet the level of public aid to which they have committed themselves for some decades. in other words, in the absence of adequate resources, we may once again be in the realm of good intentions, as many of those expressed in rio and which experience has shown to be no more than that. but fundamentally, the johannesburg summit did not clearly and objectively define a course, the course to be followed in order to achieve the central objective of the summit, and which was also its motto: sustainable development. we would like to see the level of commitment, the level of accountability and the clarity of the targets in this area to be similar to those adopted at the trade summits. it is clear, however, that the political will and priorities are not the same, especially on the part of those who determine these events. and in this connection it is worth recalling that secretary of state collin power set the tone by emphatically stating that trade is the engine of development and that johannesburg is an important step on the road between doha and the future. much of what happened in johannesburg can be understood in the light of these two statements which all point to trade.
mr president, the wording of paragraphs 6 and 7 on the cap and agricultural subsidies reveals, in my view, three major political errors. the first is a complete disregard for the doha declaration, where the european union agreed to reduce its export subsidies, but managed to impose that all other types of subsidies, in particular the credits given by the united states of america and the state-owned enterprises of other countries, should also be reduced. and this is a great victory that the european union has achieved. the second reason is that this wording reveals a complete naivety in the analysis, thinking that a unilateral reduction of subsidies by the european union could solve the problems. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support them. the third reason is that it is incomprehensible in this declaration to be silent about the european union's initiative known as everything but arms, which is to date the strongest, most relevant and most generous offer from the developed countries to the poorest countries in the world. and this has been silenced, not mentioned, incomprehensible. in other words, we don't value our own efforts. and that is why the way points 6 and 7 of this declaration on agriculture are worded is what is known in politics as 'shooting in the foot': it weakens the european union, the european union which has hitherto been the strongest engine for the interests of the poorest countries. it is therefore essential that we correct these aspects in the voting period that we are going to have tomorrow.
- mr president, commissioners, i remember that the french philosopher voltaire caricatured in mr pangloss an optimism which, moreover, derives from the philosophy of the german philosopher leibniz, who said that 'everything is for the best in the best of all possible worlds'. but if voltaire were to come back, he would also make a very impressive caricature, i'm sure, of those who today think that everything is going to be the worst in the worst possible worlds. the almost ghostly pessimism that affects many commentators, many politicians and many members of this house has no basis in fact. it is not because, in my view, the right path was shown at the johannesburg summit, the right direction was given, the right diagnosis was made, and the european union acted correctly at the johannesburg summit, and so it should be congratulated for this and not be in a permanent critical attitude. the summit has shown the right way forward, but it is also necessary to take account of the fact that the rich countries have to pay the costs of the policies that were outlined in johannesburg and that it is necessary to establish control mechanisms so that, as has already been said here, we can move from words to deeds. i also believe that the johannesburg summit served to show some fears and some weaknesses in the analysis of the problems of the world. and i just want to point out one, if i may, which is the issue of regulating the birth rate and the population of the globe. it is not reasonable to avoid this problem because one is afraid of religious dogmas, the kind of dogmas that voltaire condemned, or because one is afraid of being considered malthusian. we all know that it is exactly where the population is growing at the highest rate that there is the most poverty and the most degradation of nature. and so, too, this issue of birth control, of the global regulation of the world's population, cannot be ignored when we talk about the survival of the earth and a more humane life for humans.
- mr president, as you know, i was the head of the parliament's delegation in johannesburg and i would like to make a small clarification, because i think commissioner nielsen has got the plans a little mixed up. commissioner nielsen did not understand why we praised the council and criticized the commission for not giving parliament the institutional role it should have. i'll try to explain it one more time. we must separate the institutional aspect, the role of parliament, from political cooperation. on the institutional level, we want the support of both the commission and the council to enable us to participate in the daily coordination meetings of the european union. this is a matter to be settled in the context of the next revision of the interinstitutional agreement. political cooperation is a very different matter and what we felt was, in the context of political cooperation, transparency on the part of the council and omission on the part of the commission. and that is what creates a certain difficulty for us because we were not used to this kind of behaviour on the part of the commission; we were used to their commitment, to joint press conferences between parliament, the commission and the council, to an openness, to an involvement of parliament which this time we unfortunately only felt from the council.
in writing. - (pt) i voted for this important report with an eye to the commission's worrying forecasts for a roughly 50% increase in road freight traffic by 2010. i fully share the objectives of the marco polo programme and the rapporteur's vision of reducing road congestion and improving the environmental performance of the world's transport system. i too believe that congestion is a threat to both the environment and the vitality of the economy, which is why i am in favour of measures that reduce congestion and pollution, that stimulate the shift of freight from road to rail, inland waterways and short sea shipping. but attention must also be paid to the areas and bottlenecks in these other modes of transport, so that the 'marco polo programme' does not lead to a transfer of congestion from one mode of transport to another.
in writing. - (pt) while disagreeing with some paragraphs, i decided to vote for this report on the five-year review of the european employment strategy, particularly welcoming the fact that, in the context of the remarks made by the rapporteur on the future of employment in the european union, companies are invited to attach greater importance to a work organisation compatible with the family. i also welcome the prospect of lifelong employability as a preferred mode of social integration, particularly in view of the increasing ageing of the labour force in all eu countries. it is becoming increasingly important to focus national employment policies and the european employment strategy on the dignification of work and not just the economic aspects of bringing the eu's productivity index closer to that of the us, which is mentioned several times in the commission communication. employment must not only be 'productive employment', but also a form of human fulfillment and social integration.

- madam president, the creation of an international criminal court is a step forward for civilization. but for the aims of the icc to be fully achieved and for a more credible affirmation of international law, the universality of its application has to be ensured. the european union and its member states are therefore called upon to mobilize to stop the efforts of the united states of america to deprive the icc of its legitimacy by granting themselves, through illegal bilateral agreements, a privileged status of immunity. the rule of law in the international order, at the expense of the balance of power and the law of the strongest, is a generous idea, but it is far from being fully realized. but the rule of law in the international order will remain a pious fiction if the only superpower in existence refuses to submit to the jurisdiction of the institutions whose job it is to see that international law is applied. this refusal will be a serious setback indeed, because it sends a very bad signal to the world community, a signal of the irremediable weakness of principles and the persistent force of cynicism in international relations. the great dilemma facing the international community today is therefore to choose between an order based on law and an order based on a simplistic opposition between the forces of good and the forces of evil, against which law is understood as an unpleasant hindrance.
- madam president, we are all aware of the crisis affecting certain countries in latin america and, in particular, in mercosur. these difficulties must therefore be brought to our attention, to the attention of parliament, but also to that of the council and the commission. these countries are facing political, economic and social challenges which require permanent international cooperation based on a spirit of solidarity and mutual respect. we generally share this objective, although it is naturally particularly felt by those member states, such as my own, portugal, which have special cultural, historical and linguistic ties with these countries. we regard relations between the european union and the mercosur countries as strategic, comparable to those developing with other parts of the world and to which we usually give priority status. we affirm this not only in the light of the principles of solidarity or defence in the region, the rule of law and the economic model based on free initiative and the dignity of work, but also because of the opportunity that a renewed and concrete partnership between the european union and mercosur would represent for a more balanced political and economic outlook for the union and its member states in that region of the american continent. in this context, we call on the council - in the wake of the recent madrid summit - to give priority to cooperation with the mercosur countries, translating this cooperation into concrete measures that do not stop at rhetorical gestures of solidarity with their peoples. strong political signals are needed to reaffirm the european union's confidence in the region, and to ensure that political cooperation is based on the principle of association for the benefit of both parties. and may i have another thought: the problem is that the phenomenon of unilateralism on the part of the united states - which is particularly evident in terms of foreign and defence policy, of course - cannot be tackled with the criticisms we are hearing in certain circles of european thought alone. these criticisms, which are not accompanied by proposals for political and financial measures to overcome the causes of a certain european subalternity, could well be summed up in the strengthening of strategic actions of this kind, which would better help to balance relations between the two continents in an environment of cooperation and the sharing of values which are common to both. finally, mr president, the problems in the region must not affect the course of the negotiations currently taking place between the european union and mercosur. on the contrary, the crisis - which we know is complex and difficult - must be an incentive to reach an agreement as quickly as possible that is satisfactory to both sides. finally, we have no doubt that everyone would benefit from taking advantage of the opportunities that these closer ties normally provide for the rapid recovery of the economies of the mercosur countries and the strengthening of their relations with the european union.

the commission has already taken a number of initiatives to improve the quality of the information and communication services provided by the member states. however, there is clearly a lack of occupational and geographical mobility in the union. in the first case, european citizens do not change jobs very often. however, there has been an upward trend in most member states since 1995. the situation is no longer encouraging for the movement of workers between member states and within member states: we have almost half the mobility of american citizens, only a small elite of 225,000 european citizens have moved to another eu country, which represents 0.1% of the population of the 15. in february 2002, the commission therefore established an action plan for skills and mobility which identifies 25 measures to address the shortcomings and needs identified by the high-level task force. i cannot help criticizing the commission for having presented its proposals directly to the barcelona european council without parliament having had the opportunity to express its views beforehand. the coherence of the recommendations in the action plan is noteworthy, but there are still many obstacles facing european citizens. in this report - which i had the honour of drawing up in accordance with the hughes procedure, in collaboration with the committee on culture and with the opinions of the committee on women's rights and the committee of the regions, which have proved to be important and valuable contributions - i highlight a number of measures aimed at promoting occupational and geographical mobility and removing obstacles to their full realisation, some of which i am going to list. - firstly, it is essential that local authorities be involved in setting up a network of advisory, sectoral and educational bodies with a view to bringing the world of work closer to the educational systems; other more effective actions need to be put in place to reduce regional imbalances, as, in terms of employment growth, these inequalities are mainly explained by differences in the levels of skills and education of those in the labour force, as well as in their mobility and adaptability; - secondly, one of the pillars of the european employment strategy, entrepreneurship, must be supported by the appropriate funds from the member states and they must strengthen coordination in the field of training; - thirdly, the importance of the role of the social partners in promoting access to training with a view to achieving more and better qualifications and skills for workers employed by others should be stressed; the involvement of the social partners is also essential in the follow-up of the action plan at european, national and regional level; - fourthly, the community legislation on the mutual recognition of qualifications must be applied more strictly; - fifthly, youth exchange programmes, awareness of cultural diversity, language skills and citizenship must be promoted throughout the training; - sixthly, in order to combat the under-representation of women in high-technology sectors, it is necessary to support the implementation of training measures, the introduction of information and communication technologies and new learning systems which guarantee women priority access, without forgetting the training and retraining of older workers, disabled people and other disadvantaged groups; - seventh, the fundamental role that teleworking can play in responding to geographical mobility which the worker does not want; - eighthly, the transferability of pensions is one of the most sensitive points on this subject, since pensions are a very important obstacle to mobility and a worker who has acquired pension rights in his member state may risk not being able to transfer them; the updating, simplification and extension of directive 98/49/ec and regulation 1408/71 must be ensured and the coexistence of situations of double taxation and absence of double taxation must be ended; another important contribution to geographical mobility is the creation of the european health card, which is expected to be implemented in 2004. - ninthly, the usefulness of the eures system must be emphasized; however, it needs to be extended and modernised. i would ask the commission to take account of the importance of this instrument and to honour the good intentions expressed in its communication on this particular point. the objective of transparency and centralisation of information on employment opportunities in the union is achieved through the creation of a single website for information on mobility and the organisation of sectoral campaigns. i also stress that the applicant countries must fight against the loss of highly qualified workers; in this context, we must support them in setting up their own innovation structures and in encouraging the exchange of experience. member states, local authorities, employers, social partners and community institutions must play an active role in making this action plan a success, and the measures now presented must be taken into account in the review of the european employment strategy. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, at this late hour we must talk about fish species that live in deep waters and therefore also take part in the night, in the lack of light, because of the aquatic atmosphere in which they dive. but, without irony, i will say that these fish species that live off the continental shelves are precious species and part of the rich diversity of marine life. and we all agree that it is urgent to protect these species, these stocks, from the intensification of exploitation to which they have been subjected in recent years. in fact, the shortage of fish on the continental shelf and in the large fisheries has led many ships and many fishermen to turn to these surviving or hitherto virtually ignored species, which are therefore in danger of being subjected to real predation. it is therefore absolutely necessary to protect these fish species, and we all agree that scientific research is indispensable because the unknown about these species is still very great, since they are, let us say, a bit mysterious like anything deep, like anything distant, and scientific research will have to be much more concerned with observing these populations, their ecosystems, their ways of life, their way of reproducing, their fecundity, because there are strong justified fears that, as these species are slow to grow, they will be much more vulnerable to the exploitation to which they are subjected. there is agreement on the diagnosis, there is agreement on the shortcomings of the research, there seems to be some disagreement on the methodology of protection, as i have heard from commissioner franz fischler. however, i do not see how we can escape the need for tacs and quotas, an old methodology which has produced some results, although it has limitations; nor do i see how we can escape the limitation of fishing effort. i know that this is a very sensitive issue, because of course at this stage the activities of the fishermen, the fishing communities, the investments made always come first. but here, the precautionary principle has to work. when there are no certainties, you have to be cautious and you have to be prudent. and so, whatever can be done, even if there is no certainty of the result and the efficiency of it, whatever can be done to protect these fisheries species, these dozens and dozens of species, some of them perhaps even unknown or almost unknown in their way of life, whatever can be done is indispensable. and so i congratulate the commission and mr michael holmes on the work they have done, because that work is invaluable in the defence of nature.
the rapporteur. - mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, my first words are to thank you for the excellent cooperation i have had with the rapporteurs for opinions and with the shadow rapporteurs from all the political groups. only this cooperation has enabled a good compromise to be reached in the committee on the environment. i believe that this directive creates the conditions for climate change and the kyoto protocol to be no longer a purely theoretical concern in europe but an essential part of the environmental and economic policy of all the member states. in the end, this directive will form the basis of the carbon economy; the winner will be the one who can achieve the same production levels with fewer emissions. i therefore congratulate the commission, in the person of commissioner margot wallström, on the initiative it has taken in proposing this directive to us, particularly because it will bring down the cost of complying with kyoto in the european union very significantly. i believe that if we can prove that this system works, that this system will reduce the economic cost of complying with the kyoto protocol in the european union, we will have the best reason to convince our american friends to join the kyoto protocol. however, although the proposal for a directive is generally positive, there are areas where parliament wants to go further and improve the basic proposal. and on these matters a broad compromise has been reached. so rather than defend my own point of view, i speak for all those who have approved this compromise. firstly, we think it is important that this directive should have quantified targets for compliance, we think it is essential that there should be a ceiling of emission allowances per member state that is linearly convergent with the kyoto protocol. secondly, we believe it is important that member states can add even more sectors and activities to the system than are included. i am thinking of voluntary optings from sectors such as transport or small and mediumsized enterprises in the member states which see it that way. thirdly, it is essential that the use of appropriations be completely prohibited during the first period of application of the directive. fourthly, we think it is important that the directive covers all six greenhouse gases and not just carbon dioxide, provided that the inclusion of these six gases is made on the basis of transparent criteria which are identical to those used for carbon dioxide. fifthly, we think it is important that the chemical and aluminium industries should also be included in this directive. finally, as regards the areas where i think the commission proposal should be extended, made even more ambitious - i am referring to the system of initial allocation of emission allowances - i believe that a hybrid system based on free allocation but with some auction (5% of the auction in the first period, 15% of the auction in the second period) is better than a 100% free allocation as proposed in the proposal for a directive. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's environmental policy is not distorted. i leave for the end the subject which i think is the most important: legal participation. i think this system only works if it's legally binding, mandatory. i think the environment committee has made a good compromise on legal participation, a binding system with the temporary exclusion - limited in time and limited in terms - of some companies in the first period. i think that the adoption of amendments nos 75 and 81 is extremely unjustified and even dangerous: they basically mean turning a binding system into a voluntary system and therefore increasing the distortion of competition, reducing market liquidity, increasing the price of the tonne of carbon dioxide and reducing the economic efficiency of the system. in writing. - (pt) as i already argued in my report on the implementation of eures in 1998/99, some one year ago, very important efforts were made to increase occupational mobility within the european union and to make it easier for all citizens to access it. i support measures to promote cooperation in the field of education and training with a view to a common framework for the recognition of professional qualifications, in cooperation with the social partners and vocational education and training institutions. i would like to stress once again the importance of an information campaign on the opportunities offered by the internal market and the european labour market and the modernisation of the eures system (so that all the information it collects reaches jobseekers and entrepreneurs), as well as the importance of a permanent mechanism to analyse obstacles to geographical mobility. but i regret that mrs bastos' original text, which was balanced and realistic, has been distorted by amendments which give it a negative interventionist character and a totally misplaced ideological political vision on skills and mobility.
- mr president, in calling for this debate we wanted to say that it is time to suspend the stability pact for the purpose of revising its irrational and incongruent nominal convergence criteria, which do not take account of the problems and difficulties of the member states or their different levels of development, which contribute to aggravating economic problems, hindering growth and the implementation of the community strategies on employment and the fight against poverty, as was clear from the first round table on social exclusion that the presidency and the commission organized last week in aarhus, denmark, and in which i participated as a rapporteur for the european parliament. now, after the statements by commissioner lamy and president prodi, i can say that we agree with them. this pact is stupid, like all decisions that are rigid. this pact is a crude instrument of economic governance and should be replaced by something smarter, given that the 3% rule is medieval. for all these reasons, it makes no sense to maintain the stability pact. it is pointless for commissioner pedro solbes mira to confirm that the objectives of the stability programmes will not be met in 2002, as is very evident in germany, france and italy, but to initiate an excessive deficit procedure in portugal by applauding the application of anti-social policy measures which generate more unemployment and poverty in the very country with the worst economic and social situation and the lowest levels of development in the european union. what is clear is that the pact serves above all as a pretext, as a political cover for the most onerous, disastrous and vindictive government policies that portugal has had since the revolution of 25 april 1974. i ask you, will commissioner solbes mira resign? or will it opt for the proposal to suspend the pact in order to hold a broad debate on the economic and social guidelines of the european union? and will it annul the excessive deficit procedure it has applied to portugal? or will mr prodi do so to meet the objectives of sustainable economic development, of more jobs with rights and greater social inclusion? i'm waiting for answers.
- mr president, i welcome, understand and support the recent statements by mr prodi on public finance policy in europe. if i understand correctly, the commission has now intelligently started a process of defending the stability and growth pact, which will only be possible with political courage and a few breaks. the european parliament, which by definition is the most open and representative community institution for the ordinary citizen, is expected to understand and support the positive process that mr prodi has set in motion. only those who have been completely distracted and divorced from the economic reality of europe can now appear surprised. from the very beginning it was clear that the stability and growth pact was based on rules which would work reasonably well in periods of economic growth, but which could block the growth in the face of certain unforeseen events and moderation. the euro's europe is based on a single monetary policy framework and 12 autonomous budgetary policies. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. budgetary rigor is the guarantee that the present generation will not leave an exorbitant debt to the future generation. forcing states to rationalize their spending and to make their services more efficient is an imperative of a true development strategy. we can all agree on that. however, it is necessary that the rules on discipline are understood by the markets and do not run counter to national policies and the legitimate interests and commitments associated with them, which implies that they are formulated in a flexible manner, adjusted where necessary from time to time. this is the original sin of the stability and growth pact, and that is why the voices calling for its amendment are growing. to turn a blind eye to this new reality is to reject all commitments, condemning the pact and its objectives in particular to complete failure. the immobilized interpretation of the stability pact is becoming untenable, because it does not respond to the economic situation and is even less in line - mr president, one of the main tasks of the convention on the future of europe is to answer the question raised at laaken almost a year ago about the desirability of including the charter of fundamental rights in the treaty on european union. a working party of the convention has been set up specifically for this purpose, chaired by mr antónio vitorino, who is about to make his conclusions public. today, through the report by mr andrew duff, the european parliament wishes to reaffirm its longstanding position. i would like to publicly endorse your conclusions and take this opportunity to congratulate the rapporteur on the very broad consensus he has been able to build around him in his committee. we have no doubt that we must base our common life on the citizen and his rights. before the market, we have the men and women who give meaning to this community. and that is why we are unhesitatingly advocating the inclusion of the charter of fundamental rights as the most noble part of the future constitutional treaty of the european union. of course, we are aware that many of the rights, principles and freedoms could have been formulated differently and will have to be revised in the future. no human work is perfect. but from our point of view, that time has not yet come. the time has come to clarify and strengthen the legal status of the charter, making it binding and increasing the legal protection of european citizens before the european institutions and the member states when they apply community law. we therefore support the strategy advocated in this report and its main conclusions: to make it the noble part of the new constitutional treaty, to provide solemn rules for its revision and, after explicitly conferring a single legal personality on the european union, to advocate accession to the european convention on human rights.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, our parliament would be doing a great service to democracy if it were telling the truth. and this is indeed another excellent opportunity to reflect on this central theme: truth in the construction of europe. the rapporteur, for example, proposes the following: 'the charter does not confer any competence on the union. on the contrary, it limits the exercise of powers by the institutions of the union by the fact that they are obliged to respect them.' (end of quote). it adds: 'the charter does not limit the competences of the member states, it does not replace the fundamental rights systems of the member states, but rather complements them' (end of quote). in fact, this should be the case for two reasons: first, because the charter itself has not yet been given legal force and the only body with competence to do so is the intergovernmental conference; and secondly, the charter itself tells us in article 51, as already mentioned here, that the positions of this charter are addressed to the institutions and bodies of the union and to the member states only when they are applying union law. but unfortunately, that is not the case. there are many facts which show that the charter is intended to be an instrument of unitary imposition and super-constitutional protection over the member states. we have, for example, the swiebel report which will shortly be outlining this manifest deviation in the matrix and methodology used to assess the human rights situation and examine the member states, or we already had last year the cornillet report in the same area. in short, if the rule of law is still the rule of law, we can only conclude that unfortunately the charter is already inspiring movements against the normal rule of law. and that's what we should report. but that is not the approach of the rapporteur, who on the contrary seems to welcome and applaud the moves, steps and guidelines of the commission, the council, parliament, the ombudsman and even the european courts, which disregard the powers of the intergovernmental conference and want to act as if the charter were already law. well, it's not like that. the rule of law is not "as if", the law is or is not. and so i fear that the rule of law is being subverted and that we are doing so through this ambiguity. and so this report can only be supported enthusiastically by those who...
- mr president, the constitution and the charter of fundamental rights are the two faces of the new europe we are building because it is not possible to organise democracy for 25 without binding fundamental rights. firstly, because credibility and confidence in enlargement must be based on a common set of principles and rights that solidifies the relations between states and citizens who have been deprived of coexistence and mutual trust by history. secondly, because the new citizenship of the union, by placing the european citizen at the centre of european legitimacy, requires his defence and protection against any abusive action that the centralisation of power may produce in the name of the effectiveness of the decision. the commission has therefore proposed that the court of justice should be able to give its opinion on the application of the charter to the member states. the charter must therefore be made primary law, the fundamental reference for the court of justice and national courts, and this instrument must become the basic law of the union and a genuine part of the community legal acquis. what kind of space of freedom, security and justice would we be building without a charter of fundamental rights? what model of civic and political coexistence can survive without it? mr president, no new powers are created by the charter. on the contrary, it limits the powers of the future union. nor is any right removed from the sphere of rights which the citizens of the member states already enjoy, nor is any higher protection diluted in a charter which sets only minimum rules. so if we want an organised democracy based on common values, let us constitutionalise the charter, giving it the value of reference and the fundamental protection of the citizens of the union. that is what the report says, that is what mr duff thinks, and that is what commissioner vitorino thinks, i am sure. happy birthday to you both!
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the house has been considering a number of proposals for the re-establishment of the common european asylum system, thus pursuing the objective of the progressive development of a genuine area of freedom, security and justice, which should be open to persons who, for various reasons, legitimately seek protection in our community. the epp recently gave importance to these matters at the estoril meeting, organising its own seminar on the subject, the conclusions of which were recently reproduced in the speech by mr pirker. we agree with a common asylum policy which includes minimum standards which aim to ensure a minimum level of protection for those who really need it. we agree that the aim is to limit secondary movements of asylum seekers by reducing the differences between the laws and practices of the various member states in this field, while at the same time requiring a fair distribution of efforts between the states. we also agree that refugees, like all other people who benefit from other forms of protection, should be able to live in dignity within the community in which they are integrated, which can only happen if we safeguard their individual and social rights. but in these matters it is better to go slow and good than fast and bad. the result of seemingly very generous policies is to create feelings of rejection and to increase xenophobic reactions, which we cannot but criticize. even after 11 september 2001, we have seen growing suspicion and hostility in public opinion towards asylum seekers, refugees and migrant communities. in this respect it must be acknowledged that mrs lambert's report has not helped to create the consensus that would be useful and desirable in these matters.
- without any drama, mr president, this proposal contains a core of minimum rules which show the courage to legislate effectively to deal with the question of third country nationals in need of international protection, while at the same time avoiding secondary movements by eliminating differences in national treatment. at least, that's what we believe. i therefore welcome the commission's proposal to extend the concept of the double status of international protection beyond the narrow conceptions of the 1950s to include subsidiary protection; to clarify the concept of persecuting agent, which is not strictly linked to the state and its agents but also to the material and other circumstances in which the state is unable to provide protection, or because it cannot or does not want to (we all remember africa and we all remember latin america); to include permanent authorities similar to the state in the concept of protection institutions (the aim is to give them concrete responsibilities for the protection of individuals which go beyond the routine tasks of the simple peace and military security mission, which often take second priority to the effective protection of individuals and respect for human rights); and finally to extend the rights of refugees to the mechanisms for integration into employment, residence and citizenship, whatever the refugee's status may be. violence arising from poverty and other social scourges will be left out, but i hope that this will be covered in the future by a broader concept of a common asylum policy. but for now, much of the ground is clear and the foundations of a common european asylum policy are laid. we congratulate the commission on its work, but we do not want the council to continue to deceive us by showing its inherent inability to decide, as is traditional and normal in these matters. and do not, commissioner, take from this house and this parliament a guarantee of ineffectiveness, a guarantee of inefficiency, a guarantee of not voting.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by congratulating mrs charlotte cederschiöld on her excellent report, which the european people's party supports and which we shall be voting in favour of. we are facing a very urgent problem, which is taking on worrying dimensions and which is now emerging as a form of transnational organized crime, to which it is necessary to respond at community level because the new information and communication technologies have a fundamental impact on the daily lives of citizens and on the economy of our societies. it is therefore essential that there be strict protection against possible abuse or criminal acts. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the draft directive on the protection of personal data. these attacks target not only network operators, internet providers and e-commerce companies, but also private individuals who have no commercial purpose in mind. protection of information systems is an essential element in creating an area of freedom, security and justice. at the same time, however, it is necessary to ensure that fundamental rights and freedoms are not limited, or fundamental principles of law are not changed, or individual acts are not penalized that have occurred solely because of errors in the use of new technologies. as i have said in this house, security is and must be only an instrument for ensuring these essential rights and freedoms. five key points in the cederschiöld report: firstly, the approximation of criminal law in the member states in the field of attacks against information systems; secondly, the strengthening of police and judicial cooperation in this field; thirdly, the promotion of prevention, the promotion of a culture of security, the raising of awareness among agents of the risks to the security of information networks; fourthly, the existence of criminal flexibility for matters of minor or non-serious importance, avoiding the risk of excessive criminalization binding on the european level; fifthly and lastly, there is a special consideration for young people, giving the judiciary the possibility of not punishing young people who respond for the first time for this type of crime, provided that they have not done so with the aim of creating damage or obtaining economic benefits, or obtaining future benefits for a criminal organization.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, there are many positive points in mrs fraisse's report which require some answers at community level if we are to continue to expect the commission's policies to point to a real european added value in projects. it is rightly emphasized in recital f that the dynamism of the theatre and the performing arts should not depend essentially on public subsidies. it is very important to encourage the diversification of sources of finance and to strengthen policies for attracting patronage, with the corresponding fiscal counterpart capable of attracting patrons. but it is also important that the performing arts find a satisfactory business dimension. we live in a market economy, and neither the state nor the european union has to be the wet nurse or dry nurse of cultural creation. there is no justification for the performing arts to be directly supported by a system of public subsidy for productions as a rule. it will be more important for cultural creation to take the risks that are inherent in it and for the public media to make it possible internationally to promote, circulate, make content more accessible through translation and subtitling, and co-produce itself in the spirit of the culture 2000 programme. there is, however, every reason why artistic education from childhood and contact with cultural creation in all areas - and therefore also with the arts and performers - judiciously promoted from school, make a decisive contribution to the widening of audiences, which is expected to result in an increase in demand for cultural consumption capable, at least in part, of sustaining the corresponding supply. what is not sensible is to multiply the supply and the financing of the supply without seeing the significant increase in the audiences, which still do not exist. i hope that the adoption of the fraisse report will also be a step in that direction.

mr president, we have already had the opportunity to hear many of our colleagues stress the importance of the discharge mechanism in the context of the budgetary procedure and of the powers of this parliament, so i shall not repeat those words, but i would also like to say that a very important power must necessarily be matched by a great deal of thought and moderation in its use. and i think that is exactly the concern that brings us here and that has led our rapporteur to find a way of limiting in a maximum period of time what we can do in the context of the commission's assessment of the budgetary year. it seems to me that from this point of view this is a legitimate concern and that the formulation found is perfectly reasonable. but i think we cannot forget in this debate that behind the discharge procedure there are very clear objectives expressed in article 276 and in particular that the european parliament should examine the accounts and the financial balance sheet which describe the assets and liabilities of the community. and in this context it is essential to bear in mind that the accounting and treasury systems of the european institutions on which this account and balance sheet are based must be absolutely exemplary and must fully, completely and unequivocally meet the highest international quality standards. here's a first note that i think is critical to consider for this exercise that we're going to start here. a second point to be made is closely linked to the previous discharge, namely that for 1999, and relates to outsourcing. mr bourlanges is the author of many of our references. i would also like to stress the importance of this aspect.
- mr president, allow me to give a political framework to this debate: we are, after all, in the european parliament, which is supposed to be the body with the greatest political representation and the closest contact with the citizens. the use of the financial resources available to the european union is probably the most complex but at the same time the most attractive exercise for the community institutions. the structure of the budgetary authority as defined in the treaties and the still insufficient role of parliament in budgetary policy require and justify special attention to the control and supervision of budget implementation. it is true that the control of budget implementation is only effective and useful if it is based on principles of transparency and clarity and is supported by rules which are both ambitious and simple. this is clearly the aim of the new financial regulation applicable to the general budget of the european communities, which will come into force on 1 january 2003, and in particular the aim of the new implementing rules proposed by the commission. the wording which mr van hulten has found for these rules without calling into question the essence of the commission's proposal makes the control and monitoring process extraordinarily enriching, making it more understandable and easier, that is, by increasing very significantly the verification of the effectiveness of community expenditure. the current budgetary framework is admittedly a framework of precariousness which does not fully respond to the political priorities of the union. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for adoption by the end of the year. parliament will only make full use of these prerogatives and powers if it can intervene politically within a sufficiently dynamic and stabilising administrative framework, but above all one which is understandable and effective. the principles of unity and universality which the new regulation's rules enshrine - albeit in embryonic form - and which the rapporteur reinforces, as well as the status which is given to internal auditors on the initiative of the rapporteur of each institution, are, in my view, very important and significant developments. the report by mr van hulten, which i personally welcome and congratulate, should therefore be supported by parliament.
