in writing. - (pt) in line with the recently adopted community instruments to improve civil aviation safety, the proposal for a parliament and council directive on the recording of occurrences in civil aviation aims to ensure that any situation endangering in particular an aircraft or its occupants is reported. the data collected will allow the exchange of information, which will allow lessons to be learned for accident prevention. i congratulate mr collins on his excellent report on this subject and i support him, particularly on the question of the confidentiality of the mandatory or voluntary reporting of events, the only way to guarantee the disclosure of human error in the potential occurrence of accidents, which are rarely the subject of spontaneous information, unlike what happens, for example, with technical failures. i would also like to stress the need to support the creation of communication systems on a regional basis, which would complement the integrated network of national event reporting systems, thus allowing a broad knowledge of the different situations.
in writing. - (pt) the creation of information-sharing systems from different reporting systems of civil aviation authorities will enable all interested actors to learn from their mistakes and produce a safer system for all air transport users. this directive is very important in that it significantly improves the efficiency of the occurrence reporting system. it was also positive to note that a large number of the amendments tabled at first reading were taken up by the council in the common position. however, there are still differences of opinion, on which i supported the rapporteur's position, which was also endorsed by the committee responsible: on the one hand, where the event is the result of serious negligence, member states must be able to have recourse to the courts; on the other hand, the wording of article 9 in the original version proposed by the commission, which referred to confidential disclosure, must be maintained, since the 'depersonalisation' of the accounts is often decisive here for a better understanding of the factors which may be involved in air accidents.
in writing. - (pt) the worrying rates of accidents at work for the selfemployed and the need to guarantee the right to healthy, safe and dignified working conditions require this approach, which is why i support mr pérez álvarez's excellent report. although the commission's proposal is not sufficient, it is a first step towards solving the problem of protecting the health and safety of selfemployed workers at work. it should be noted that it is in sectors considered to be high risk - agriculture, fisheries, road transport, manufacturing and construction, which have a considerable number of self-employed workers, that a high rate of accidents at work is found. an effective prevention policy, accompanied by training, information and awareness campaigns, is therefore urgently needed. in the context of increasing professional mobility, it is essential to take community measures to harmonise the different levels of protection of selfemployed persons in each member state, as well as the recognition and application of the relevant legislation.
in writing. - (pt) the community data on health and safety at work show a general improvement trend. however, the situation is still very critical in some member states. in this connection, i would remind you that the situation in portugal is worrying, especially in areas such as civil construction, the textile industry and agriculture. i voted in favour of this report, emphasising: - the need to codify and simplify the current community legislation in this area and to encourage member states to apply it rigorously; - the need for the commission, together with parliament, to develop as a matter of urgency a detailed action plan with financial commitments and timetables, incorporating the gender dimension and new types of risks and diseases; - the need to encourage partnership work to enable better data collection and facilitate communication between different stakeholders; - the integration of candidate countries into the institutions and bodies involved in order to enable effective technical and financial assistance; - the importance of a preventive approach to the health and safety aspects of workplaces for workers and employers.
in writing. - (pt) this report, which we have approved, deals with a subject of the utmost importance in the european union, both for its importance for dignity at work and the creation of better working conditions, and for the little attention the commission has paid to the subject of health and safety at the workplace, as is clearly demonstrated by the drastic reduction in the number of staff dealing with these matters, a reduction of more than 40% over the last ten years as the report says. the final text, which is the result of a very thorough debate in which we participated with different proposals, addresses various issues of this issue and presents different demands, including: - the urgent development of a detailed action plan with financial commitments and a timetable for each main proposal; - special attention to gender, given the discrimination to which women continue to be subjected either because of the less attention paid to vocational training or difficulties in accessing employment, or because of the heavy, dangerous and low-paid work they often do; - the presentation of a comprehensive ergonomics directive in the workplace to improve existing directives and take account of musculoskeletal diseases affecting more than half of europeans with work-related physical problems.
in writing. - (pt) i warmly congratulate the rapporteur on his work and i am glad that we now have written standards which clearly show a very important step forward in the functioning of the community institutions and their political transparency, including in such sensitive areas as security and defence. today's final steps are the culmination of a long, careful and laborious process of political deliberation and interinstitutional negotiation, which has resulted in a very positive and balanced outcome. it was indeed important to be able to strike a reasonable and fair balance between, on the one hand, the transparency required and, on the other, the caution required by the highly confidential nature of this type of information. without this, no progress would be possible in this sensitive area. the practical solutions that have been found, the good sense that has prevailed and the rigorous political balance, therefore, merit the congratulations of parliament and, in general, of the citizens and of all the community institutions.
in writing. - (pt) i believe that there has been progress in a positive direction compared with the previous regime, which caused frequent problems and embarrassments. the new system, created by this revision of annex v to the regulation, is probably clearer and more effective. however, i regret the withdrawal of mrs theato's amendments, tabled by the ppe/de group, since i believe that the position of the rapporteur and the majority of the committee on constitutional affairs - which would eventually prevail in plenary too - can go a little further by admitting, from the first assessment in april, the possibility of a tacit granting of discharge by the mere indirect effect of the absence of approval of a motion for a postponement.
in writing. - (pt) the definition of an internal market for medicines should not only aim at better health systems in the european union, but also stimulate scientific and technological innovation with a view to the emergence of new medicines and the acceleration of the manufacture and marketing of generic medicines. as regards the main issues discussed at first reading, i consider that: 1. it is essential to always rely on the centralised procedure (via the european agency for the evaluation of medicinal products), without this implying the total elimination of the decentralised procedure (national with mutual recognition), at the level of the aim, in particular as regards new active substances, with a view to faster access for patients to a new medicinal product and the non-penalisation of small and medium-sized enterprises; 2-that marketing authorization should be granted for generic medicinal products whose reference medicinal product has been authorized for more than 8 years in a member state or in the community (and not 10 years, as proposed by the commission); the commission's proposal is ambiguous as regards information, since it is essential to ensure that direct advertising is prohibited.
in writing. - (pt) this proposal for a regulation is very important in that it aims to ensure a high level of protection of human and animal health through more rigorous market surveillance and pharmacovigilance procedures, to increase the number of medicines available, to complete the internal market for pharmaceutical products and, finally, to change the objectives and working methods of the european medicines agency, with a view to eu enlargement. the differences do not concern these broad objectives, nor the wish of all patients to have medicines containing new, safer and more effective active substances available more quickly at community level. they concern certain specific details of the regulation, as my vote shows. in particular, as regards the central question of whether or not the authorisation of medicinal products should be completely centralised at the head of the european agency for the evaluation of medicinal products (ema), i would like to say that, like the majority that has been formed in the relevant parliamentary committee, i am in favour of medicinal products for human use containing a new active substance being authorised by national agencies and therefore oppose further and harmful centralisation. in other words, i support the possibility of choosing between the centralised procedure and the national authorisation, possibly accompanied by the mutual recognition procedure.
in writing. - (pt) we do not agree with the report because of its excessive ambition in relation to a very poor document on the defence of fundamental rights. for example: - the intention to clearly inscribe the binding incorporation of the charter of fundamental rights into the treaties as part of the constitutional provisions of a future 'european constitution'; - the ambition to create a legal instrument which would make it possible to subordinate as much as possible the national constitutions of the member states, despite claims to the contrary (note the aim of increasing community competences in various fields). maintaining the content of the report will only make it more difficult to defend the incorporation of the charter into a constitution. the content of the constitution is characterised by a formulation of rights which in practice represents a step backwards from that of national constitutions, for example the portuguese constitution, or from the european conventions signed by the eu member states. finally, we also disagree with future methodological amendments to the content of the charter which enshrines, in practice, the existence of a permanent "convention" (or the newly proclaimed institution - the "congress") or other mechanisms for amending the so-called "constitutional" contents, which once again exceed the current exclusive competence of states (governments and parliaments) regarding the amendment of the treaties.
in writing. - (pt) to complete the reasoning of my speech in the debate, i would like to stress that this report can only be supported with enthusiasm by those who follow the profound thought of the rapporteur, who expressed it more frankly here when he spoke on the same subject on 14 march 2000, saying that the charter represents an opportunity to build a modern post-national society. in fact, contrary to the proclamations of the resolution and the debate, there is still a great risk that a militant and enlightened minority will immediately appropriate and overthrow the charter if it is incorporated into the treaties and given legal force, and then divert it from its axis, in order to effectively limit the competences of the member states in a very significant way, precisely in the central core of the sovereignty and self-determination of the respective peoples, by tending to overlap with the fundamental rights regimes of the member states and subordinate them entirely. a year and a half of de facto application with different bodies behaving as if the charter were directly applicable shows the danger. i regret that parliament is following the trend and is not making its voice heard with vigour in defence of the rule of law and the rules laid down by the treaties.
- mr president, the attacks of 11 september 2001, and more recently on the island of bali, have made clear the unpredictability of the terrorist threat, its lethal gravity and our vulnerability to it. in the 1960s and 1970s, europe was confronted with the violence of communist-inspired extremist movements such as the red brigades or baader-meinhof. today, it is extremist organizations like al qaeda that represent a destruction of modernity. they consider themselves entitled to use violent means to overthrow the democratic systems in force, believing themselves to be involved in an all-out war to defend islam from western hegemony and to impose it on the so-called infidels who do not share it, attacking even moderate muslim countries. it is true that there are other nationalist or separatist political motives for terrorist activity, including on european territory, which are equally repugnant to us but which are unfortunately now overtaken by this predominant form of terrorism, which is particularly dangerous because it attacks everywhere, uses indiscriminate violence and considers itself to be free from any moral or human constraint. we cannot ignore the fact that, because of its liberality and its tradition of welcoming political exiles, europe has become a haven for many radical and terrorist groups. despite this generous policy, these extremists are planting the roots of hatred and taking advantage of these facilities granted by democratic systems, organizing themselves, financing themselves, recruiting new members and architecting attacks against targets on our continent and in the world in general. the fight against terrorism, as a truly transnational phenomenon which can at any time threaten the rights, freedoms and guarantees of all of us, must therefore be the subject of a comprehensive strategy by the union and its member states which, either on its own, in the united nations or in cooperation with the united states within nato, prevents and represses terrorist acts and at the same time strengthens the rule of law. at european level, the member states' strategy must be based, as has already been said, on the effective use of all the mechanisms for judicial and police cooperation and the exchange of information, while also reinforcing a broad concept of security and defence which takes account of the need to deal with this renewed international threat. we are aware that the prevention and suppression of terrorism also requires the promotion of dialogue between civilizations and the adoption of effective policies to combat the social, economic and cultural factors which, although they cannot justify terrorist violence in any way, are systematically exploited and exploited by terrorist activists. it is on the basis of these principles of unity of purpose in the fight against terrorism, which are obviously beyond disagreement with some of the conclusions of the joint motion for a resolution we are discussing here, that we will support it and vote in favour of it.
- madam president, i would like to make a few comments on texts which i think are somewhat ambiguous. firstly, although the most important terrorist attacks of the last year took place in new york and bali, it would be interesting to reflect on what europe's response would have been if the original attack that drew our attention to the importance of terrorism, the attack of 11 september 2001, had taken place in europe, if the planes, instead of hitting the towers in new york, had hit the eiffel tower, the european parliament buildings or any other important european building. perhaps we had, in that self-reflection, more awareness of our ambiguities and our difficulties in dealing with the phenomenon of terrorism that we have not yet completely abandoned. the second point i would like to make - in addition to praising the council's intention to maintain cooperation with the united states on this matter - is that in fact, and this is an important point, terrorism has little to do with poverty. it is clear that there are poor people involved in terrorist acts, but those who are in charge are not poor. and if you look around the world, the poorest world is not involved in the phenomenon of terrorism: the world's top terrorist acts would come from africa if there really was a correlation between terrorism and poverty. there is no way! terrorism is a phenomenon in which there is a political rationality, and this political rationality implies the achievement of certain objectives and implies a logic of power. and this doesn't come, of course, from the poorest, or at least it doesn't come directly from the poorest. finally, i would like to quote a few words from commissioner chris patten, quoting popper. in fact, our problem is also a problem that popper was able to state: it is not so much the dilemma between liberties and our perception of security and the defense of the open society, it is our inability and difficulty to believe in the moral superiority of democracy, the moral superiority of freedom, and acting on this notion, which is a civilizational notion and a notion that people all over the world and particularly the poor wish for, perhaps we would have the capacity and resourcefulness to act against terrorism that, unfortunately, some european ambiguities do not allow us to have.
- madam president, today's debate is about taking stock of the fight against terrorism one year after 11 september 2001. how did the european union react to this tragic event? it expressed its solidarity and support for the united states, warned of the risk of confusion between terrorism and the arab and islamic world, accelerated its integration in the fight against terrorism through the action plan comprising more than 60 measures and a series of framework decisions including the european arrest warrant and police cooperation in the fight against terrorism, and stressed the urgency of finding political ways of resolving the conflicts which fuel terrorist fanaticism, in particular the israeli-palestinian issue. the determination to eradicate terrorism and the urgency to put in place new and effective instruments to that end must not in any case be disproportionately harsh on individual rights, freedoms and guarantees. it is the affirmation of the intangible nature of fundamental rights that underlies the legitimacy for the use of force by public authority in democracies. the same applies to the relentless fight against the terrorist threat, which must be limited by international law. totalitarian societies disregard the rule of law in the internal order as well as in the field of international relations. the idea of international legality is an invention of democracies. if the states which are victims of terrorism react against this threat in violation of the rules and principles of international law, they will be giving the criminals an unexpected gift which will constitute a retreat from civilization. the fanatical hatred that drives humans to suicide and suicide bombing is often fueled by persistent situations of injustice or humiliation. this should not be understood as a justification for the heinous attacks on civilian populations, but as an element of truth indispensable to the understanding of this phenomenon of violence. on a strictly european level, it is important to recall and denounce here the persistently demonstrated inability and unwillingness of the governments of the member states to implement the proposal for the harmonization of policies and the creation of common instruments to combat terrorism which was put forward in due course by the commission. terrorism is a threat that requires an integrated, supranational response. in the european context, this response is still not showing sufficient signs of life.
- madam president, mr commissioner, mr president-in-office of the council, ladies and gentlemen, parliament has carried out a year of exhaustive work in the committee which i have had the honour of chairing. many experts were consulted, missions were carried out, and numerous documents were analysed, culminating in the excellent report by mr gerhard schmid, whose conclusions were adopted by an overwhelming majority in this parliament. almost all our work was public, with the rare exception that the nature of the actions or entities heard required that it be done behind closed doors. we conclude that there is an echelon network, with the capacity to intercept and process communications involving the united states, the united kingdom, canada, australia and new zealand. we have made clear our concern about the low level of protection of our individual freedoms against this threat and about the security of our companies' communications and about fair trade in the international market. and we have adopted 44 recommendations in this plenary session, mainly addressed to the council, the commission and the member states. i have the feeling that everyone has preferred to forget this report and its conclusions. first of all, i must deplore the decision of the bureau of this parliament not to promote the publication of the report. and, turning to the council and the commission, it is time to say clearly that this year has seen little. the situation we are in today vis-à-vis our citizens and our companies is not substantially different from that in which we were a year ago. and we even see a trend that we can't help but fight - i would like to stress that the commission has not yet taken a decision on the matter, and that it is not yet ready to take a decision on the matter. it is important to reiterate and emphasize that security is an instrument for defending our freedom. when the demands of security stifle freedoms, we are betraying our values and falling into tyranny. finally, i would like to see that in a year's time we will not have to go back to the council, the commission and the member states and repeat what we said today: that little has been done and almost everything is the same.
- mr president, mr president-in-office, i thank you very much for your direct answer. however, i would point out that on the very eve of a visit by european union experts to iran, in october, there were a series of five public executions in iran which were reported by the entire press (and i hope the president-in-office of the council will not tell me that he did not take note of what was said in the press), which corresponds to a clear position of the iranian authorities not to bend one step in their logic with regard to human rights. the number of reported executions in iran has tripled in the last three years, and in these circumstances, the question is this: will the european union make a trade deal with iran after all these systematic human rights violations? and that's what i'd like to hear from you.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, liberalized air transport has brought passengers many advantages. however, as we all know, lower prices and increased demand do not stop consumers from complaining. as our colleagues have already said, there are flights cancelled or delayed, there are denied boarding, problems with luggage and so on. this is unacceptable! the liberalization of air transport requires, for the protection of the consumer, better management and rationalization of the market. the adaptation of existing legislation to the new realities is essential. it is entirely appropriate to involve the operators in the sector by obliging them to adopt standards for the protection of citizens, making it possible for the authorities to intervene if the guarantees of protection announced are insufficient. and i think it's very important that the authorities intervene. the involvement of passengers by operators through the call for volunteers, involving them directly in negotiating and accepting exchange conditions, is a good approach that makes the management of reservations more flexible and contributes to the protection of the passenger, improving the efficiency of the operation. i would like to emphasize the question of authority here: the proposal for a regulation shows the importance of article 14, which refers to the obligation of the authority to inform passengers of their rights in this field. it is essentially a reintroduction of an amendment adopted by parliament in response to the commission's 1998 proposal. the passenger must be clearly and transparently informed of how to contact the competent national body - which he often does not know, most of the time - responsible for dealing with complaints about the regulation. the importance of article 17 on enforcement, which obliges the member state to designate the body responsible for enforcing the regulation, disseminating it and enforcing its observance and the measures to ensure respect for passengers' rights.
- mr president, i would first like to thank mr bashir khanbhai for his excellent report. despite his moderation, he is still making some self-criticism of the european union and the cap, which i think is unfair in some cases. firstly, the report fails to recognise that the european union is now the largest market in the world for agri-food products from the poorest countries. it is enough to recall that it is the world's leading importer of agricultural products and that imports from developing countries amount to more than 27 billion euros a year, or more than the sum of all imports from these countries by the united states, japan, new zealand, canada and australia combined. secondly, the european union and its member states are by far the biggest supporters of these countries, with some 28 billion dollars in development aid, compared with, for example, 11 billion from japan or 9 billion from the united states. thirdly, it should be remembered that in the world trade organisation negotiations it was the european union which was the strongest in advocating the need for positive discrimination in favour of developing countries. and that it has the responsibility for what is so far the most important initiative in favour of the 50 poorest countries in the world, which is the 'everything but arms' initiative, which proposes to receive the main products of these countries on the community market without any tariffs and without any counterpart. finally, i would like to point out that, in essence, the european union's agriculture is complementary to the agriculture of the developing countries, since these are major importers of products from temperate zones, whereas the european union is a major importer of tropical products. the conflict is often seen more in terms of the interests of some companies operating in these countries than in terms of the interests of the local population.
- mr president, the absence of an institutional agreement would lead to a delicate situation, as the rapporteur has already acknowledged, given that it is urgent to adopt a new fiscalis framework which could be put into operation on 1 january 2003. it is a pity, however, that the council's agreement with parliament's positions was based on a minimum threshold. it is also significant that the commission, while recognising that the aim of the fiscalis programme is not to harmonise tax legislation, welcomed the fact that a statement to that effect was entered in the minutes. even if it is acceptable to describe the fiscalis programme as a mere instrument to strengthen administrative cooperation, parliament will always have to be concerned about the political effects of the implementation of such a programme. while tax evasion and tax fraud have a country-specific national dimension, they also have, and increasingly do, a supranational dimension based on the use of sophisticated techniques and schemes. at a particularly sensitive time for the union's public finances, these factors cannot be ignored, much less the opportunities that are emerging to change and improve the fiscal situation. in addition, the european union is on the verge of a historic enlargement, which means that new countries and different tax policies will create new and unprecedented problems. the new programme also extends its scope not only to direct taxation but also to the applicant countries. the improvement of the functioning of taxation systems in the internal market is in itself a laudable objective, but the fight against tax fraud and evasion and the establishment of minimum harmonization bases are also opportunities that should not be missed. i therefore regret that the commission has invoked article 95 of the treaty on this matter and has not invoked article 93. with the reservations expressed and in view of the need for the new programme to enter into force, i support the rapporteur's recommendation.
in writing. - (pt) i voted on the first reading of the draft budget of the union for 2003 because i found it to be a balanced and rigorous document, following the guidelines laid down in berlin, particularly as regards the financial perspectives for the use of the structural funds. i would like to highlight two points in this proposal: firstly, the refusal of the majority of members, including myself, to reduce the appropriations for supporting the functioning of european parliamentary parties and groups; secondly, the maintenance of the specific budget line for financial assistance for the rehabilitation and reconstruction of east timor, as well as the significant increase in its amount compared to the previous year. the european parliament took this decision by a very large majority, giving substance to the amendment proposal presented to the committee on budgets, which i also signed.
in writing. - (pt) i warmly welcome the adoption, in the framework of the general budget of the european union, of the maintenance of both a specific line for support for the reconstruction and development of east timor and an appropriate level of funding. the vote on both parts of amendment no 674, as made clear by the roll-call vote, is only an extension of the spirit of intense collaboration and solidarity which the european union has given to the youngest nation in the world. we recall the award of the 1999 sakharov prize by the european parliament to the current president of east timor, mr xanana gusmão, and the promise to help consolidate the new nation, which was exemplaryly expressed in the european parliament's resolution of 4 october 2001. the progress of this specific budget line in parliament is a reward for the attention, tenacity and good coordination between the members who are best informed and most inclined towards east timor - in particular the portuguese members of parliament belonging to the different political tendencies. however, in the face of the much larger majority obtained in plenary, it also sends a signal that the parliament is living up to its political and moral responsibilities, that it is not betraying its commitments to the timorese people. this is really very rewarding.
in writing. - (pt) i congratulate mr asensio on his report on the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council establishing common standards for the compilation of community statistics on rail freight and passenger transport, including safety information. i'm giving him my full support. as regards a legislative proposal which has generally been agreed by the various parties concerned, i would just like to stress that the adoption of this legislative instrument will make it possible to assess the common rail sector in europe within the framework of the common transport policy and thus to take informed decisions, in particular as regards greater future aid for the speed, comfort and safety of rail traffic within the member states, between them and between member states and third countries, as an alternative to road and air congestion, to the benefit of users and, undoubtedly, the environment itself.
in writing. - (pt) the promotion of rail transport - as well as short sea shipping or cabotage - as a greener and safer alternative to excessive road traffic has long been an important policy orientation. however, for this to be based on a fruitful and objective debate, it is essential to have a rigorous and reliable knowledge of the realities of the sector, both to determine the urgency of the strategic decisions to be taken - in particular the priority destinations and routes - and to develop measures aimed at a desirable reduction in road transport. this is the importance of this proposal for a regulation, which aims to update and adapt rail transport in order to provide better monitoring and a more detailed analysis of the development of the sector, taking into account the measures to be taken under the railway package. at this stage i welcome the council's common position - and the amendments tabled by the european parliament. it shows that this is an area that brings together more than it divides and can have a huge positive impact on the quality of life of future european citizens. that is why i openly supported the report.
in writing. - (pt) as the rapporteur, mr goebbels, has said, the proposal we have just voted on on insider dealing and market manipulation (market abuse) is indispensable from the point of view of principles. the importance of this directive, together with the urgency of its adoption, explains why i am exercising the right to a written declaration of vote, even though i am not a member of the economic committee and, therefore, this is not a subject in which i have been particularly involved. i therefore use this opportunity to reiterate the need for a vote in favour of the recommendation in question. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the directive is implemented. it is urgent not only on principle, as i said at the outset, but also to help restore confidence in the financial markets. this explains the spirit of consensus reached at first reading, which i welcome.
in writing. - (pt) the succession of scandals on the us financial markets has exposed the lack of professional integrity of certain financial intermediaries, as the rapporteur says; this is undermining the confidence of public and private investors, while creating doubts, suspicions and uncertainties among all economic actors, and generalising a general malaise for the global economy. i voted in favour of the amendments to this report because i believe they contribute more effectively to a fight which must be fought with tenacity against the bad side of globalisation, which has also encouraged the emergence of new economic crime, certain distortions, concealment and manipulation. i believe that this must be the way to regain the confidence of economic agents, a prerequisite for a genuine area of freedom, security and justice, and a step towards the most dynamic and competitive economic area in the world, based on knowledge and capable of guaranteeing sustainable growth, with more and better jobs and greater social cohesion (lisbon strategy). the committee also welcomes the fact that the commission has taken the initiative to introduce a new system of taxation of capital, which is based on the principle of equal treatment for men and women.
in writing. - (pt) i would like to express my support for the amendments by the rapporteur, mr giorgio lisi, to the european commission's initiative establishing rules for compensation and assistance to air passengers in the event of overbooking, cancellation or delay of flights. we all know or have been victims of such practices, which upset travel plans. the initiative will involve airline operators in recruiting 'volunteers' who will give up their flight and opt for reimbursement or re-routing to their final destination. customers who are denied boarding against their will will receive financial compensation proportional to the distance of the flight. these rules apply both in cases of overbooking, cancellation or delay of flights operated by scheduled and charter airlines.
in writing. - (pt) the liberalisation of air transport has brought clear benefits to users. just think of the sharp reduction in the price of air tickets and, consequently, the impressive increase in the number of passengers. in an overall positive environment for the consumer, there are still (apart from safety problems) aspects which require some attention and which often cause justified complaints from these same users, especially as regards the disturbances caused by delays or cancellations of flights. i therefore voted in favour of all the proposals in the report, which aim to set appropriate compensation rates for the harm suffered by passengers as a result of overbooking by airlines, as part of a policy of attributing specific responsibilities to these carriers.
in writing. - (pt) the liberalisation of air transport has brought clear benefits to air transport users. just think of the sharp reduction in the price of air tickets and, consequently, the impressive increase in the number of passengers. the internal market itself is also a matter of freedom of movement. however, from the consumer passenger's perspective, some aspects require new attention, notably overbooking by airlines (a fairly common practice) and cases (not covered by the previous regulation) where passengers are rerouted on other flights to their final destination, with some delay. the current situation has given rise to justified complaints from users, especially concerning disturbances and harm caused by delays or cancellations of flights. many airlines have committed themselves to responding better to passenger expectations, but a new regulatory framework is needed to protect passenger interests. the commission proposal is in that direction. this is also what this report contributes to, and i voted in favour of it in particular because it has weighed the divergent interests in this area and it sets out a series of reasonable commitments. this is precisely why it was widely agreed upon by the committee on regional policy, transport and tourism.
in writing. - (pt) this report is part of the liberalisation of the electricity and natural gas markets; it takes account of the decisions of the stockholm and barcelona european councils in defending the new guidelines for these sectors after the liberalisation decision. it therefore considers that the construction and maintenance of energy infrastructure must normally be subject to market rules. this means that there is pressure for privatisation of the sector, including infrastructure, which we do not agree with. although the rapporteur supports the objective of competition and the completion of the internal energy market in the light of universal service obligations, his options for supporting the financing of priority projects, even in difficult areas, cannot exceed 20% and only in exceptional cases. it is, however, higher than the commission's proposal, which was only 10%, but it refers only to the construction phase, which remains subject to non-distortion of competition between firms, which means defending liberalisation in this area too and subjecting it to the criteria of economic rather than social profitability.
in writing. - (pt) the proposal we have debated and voted on today on the revision of a set of guidelines on trans-european networks in the energy sector provides for a redefinition of the priorities for trans-european networks (tens), by identifying, among the projects of common interest, the priority projects of european interest which will receive priority when funding is decided under the regulation on the granting of financial support to the tens. the priority project category covers a set of energy network projects with a sufficiently significant impact on the completion of the internal market and the strengthening of security of supply. however, i would like to emphasise the strategic importance of tens as a fundamental instrument for overcoming the constraints of geographical peripheral location in countries such as portugal, especially in the case of the outermost regions such as the autonomous regions of madeira and the azores. this explains why i advocated differentiated treatment for these regions, given their inability to connect to the european electricity and gas networks. i therefore consider it essential to support energy production projects based on renewable energy sources, within the framework of the endogenous potential of each of these regions and to frame them in the priority axes.
in writing. - (pt) i think it is essential that the regulation of transeuropean networks in the energy sector complies not only with criteria of security of energy supply and non-distortion of competition, but also with criteria of environmental sustainability. i therefore argue that the challenge must be to produce energy from renewable sources, as this is the sector with the least environmental externalities. it is also important to focus on research into new energy sources in order to reduce their production costs, and on decentralized sources.
in writing. - (pt) i voted in favour of this report because i believe, first of all, that the commission's approach to making greater use of the tens (trans-european networks) financial support instrument for the internal market in electricity and gas is correct. this is in line with the european parliament's report, approved on 13 march 2002 at first reading, on the proposal for a regulation on the development of crossborder trade in electricity. in fact, since progress has been so slow in recent years, as the commission points out - only 3 of the 14 specific projects have been completed - the rapporteur's suggestion to identify a new category of work is appropriate. within the projects of common interest, it is proposed to identify a category of priority projects of european interest. this category will include a limited number of energy network projects with a highly significant impact from the point of view of the essential energy policy criteria, namely the completion of the internal market and the strengthening of security of supply. i also followed the line of increasing the connection of renewable energy production to interconnected energy networks, which will lead to the desirable increase in the production and consumption of these energies.
in writing. - (pt) the amendments tabled by the european parliament in the areas of the deadlines and frequency of export notifications, the amount of information required, technical assistance and training are fundamental to better complying with the objectives set by the rotterdam convention. it is therefore to be hoped that the council and the commission will accept these amendments. i congratulate the rapporteur on his excellent work.
in writing. - (pt) the terrorist threat has taken on a global dimension. this threat to democracy and the free exercise of human rights calls for a global response if it is to be effectively combated. the eu is proud to stand for these fundamental values throughout the world and the eu. the eu's swift and effective action in defining the comprehensive counter-terrorism strategy (the european counter-terrorism action plan) is to be commended. the overall strategy of the eu and the member states should aim at: 2 - to strengthen democratic control - it is regrettable that both the ep and the national parliaments were only partially informed of the measures decided by the council; 3 - to strengthen the means of preventing and punishing terrorist crimes. it is essential that member states have effective criminal legislation and adopt measures of police and judicial cooperation. the area of freedom, security and justice that we are gradually building only makes sense if these three components are balanced, without any of them being detrimental to the others. the tragic events of 11 september 2001 cannot be used as an excuse for excesses or exaggerations that lead to security over freedom and justice.
in writing. - (pt) the resolution on the 'evaluation and outlook of the eu strategy on terrorism one year after 11 september 2001' contains several contradictions. i would like to highlight some of its most negative aspects: the defence of the militarisation of the 'fight against terrorism', with for example the change of the current concept of defence in favour of a 'broader concept of security' which allows for the 'preventive strikes' so topical in the eyes of mr bush, by conflating the internal security missions of the states with defence policy; the reaffirmation of nato and the partnership with the usa as the european union's reference framework in the 'fight against terrorism'; - support for the reform of nato (its political and military structures) to change its missions from a defensive alliance to an offensive one; - the desire to criminalise the actions of organisations and movements which fight, sometimes with the use of arms, against the repression suffered by their people, by confusing it with 'terrorism'; that is why we voted against.
in writing. - (pt) i opposed some of the really regrettable paragraphs in the joint resolution, but i voted for it in the final vote because i consider the fight against terrorism to be fundamental - it is the most serious global threat of our time - and because i did not want to contribute in any way to a weaker signal in this area. the hostage-taking of hundreds of innocent people in a popular theatre in moscow yesterday, which has led to a new drama whose outcome is still absolutely uncertain, confirms in reality all the worst concerns after the attacks of 11 september 2001 in bali and other actions around the world. this brutal - and increasingly appalling - framework exposes the vulnerabilities of modern societies and should serve as a warning against the ambiguities and the traps of pseudo-justification which unfortunately abound in the text of the resolution. under the pressure of successive compromises, the text is too long and resembles a tiresome waltz or a tiresome tango, with steps left and right, front and back - so that many of us forget the direction it is taking. we will not go far with this and terrorists of all stripes will be able to continue to smile as they target innocent and defenceless civilians and sow hatred, brutality and destruction. the decisive fight against terrorism is one of the fundamental political struggles of the beginning of this century, if we do not want to end it worse than we started it. without yielding to any political or moral exculpation, we must continue to use all the political, diplomatic, military, police and judicial instruments, as the concrete circumstances and the effectiveness of deterrence or repression dictate, by refusing the delay that conditions the victims and favors the aggressors.
- mr president, commissioner, kyoto is no longer a matter of negotiation, it is a matter of action. and i think that from the point of view of the new political agenda of the european union, there are a set of three objectives in this area which must be achieved in the short term. first, to ensure that the protocol enters into force. for the protocol to enter into force, russia must ratify it. and the problem is that russia has already promised several times to ratify the protocol and this has not yet happened. i ask the commissioner what concrete political and diplomatic initiatives are being taken and will be taken with the russian government to have the protocol tabled for ratification in the duma as a matter of urgency? secondly, i think it is important not to give up - we cannot give up - on persuading the american administration to join the kyoto protocol. i think the emissions trading directive is helping the european campaign to persuade the americans to join the kyoto protocol. let's say that with emissions trading, we can show that it is possible to meet kyoto at a lower economic cost. but i don't think charm is enough here either. i think it is essential to get kyoto to the next round of the world trade organization. and my question to the commission is whether it is prepared to put forward an initiative at the next round of the world trade organisation to ensure that the competitive and legitimate advantages which american products will have because their economy is not covered by the quito protocol are eliminated. thirdly, mr president, i think it is important that the european union should strengthen its international leadership through action, not so much through speeches. and on this subject i would like to congratulate commissioner margot wallström on the excellent work she is doing on this subject. the commission has shown initiative, in particular with the emissions trading directive and the european climate change programme, but i think we have a serious political problem here: the commission's proposal is that the member states should be able to take part in the negotiations. and i think it is essential, for the credibility of our leadership, that action is taken at member state level too. as regards the role of the parliament's delegation in new delhi, i would like to reiterate what mrs caroline jackson said: it is essential that parliament's delegation has a seat at the daily meetings of the european union.
mr president, under rule 142 i would like to ask the bureau how it is possible that under rule 50 of our rules of procedure we are going to discuss something like the human rights dialogue with iran. what does this have to do with urgent cases of human rights violations? this has nothing to do with article 50 (1). the motion tabled by the socialist group, which concerned the stoning of women, a common practice in iran which affects girls as young as nine, made perfect sense. this one, i cannot understand, mr president. but i would like to put a second question to the bureau: if we are going to have a dialogue on human rights between iran and the european union, i would like the bureau to clarify for me the exact meaning and scope of this debate, because according to the iranian authorities, according to what we can see on the internet about the dialogue, what they are saying is that there is persecution of islam in europe, particularly in the united kingdom. it's published there. i would like to be clear on this matter.
- mr president, a point of order to record my indignation at the undemocratic method followed by the presidency in this debate. we have been here since 5 p.m. to start this debate, but the various presidencies have been profoundly incompetent in their management of time. and now the president is coming to announce that the coordinators are speaking, commissioner franz fischler is speaking and then the debate is interrupted, so that the other registered members are speaking after dinner. this, mr president, is very undemocratic and unworthy. i want this on the record.
- mr president, madam president-in-office of the council, to whom i thank for your availability to be here, i must say that i am of the opinion that the brussels summit had the undeniable advantage of unblocking the financial process of enlargement and of fixing the rules for financing agriculture for the next ten years. but it would be wrong to confuse this stability of funding with the status quo in the cap. there are four reasons for this:- the first is that the present cap is at odds with the multifunctional approach to agriculture, which implies a substantial strengthening of its second pillar. and if it is not by transferring the first pillar, i do not see where to find resources for this in this framework of enlargement to 25, to 27 and then even possibly to 28 states; - i know . the second reason is that it will cost us very dearly to defend the status quo of the blue box in the wto negotiations. the price to be paid may be terms to be given up on such important matters as market access, protection of designations of origin or even the blue box supports themselves; - i know . the third reason is that we have a direct aid system in the cap which is actually schizophrenic, that is, it gives aid to some farmers while denying it to others. this is unsustainable in the long term and must be changed by means of an aid system which does not discriminate between products and producers and which corrects this imbalance. the fourth reason for action is that the current system of aid is too complicated to be applied in the union of 25 or 27 members. i think it is preferable to make a reform now, before 2004, in a balanced way, ensuring the management of the markets and their regulatory instruments, ensuring community preference, ensuring support for farmers, than to do it after enlargement with the inherent risks of uncertainty about what the negotiating and decision-making processes will be in these first years of the enlarged union.
- mr president, madam vice-president of the commission, ladies and gentlemen, i shall confine my speech to the central political question underlying the future of cohesion policy. the question is, as can be easily calculated, how much money will be devoted to this crucial pillar of the european construction process in the period 2007-13. this is the central political question, because europe can only be viable if it is based on a project of solidarity for its most deprived regions. the european union cannot be built on excessive regional disparities. the last european council in brussels, when it signed the franco-german agreement on the freezing of expenditure on the common agricultural policy until 2013 on the basis of 2006 expenditure, assumed that the same principle of freezing could also be applied to the financial resources devoted to cohesion policy. in other words, the same union budget that has served a europe of 15 countries will also have to serve a europe of 27, and the illusion that the savings from the cap could be used to support cohesion policy will be put to rest. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. it can be said that a cohesion policy financed at a level of around 0.45% of community gdp would be a good achievement. the scenarios of renationalisation that have been looming over cohesion policy in some countries would at least be put to rest. however, i would like to emphasize the lack of resources which jeopardizes the existence in the future of a balanced cohesion policy which, on the one hand, does not forget the cohesion deficit still existing in the present european union of 15 and, on the other hand, is up to the greatest challenge ever posed to the process of european integration. finally, i would also point out, quite appropriately, the contrast with the recent past, when, in the face of other major challenges, the financial resources for cohesion were doubled.
in writing. - (pt) i voted in favour of this report because i consider the legislative procedure which is now being completed to be exemplary, not only because it responds to shortcomings which were tragically identified in the past, but also because it is based on a properly tested and consolidated normative procedure. after the terrible disaster in estonia in 1994, eight northern european countries decided in 1996 to adopt and enforce stricter standards than those in place to ensure greater strength through specific technical requirements for ships and to improve the stability of passenger ships, to make them safer in the event of an accident by delaying their capsizing and allowing the complete evacuation of passengers in good time. it is precisely the assessment of the effectiveness of this agreement and, at the same time, of its very positive impact on security that led the commission to consider - in my view, with reason in the context described - that it was justified to take a legislative initiative in the form of a directive to extend this normative progress to all the member states of the european union. the aim is to adopt a package of measures to improve community legislation in the field of passenger ship safety, in full accordance with the policy objectives outlined in the white paper on this subject - which of course deserves my full support.
in writing. - (pt) i voted for this report for the same reasons as those which led me to support the poignant report on the specific stability requirements for passenger ships. with the stockholm agreement, the nordic countries committed themselves to adapting their passenger ships to more stringent safety standards than the existing ones, in order to provide more stability to ships in the event of a shipwreck, through certain construction-related measures. in short, the aim was to improve the ship's buoyancy in the event of a sinking. since these measures have been found to be effective and their validity has been recognised also in seas other than those of northern europe, the commission now proposes to revise directive 98/18/ec of 17 march 1998 by extending the safety measures mentioned to passenger ships operating in european national waters. in a parallel directive, the commission proposes to extend these measures to ships on international voyages in all european seas. i therefore join in this concern and particularly welcome the fact that this report pays special attention to promoting actions to improve accessibility for passengers with reduced mobility.
in writing. - (pt) the admission by the north korean authorities that they were developing a clandestine nuclear weapons programme would require a stronger position from the european parliament than that of the joint proposal on which we have just voted. the text should in particular require the programme to be terminated and its activities to be verified as complying with the rules laid down by the international atomic energy agency. furthermore, the european parliament's position should make the further eu dialogue with north korea in the areas of cooperation, humanitarian aid, investment and support for democratic institutions conditional on clarification of this issue. that is why i can only abstain, at most.
in writing. - (pt) i supported this report because i believe that without a genuine stabilisation of the european territorial area as a whole, it will be difficult to respond to the anxieties and expectations of the european peoples regarding lasting peace and the consolidation of a dynamic, competitive and socially fairer economic area throughout the continent. it is well known that the strategy pursued by the european union with regard to the five countries of the western balkans (albania, bosnia and herzegovina, croatia, the former yugoslav republic of macedonia, serbia and montenegro, including kosovo) is being applied in the context of the stabilisation and association process, and that this strategy aims to develop, in this particularly sensitive region, forms of democracy and strong market economies in the same way as the eu. therefore, in terms of promoting a european area of rule of law and economic progress, i must emphasise certain aspects mentioned by the rapporteur, which i also support, namely: clarifying the status of kosovo; clearly implementing a refugee return policy and an active policy to combat organised crime and corruption; strengthening the rule of law, in particular through the establishment of an independent judiciary, following the democratic model; strengthening the education system in consolidating peace between peoples by promoting an objective knowledge of history, mutual understanding and respect for other nations and their cultures; promoting the role of independent media and independent political control, capable of functioning as a real counter-power.
in writing. - (pt) we support this report when it says that 'the cohesion policy of tomorrow must be based on the principles of solidarity, partnership and complementarity' and that 'the outermost regions must be a priority of the new regional policy'. we also agree that there is a need to 'ensure a clear improvement in the coherence of eu policies affecting economic and social cohesion'. we must therefore denounce the harmful effects that the proposals under consideration concerning the mid-term review of the cap and the common fisheries policy will have on the union's cohesion policy. however, as regards the financing of cohesion policy in a future enlarged europe, we consider that it is not enough to simply recognise that it is not possible to reduce the current percentage of 0.45% of the union's gdp. given the implications of the restrictions imposed by the stability pact on the least developed countries - particularly in portugal, where the budget proposal submitted by the government to meet the convergence criteria will considerably accentuate regional asymmetries - this response will have to be made on the basis of a real assessment of the needs of cohesion policy and with a revision of the financial perspective for enlargement.
in writing. - (pt) in its analysis of this report, the committee on regional policy, transport and tourism has had the merit of highlighting certain important aspects in terms of economic and social cohesion policy, such as the recognition of the effectiveness of the structural funds system for the economic growth of developing regions, the opposition to the attempt to rationalise this policy and the recognition of the specific nature of the outermost regions, among others. the report, however, omits a principle which is essential to our wholehearted support. the continuation of the european cohesion policy in an enlarged europe, which will be much less balanced from the point of view of regional development, cannot be at the expense of the solidarity which remains due to the present cohesion countries, the foremost of which is portugal.
in writing. - (pt) i abstained from the final vote on this report mainly because of the oddities that characterized it and certain ambiguities that arose from it. the original version of the text proposed by the rapporteur was not clear, and in some parts it even contradicted the previous resolution of the european parliament, which was approved by a large majority last february. the final text would thus be the result of various amendments proposed by virtually all the political groups, which have made it possible to make certain improvements, although, as always happens in these circumstances, the final mosaic does not shine with a high formal quality. it is certain that some fundamental and consensual points concerning the principle of economic and social cohesion and the respective policies - including the defence of the specificity of the outermost regions - will eventually be reaffirmed, but the text does not end up having the force i would wish, especially in the context of the transformations that are looming on the horizon: enlargement and institutional reform. thus, my final vote, together with the various part-votes, was intended to express the essential value i attach to this community principle (cf. article 158 of the ec treaty) as a fundamental founding principle and permanent guiding principle of the european communities and the european union, which will never be reaffirmed and given sufficient value. in fact, only the continuity of cohesion policies can lead to the desired reduction of economic and social disparities and the strengthening of common values within a european union.
- madam president, commissioners, members of the european parliament, the reform of the common fisheries policy has been met with sometimes alternating waves of opposition. some objections are reasonable and justified, others are hasty and disproportionate. the common fisheries policy needs to be reformed. no one in his right mind can deny it. the depletion of resources is not a fantasy or an exaggeration of environmentalists, or marine conservationists, or pessimistic scientists. it is a dramatic reality for many species. in the past, certain stocks have also collapsed, but they have been restored by shifting fishing effort to other species and geographical latitudes. today that is practically impossible. the example of the collapse of the cod stocks in newfoundland, where so much history, so much legend has been made, for example in my country, portugal, should serve as a warning to us. of the more than thirty-five thousand species that inhabit the seas, only a hundred are of commercial and consumer interest. that's right! however, there is now an impoverishment of marine biodiversity and the progressive destruction of habitats and ecosystems. it is not only the pressure exerted by fishing that is causing concern, but other well-known causes are also contributing to this phenomenon. fisheries are now subject to a double requirement: the ecological requirement to preserve fish stocks and the political and social requirement to maintain the economic activities of the sea fishing sector, which requires that these activities be closely linked to the state of the resources and not assume a predatory or simple collecting character as if we were still in the paleolithic. it was a happy inspiration to combine the souchet report on the community action plan on the integration of environmental protection requirements into the common fisheries policy, the stevenson report on the reform of the common fisheries policy and the busk report on the control and repression of illegal acts. the reform of the common fisheries policy must be a compromise that balances all aspects: social, economic, cultural and ecological. to do that, those members who are more in favour of - as they say - fishing, have to consider this truth from la palice: no fish, no fishing.
- madam president, i also support mr souchet's report and i am sorry about the pollution caused by the tanker in galicia. as for the stevenson report, although contradictory, it takes account of a significant part of the proposals and positions we support and which are important for portugal, namely the maintenance of the twelve-mile limit as a permanent factor in the common fisheries policy, the participation of fishermen's organisations in the decision-making process, socioeconomic measures to compensate for the effects of the reform, which must take account of the efforts of the past, greater investment in research and the absence of a legal vacuum. two of our proposals, which we consider to be of the greatest importance, were also adopted: the exemption of free access up to 50 miles for the outermost regions, in accordance with the treaty, and the strengthening of the mechanisms to support the processing industry, particularly the canning industry. however, we disagree with the proposals which seek to amend the report, and we take up some of the proposals which were rejected in the committee on fisheries and which go against the positions and proposals we have put forward. but we will vote in favour of the proposal which is equivalent to an amendment of ours, of great importance for portugal, which was already adopted in the other report and which considers that the six/twelve mile zones should be maintained and defined as an element of the common fisheries policy for an unlimited period of time, in order to promote conservation and encourage local development of small-scale fishing.
- the present parliament report on the reform of the cfp corrects some of the shortcomings of the commission proposal, but omits others. on the positive side, i would stress three main aspects: - first, sustainable fisheries management must be based on a balanced set of measures, not only on the simplistic solution of fleet reduction but also on greater selectivity of gear, the appropriate use of pops, tacs and quotas. in particular, the recommendation that the reduction in fishing effort should take account of fleet reductions which member states have already made in the past; - i know . the second aspect is the clear recommendation that fisheries agreements with third countries should remain an integral part of the cfp, which, if properly implemented, is of mutual benefit to both parties. in this respect, it is justified that vessels which have been scrapped from the community fleet may continue to be included in the fleets of the mixed companies; - i know . the third aspect is the desire to find a specific solution for access to the waters of the outermost regions with balances, with very sensitive ecosystems - the case of the azores, madeira and other regions of the european union. the most negative aspect of this is that it omits the entire structural component. there are two main reasons why the rules laid down by the berlin european council on the financing of fisheries structures should not be changed. the first is that fleet modernisation is in itself a priority, otherwise the community fleet will lose competitiveness: in the case of portugal, 40% of our fleet is over 25 years old and therefore, if it is not supported, it is not viable; and the second reason is that, having set a budget for the modernisation of the fleet in berlin by 2007 and having several shipowners taking on burdens on that basis, it is politically unacceptable to change the rules now in the middle of the game.

- mr president, i would like to make a brief point of order. the president of parliament closed the debate on the future of the enlarged european union this morning by praising the professionalism of the interpreters and the quality of the work they did this morning. i would like to say, as i have been informed, that the portuguese booth was the one which provided the greatest number of live interpretations, that is, without a relay. i am not saying this to compete for booths, but to join in your greetings and to wish that in future parliament will be politically equal to the professionalism of interpreters and will follow the signal they have given us: a practical demonstration that enlargement should not be a pretext for reducing the quality of our interpretations but should, on the contrary, be used as an instrument to strengthen the linguistic and cultural pluralism of europe, as the president has rightly stressed.
the european parliament has been a very active participant in the process of european integration, and it has been a very active participant in the process of european integration. it was as a result of this reunification that the copenhagen european council in 1993 decided to integrate into the european union the associated states of central and eastern europe which wished to join. the copenhagen european council made it clear that the accession of these countries would depend on their fulfilling the obligations inherent in their membership of the union. the copenhagen criteria are well known to everyone, and there is no need to mention them specifically in this context. in addition to german reunification, we cannot ignore two other political events which, although not carrying the same symbolic weight, have assumed particular importance in the development of the next steps in this process of european reunification. i am referring first of all to the nice summit, where the european heads of state or government reformed the community institutions to make them fit for an enlarged europe with 12 more countries. we are all aware that failure in this area would jeopardise the enlargement process for many years to come. apart from the opinion that each of us has about the institutional solutions found, the truth is that from nice onwards the applicant countries at least became aware of their relative positions in the union and what they could expect in the future. secondly, i refer to the gothenburg summit, which was the culmination of the swedish presidency. in their conclusions, european leaders emphasised that the enlargement process was irreversible, setting for the first time a time horizon of the end of 2002 for the completion of negotiations with the candidate countries which were eligible at that time, with the stated aim of those countries being able to participate as members in the 2004 european elections to the european parliament. today, as the end of 2002 draws near and the european parliament has met for the first time this morning in plenary session with the representatives of all the candidate countries, it is our duty, and our pleasure, to applaud the extraordinary efforts made by these countries to adapt their political, economic, social and judicial structures to the model of the european union, efforts which, above all, have made this moment a victory that belongs to them and should be recognized. as a hungarian colleague said this morning, we only have to regret that the communist dictatorship which has subjugated these countries for more than forty years has prevented them from participating from the outset in this formidable adventure of european integration. these words do not mean, however, that there is no awareness that the union is a risky venture and that enlargement will increase this risk by bringing together two worlds which, unfortunately, have very different levels of development, which will accentuate the differences between the richest and the least prosperous regions and increase the population living in the least favoured regions. it is therefore natural that questions arise and fears increase, for example, about the reduced effectiveness of regional cohesion policies or the difficulties in financing economic and social structural policies. but it is in the face of this europe of challenges and difficulties, whose recent history has accelerated at the speed of light and has seen all its balances altered in little more than half a dozen years, that the strong are distinguished from the weak and the winners from the well-to-do and the resigned. mr president, among the countries which have distinguished themselves in their efforts to move closer to the european union with a view to its accession is hungary, whose developments and reality i have tried to follow as rapporteur for this parliament. as expected, the latest periodic report of the commission on hungary concluded that, in view of the progress achieved up to the date of its publication, hungary should be in a position to conclude the negotiations by the end of the year and to assume the obligations of accession in accordance with the timetable envisaged. this is not surprising. hungary has always been among the first candidate countries in terms of the dynamics of the negotiations and its results, particularly in the economic field, have been hailed as among the best in the region. the hungarian economy continues to be one of the best performing in the region, with one of the highest growth rates in europe, the lowest inflation in the last ten years, a relatively low unemployment rate, and a growing trade turnover with the european union. in its resolutions adopted over the past two years, parliament has made a number of recommendations, in particular to encourage the continuation of reforms in the field of roma integration, the improvement of social dialogue and the adoption of the law on the media, or compliance with the rules governing public markets. today we can see that progress has been made in these areas as in many others and that the efforts made by the hungarian authorities have been consistent. finally, we must conclude that hungary is in a good position to join the european union in 2004 and should continue its preparations in accordance with the commitments it has made during the negotiations, and that we hope to have you with us in the next parliamentary term with the same parliamentary representation as the present member states of equivalent population size.
