- mr president, the enlargement of the european union from 2004 is a historic milestone in the construction of europe, which we all applaud with all our heart. and this for a number of reasons. first, because it symbolizes the reconciliation of two parts of the same continent that were divided for more than half a century, until just over a decade ago. secondly, because this is the biggest enlargement in the history of the european union, with the almost doubling of the number of member states and an increase of one third of its population and territory. thirdly, because it covers countries which until recently lived by the logic of a state economy, which are going through a painful but necessary phase of restructuring their economies and with a relatively low average level of income per head. and fourthly, because most of the new countries have an agricultural sector which is still very large compared with the present european union. it is in this context that i would like to stress two aspects: - firstly, it is necessary to concentrate support in these countries, community support, financial support, technical support, in order to give them the means to speed up their process of economic recovery and development. but we must also be aware that there are some regions in the present european union of 15 which are also very poor, and therefore the european union cannot abandon the policy of economic and social cohesion which it has pursued for some years now. - i know . secondly, it should be noted that the doubling of the number of farmers, with the accession of romania and bulgaria, will require a major simplification of the common agricultural policy, which will also entail a reduction in support for agriculture for all future and current farmers, following the new limits on the financing of the cap set by the european council in brussels on 25 october. that is why i think the european union should take seriously the proposal for reform of the cap recently put forward by its commissioner for agriculture, in order to prepare a better future for farmers, those of the present union and those of the future union.
in writing. - (pt) the best way to combat the concentration of media at european level is not to create a european internal market. the increasing concentration and domination of the media by large economic groups also stems from the recent disengagement of public entities from responsibility by liberalizing and privatizing the media. the current situation in portugal is an example of this. i regret that the resolution does not include a specific defence of the role of the public service and the need to ensure the maintenance of publicly funded television stations, as proposed in my group's resolution. the best way to contribute to the guarantee of democracy and pluralism is to defend a strong, diversified and modern public sector, i.e. public ownership in the field of communication, through the existence of mechanisms and instruments of effective democratic control. it is also a strategic sector for ensuring the sovereignty of a country.
in writing. - (pt) although the report follows in the footsteps of previous reports, we are now faced with a new situation of discriminatory and unacceptable conditions imposed on the applicant countries by the extraordinary council in brussels on 25 october, following the franco-german agreement. thus, not only have the financial resources foreseen for only six candidate countries in agenda 2000 not increased, despite the acceptance of the accession of ten countries, but they have also gone from eur 25 billion in the commission's proposal to only eur 23 billion in structural policy. moreover, the cap is claiming to distribute among 25 members what 15 countries are currently distributing, without even changing the current inequalities in distribution, by providing only 25% of direct aid for the first year and excluding small farmers, which we reject. the council's proposals have not met the expectations raised and may even further alienate the people from the european union project. but referendums will not be held in the candidate countries until next year. only then will we really know the will and the wish of the peoples. we will continue to fight for effective economic and social cohesion, which the current conditions of accession do not guarantee, against discriminatory proposals and for the rights and interests of portugal, which may be seriously jeopardised if the current proposals are not reviewed.
in writing. - (pt) i voted for the brok report, but against paragraph 31 for two reasons: (1) since portugal did not take part in the second world war, it would not make sense for my country to sign the joint 'european declaration' provided for in that paragraph. we cannot acknowledge crimes and atrocities against humanity that we have fortunately not committed or allowed to happen; 2. the there is no mention of the crimes and atrocities committed by the soviet communist regime in many candidate countries after the second world war, whose enlargement today represents the final liberation. history cannot have two standards, two standards, for facts which deserve to be condemned with equal force, both from the ethical and moral point of view.
in writing. - (pt) despite differences on certain points, i voted for the brok report in line with the support that the cds/pp has always given to the current process of enlargement of the european union. as we have repeatedly said, in the debate between enlargement and the so-called 'deepening' of the eu, the priority should - and must - always be enlargement. we believe that maastricht was a historic mistake of timing and that at the time, with the fall of the berlin wall just a few years ago, european leaders should have had the political vision to prioritise enlargement rather than launching political union and emu. many of the additional difficulties that enlargement is unfortunately facing today are actually the result of this mistiming. in the same vein, i regret that the brok report does not affirm and recommend a serious basic commitment to the new member states: the last revision of the treaties before their accession took place in nice; no new revision of the treaties will take place at least until the first contingent of new member states can participate in it, as a matter of right and after sufficient time for reflection and maturation.
in writing. - (pt) i congratulate our colleague margie sudre on her report on the renewal of the maritime concession, which allows partial or total exemptions from this tax for local production in the french overseas departments. as i have argued both in my plenary speeches and in my explanations of vote, there can be no doubt and there must be no doubt about the interest in maintaining instruments of aid to the outermost regions under the recent article 299 (2) of the ec treaty. i therefore fully support the proposal to extend the validity of the scheme in question until 31 december 2003, a prolongation justified by the persistence of the handicaps of these territories and the need to adopt measures to compensate for these factors.
in writing. - (pt) several factors have contributed to the problem of crossborder corruption. a common, integrated, flexible strategy is urgently needed to introduce more transparency, less bureaucratization, more simplification of standards and effective control mechanisms, aimed at preventing the emergence of ideal conditions for the proliferation of corruption and increasing the possibilities of detecting and punishing such practices. corruption must be combated both in the public sector, where it poses a risk to the functioning of the democratic system and to citizens' confidence in the integrity of the democratic rule of law, and in the private sector, where it is liable to distort competition rules and to hinder sound economic development. i regret that this proposal for a framework decision is only aimed at regulating the private sector and not the public sector, especially in view of the lack of harmonisation of national legislation (4 member states have not yet ratified the eu convention on corruption in the public sector), which can give rise to situations of objective legal uncertainty. furthermore, if we approach the regulations on the fight against corruption in the private sector (the subject of this framework directive) and corruption in the public sector (the subject of the 1997 eu convention) differently, we risk also creating difficulties in the application of the framework decision on the european arrest warrant. i therefore call on the commission to come forward with a proposal to regulate these two types of crime.
in writing. - (pt) in this matter, our vote was determined by the position, which we considered to be well founded, of the committee on legal affairs and the internal market. while it is necessary to adopt a legislative act governing the types of offence in question (corruption in the public sector and corruption in the private sector), this issue requires further analysis in order to avoid the adoption of vague and indeterminate concepts, disregard for the principle of subsidiarity, the risk of hindering fundamental freedoms or distortions of competition, or the disparity of legal approaches between the two types of offence. that is, in short, why i voted against this report.
in writing. - (pt) although i agree with the rapporteur on virtually all points and share his concerns, i voted against his report because i believe, along with the legal affairs committee, that the proposed text infringes the principle of subsidiarity and because i believe, especially at a time when the convention is discussing this issue in the context of the future of the european union and is trying to clarify the unequivocal division of competences between the union and the member states, that it is the responsibility of all community institutions - and especially our parliament - to show scrupulous respect for this basic principle. in addition, like the legal affairs committee, i believe that legislation is needed to regulate both types of crime - corruption in the public and private sectors - and to establish a more coherent and effective regulatory framework. the european commission is also preparing to present an initiative in this respect in the form of a communication. the committee considers that this report provides a good starting point, particularly as regards the need for coordinated action by the european structures responsible for promoting cooperation between member states in the fight against corruption (eurojust, europol, olaf and the european judicial network), both public and private. coordinated action by these structures should strengthen the action of national police forces and facilitate transnational investigations on the ground by sharing the information capacities of the member states.
in writing. - (pt) once again, we are faced with an initiative by a member state which is lacking in boldness and ambition, which does not lead to the normative transformation that would be desirable and necessary in this area. the amendments tabled by the rapporteur, mr ribeiro e castro, improve the original proposal. the effective fight against crossborder crime requires particular attention, and cooperation on the posting of liaison officers to third countries and international organisations needs to be strengthened. while cooperation already exists that contributes to the prevention, detection and investigation of crime, it is essential to strengthen certain aspects of this cooperation between liaison officers in order to optimise member states' resources, enabling them to cover a larger part of the world. the fight against international crime must be a common struggle, based on the utmost cooperation and mutual trust between member states. it is essential that there are binding instruments at community level which establish common rules on the competence of liaison officers and data protection, which subject the lawfulness of this exchange of information to judicial review. the fact that there is a wide variety of standards creates enormous difficulties for effective cooperation between national and europol liaison officers and complicates judicial review. this is the reason for this proposal for a decision, which aims to update and improve cooperation in this area and to bind the member states legally, unlike what happened with the 1996 joint action.
in writing. - (pt) we were already opposed to the european commission's proposal and we are even more opposed to mr ribeiro e castro's report, because it accentuates certain negative aspects of the original proposal. for example: - the seriousness of the prospect of the existence of 'liaison officers ... seconded by the various member states' without any control by the police authorities; - 'cooperation' is not limited to the exchange of information, particularly on crime; it covers the prevention and investigation of criminal offences, which is unacceptable. since police cooperation within the european union is needed to deal with multinational crime, cooperation can be carried out through institutional, judicial and criminal channels without compromising national sovereignty. it would therefore be more important for the european institutions to be more concerned about the myriad of existing police organisations created under the auspices of maastricht and schengen, which operate without any democratic control or oversight and which seriously undermine the rights and freedoms of citizens and the very sovereignty of the member states.
in writing. - (pt) we note that the report was generally well received by members of all the political groups, all the amendments tabled by the rapporteur having been adopted, as well as those which he did not author but with which he agreed. only a new recital was adopted without its support. it was proposed by mrs buitenweg and aimed at creating binding instruments at eu level. the report reflects the prudence of the danish initiative, which respects the legal framework in force; we therefore feel that we must support it and congratulate its rapporteur, my colleague josé ribeiro e castro, on his progress.
in writing. - (pt) i voted for my report, of course. this initiative by the kingdom of denmark is not only extremely positive and timely, but it is also sufficiently balanced, since it fully respects the status quo of the institutional development of the union and the individual competences of the member states, by adopting now a binding normative instrument. by providing for common usage patterns, the overall capacity of liaison officers seconded to third countries by the law enforcement authorities of the member states is in fact enhanced, which obviously benefits all of them. this specific intensification of police cooperation is particularly important in the field of information and is probably becoming increasingly necessary in the fight against organised crime, especially in the face of the intensification of international crime. my amendments, which have received the support of the committee responsible, all aim to make cooperation mechanisms more effective and to make common use of liaison officers seconded by the police services in this area, while at the same time attempting to address certain shortcomings, particularly in the following areas: guaranteeing fundamental rights in the field of personal data protection; regular monitoring of the liaison officers system, through annual reports and their respective evaluation; identical treatment for europol liaison officers; and funding.
in writing. - (pt) the common fisheries policy is of paramount importance for portugal. the reform under way has extremely serious implications for my country and for the algarve. the implementation of this reform as it stands would seriously harm portuguese fishermen, both economically and industrially and socially. however, despite the consequences, i think everyone agrees that strengthening the fight against illegal fishing is a priority. this fishery does not allow for an efficient and sustainable management of fishery resources. that is why i support the busk report unequivocally, as well as all measures to eradicate illegal fishing. in this context, i agree to refuse access to european ports to vessels flying flags of convenience or engaging in illegal fishing. this is to prevent these products from reaching the european market. we cannot impose strict fishing quotas on our fishermen and remain passive in the face of wild fishing activities. i therefore support new control measures at community level, in particular through: - better marking and identification of vessels; - compliance with the rules, monitored by regional organisations; - deterrent and uniform sanctions. the commission will have to allocate appropriate financial resources to this fight.
in writing. - (pt) although i understand many of the rapporteur's aims to give this directive greater legal force - and thus avoid courts preventing its application - i believe that he ends up undermining the public health objectives of the directive. i find that intolerable. i therefore prefer the original position of the european commission to the outcome of the committee on legal affairs and the internal market. amendment no 26, which i support, aims to avoid this excessive weakening of the protection of public health.
in writing. - (pt) we know that the court of justice annulled directive 98/43/ec on the grounds that it had been adopted on the basis of an article of the treaty which prohibits harmonisation rules in the field of the protection of human health, its action being rather complementary to that decided by the eu member states. in fact, the general prohibition on advertising and sponsorship of tobacco products laid down in that directive exceeds the powers conferred by the treaty and even infringes the rules on freedom of movement which constitute the internal market. the new proposal for a directive therefore establishes, in terms already in line with the ec treaty, a less restrictive framework aimed at new objectives of public health protection, given that tobacco is a dangerous product for health and that it is important to discourage its consumption - especially by the youngest - and to curb its widespread promotion in the media, while respecting the rules of competition and the internal market. that is why i have joined this report.
in writing. - (pt) i congratulate mr di lello on the quality of his work. the citizens of the european union have the right to demand that the union address effectively the problem of the growing threat of crime, which knows no borders and which infringes on their freedoms and rights. it is necessary to take rapid, joint and concerted action which effectively coordinates all efforts to combat and prevent crime throughout the territory of the member states. confiscation of proceeds of crime is one of the most effective instruments in the fight against organised crime. however, there are difficulties due to the existence of disparities between the laws of the member states. the union should endeavour to bring criminal law and criminal procedure closer together in relation to money laundering and confiscation of property. the adoption of the framework decision on money laundering made progress, but proved insufficient because of the considerable remaining margins of criminal impunity due to the fact that it established criminal sanctions only for serious crimes. this matter was governed by two acts with different legal value, since the 1998 joint action was not revoked. this proposal for a framework decision is a positive step in ensuring that all member states have effective regulation, by advocating confiscation in the case of an offence punishable by a prison sentence of more than one year. i agree with the rapporteur's position on the proposals of the danish presidency on the possibility of extending confiscation and reversing the burden of proof. any legislative initiative must respect the individual rights and fundamental freedoms of citizens.
in writing. - (pt) as the rapporteur has pointed out, one of the most effective instruments in the fight against organised crime is the seizure and confiscation of the proceeds of illegal activities, especially when the objective is money laundering. the lack of coordination between crime fighting structures can only serve the interests of the people who are involved in the fight against crime, which is why it is important to improve mutual assistance in the fight against the proceeds of organised crime. both in these initiatives and on the part of the rapporteur, i believe there is a desire to establish a framework for action, delimited by framework decisions, to protect the necessary independence of national authorities in the specific handling of incidents occurring within their territory - while providing for common regulatory mechanisms for the continuation of the common commitment to fight all forms of organised crime, in line with the conclusions of the tampere council. furthermore, the rapporteur was careful to provide that, in the assessment of the unlawful origin of property, the inversion of the burden of proof applies only where the prosecution proves the disproportionate nature of the property owned to the declared income or activity of the suspect, who must then provide proof of the lawful origin of the property. that is why i voted for this report.
in writing. - (pt) the souchet report is very positive and deserves our support. on the one hand, it is concerned with the role it attributes to the participation of fishermen and their trade associations in research and information on the state of fisheries resources, given that fishermen share the concern to protect the environment, since they are among the main victims of its degradation. secondly, because i agree with the rapporteur that the pressure exerted by fishing on resources should not be tackled in a uniform and global way, but should take account of the specific characteristics of each fishing area and the specificities of the different fish species. the report rejects the use of rigid instruments such as ship-breaking, with irreversible consequences and significant socio-economic impacts, in favour of other, more flexible instruments for resource management. i support the maintenance of public aid for the modernisation of the fishing fleet, which has a direct impact on working conditions, safety and product quality, thus rejecting a philosophy which, under the guise of the environment, intends to challenge aid in what we could call a liberal 'greening'.
in writing. - (pt) i voted in favour of this report because i believe that it is the right approach to the problem of protecting the marine environment. as the rapporteur has said, decisions on fisheries management must be taken in close consultation with the fishermen, who are the main stakeholders and the most aware of the problem. they are of course motivated by the urgent need to ensure the sustainability of their activity. they are the main victims of damage to the marine environment. unlike the commission's approach, which focuses on a global and uniform reduction of the pressure exerted by fishing on resources, without taking into account the specificities of the different species and areas, the position adopted by the european council in santa maria de feira, which advocated a sustainable balance between resources and exploitation, should be addressed. in addition, we should not forget that the fisheries sector is directly exposed to the often enormous consequences of the most devastating marine pollution and, in particular, to accidents linked to the loading of dangerous products, as in the case of the very recent sinking of the prestige, on the west coast of galicia, a dramatic extension of the erika.
- madam president, let me get right to the point: although today's discussion should remain focused on the recent elections in pakistan, it is clearly a moment for evaluating the process of transition to democracy itself in this important country in south asia. it is also clear that, more than ever, international relations at all levels, including economic and trade, must be based on respect for commitments and careful management of expectations. we all welcome the support which the president of pakistan has given to the fight against terrorism, which has helped to dilute a strongly negative image created by the military coup which ousted the elected government led by nasual sharif. we also know that we cannot speak of a consolidated democracy while the military institution plays an intervening role in the political arena. but we also know from several examples in countries in southern europe and latin america that it is possible to start a process of transition to democracy agreed with the civil political representatives, provided that the different stages and the agreed modalities are respected. it is precisely the respect of these requirements that gives the military, during the transition to democracy, the necessary, though not sufficient, legitimacy for the continuation of a political role. i am not saying that we should be concerned about the future of the community. the president of pakistan in august 2001 announced a roadmap to democracy, which included elections on 10 october. it was a historic moment. we regret that this opportunity has been largely lost. we do not wish to pass any kind of judgment on the process of democratic transition in pakistan, but we can say that this was a bad way of promoting it and we expect the elected members to have the lucidity, the political conditions and the courage to lead a phase of real transition to democratic political power.

- mr president, it is well known that the proposal for a directive comes after the european employers' association blocked the negotiating process between the social partners. but while the proposal seeks to improve the working conditions of temporary workers by guaranteeing them equal rights and equal treatment, it only guarantees them through minimum standards of protection, based on the principle of non-discrimination with numerous derogations and a dangerous prospect of the development of temporary work enterprises, which contributes to the increase in the precariousness of employment. so during the debate in the committee on employment and social affairs we supported the rapporteur in all her efforts to improve the proposal for a directive and to guarantee workers' rights, and we put forward a number of amendments, including the abolition of a number of derogations which call into question the principle of non-discrimination, the guarantee that temporary workers should not be used to replace workers on strike and the principle that the work of temporary agency workers should not contribute to the increase in the precariousness of employment. we regret that most of the proposals were not accepted, although we welcome the acceptance of some important ideas, in particular on the restriction of derogations, the enshrining of the principle of non-discrimination from day one with the abolition of the six-week derogation, the guaranteeing of certain rights in the area of information and participation for temporary workers and the non-use of temporary workers to replace striking workers. but other articles are unfortunate, incorrectly worded and in some cases dangerous for workers, and they open the way to new derogations to be agreed between the social partners and seek to enshrine an active role for temporary employment agencies, contributing to greater flexibility in work. that is why we have tabled the proposals for a vote in the house. we want to help ensure that the european parliament's proposal actually aims at a significant improvement in the rights of temporary workers and does not open the way to greater precariousness in employment. our commitment is to the value of work, to the creation of more quality jobs with rights and not to the precariousness and devaluation of those who work. we therefore hope that you will support our proposals and reject all those which in practice will create more precariousness and harm the rights of temporary workers, who in most cases only resort to this type of work when they cannot find permanent employment. so we do not contribute to double discrimination.
- mr president, in the last 10 years, temporary work in the european union has been steadily increasing. this rapid growth is justified by the fact that temporary work makes it possible to respond better to the demands of flexibility and adaptation to economic reality. the objectives of the proposal under discussion are:- to ensure that temporary work is a factor in creating jobs that foster more stable and longer-lasting employment relationships;- to guarantee the rights and security of temporary workers;- and to improve flexibility in the labour market. its basic premise is to extend the principle of non-discrimination between temporary workers and other workers to the community level. the guarantee of minimum rights will make this sector more attractive and better benefit from its reputation, and will put an end to the prejudices that still exist. this report is balanced and represents a step forward in relation to the commission's proposal for a directive, and i thank the rapporteur and the shadow rapporteurs from my group for their contributions. this balance is reflected in particular in the definition of a community minimum level, leaving it to the member states and the social partners to adapt to the different national realities. other issues are being taken into account - and rightly so - such as the guarantee of basic protection, the possibility for women to reconcile family life with working life and the integration of young people and the least qualified into the labour market. i would point out that in portugal article 4 is the provision which may cause the greatest difficulties: the court has held that the national legislation in force imposes a restrictive regime on such work in order to ensure that it is used only for the specific needs of the employer.
the committee on budgets - mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i have the delicate task of presenting the report of mrs elisa damião, who has suffered from an illness which prevents her from being in this chamber, although she still has the intelligence and lucidity to have followed this report step by step and therefore this report bears her name with all legitimacy. i do so with emotion, i welcome the honourable member and i hope that she will be able to return to this house shortly, because she is a person of great political and parliamentary ability and a great citizen. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the breakthroughs in molecular biology and genetics are both fascinating and disturbing. scientific knowledge has increased at such a rate that we are in a position to profoundly modify not only the plant and animal world but, in time, our own species. it's a mutation, you know. the capabilities of science and technology have far surpassed the fictions contained in literary masterpieces such as huxley's brave new world or george orwell's 1984. however, the truth be told, fortunately the visions contained in those works of societies and human beings oppressed and dehumanized by the manipulation of science and technology have not been fulfilled. but the public's concern about, for example, genetically modified organisms and cloning is a very clear signal which must be interpreted by the political, scientific and industrial world. a first response was drawn up by unesco when it adopted by consensus in november 1997 a universal declaration on the human genome and human rights. but of all the revolutions in knowledge, molecular biology will perhaps be the greatest, and genetic technologies the most economically, culturally and ethically significant. biotechnology is the technology of the future. the potential development of these indicates that there will be major changes in the markets for medicines, food, agriculture and the environment in the coming years. however, these technologies, in their practical applications, raise ethical and philosophical questions which are now part of the culture of our time. and in the face of its implications, there will always be pessimistic spirits, as there have been throughout history, science and technology, who see apocalyptic threats to humanity, awakening ancient fears and fears. we must counter the exploiters of fears, ghosts and superstitions with a prudent but optimistic attitude towards science, aware that it must be inspired by high ethical values and legal frameworks which, by favouring intellectual freedom and scientific creativity, prevent over-reaching. classical humanism, as formulated in the famous phrase of the philosopher seneca, said: "man is a sacred thing to man". this classical humanism should inspire contemporary science and all citizens. the european union has developed over the past few years a range of directives and regulations in the field of biotechnology and science. however, the numerous legislative acts that have been adopted are a veritable patchwork of details, lacking a coherent, global and systemic vision of this vast sector. there are laws that have been passed but not applied, others that have been derogated from and even moratoriums on, and, worse still, there are numerous gaps. the world battle in the field of biotechnology is, however, decisive. the united states is clearly ahead. however, the lisbon summit set an ambitious goal for the eu for the next decade: to become the most competitive and dynamic knowledge-based economy in the world. how can this be achieved without a strong european commitment to biotechnology? europe is lagging behind the united states. while it is true that the european union has 1570 companies operating in this sector and the united states 1,263, the truth is that these companies employ 61 000 workers in the european union, producing a turnover of 42 billion euros, while in the united states 162 000 workers work in this same sector, producing the equivalent of 365 billion. the cultural, political, legal and economic context in which the commission's communication, which is widely supported by the report by mrs elisa damião, is set out in brief, mr president. the action plan and strategy presented by the commission in fact magically solve the dispersion, inconsistency and gaps in the european legal and policy framework in the field of biotechnologies. obviously they can't do it in this magic pass, but they point a way and they point in the right direction.
- mr president, in her absence i would like to greet mrs elisa damião and wish her a speedy recovery. but i disagree with your report, which has been tabled by our colleague carlos lage: the report fully reflects the positions and interests of the big biotechnology, agrochemical and agro-industrial multinationals, as can be seen by comparing the report with the position of unice. we are in favour of maintaining the de facto moratorium on the approval of new genetically modified organisms in the food chain, which has existed since 1998, and we categorically reject the report's support for lifting the moratorium, which we consider to be a real environmental crime. beyond the profound ethical and human rights issues related to the commodification and patenting of life and natural heritage, it must be borne in mind that the release of gmos into the environment is an irreversible and uncontrollable factor with direct consequences in the pollution of agricultural activity, both conventional and biological, as well as in the regression of biodiversity, and has incalculable effects at the level of ecosystems. this means that there will be no freedom of choice, so much touted and so false, for either organic or conventional farmers or consumers. it also increases the dependence of farmers and food policies on multinationals. the degree of interference and pressure exerted on developing countries to introduce gmos into their food chain is shocking. it is indeed mystifying and unacceptable to insist once again that the world's food shortages and hunger can be solved by technology when the issue - as has been stressed in various international forums and organisations - is political and political. just remember the much-celebrated "green revolution" of a few decades ago. we are committed to promoting the scientific and technological development of mankind, but we must always take into account the precautionary principle.
- madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the report under consideration, which is of excellent quality, emphasizes very clearly the importance of the european investment bank as an essential instrument for financing projects throughout the european union, but it also stresses the decisive role that this bank plays in the economies of the applicant countries, for which it also proposes the creation of a special programme on social policy. in this sense, the recently decided increase in share capital can be considered in line with and consistent with these structural objectives, which are so important for the achievement of a prosperous and solidly supported european global economy. the european investment bank is already responsible for more than 5% of investment in the union, creating more than 400 000 jobs, particularly through support for small and medium-sized enterprises. it is recalled that the european union has recently taken on increased responsibility for risk capital financing, especially for small and medium-sized enterprises, through the eib. unfortunately, as the rapporteur also points out, the european investment bank's efforts are sometimes limited by the orthodox and rigid rules for consolidating public finances required in the framework of the implementation of the stability and growth pact. it is important to remember that much of the eib's vocation is structural investment, such as infrastructure or risk capital for small and medium-sized enterprises, and that the success of such investments is often dependent on the possibility of mobilising in partnership with public spending. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the financial perspective for the period 2000 to 2006 is a very good one. it is now becoming increasingly clear that europe has an objective which is at the same time an unavoidable necessity: to become a highly competitive area and thus to create wealth and harmonize its distribution. this objective is only possible with support for structural investment and therefore no restrictions are tolerated which could jeopardise such investment. the quality of public and private spending should be the guiding framework for investment policy and the european investment bank has an irreplaceable role to play in this context. i congratulate the rapporteur on the content of her report and on her interpretation of these aspirations.

in writing. - (pt) the aim of this report was to propose an interinstitutional agreement on the financing of the european union solidarity fund and the conditions governing the use of this fund. the interinstitutional agreement in question involves the commission, the european parliament and the council; it is intended to lay down the technical arrangements for mobilising the revenue necessary for the fund. under the terms of the agreement, an accelerated procedure is provided for, which guarantees the necessary financial flexibility and the rapidity of the fund's intervention, from the 2002 budget year onwards and until 2006, i.e. within the timeframe of the current financial perspective. the recent disaster of the oil tanker prestige, which affected the galician coast and also threatened the northern coast of portugal with the crude oil that was leaking, is one of the typical examples of the efficient and rapid use of this solidarity fund, which of course led me to vote unreservedly for this proposal.
in writing. - (pt) although the court of justice of the european communities annulled the directive of 14 may 2001 on the advertising and sponsorship of tobacco products on the grounds that it exceeded the community's powers, we note that the report under examination contains a set of non-binding recommendations which focus on reducing tobacco consumption through consumer awareness. it also presents a series of proposals to discourage smoking, particularly among children and adolescents, who of course deserve our support. in defending this position, we would like to stress once again that the european parliament's concern for public health must not interfere with the powers of national legislators and, in particular, with the protection measures and the search for economic alternatives that must be found for all those who are economically dependent on tobacco production.
in writing. - (pt) i decided to support this report first of all because of the important impact of the european commission's proposal on the quality of products offered to the final consumer. in fact, the purpose of this proposal is to regulate additives in animal feedingstuffs by clarifying existing standards and thus the procedures for authorizing their marketing. it considers that strict rules on the quality of additives used in feedingstuffs intended for human consumption should be promoted. this will help to prevent future 'mad cow' outbreaks by sorting, identifying and eliminating potentially dangerous components from the food chain and the commercial circuit. it will also allow for upstream control, both through the obligation for companies to prove the effectiveness of the product and the absence of risks to human health, animal health and the environment, and through the definition of conditions for the use and marketing of additives.
in writing. - (pt) the use of medicinal plants dates back to the beginning of time. while recognising the predominant role of modern pharmacology, it must be recognised that medicinal plants continue to have their place in medicine and that their therapeutic potential is likely to be exploited. we therefore welcome the commission's proposal to set up an autonomous committee on herbal medicinal products, separate from the committee on proprietary medicinal products, which will be responsible for regulating and monitoring all aspects of herbal medicinal products. however, it is essential to distinguish between medicinal products, even those based on plants, and food or cosmetic products, and to ensure appropriate time limits for the herbal products industry to adapt to the new legislative reality. we rely on the national authorities to do this and that is why we voted in favour of this report.
in writing. - (pt) the main reasons for contracting temporary workers and temporary employment agencies are related to the need to cope with temporary fluctuations in demand. this is why statistical information shows that assignments within user firms are normally of short duration (between 1 and 6 months). in the specific case of portugal, there is specific legislation on this type of work, which guarantees the essential rights of these workers, while respecting the special conditions governing the use of temporary agency work. even if the commission considers its proposal to be sufficiently flexible, the truth is that the attempt to include in this report the regulation of various basic conditions of temporary agency work is, in my view, beyond the competence of the community and has no precedent in european legislation; it is therefore contrary to the principle of subsidiarity. in short, that is why i voted against it.
in writing. - (pt) i voted against this report because, through various amendments to the commission's original text, a framework was created which is clearly counterproductive to the interests it is supposed to defend. as the rapporteur admits, the various social partners have never been able to reach agreement on certain aspects. furthermore, aligning the legal relations of temporary work with those of permanent employment will eventually make it, in a short time, an unsustainable solution for companies and, therefore, a less viable opportunity for many workers. we are not and will never - we insist on this - apply different provisions on the safety, health and social rights of temporary workers - protection which already exists in various regulatory instruments. it would have been better to have checked in statistical terms the use of temporary work in europe (which successive european councils have seen as another instrument to combat unemployment) and to have suggested that limits be imposed on its use, by fighting not temporary work but the unacceptable abuses.
in writing. - (pt) like all compromise texts, this joint motion for a resolution includes very positive recitals and conclusions (references to the kaliningrad agreement, for example) as well as a dubious and weak discourse of rejection of terrorist acts committed in russia, in particular the recent attack on a theatre in moscow by chechen extremists. we believe that the fight against terrorism must be constant and unwavering, because it is a cowardly and anonymous form of terrorism which threatens the lives and security of defenceless citizens, who do not admit any justification or any less firm support for the authorities who are fighting it. that is the only reason why i voted against the motion for a resolution under consideration.
in writing. - (pt) this report is based on certain facts and on a fundamental ambiguity. the evidence is what we see in the media: the worrying and unprecedented degree of instability in the world's financial markets and its negative impact on the so-called 'real' economy, with the emergence of financial crises that have turned into economic crises due to the degree of 'financialisation' of the economy, to which the gradual integration of pension systems into private profit-making logic and the growing volume of pension funds have contributed greatly. the misconception is that the problem of financial market volatility will be solved by integrating eu financial markets and establishing minimum prudential rules, relying on new supranational bodies and strengthening the commission's powers, when the central issue is the 'free' movement of capital at international level and its speculative and parasitic use, with huge financial volumes concentrated in pension and speculative investment funds, which are often established in tax havens and which use instruments that encourage speculation, such as derivatives. in addition, the fragmentation of financial markets can be a positive factor in circumventing the risks of financial globalisation by maintaining more localised supervisory regimes and more flexible markets better adapted to local specificities.
in writing. - (pt) we voted against the report for the following reasons: - it insists on accelerating the liberalisation of energy, transport and postal markets and regrets the lack of progress in the effective liberalisation of gas and electricity, which we reject; - we reject its position on the takeover of private electricity companies by public companies in liberalised or liberalised sectors, which it intends to challenge; - we do not agree with its proposal to reduce state aid and its approach to state funding of private providers of services of general interest; - it does not take into account the social consequences of mergers, including employment, or the loss of a dominant position, neglecting the participation of workers in the decision-making process; - we do not agree with its support for the policy of co-decision in competition matters.
in writing. - (pt) i congratulate our colleague mr lipietz on his excellent report on the commission's xxxi report on competition policy 2001, which i fully support, particularly as regards ensuring constant and rigorous monitoring to avoid serious distortions of competition and the need to create a specialised body for competition and state aid at the judicial level for the rapid handling of proceedings in these matters. while i generally agree with the rapporteur's position of supporting the commission's commitment to reduce state aid in the community, i would nevertheless like to stress that there are still european regions, particularly the outermost regions, for which state aid is vital in supporting economic and social development. this was expressly recognised in article 299 (2) of the treaty of amsterdam. in these specific cases, a certain flexibility will have to be maintained in the admission and authorisation of aid which contributes effectively and justifiably to the development of these particular territories and economies.
in writing. - (pt) i supported this report, which is about the commission's assessment of competition policy in 2001, because i believe it is a balanced and constructive criticism. as the rapporteur says, it must be admitted that the commission is pursuing its objective successfully, by applying decisively the instruments at its disposal and thus ensuring the proper protection of consumers and their best interests. the decrease in the number of cases examined and, at the same time, the explosion in the level of fines imposed objectively contribute to this conclusion. in the face of the astonishing speed of technological progress, increasing globalisation and a european economy characterised by a high rate of mergers, a rigorous competition policy and scrupulous monitoring by the commission are essential. this policy is essential to promote confidence and to ensure the proper protection of economic operators, in order to avoid concerted practices, abuse of dominant positions and other distortions of competition. i also particularly welcome the rapporteur's proposal to give top priority to policies with horizontal aid to help industry meet the kyoto commitments. the future of a european economy which is always wanted to be 'more dynamic and competitive' but ecologically balanced depends on this.
in writing. - (pt) i congratulate our colleague, mr philippe herzog, on his excellent report on the 2001 state aid scoreboard (updated in spring 2002), presented by the commission, which i fully support. in the context of competition policy, the publication of the half-yearly aid scoreboard since 2001 has been an important instrument for transparency and debate. however, i too believe that the data in this scoreboard are not yet sufficient and appropriate to allow a comparison to be made between the behaviour of the different member states and the actual benefits of state aid. it is desirable that this should be possible in the short term, as only in this way can a comprehensive and informed analysis be made in order to decide whether to reduce the level of aid granted and/or to reorient it towards horizontal objectives of common interest, such as research and development, the environment, smes, training and employment.
in writing. - (pt) in stockholm in june 2001, the european council set itself the objective of reducing the overall weight of state aid in relation to gdp by 2003, especially the weight of ad hoc and sectoral aid. the barcelona council (march 2002) confirmed this orientation and called for the reorientation of such aid towards horizontal objectives of common interest, such as economic and social cohesion; it weighs them against the market segments which are recognised as being loss-making. considers that state aid is a necessary flexibility mechanism to correct asymmetries and deal with special difficulties in the context of european competition law. the legitimacy of the use of such aid cannot therefore be called into question, although it must be moderate and justified. in the concrete circumstances which have shaped this report, i welcome the clear reduction in the amount of state aid granted and its reorientation towards horizontal objectives - smes, environment, research and development, employment and training - which are outside the narrow framework of economic efficiency, but rather favour better preparation of economic agents and member states in the context of the internal market. finally, i do not agree with the rapporteur's request to extend the codecision procedure to this area. i welcome the amendments adopted in various special votes which significantly improve the final text of the resolution - even if they do not please the rapporteur.
- mr president, commissioner, i would first like to congratulate the european parliament delegation to the new delhi conference and in particular my colleague mrs cristina garcia-orcoyen tormo, who led this delegation. i regret that once again the commission and the council have not allowed the parliament's delegation to participate in the meetings of the european union's coordination areas. we knew, even before the start of this conference, that the result would be extremely difficult to achieve: the conference was intended to prepare the second period of the kyoto protocol, when politically the priority, unfortunately, remains to ensure that the kyoto protocol enters into force so that the first period can begin. and while it is regrettable that no result has been achieved, it is nevertheless worth noting that the definition of the clean development mechanism and the procedures for monitoring emissions have been improved. what i find most serious is not so much the lack of results; it is rather what politically constituted the attempt to dilute the kyoto protocol. there was a clear attempt by some delegations, notably the us delegation, to push back the debate on climate change in 1997 to a time when there were neither targets nor protocols for action. and it is a pity that the developing countries have fallen into this trap. the consequences of climate change will be particularly severe in developing countries. the choice for us remains clear: firstly, the kyoto protocol is the only instrument capable of mitigating climate change. secondly, the second period of the protocol has to meet even more ambitious targets than the first period (the first period is a baby step). and thirdly, action must begin in the industrialized countries and only later in the developing countries.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the prestige was a disaster in the making. this is what makes it particularly intolerable and makes it more difficult for us to respond to the concerns, anxieties and despair of the people of galicia and minho in northern portugal. it is announced because we know the enormous vulnerability of our coasts, in particular those of brittany, galicia and the portuguese sea, both continental and adjacent to madeira and the azores, all of which are avenues and crossroads of intense sea routes. it is also announced because we know the intolerable conditions in which many ships with dangerous and highly polluting cargoes continue to sail, while we hesitate and are slow at community and world level in adopting strict and unavoidable measures of prevention and guidance, of prohibition of navigation, of trapping and of killing. it is announced, finally, in this particular case, because it was clear that the prestige would eventually break and sink, leaving the question whether the measures taken since the declaration of the emergency a week ago were correct and sufficient. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's fishing fleet is not affected by the oil spills, and that the community's fishing fleet is not affected by the oil spills. we have to give three kinds of answers: to the present, to the future and to the past. the response to this must be based on four pillars: mobilizing all means to control and reduce the effects of pollution; rigorously investigating the emerging risks of the ship sinking with 60 000 tonnes of fuel in its tanks and keeping them under strict control; making those responsible pay; reaching out with concern to the frightened and anxious populations, providing them with all the necessary support, and reviving the local economy. then answer to the future: the commission has already taken a number of initiatives to improve the safety of ships, including the establishment of a european maritime safety agency (i would like to see this in portugal) and to increase the means of port and coastal surveillance, but above all we must follow the position of commissioner de palácio. commissioner, do not hesitate to go ahead and do it. and parliament and the council must be able to meet the demands of the people. finally, we must respond to the past: preventing future disasters of this magnitude and limiting the damage caused by them requires that all the facts that have occurred and the way in which they have been dealt with be objectively and rigorously investigated and established, so that we can technically assess whether they were right or wrong. we must ensure that, in the face of any similar emergency in the future, all the competent authorities will be able to respond promptly and courageously to the most effective responses in terms of anticipating real environmental damage and risks and thus limiting and controlling them as effectively as possible.

- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the reports under consideration are well structured, they are well reasoned, well argued, they are overall positive, as mrs ilda figueiredo said, but i have a few remarks to make. i believe that the golden rule of a fisheries policy is the conservation of resources, and the fundamental priority must be the conservation of resources and marine life, because, using a culinary metaphor, i will say that just as you cannot make omelets without eggs, you cannot make fish without fish. the problem arose long ago, when, in the 1600s, a famous jurist, mr grotius, wrote a book entitled mare liberum, in which he defended the principle of freedom of navigation on the seas, which later became a principle of international law. some legal experts added that this also implied freedom of fishing, which could also lead to depletion of resources. you see how this problem is an old problem. in other words, the freedom to fish must be conditioned by the marine resources. and defending marine resources is not just about ensuring the future of fisheries, it is also about defending a biological value, a fundamental value of humanity and life on earth. and so we cannot make inherently contradictory speeches, in which we advocate one thing and its opposite; speeches in which on the one hand we advocate the preservation of resources, but immediately afterwards we want to fish practically without limits and without conditions. with that said, i would like to address a word to commissioner franz fischler: as mrs rosa miguélez said, what impresses mr fischler is a certain indifference to the fate of fishermen and fishing communities, his lack of emotion. and indeed, there is a need for solidarity and understanding with the fishermen, particularly those who, as has already been said, have now suffered the consequences of a terrible and criminal shipwreck.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i represent a country where fishing and the sea are intertwined with its history and where the fishing industry still represents some 50 000 jobs. it is in this context that the commission's proposals merit some fundamental criticisms. as they are formulated, they are unacceptable to us. the first criticism is that it is completely unacceptable to end support for modernisation. as 40% of the portuguese fleet is over 25 years old, this would mean that the overwhelming majority of portuguese fishermen would be permanently marginalized and unable to undertake any modernization. the second criticism is that the prohibition of aid for the setting up of joint ventures for fishing outside community waters only serves to increase our imports from third countries; the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community fleet is not deprived of its fishing opportunities. finally, it is necessary to take account of the effort already made by several countries, in this case portugal, in the context of the multiannual fisheries guidance plans, in which the effort already made to reduce the fleet should now be considered and deducted. finally, commissioner, i would also like to say that other issues have not been properly considered. for example, if there is a resource programme, why continue to allow fishing for fishmeal? and why, for example, have there been no specific measures for the outermost regions where fisheries ecosystems are highly sensitive? it will be essential in this respect to find a specific solution for these regions, which in the portuguese case concern madeira and the azores. i hope that the european parliament's opinion will serve to re-examine what has not been considered and that the council will be able to reach a reasonable agreement.

the commission has already said the essential points. the report approves the outcome of the negotiations between the commission and angola, it even welcomes the way in which the negotiations have ended, and the protocol offers interesting fishing opportunities for the fleets of the member states and is now, in my opinion, second in importance after the agreement already signed with mauritania. the new protocol is the ninth since the fisheries agreement between the european community and angola entered into force in 1997. the new protocol applies for the period from august 2002 to august 2004, offers access to 33 tuna vessels and 22 shrimp vessels, tuna fishing quotas are shared between france, spain and portugal (note that the previous protocol offered access to 43 european tuna vessels, slightly more than the current one). as regards demersal fisheries, spain receives the lion's share, portugal, italy and greece share the rest. the annual financial contribution has increased from 14 million to 15.5 million, which is commendable; the european union is making a contribution to the development of fisheries in angola. the commission has already said that it is right that €5.5 billion per year should be allocated to specific measures for research, monitoring and control. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, i would not like to end this brief speech without mentioning the fact that angola is a terrible and shocking paradox: angola is a huge territory, with a population of about 13 million, and it has truly fabulous underground resources, such as oil and diamonds. suffice it to say that oil, whose production is now set to increase over the next two years, would represent something like 15% of the consumption of the north american states - but the bulk of that money is not going to the people in angola who are living through a terrible tragedy of hunger and misery. a report by the international monetary fund even goes so far as to say that 4 billion euros have been diverted from the state accounts and have therefore followed a tortuous and tortuous path. does this mean that the european union should not support angola? i'm not going to. it means that angola has entered a new phase of its history, so troubled by civil wars, death and destruction. and the angolan fishing industry is very interesting. i remember, although it is always a risk to resort to memory, that in 1973, when portugal was still a colonial administration, some 490 000 tonnes of fish were landed in angolan ports - i read it in a book on the history of angola, on the angolan economy. in 1999, some 190 000 tonnes of fish were landed. this means that angolan fisheries have fallen sharply and are now recovering, partly because they have ceased to be a state industry and have become essentially private. but it also means, if the scientific data do not contradict me, that there are still many conditions for fishing in angola and that angolan waters are rich in fish. the texts also say that angolan fisheries account for 3% of gross domestic product, so any effort to support the angolan fishing economy is a positive one. i would therefore like to congratulate the commissioner and the commission on the result achieved. - madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by congratulating our rapporteur, mr lage, on this very well-structured report, which is very clear in its proposals. as he said, this is another renewal of the fisheries agreement with angola which began in 1987 and now has another one of its renewals. as has also been mentioned, european union vessels fish in these waters for tuna, shrimp, demersal species and, on an experimental basis, some pelagic fish. the new protocol is notable for a slight increase in the fishing of demersal and a clear decrease in the fishing of tuna, which is of concern, especially since it is known that for some years now, with all the tuna migration, there has been a great decrease and largely due to what we call iuu, that is, illegal, unregulated and unreported fisheries. so there's a lot of illegal tuna fishing here, especially in tropical waters, in the gulf of guinea, for example, where the tuna is killed and there's not even time for it to reproduce. and perhaps this reduction in the tuna fishing quota in the agreement with angola will not be unrelated to the situation of illegal tuna fishing in all tropical waters. it should also be noted that in addition to the financial compensation from the european union, which is also increasing, the fees paid by the shipowners must also be taken into account. the european union's compensation, as is also clearly stated in the report, has increased from eur 14 million to eur 15.5 million, and it is important to note that 35% of this money is specifically earmarked for the development of the fisheries sector. i think it is also important to mention that this agreement with angola represents a progressive development in the field of fisheries agreements with third countries. this considerable allocation of funds to the fisheries sector is important and shows that it is possible to make fisheries trade agreements with a development component. i think that there must be no prejudice on our part, that is to say, fisheries agreements must be an integral part of the common fisheries policy: the commission has already taken a number of initiatives in this area, including the establishment of a european centre for the development of vocational training. finally, i think it would also be reasonable, in connection with the reform of the cfp, that vessels shot down in the european union could become part of the mixed companies fishing in these waters. i would also like to say on behalf of my group, the epp, that we will support this report, which we consider to be very well founded.
