- mr prodi, we naturally listened to your speech with great attention and we took positive aspects out of it and others from which we distanced ourselves. it's normal and welcome in a democracy. what is important to stress is that we are looking at a significant contribution to the definition of the europe of the future, which, together with the others, will enable the convention to continue its work towards a consensual and politically realistic result, the only way we can hope to have some strength and influence at the future intergovernmental conference. in addition to the various proposals and concrete solutions which we will discuss at the convention and which naturally reflect the commission's position and its intention to strengthen internal power within the existing institutional framework, we have taken from your words above all the concern to answer a central question. the fundamental question facing us all, including the commission, is how to maintain the capacity of the enlarged european union to pursue its fundamental objectives of development and economic and social cohesion, as well as freedom and security for its citizens, the citizens of the present and future member states. the construction of europe is, we must admit, an increasingly complex process, not only because of its internal dynamics, which are subject to the natural conflict between national sovereignty and the aim of consolidating european sovereignty, but also because it is taking place in an international environment increasingly subject to the consequences and dangers of globalisation, the threat of terrorism, violent conflicts almost everywhere, financial, environmental and energy crises, unemployment, exclusion and poverty. however, we must recognize that in the convention all the questions have been put openly, frankly and honestly. there can be no closed questions here, nor should there be any barriers to debate. the people of the present and future member states demand and deserve it. the fact is that we are increasing our confidence in the convention because, in addition to the traditional community institutions, it also includes national institutions, i.e. the governments and parliaments of the member states and the applicant countries. without wanting to define two sides, because by force of circumstances everyone has been mixed up, and well, within the convention, the fact is that this national component is an added value for the debate. why would you do that? because there is not just one european public opinion, but there are 15, and soon 25, and then even more national public opinions, and it is to them that we must turn because they have the capacity to influence the direction of europe. but let's be specific. what's the matter? politically, it is a question of questioning, debating and defining 'things' as simple as the democratic legitimacy and the governance of the european union; the objectives and place of the union in the world, the distribution and definition of the union's competences and their alignment with the acquis communautaire, the simplification of the union's instruments, its democracy, transparency and effectiveness, the role of national parliaments, the effectiveness of the decisionmaking process and the functioning of the institutions in an enlarged union, and the drafting or not of a european constitution are also at stake. so everything is at stake and fortunately that is how the debates should be. so the first question we should ask is this: europe that we want to be built? and for that we must politically support this european model. each of us has the responsibility and the duty to do so. the commission has done so again today, and we can say that we have confidence, that we continue to have confidence in the nations and that is why we have confidence in europe. we believe in the nation state, and that is why we believe in a europe founded on the freedom of decision of its nations and based on the legal equality of the states. we trust in its people, and that is why we believe in a europe which means more democracy and not the reverse, namely more bureaucracy. we believe in diversity, and that is why we believe in a europe which respects the traditions of the states, preserves their cultural heritage and safeguards their respective languages. we believe in subsidiarity, and that is why we believe in a europe which does not claim to take over decisions which can be taken by the states with advantage. we believe in solidarity, and that is why we believe in a europe in which economic and social cohesion equally translates into prosperity for the peoples of the peripheries and the outermost regions without exception. we believe in peace, stability and security, and that is why we believe in a europe united but not uniform, mobilized for great causes, governed by values and guided by principles. i would like to conclude by saying that the natural addressee of the commission's contribution under discussion here is the convention. the convention is now entering the decisive phase of its work, and we hope that the proposals it will present, which we hope will be politically realistic and consensual, will fulfil its mission of enabling the heads of state and government present at the next intergovernmental conference to take the best decisions on behalf of the member states and the peoples they represent. that's our duty.
in writing. - (pt) the rapporteur accepts the basis of the commission's proposal to adapt the current regulation 2081/92 to bring it into line with the agreement on trade-related aspects of property rights (trips agreement, 1994), in the context of the wto. in its opinion on protected designations of origin (pdos) and protected geographical indications (pgi), the report, after supporting the commission's position, which it considers essential for a quality food policy and for combating any attempt to create confusion in the minds of consumers, omits some contradictions and makes amendments which raise certain doubts. for example, as regards the procedure for cancelling a pdo or pgi from the community register, the report forgets that sometimes a pdo or pgi is not only private property, but may also be the property of a particular community or region, given the characteristics of production and raw material. a survey of local institutional bodies on this situation should therefore be provided for. the relationship between the mark and the pdo must also be taken into account in order to protect against the possible off-selling of a mark with regional characteristics which cannot be produced in another region or country. in portugal, this problem has already arisen with limiano cheese. that's why we abstained.
in writing. - (pt) since 1993, the eu has established a european system for the protection of designations of origin (pdo) and geographical indications (gis) for agricultural products and foodstuffs. this mechanism has helped to boost the agri-food sector and to preserve traditional production methods, offering high quality products to consumers. i welcome the commission's proposal to adapt this system, especially as regards the continuous monitoring of counterfeiting and illegal imitation, both within the eu and in third countries. an important measure would be to impose the obligation to pack the product in its own production area, as a guarantee of control as to origin and quality. the current control mechanism must be effective, because it is necessary to harmonize existing systems, coordinate the activities of national and european bodies, and give greater responsibility to the member states. i also maintain that the proprietor of a trade mark must be able, subject to certain conditions, to have a right to register the product of his trade mark under a pdo or a pgi. similarly, the procedure for registering products must be simplified, accessible and allow for greater analytical power of the member states, limiting the european commission's evaluation to a simple formal control.
in writing. - (pt) in a few words, i would like to say, as does the rapporteur, how i sincerely hope that, thanks to the new framework which makes it possible to hope for a lasting peace in angola, the fishing industry, particularly through instruments agreed with the ec, will be able to contribute to the development of the economy of a country which has suffered so much from a long and devastating civil war since its independence in 1975. this is the political salient feature which marks the difference between this implementing protocol - valid for the period from 3 august 2002 to 2 august 2004 - and the eight previous protocols which have followed each other since the 1987 agreement. it is also an important instrument for portuguese shipowners - as well as for spanish, french, italian, greek and irish shipowners. it is regrettable that the commission has not respected the allocation key of the previous agreement and has penalised portugal by 20 tonnes. finally, it is worth noting the significant increase in the financial contribution granted and, in connection with this, not only the corresponding increase in fishing opportunities, but also the establishment of a partnership with angola for the responsible and sustainable development of fisheries, including the development of scientific research, monitoring, artisanal fishing, training and aquaculture. the practical effects of these developments will have to be monitored in the future.
in writing. - (pt) i voted in favour of this report, which stresses the need to streamline and synchronise the procedures for coordinating economic, social and employment policies, not forgetting the environmental dimension, so as to give them a medium- and long-term perspective. the emphasis on the implementation of measures rather than on the development of guidelines seems to be a more appropriate criterion. this analysis of the implementation of the guidelines, which will take the form of a new implementation package, will involve a systematic exchange of information by the member states as well as a specific timetable. consideration will need to be given to the appropriate commitment of all the institutions and all the partners concerned (council, european parliament and social partners). the importance of strengthening the democratic legitimacy of this process and of giving parliament powers of participation and co-decision throughout the policy coordination cycle should be emphasised. in this respect, we hope that the convention will propose this improvement in the new treaty. finally, i stress the need to strengthen the role of the social partners through the creation of a tripartite social summit and a macroeconomic social dialogue, and the participation of the candidate countries in the implementation of the lisbon strategy.
in writing. - (pt) the commission communication on which this report is based follows up the request of the council, expressed at the barcelona summit in the spring, for the relevant procedures for policy coordination to be streamlined and the implementation of the 'lisbon strategy' to be strengthened. it is around this issue that the rapporteur seeks to improve a little the inadmissible position of the european commission, which systematically forgets the social and environmental aspects. however, these improvements are clearly not enough. the proposals we made on the need to change the objectives, criteria and foundations of the stability pact were rejected. we have been working on the principle that the budgets of the member states must reflect the needs of each people and each country, not the interests of the financial groups which the european central bank seeks to express. the conditions for giving priority to employment, economic and social cohesion and the fight against social exclusion, which are essential to combat the domination of neoliberal policies within the european union, have not been guaranteed. so, despite some of the positive aspects, we voted against this report.
in writing. - (pt) in the context of work, noise can cause fatigue, communication difficulties and total loss of hearing sensitivity. i support this report because it sets out in a balanced way a set of requirements to protect workers against the risks arising from exposure to noise by setting exposure limit values. the application of this directive will thus lead to a significant reduction in existing noise levels and, consequently, to an improvement in working conditions. the current problems in this area could be largely reduced or avoided by the action of employers in assessing and improving the noise levels to which workers are exposed. this is without neglecting the adoption of preventive measures from the design stage of workstations and workplaces, through to the improvement of their layout and the provision of appropriate work equipment. finally, workers must be made aware of and trained to use the equipment correctly.
in writing. - (pt) i voted in favour of this report, which is already the result of the conciliation procedure we have achieved, because i believe that it directly contributes to the protection of workers in the course of their work, in the particularly sensitive area of noise, which is indeed a worrying and very frequent cause of health problems. i welcome the development of a code of conduct providing practical guidance to help workers and employers in the music and entertainment sectors to comply with the legal obligations created by this directive. i believe that the directive under consideration corresponds to legitimate expectations for improvement of working conditions. i also agree with the rapporteur that he would like to see rules adopted soon covering the other risk factors for health and safety at the workplace, namely electromagnetic fields and optical radiation.
in writing. - (pt) the european parliament has once again recognised the distinctive identity of the outermost regions, which is reflected in a specific problem which requires special treatment by the european union. this time, parliament recognised the distinctive identity of the ors within the framework of the common fisheries policy - a sector in which the specific features of these outermost regions are particularly evident - by agreeing to vote in favour of the need for an exclusive fishing zone, from 12 miles to 50 miles, for regional fishing vessels. this is a very reasonable measure for two reasons: 1°- as the opportunities for fishermen in these regions are diminishing, they must be increased by extending the exclusive fishing zone to 50 miles. 2°- it is also a question of guaranteeing the conservation and sustainable exploitation of fisheries resources in the seas of the ors. in addition, the fishing capacity of regional fleets is limited and the fishing methods used in these regions are environmentally friendly, as they are fully compatible with the imperative to ensure the conservation of fisheries resources. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' to cover the whole of the territory of the union.
in writing. - (pt) the commission pretends to envisage a cfp more focused on the short-term interests of the member states than on fishermen. the time has come for the cfp to commit itself to keeping regions that depend on fisheries alive, not to make this a bone of contention between member states. fishermen share common interests, they all need fisheries resources to continue their activity. decisions and objectives of recovery plans cannot ignore the consequences they may have. recovery plans must provide a balance between the recovery or preservation of stock units and measures to limit the impact on the fishing industry. some of the amendments which have been adopted, including those tabled by my group, are the reason why i voted in favour. i would stress the maintenance of the exclusive 12-mile zone, which is essential for portugal.
in writing. - (pt) i abstained in the final vote because important proposals from the committee responsible were not approved and the amendments proposed by the neau were rejected, although they significantly improved the position of parliament. nevertheless, i believe that the treatment reserved for the precautionary principle is generally correct, as is the treatment reserved for the principle of relative stability, which is a fundamental economic guarantee for the sector, especially in regions that are highly dependent on fisheries. however, it should be stressed that the adjustment of fishing capacity in each member state must take account of the contribution already made by each member state to the conservation of fisheries resources. once again, we are saying that the current critical situation in various settlements urgently requires not only the maintenance of the regime of access to the zone between 6 and 12 nautical miles, but the extension of the exclusive zone to 24 miles, if we really want to have a serious policy in this area. i also share the concern to ensure that the management of multiannual cycles takes account of social impacts, beyond the variations in resources, by meeting the promised coherence with other community policies. at the same time, i welcome the creation of regional advisory councils to include interested and concerned actors, although i think the decision-making power should remain as it is. finally, and most especially, i consider it of the utmost importance to extend the exclusive zones of the outermost regions to 50 nautical miles, thus giving a translation to article 299 of the treaty in this field.
in writing. - (pt) by amending the legislation on structural aid to the fisheries sector, the commission simply wants to put an end to public aid for the construction of new vessels. it is essential that public aid for fleet renewal should be maintained. i therefore support the rapporteur unequivocally. i believe that the fight against the disappearance of fisheries resources does not have to be fought exclusively by the tearing up of vessels. aid for the modernisation of the existing fleet cannot simply be redirected towards the scrapping of vessels, thus calling into question the budgetary measures which have been decided and which remain in force until 2006. the reduction of the fleet is perfectly compatible with the maintenance of public aid. in the meantime, we cannot follow the policy of the ostrich. verification measures are needed to ensure that public aid does not lead to an increase in fleet capacity. i therefore support the creation of a single register of vessels (classified by segment, capacity and power), which would have the specific aim of controlling fleet growth. i am in favour of increased control with exemplary sanctions, including the loss of public aid for fleet renewal for member states which do not comply with the capacity reduction provided for in the multiannual guidance programme.
in writing. - (pt) as the rapporteur has said, one of the objectives of any fisheries policy is to control the fleet and its adequacy in relation to the resources available, by seeking a balance between the fleet and the resources, avoiding overcapacity and overexploitation. but this balance must take due account of all the values involved. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the application of the principle of equal treatment for men and women in matters of employment, vocational training and promotion, and working conditions, which was adopted by the council on 16 december, is designed to ensure that the right to equal treatment for men and women is guaranteed in all member states. this would cause enormous damage - penalising the most conformist states - and unforeseeable effects which could jeopardise the survival of an entire social group and a vital way of life, especially in highly dependent regions with a strong maritime vocation and a strong fishing tradition. i therefore generally followed the rapporteur's position, which is consistent with the views i hold as a portuguese and as a european: maintenance of aid for the renewal and modernisation of the fleet; setting global fleet capacity reference levels corresponding to all the objectives set out in the pop (by 31 december 2002) for all segments; a fleet entry and exit management system that does not allow the reference levels in question to be exceeded; penalising member states that do not comply with the objectives of the pop; maintenance of premiums for permanent cessation of fishing activities, beyond demolition; maintenance of aid to joint ventures; the commission has also adopted a proposal for a regulation on the application of the principle of equal treatment for men and women.
in writing. - (pt) the varela report is harshly critical of the commission's proposal. the only figures the commission presents in this proposal for a regulation establishing an emergency measure for the demolition of fishing vessels are purely indicative. the majority of member states considered that these data on the fishing fleet were unrealistic and in many cases incorrect. the commission has also failed to present a study, analysis or report on the social and economic impact of the drastic changes to the structural fisheries policy it intends to introduce. in addition, the commission is calculating the budgetary forecasts needed to finance these proposals, which is totally illogical because it is based on mere assumptions, without any real basis as the commission itself acknowledges by pointing out that these are only probable working hypotheses. member states have already programmed these funds for other purposes, which is why it is unlikely that they would be willing to accept this reprogramming. it is therefore likely that serious funding difficulties will arise in the context of this unrealistic programme. i would vote in favour of such a clear report.
in writing. - (pt) i welcome the rapporteur's positive work in general, and my vote reflects my complete identification with the portuguese positions in this area. indeed, it is incomprehensible that the new measures should penalise even more those member states which have already made large capacity reductions (such as portugal, which has far exceeded the targets set), instead of directly affecting the fleets of those countries which have not reached the reduction targets of the fisheries operational programme (fp) and which should therefore bear the necessary reductions. the european commission must understand once and for all that its system - encouraging the demolition of ships by increasing the scrapping premium, but only by selecting vessels from member states which have met the objectives of the pops - ends up in serious contradiction with the stated objective of this measure. it is also hard to overstate the profound injustice of imposing, for example in portugal, a policy in which the amounts earmarked for these measures would come from the amounts currently allocated to aid for the renewal of the fleet, whereas it is quite clear that aid programmes for the renewal and modernisation of the fleet - particularly the portuguese fleet - must be maintained and that community structural support cannot be limited to the scrapping of ships.
in writing. - (pt) we voted against this resolution to reiterate our total disagreement with the creation of a monthly 'parliamentary allowance' which is the same for all members, regardless of the country they represent, because we consider that the salary of a member of the european parliament should be based on the salary of members of parliament in the country in which he was elected, with different social realities. if the proposal in the draft report on the draft statute for members of the european parliament, with mr rothley as rapporteur, were to be implemented, we would stress the immorality of a 'parliamentary subsidy' of 8 500 euros per month (about 1 700 000 escudos), particularly in the case of portuguese members of the european parliament, compared with the wage situation of workers in portugal. members of the european parliament are elected in national constituencies. they are therefore linked to these national constituencies and must continue to be so. the proposal to defend a single parliamentary subsidy also fits into a federalist conception of the mandate of a member of the european parliament, which aims to transform the current 'national member of the european parliament' into a future 'european member of the european parliament', a conception with which we do not agree.
in writing. - (pt) it is essential to tackle the problem of illegal immigration at its source and to combat effectively those involved in trafficking in human beings (the third most lucrative activity for organised crime) and in the economic exploitation of migrants. there needs to be a common eu policy aimed at preventing, suppressing and punishing effectively and in a dissuasive manner these criminals, but it is also essential to adopt measures to protect the safety and dignity of victims, paying particular attention to the situation of the most vulnerable people such as women and children (according to an estimate by the international organisation for migration, trafficking in human beings affects between 700 000 and 2 million women and children every year). i voted in favour of this report because this proposal for a directive, by introducing a short-term residence permit for victims of illegal immigration and trafficking in human beings, will encourage these victims to cooperate in identifying and prosecuting the perpetrators of these crimes. victims of these networks, who are often subject to violence, threats, ill-treatment and segregation, must be able to defend themselves and be helped by gaining autonomy from traffickers who exploit their economic and social vulnerability. they will need to be given the opportunity to start a new life, by giving them access to adequate housing, medical and psychological care, the labour market, education and training. the aim is to strengthen the instruments to combat illegal immigration and trafficking in human beings.
in writing. - (pt) this report closely follows the proposal for a directive presented by commissioner antónio vitorino which aims to combat the trafficking of migrants and the trafficking of human beings, without making a clear distinction between these two very different types of trafficking. in fact, the directive is more concerned with combating illegal immigration than with protecting the victims. the rapporteur is trying to make a distinction, but in an incomplete way and without drawing all the consequences. we support the idea of providing assistance to victims of migrant trafficking and we cannot accept the way in which the commission intends to do so, treating in the same way victims of trafficking for prostitution and illegal immigrants seeking work in the european union, who resort to clandestinity only because of the inadmissible positions of the community institutions, which aim to turn the european union into a 'fortress europe' instead of increasing their support for cooperation with the countries of origin and supporting their integration. the state must not use the threat of expulsion to induce victims to cooperate with the police and the investigating authorities. on the contrary, from the outset, the overall protection of the victim must guarantee the right of residence, access to asylum procedures, medical and social services, independently of the cooperation of victims in the fight against trafficking. that is why we voted against it.
in writing. - (pt) i have followed this report because i believe that it gives substance to many statements of intent concerning the urgent need to protect the many defenceless victims who are then expelled from countries where they thought they would find a decent future. moreover, this special measure is conditional on the victim's genuine cooperation with the national authorities in dismantling illegal immigration networks, which would certainly avoid the use of expedients to obtain otherwise inaccessible rights. it may in fact be a means of persuading victims to denounce the perpetrators, by freeing them, through the judicial protection of the member state, from the network of addictions which is constituted and which often represents a prison from which it is impossible to escape. on the other hand, i think that a 30-day 'cooling-off' period and the granting of a short-term residence permit should be provided for, since this short period avoids raising unfounded expectations. but i regret that the short-term residence permit is not renewed when a court decision ends the prosecution of traffickers, i think that the cooperation of the victim with the authorities should be better rewarded, regardless of the outcome of the trial of the traffickers.
in writing. - (pt) the resolution adopted today supports a european commission programme for 2003, which reaffirms the main lines of policies currently being pursued at eu level. the central axes of these policies have neo-liberal, federalist and militarist characteristics. among other things, the resolution does not give priority to the measures needed to ensure economic and social cohesion in the enlarged european union, by increasing financial allocations to the countries, regions and sectors most affected; - does not provide for any revision of the stability pact, the underlying political, economic and political objectives; - does not, on the contrary, call into question the liberalisations decided by the lisbon and barcelona european councils; - does not criticise the policy of 'demployment' based on flexibility and precariousness of work; - does not criticise the application of the 'open method of coordination' to the pension sector with the aim of combating the public social protection system and its domination by private capital; - does not question the process of liberalisation decided by the wto in doha, which has contributed to the worsening of inequalities at world level and to the liberalisation of public services. finally, i cannot help but criticize the intention of these four groups in the ep to revive the creation of 'european political parties' after the entry into force of the treaty of nice.
mr president, i would like to draw the attention of the president, the commission and the house to the events in east timor, which are very serious events, since this house has always supported the process of independence and peace in east timor, which is like a child of this house and the european union, on which we are betting. there have been extremely serious, somewhat mysterious riots and uprisings in east timor which deserve the attention of the european authorities. i would ask the president to contact president xanana gusmão to find out what is happening in east timor, given our great care for this newly independent country.
- mr president, i am one of those who intellectually subscribe to the systematization and simplification exercise so well elaborated by mr jean-louis bourlanges: because it takes account of the long theoretical production which this parliament has been deepening since maastricht, because it constitutes a parliamentary acquis which must be reflected in the text of the convention and, finally, because this work results in the clarity of the instruments and the effectiveness of the means of decision making indispensable to the normal functioning of the future union. unfortunately, mr rapporteur, in the philosophical and theoretical subconscious of the text - see the fifth paragraph, second indent - there is a growing distrust of small and mediumsized states which leads me to distance myself from their approval at this time. for a number of simple reasons. because it has been the disagreements between the great states on the great issues that have impeded the union's progress. it is not for want of the will of the little ones that britain has not joined the euro or the schengen area! the absence of a social europe, the lack of a budget, the lack of reform of the cap, the crisis in structural policy or the limitations of the judicial instruments in the area of freedom, security and justice are not the fault of the small. it is therefore not appropriate, given the size of the states, to recall the chronic response of the unforgettable gendarme of casablanca: 'let the usual suspects be arrested'! because it is not the square kilometres, the longitudes, the latitudes and the gdp per capita that measure the degree of relevance to the common interest, to the european interest! that is why these criteria cannot be used to impose finished constitutional solutions, leaving those who do not agree to stay in the lobby, that is, one step away from the exit door. perhaps mr jean-louis bourlanges, with his intelligence, his knowledge and his unfailing europeanist sentiment, should have gone into this matter more deeply and should have fought harder to find a reasonable and consensual solution. i hope so, at least until tomorrow.
the schengen information system (sis) is a computer network system in which information provided by the schengen states is stored and consulted by the police and customs authorities of other schengen states. the sis is the largest database in europe and has a dual function: on the one hand, to maintain public order and security, and on the other hand, to support measures to compensate for the free movement of persons. it is in this context that we have received two spanish initiatives aimed at introducing new functionalities into sis, particularly in the fight against terrorism. i regret that this report, for internal reasons of the european parliament, is limited to these two spanish initiatives and does not also cover, as originally planned, a resolution on the commission communication on the development of the future sis ii (which contains proposals to extend the capacity of the system and to introduce new possibilities at technical and research level), especially since the two spanish initiatives intend to include in the current version of sis improvements which must necessarily reflect the long-term objectives of sis ii and therefore must be considered as an integral part of the process of transition from sis i to sis ii. after a thorough examination of the spanish initiatives, we have come to the conclusion that, although we cannot support them in their entirety, they can be accepted if certain amendments are made to them, the main aim of which is to ensure that the guarantees of protection of citizens' rights are not jeopardised, thus addressing the main concern of the european parliament throughout this process. certain proposals contained in the spanish initiatives are admissible, such as europol or eurojust access, provided that appropriate safeguards are in place to ensure the legitimacy and legality of any and all use of the data. as such, the conditions laid down in my amendment no 7 (on europol) and my amendment no 10 (on eurojust) must be met before access is granted. if such access to new users is made possible, the purpose for which it has been granted must always be respected, by limiting it to the information to which it has access and by ensuring that there is always at least the same level of protection of the rights of our citizens if it cannot be strengthened. it is also necessary to examine more rigorously and to justify the need for europol and eurojust to access sis data. proposals that all consultations should be recorded should be welcomed, and it is appropriate to specify in the article what information should be recorded. it is also important to provide a legal basis for the operation of the sirene bureaux, as i had already suggested in my report of june 2001, and to require these bureaux to delete data when this is necessary. on the other hand, there are other proposals contained in these two initiatives which, in our view, should be left pending the development of sis ii when appropriate safeguards are in place. this is the case with the proposal to extend access to data relating to stolen, misappropriated or lost identity documents. although i have no objections in principle, i believe that in the absence of additional measures to protect the rights of persons whose documents have been stolen, whose identity is being used illicitly or whose data has been incorrectly entered in sis, the european parliament cannot at this stage approve this proposal which is likely to aggravate the problems of citizens whose identity has been misused. the same applies to the proposal to insert additional data on wanted persons and identification materials in the person alerts, in particular photographs and fingerprints, for which appropriate safeguards should be adopted in advance. the commission has commissioned a feasibility study on the development of sis ii, which is expected to be available in march 2003. i hope that the european parliament will then have the opportunity to comment on these conclusions. i would again like to regret that this feasibility study does not examine the possibility of bringing together the customs databases of schengen, europol and possibly eurojust into a single database, so as to eliminate duplication, streamline resources and improve accuracy, a common database which would be made available to different users in a differentiated way, ensuring restricted and precise access to different parts of the database. there already seems to be political agreement on the need to create a new sis (there is a council decision with the legal basis for the development of sis-ii, as well as the budget). the way in which it is to be managed will be determined in a future diploma. i would like to recall here what has been the clear position of this parliament: the commission must be responsible for its management and must be subject to parliamentary and judicial control. the development of sis ii is expected to take place by 2006; however, given the risk that this development will take longer than expected, it seems to me acceptable and appropriate that we introduce some improvements (which are possible at this stage) in the existing sis as soon as possible. finally, mr president, i would like to thank all the members of the committee on civil liberties who contributed with their amendments to improve this report, as well as the collaboration of the shadow rapporteurs and, in particular, the socialist shadow rapporteur, mr sergio sousa pinto.
- mr president, commissioner, mr carlos coelho, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by congratulating the rapporteur on the work he has done. the report gives a balanced assessment of the two spanish initiatives to introduce new functionalities into the schengen information system, which is important, particularly in the fight against terrorism. following the attacks of 11 september 2001, the council, together with the commission, drew up a package of measures to increase the level of security in europe and to ensure the greatest possible effectiveness in the fight against terrorism. some of these measures have a direct effect on the schengen information system. since sis ii will not be operational until 2006, the improvement of the current system is an imperative need which should be understood as part of the transition process to the new system. these new functionalities must of course be adopted with due respect for the individual rights, freedoms and guarantees of union citizens, while preserving a controlled use of personal data available in sis. we all understand the need for efficient information systems to meet the challenges posed by international crime, but this action must never lead us to disregard or forget the fundamental principles on which the union as a community of law is founded. the aim is to amend certain articles of the schengen convention to implement new functionalities. the granting of access to certain sis data to europol and the national members of eurojust is one of the most important concrete proposals of this initiative. the need to improve cooperation in the field of justice and home affairs, and in particular the linking and cross-referencing of information between different databases, is one of the objectives which the european union and the member states must pursue. in this context, the fact that sis was set up to support the member states in areas relevant to public policy and internal security, areas covered by the activities of europol and eurojust, leads us to approve the principle of exchange of information underlying the proposal of the spanish initiative. however, as the schengen joint supervisory authority, the body responsible for supervising sis, has pointed out, access to the database by europol and the national members of eurojust raises some data protection concerns and can only become a reality by respecting the basic principle of the legitimacy and legality of access to and use of the database. it is in this context, with some reservations about the initial spanish proposals, that we welcome the introduction by the rapporteur of amendments which seem to us to respond to the concerns expressed by the joint supervisory authority and by different members of the committee on civil liberties. the european police office will thus have the right to consult and examine sis data only for the purposes for which they were provided. as regards access by the national members of eurojust, they shall have the right to consult sis data only if this right is compatible with the purposes for which the data were provided. the national members of eurojust will have to record all consultations they carry out and will not be able to transfer data to third countries or other entities. other proposals contained in the initiative were, in my view, correctly rejected by the committee on civil liberties on the rapporteur's proposal, as they did not offer sufficient guarantees and were therefore preferable to rely on sis ii. this is the case, in particular, of the proposal to give the authorities responsible for issuing and analysing visas the possibility of accessing data contained in sis. for the reasons i have just given, the report by mr carlos coelho deserves the applause and support of the socialist group. - mr president, i too would like to join my colleague herbert bösh in congratulating our colleague brigitte langenhagen on the content of her report and to say that the question of simplification and transparency of these programmes - and this can be seen in the report of the court of auditors - is by far the most important and decisive question in their success. i think that when we embark on a process of creating new agencies - and i think that at this stage there will be a few dozen agencies to manage programmes of a size not very different from this one - i think that before we embark on that process we should think very carefully about what problems we want to solve, whether indeed an agency is going to be different and will solve the problems that those science and technology offices have not been able to solve. i think that more important than thinking about the agencies is to think about how to make all this much easier, more accessible and standardised in all the member states so that everything can be processed more quickly, both in the matter of payments which was raised here by our colleague herbert bösh and also in the question of the design of the programme itself. and it was this call for reflection that i wanted to leave here especially to the commission.
in writing. - (pt) i believe that this danish initiative, which aims to promote closer cooperation in the field of investigations and prosecutions relating, inter alia, to war crimes and crimes against humanity, is an effective and operational contribution to the important objective that the eu has set itself in the action plan adopted by the council on 20 march 2000 entitled 'prevention and control of organised crime: a european union strategy for the next millennium'. also considers that certain amendments proposed by the rapporteur and approved by the committee responsible are appropriate and justified; the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a decision on the application of the principle of proportionality to the application of the principle of proportionality to the application of the principle of proportionality. that is why i voted in favour of this report.
in writing. - (pt) i congratulate mr sousa pinto on the quality and courage of his report. i agree with the criticism of the lack of ambition and substance of the danish proposal. there is no progress in the mutual recognition of criminal decisions; we are still a long way from the application of the principle of mutual recognition of judgments. the proposal which is being put forward only provides for the establishment of an information system between member states concerning the forfeiture of rights imposed by a criminal sentence, but does not provide for any form of mutual recognition of the sentence pronounced for the purpose of its enforcement, which means that persons convicted and deprived of rights or incapacitated in one member state can exercise such rights with impunity in another state of the union. the wish expressed in tampere and laeken to make the principle of mutual recognition of decisions in criminal matters effective must be respected. the question of the recognition of judicial decisions in criminal matters is a very delicate one. there are differences between member states. the existence of 'criminal havens' must be avoided, by preventing the freedom of movement from benefiting those who act outside the law. a clear response to citizens' demands is needed if the eu is to effectively address the growing threats of crime against freedom and rights through a strong and common policy to prevent and combat crime. the rejection of the danish proposal is therefore a strong political signal to the council and the member states: the european parliament expects consistent and coherent progress in the field of the third pillar, it does not want small-scale initiatives that seem to be limited to media exploitation.
in writing. - (pt) the creation of a genuine european area of security and justice throughout the union requires better coordination between the national authorities of the member states, with a dual effect: on the one hand, to promote a coherent and coordinated policy to combat organised crime, which has been able to exploit very well the completion and deepening of the single market; the second is to give a full answer to what the rapporteur says is the third of the nine most important concerns of european citizens, after peace and security and the fight against unemployment. however, that danish initiative, although it aims to establish a system of information between the member states concerning the forfeiture of rights against an individual in the context of a criminal decision, does not provide for any form of mutual recognition of the judgment for the purpose of its enforcement. that is why i have followed this report and i have joined the rejection of this danish initiative, as proposed by the rapporteur and adopted by the committee responsible in parliament. in fact, this initiative is only about creating an information system between member states. there is therefore no really operational instrument for the fight against organised crime, since this initiative does not aim at creating, in parallel, a kind of mutual recognition of the sentence for its enforcement, a prerequisite for the alleged loss of rights.
in writing. - (pt) the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs proposes that the house reject the initiative of the kingdom of denmark on the third pillar. the commission and your rapporteur believe that this is necessary in order for parliament to make clear its deep dissatisfaction with the paralysis of the third pillar by the member states. important commission proposals, both before and after 11 september 2001, including the counter-terrorism package, are blocked in the council or awaiting transposition or ratification by states. in the absence of the necessary consensus within the council or of the member states' ability to respect their commitments under national law, they use and abuse their right of initiative. the wild proposals are pouring in, without being part of any plan and without entering the commission's 'dashboard'. it is arbitrary, small, incoherent proposals, sometimes dictated by the national political agenda, that transform the third pillar into a universe of continuous expansion, chaos, a bureaucratic framework. the european parliament has a duty to denounce this smoke screen, made up of apparent legislative hyperactivity, which helps to disguise the union's lethargy in a vital area - especially after 11 september 2001 - and the political inability of the council and the member states to enforce a single substantial measure from the commission and provided for in the 'scoreboard'. the gap between the discourse of political leaders, especially government representatives, and its translation into concrete actions is reaching scandalous proportions in the fight against transnational crime. we have to put an end to this. this is the message that the european parliament wishes to send to the council and the member states.
in writing. - (pt) this report is based on the erroneous assumption that the aid granted by the edf is part of the community's vocation. it is well established that the nature of the relations of the various member states with the countries receiving development aid varies. many historical factors and long-standing links, as in the case of portugal, often result in very close cooperation relations. these relations are expressed in frameworks which can only be identified and duly weighted in the context of the council's consultation. i do not agree with communitization, which is why i voted against this report.
in writing. - i congratulate mr langenhagen on his excellent report on special report no 2/2002 of the court of auditors on the evaluation of the implementation of the socrates and youth for europe community action programmes for the period 1995-1999, which i support, particularly as regards the need to impose clarity, precision and rigour from the design phase of the various community programmes to the evaluation phase of the same. in this context, i would also like to stress that the european parliament should, in the decision-making process leading to the adoption of community acts, defend the clarity of the texts, simplified procedures and, where appropriate, mechanisms to ensure effective management and evaluation of the act in question.
in writing. - (pt) i voted in favour of this report because i consider it extremely important to maintain the community funds for promoting european student exchanges and, more specifically, in the framework of the two community action programmes: socrates and youth for europe. despite the shortcomings identified by the rapporteur and the committee responsible in parliament, in particular the shortcomings in the way programmes are developed and administered (which are also the subject of some suggestions), it must be acknowledged that a participation rate of around 90% shows the great importance and, necessarily, the great success of this type of programme in the past, present and future for the enrichment and development of future generations of europeans and for contributing to the increasingly necessary lifelong learning. i therefore support the continuation of these programmes and the proposed budgetary envelope, as it is vitally important to continue to encourage the creation of a european education area through the implementation of informal education experiences among young people, including: physical mobility, lifelong learning, foreign language learning, increased use of new technologies in education and the promotion of cooperation at european level between the various education actors involved.
in writing. - (pt) this initiative is part of the introduction of drop-in proposals, with the aim of strengthening discretionary powers using the argument of the fight against terrorism. the aim is to do this in the present case by creating, without justification, the sis ii. this initiative must be rejected, in particular because: - it allows entities, in particular from third countries, to have easy access to information of a personal nature, without any control; - it does not guarantee that all proposals concerning the operation of the sis will be approved by the schengen joint supervisory authority; - the argument and concern about terrorism cannot be used as a basis for the creation of citizens' files, without any other criterion than the political criterion, which could transform the sis into a political information service in the framework of a future european political police, with unforeseeable consequences for the protection of citizens' freedoms and rights.
in writing. - (pt) this resolution is in line with the federalist integration of the european union, in that it seeks to create a constitution, in that it supports the strengthening of the supranational powers of the european parliament through the generalization of co-decision and, thus, in that it defends the generalization of qualified majority decision-making in the council, with the communitization of a greater number of policies. the resolution also supports changing the method of amending the treaties by advocating the institutionalisation of the 'convention' method with enhanced powers, by providing for separate amendment processes - between the 'constitutional' and the 'political' parts - by allowing national parliaments to not play their full role in ratifying the treaties. it is a good thing that the resolution, on a proposal from my group, has removed the point which called for the end of the unanimous decision in the council to revise the treaties (when it comes to such crucial issues as, for example, the decision to transfer national competences to the eu). however, the resolution presents a dominant legal order of the eu vis-à-vis the member states, by strengthening the powers of its supranational institutions (even proposing to create a new institution, through the institutionalisation of the "convention"), by consolidating the foundations for the creation of a future "european state" and by calling into question national sovereignty and independence, notably of portugal. that is why we voted against this report.
in writing. - (pt) i largely agree with the legal diagnosis made in this report concerning the community legal order. i could hardly agree more with the rapporteur when he says that we are facing a real chaos of rules, procedures and language. i agree even more with him when he says that the inconsistency of the different categories of legal acts which make up this order makes the system incomprehensible to even the most enlightened and legally aware citizens. the current framework ignores any principle of legal specialisation of terms and is based on often obscure and sometimes misleading designations. however, i cannot follow the report when it is politically aimed at taking advantage of the need to establish a coherent typology of community acts and a logical hierarchy of standards - an urgent legal operation - to take further steps in the 'long march' of the more than questionable federalist/constitutional doctrine, assuming the de facto existence of a sovereign european state with constituent power from the outset and even going as far as omitting absolutely any reference to national parliaments or, in essence, proposing to end the procedure specific to the treaties. in fact, to give the community legal order coherence and clarity, it is not necessary to impose a federal regulatory framework. we all know that. that is why i voted against, regretting that the rapporteur - who is doing an excellent technical job - has slipped politically into what i consider to be federalist opportunism.
in writing. - (pt) the final report is positive because it maintains the current restrictive list of products. it is also positive because it insists on regular checks and the need to immediately remove all products which are incorrectly labelled, i.e. those which are labelled 'not irradiated' when the corresponding tests show otherwise. i would remind you that the precautionary principle must always be respected. the point is that any technology must demonstrate the benefits of its use and take into account the potential risks to health and the environment, which is a problem with this technology of irradiating food. in fact, if we start from the civil use of nuclear energy, there seems to be no other use than to substitute the normal rules of hygiene and safety which must be those of the foodstuffs processed. as for the benefits for consumers, they also seem to be non-existent since, beyond the risks of misuse with the possibility of intoxication, it is more difficult for the consumer to determine the actual state of the product. thus, the big goal of this technology seems to lie elsewhere - to accelerate the liberalization of international trade and to strengthen the centralization/concentration of the production and mass distribution of food in the world, with consequences on the offshoring of agri-food production and the decline of small local farmers, which puts sustainable development in question.
in writing. - (pt) we generally agree with the report presented and stress the need to change the policy on the universal ban on vaccination against foot-and-mouth disease, which has been in force since 1992 and which does not even provide for prophylactic vaccination in the event of an outbreak of foot-and-mouth disease. but we cannot fail to stress the link between the increase in animal diseases (bse, foot-and-mouth disease, etc.) and other food safety risks (dioxins, sewage sludge, etc.) on the one hand, and the concentration and intensification of production as a result of an agricultural model based on export and the liberalisation of agricultural markets, which favours slaughter over vaccination, on the other. we would point out that it is not rational to promote the transport of live animals by centralising markets and closing local slaughterhouses, as this increases the risk of the spread of these diseases, especially in an internal market without borders between member states. the localisation of production, the creation of local slaughterhouses and veterinary centres with more decentralised resources and local and regional markets should have been promoted, by limiting the transport of live animals as much as possible and by carrying out more thorough checks, even at the internal borders of each member state. we insist on the need to allow ring vaccination and to resume research, which has practically stopped because vaccination was banned. similarly, monitoring services should be strengthened, especially in the most remote rural areas, and 'blind' slaughter should be avoided.
- mr president, as i have already had occasion to point out, the financial engineering exercises required by the present budgetary procedure have once again made evident the precariousness of the community financial framework and, consequently, the need for its revision and readaptation. it is clear that the financial framework agreed in berlin is clearly insufficient to meet the costs of enlargement, the increased needs for economic and social cohesion and, at the same time, to respond to the new priorities that are emerging in the meantime, particularly in the field of internal and external policies. the revision of the financial perspective would therefore be the most appropriate course. or, in the absence of such a review, the legitimate and full use of all the prerogatives which the treaties grant to the european parliament in budgetary matters could have been a lesser evil, thus avoiding the games of redistribution and cuts to which we have become accustomed, often to the detriment of priorities and objectives previously decided upon. i am thinking in particular of an area which has been particularly sacrificed: cooperation and development policy. in the present context and in the face of the new priorities that have emerged in the meantime - kosovo, afghanistan, but also palestine and the pre-accession funds for turkey or the global health fund - the compression of funds for the poorest countries has become inevitable. now this compression is extended to food aid, cooperation with latin america, the balkans and the meda programme. but even the budgetary decisions adopted are not being implemented, as is the case now with the 55 million euros specifically earmarked for afghanistan. and there is also the attempt to include the global health fund in the european development fund without even consulting each other and without regard to the interests of the countries receiving the same edf. all this because the principle of the sufficiency of means, whereby new financial resources should be matched by new priorities, is not being respected. it is obvious that at least a review of the ceiling on the financial perspective at the level of category 4, i.e. external policies, was indispensable. but the question becomes even more obvious and glaring when we consider that the volume of payment expenditure that is proposed to be approved will grow by only 1.9% in 2003, i.e. less than the expected inflation, which will result in a real reduction in the payment budget. the budget for 2003 will be the lowest in the last decade in relative terms and according to the criteria of the stability pact, representing just under 1.02% of community gnp. it is not reasonable to want more and more europe and at the same time want to approve less and less budget. that said, let me make a positive note of what has been said about east timor. i welcome the results achieved, both by maintaining a specific budget line which i have always advocated and by increasing this line by 6 million compared with the council's proposal. this is a positive political signal for a country and a population which has only recently gained independence and where many shortcomings remain and which is in fact consistent with the various decisions we have taken on the same. a final word on palestine: given the dramatic situation in that country, it is important that the situation be repaired next year by the creation of a line and sufficient amounts, in particular to recover the investments made by the european union.
