in writing. - (pt) the commission sees a labour cost index as a key element in enabling the european central bank to monitor inflation trends in emu and the social partners to negotiate wage agreements. the proposal under consideration aims to remedy this situation by establishing a common framework for the development, transmission and evaluation of comparable labour cost indices. in short, it is proposed that the total quarterly costs incurred by the employer in connection with the employment of labour should be taken into account for the purpose of calculating labour costs. the commission will be assisted in the implementation of the regulation (including decisions on derogations) by the statistical programme committee. every two years, the commission will present to the european parliament and the council a report on the implementation of the regulation, including an assessment of the quality of the data transmitted. in its common position adopted at the end of september 2002, the council made a number of adjustments to the commission's original proposal, which the rapporteur, like myself, considers to be in keeping with the spirit of the original proposal. that is why i want to express my support for it in this explanation of vote.
in writing. - (pt) since the lack of a common framework for the compilation, transmission and evaluation of labour costs has been identified as a major weakness in the emu action plan on statistical needs - which has also affected euro area inflation - i have supported this report which aims precisely to fill this gap. as the rapporteur says, this common framework should enable the european central bank to monitor inflation within emu and the social partners to negotiate wage agreements. as in countless other cases, i believe that transparent and reliable information and the existence of clear and well-known criteria are essential instruments in our time. they enable the national and community authorities to adopt fair, proportionate and adequate measures upstream and serve as a means of clarifying and correctly structuring the role and interests of economic agents downstream. while welcoming this common framework, i look forward to the outcome of the implementation of this new regulation, in which the quality of the data transmitted will be assessed, the commission being of course obliged to present every two years to the european parliament and the council a report on the implementation of the new regulation.
in writing. - (pt) i congratulate mr pohjamo on his excellent report on the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council on statistical data relating to the carriage of passengers, freight and mail by air, which i fully support, particularly as regards the need to approve regulations to trigger mechanisms for european air carriers to have up-to-date and comparable statistics on this sector, which will help to ensure the safety of passengers and to manage the activities of these companies effectively. in this context, i would like to stress the importance of this regulation in solving the problem of airspace saturation.
in writing. - (pt) the report calls for the adoption of more stringent standards for the eligibility of blood and plasma donors and the screening of blood donations, the establishment of a community-wide quality system for blood transfusion facilities, accompanied by an equivalent system of control and approval in the member states, and the establishment of common standards for staff training and the traceability of blood from donor to recipient. on 12 june 2002, parliament had already approved eight amendments at second reading. in the context of the written procedure, a compromise solution in conciliation was found and accepted by the council concerning the examination of donors by qualified health professionals. all these reasons justify my vote in favour of this report.
in writing. - (pt) many complaints from citizens about the implementation of environmental policies in the member states relate to the lack of transparency of the institutions in informing the public and the lack of consideration of citizens' opinions on projects that have an impact on the environment. it is therefore essential that, in line with the aarhus convention, the commission legislate on public access to environmental information, public participation in certain projects and programmes and public access to justice. better public participation and information will give greater legitimacy to the decisions taken by our institutions and will improve the quality of those decisions. the directive which has been approved today is a significant step in this direction - general disclosure of information will become the rule and the protection of information by the authorities will become a clearly defined exception.
in writing. - (pt) sustainable development depends on our ability to 'green' the economy - so that buying green is a right and not a luxury and selling green is a competitive advantage. we will only achieve this if, through a life cycle analysis of products, we quantify their environmental characteristics (from design to disposal) and thus integrate these environmental costs into the final price of the product. the directives on electrical and electronic equipment adopted today are a very significant step forward in this respect, since they provide for a reduction in the use of certain hazardous substances, such as lead, mercury and cadmium, in electrical and electronic equipment and promote the collection, reuse, recycling and recovery of electrical and electronic waste by making the producer responsible for the financing of these systems.
in writing. - (pt) this new directive obliges the member states to guarantee the right of access to environmental information held by public authorities, and this in a way which follows from a conciliation agreement between the council and parliament, which seems acceptable to me. in summary, the joint text provides for:the obligation of public authorities to actively help citizens in their search for information, encouraging them to use new technologies to find answers to their questions; quality standards to ensure that the information provided is up-to-date, accurate and reliable; the principle of free information for citizens, while considering the possibility of requesting a financial contribution limited to 'a reasonable amount'. as this conciliation agreement strengthens the right of citizens to obtain information from national, regional or local authorities, in particular in the event of pollution of watercourses, the construction of new projects or the destruction of natural habitats of protected species of animals or plants, while improving transparency in areas of common interest, i could only vote in favour of this report.
in writing. - (pt) i naturally voted in favour of this important report, which aims to establish common criteria for access to information, public participation in decision-making and access to justice in environmental matters, since i believe that access to information - which must also be of high quality - must be regarded as a normal prerogative of citizens. the establishment of rules for such access will effectively contribute to citizen control in an area which concerns us all, a genuine citizen control in the defence of environmental heritage. it is in this spirit that i consider it particularly relevant to strengthen the mechanisms for access to information and information technologies. the trend towards free access to environmental information provided by the authorities, combined with the promotion of the use of information technologies - in particular the internet - will ensure that this access to information is widespread and free, taking into account, of course, the limitations imposed by national defence, legal proceedings, industrial rights and/or intellectual property. finally, i welcome the establishment of practical arrangements to ensure the effective use of environmental information and the granting of greater powers to the member states in this context, which will result in increased responsibility for the latter and in the application of the principle of subsidiarity.
- mr president, mr president-in-office of the council, madam commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, it is already too late for the past, but it is important that it is not too late for the future, that it is not too late for the future again. and i would like to see us discussing this matter again in the future, but in a completely different context from what we did on the 21st and are doing again today, so that we can say in the future that this has never happened again or that, having had a new unfortunate situation of risk and threat in the future, this time, thanks to the measures taken and the technical and political awareness that we have all developed, things have gone well in terms of crisis management. at this point, we are in fact still under shock and this shock is on the one hand good adviser and on the other bad adviser. it is a good adviser to us to adopt more firm, more rigorous, more demanding measures for the future; but it is bad advice if we do or want to do, in the plane of party shock and emotion, the research on crisis management. it is very important, also in this respect, that a technically rigorous and politically dispassionate inventory be made of the way in which the crisis has been managed, not to judge anyone in particular, but to prevent future mistakes. our group also wants to draw attention to a new front of preventive measures. we have heard of preventive measures as regards the technical risk of vessels or the way navigation is carried out. that's right, but it's not enough: it is also necessary to identify risk conditions, so to speak, legal risk, that is to say, the issues of flags of convenience, flags of convenience, certificates of navigability which do not deserve the slightest credit and to define mechanisms which can be required internationally and which disqualify these places, these so to speak, off shore places of maritime navigation. there is a developed doctrine on the harmful effects of offshore platforms in money laundering and we also need to know how to develop a doctrine to combat offshore platforms in the area of laundering of liabilities in relation to the navigation conditions of ships.
- mr president, it has been more than a month since the disaster caused by the oil tanker prestige, and it is not known how long we will have to endure the new tidal waves, which in itself is an indictment of the spanish and european union authorities for the delay and weakness of the measures taken to mitigate the effects of the real tragedy that struck the communities and the sea of galicia, but which has since spread to the maritime territories to the north and south, reaching portugal and france. while the tragedy makes it clear that the ecological disaster is caused by the profits that certain shipowners and oil companies are trying to make in any way, including at the expense of maritime safety and working conditions, in particular through flags of convenience, the truth is that governments have facilitated this exploitation through increasingly neo-liberal practices, including the delay in the implementation of directives decided upon after the erika disaster. the reasons for the current crisis, the persistence of the causes, the lack of decision on the matter and the delay in transposing the rules should therefore be ascertained; but it is also necessary to go further, as my group proposes in the proposal i support: the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat the spread of the disease, including the abolition of flags of convenience, the prohibition of the transport of petroleum products and other dangerous goods in single-hull vessels, the establishment of sea corridors further from the coast and increased surveillance.
- mr president, commissioner, mr president-in-office of the council, ladies and gentlemen, the sinking of the prestige has shown the impotence of the european authorities and their respective states in the face of a catastrophic situation such as this, which, having brutally struck the coast of galicia, has not ceased to be a european catastrophe, felt as such by all citizens. it is precisely this european public opinion, shaken by this incredible event, and the emotion and outrage of all those affected - in particular the admirable galician coastline and its people - that demand that such a situation cannot be repeated in the future on our common coast. it is the people who are waiting for galicia to be justly supported by the european union, and it is a commission of inquiry that is required to shed light on all the circumstances in which the catastrophe occurred and to establish responsibility, so that lessons can be drawn from this for the future. the european citizens are demanding that legislation be tightened up, that it be brought into force as soon as possible and that it be applied without hesitation. the defence of the european maritime endeavour must increasingly be a common concern. and so a common policy. the defence of the marine environment and the protection of europe's coasts are now major challenges for us all. if the prestige tragedy serves to awaken political consciousness and incite action, then the desperation and revolt of the galician people - and ours too - will not have been in vain.
- mr president, madam commissioner, mr president-in-office of the council, i would like to begin by reiterating my solidarity with the people of galicia and with all the people who may be affected by this tragedy, whether in spain or france and, of course, in my own country. as time has passed, the true scale of this tragedy has become more apparent. i hope that the inquiry requested by parliament will be carried out thoroughly and in a timely manner. and, as i have already said in this house, it is also important to clarify the mysterious deviation of the ship's trajectory which, after heading west, suddenly veers south until it sinks 30 miles from portuguese waters. but it is important now to talk more about the future, and that is why i wanted to address four points. firstly, it is essential that the european union should provide support to compensate as much as possible for the damage caused by the black tide. it is in this context that i believe the mobilisation of the european union solidarity fund, which has not yet been done, is justified. the second aspect is that, given the obvious dire impact on fisheries and aquaculture activities, it is important to think about a more integrated and comprehensive package of measures to provide fundamental answers to the populations affected. the third aspect is that it will be important to make a technically and scientifically justified decision about what is to be done to the ship that is sinking with its 60,000 tonnes of crude oil. without resolving this we will be doomed not to a black tide, but to a series of black tides for months and years. and finally, i would like to stress that it is high time that the european maritime safety agency was set up in earnest, which presupposes that the council agrees on its geographical location. in conclusion, i would like to say that we want a thorough investigation to be carried out, as parliament has already requested. what we do not want is for parliament to be used as a forum for debate on the internal problems of a country.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to begin as baroness ludford did when she said that there has been a lack of pace, a lack of speed and a lack of balance in asylum and immigration policy. i agree with what mrs terrón i cusi suggested when she said that a global approach to these matters was needed. the global approach means that we have to address the two realities of the asylum and immigration problem, the causes that drive them and how we will combat their excesses. and here, i want to say that i do not agree with those who oppose the repressive measures saying that they are not necessary. they are unfortunately necessary. the fight against illegal immigration must be a priority for the union, because it is illegal immigration that most undermines human dignity, it is illegal immigration that allows criminal networks and human trafficking to operate, it is illegal immigration and the inability to register migration flows that allow exaggerated xenophobic, racist and intolerant reactions. it would probably be easier to make everything illegal legal, but you can't do that. not everyone who wants to enter the european union can do so. and so, as mr watson said, we must have a return policy which gives credibility to our immigration policy. and that is essential, it is essential to do it without having any complexes of an ideological or other nature. so, lack of speed and lack of balance. as for the lack of speed, i am counting on the energy of commissioner vitorino. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i would like to stress the wisdom of commissioner vitorino in saying something which i think is very important: we must have a policy of small steps in this matter. small, but safe.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, despite the recognition of the role of services of general interest, by subjecting them to the rules of competition, their social role as essential public services for the population is devalued. this is the major problem today, caused by the successive decisions of the commission and the council, particularly at the lisbon and barcelona summits, which have accelerated liberalization and deregulation in many sectors, notably telecommunications, postal services, energy and transport. the consequences are already beginning to be visible, not only in the redundancies and increased precariousness of work in some of these sectors of activity, but also, in many cases, in the higher rates and prices and the poorer quality of the services provided, which has merited firm opposition from workers and the population, as was recently demonstrated by the general strike in portugal. the importance of quality public services must therefore be recognised in order to guarantee the right of access for all citizens, which also requires a firm stance against the liberalisation of public services in the world trade organisation negotiations. and what are your commitments in the context of the world trade organisation, commissioner?
in writing. - (pt) we voted in favour of this report because it guarantees the financing of eur 27 million for the conversion of the spanish and portuguese fleets operating in morocco. we also stress the fact that the proposal i signed as rapporteur for the committee on fisheries on the 2003 budget was accepted, at least in part, with a view to using the flexibility instrument. this is to fulfil the commitment made last year by the budgetary authority to enter eur 27 million in the 2003 budget. however, i can only regret the serious precedent set by the anticipated use of a transfer of the structural funds not used in 2002, including almost €15 million for future reprogramming and redistribution, knowing that this is a highly sensitive area for all member states.
in writing. - (pt) we support the idea of designating 2004 as the european year of education through sport. it corresponds to the need to promote sport in society in its most positive aspects and, in particular, in its contribution to the educational process. we recall that the organisation of euro 2004 in portugal is a contribution which will have to be included. it is essential to establish a closer relationship between the education system and the sports system, that is to say between the school and the club, the associations, the organisations, to which we must add the local authorities. this is how the value of popular associations, sports for all, will be enhanced. this also requires more dissemination and transparency in the dissemination of measures and in the preparation of projects to be financed. in the meantime, the amounts envisaged are low and do not correspond to the concerns expressed, which is regrettable. our vote in favour of the report also includes the hope that this aspect will be re-examined.
in writing. - (pt) i congratulate our colleague mrs pack on her excellent report, which i fully support. it is a work which aims to conclude, through the adoption of the council's common position, a theme which i consider very important: 2004, the european year of education through sport. sport is recognised as an important social phenomenon in europe and is a privileged educational tool. like the rapporteur, i believe that it is appropriate to emphasise the importance that the olympic ideals can attach to education. sport contributes in particular to increasing young people's social skills, such as team spirit, solidarity and loyalty. i therefore welcome the decision to organise the european year of education through sport in 2004, which will contribute positively to reversing the current practice of community action in the field of sport limited to pilot initiatives and preparatory measures. i therefore agree with the rapporteur that the council's common position should be adopted without amendment, since it largely takes account of the amendments proposed by the european parliament.
in writing. - (pt) the commission proposes that 2004 be proclaimed the european year of education through sport. we recall that portugal will host the european football championship in the same year and that the olympic and paralympic games will be held in athens. the commission's approach, like mrs pack's, is to make better use of the educational values of sport to improve school curricula and, above all, parallel learning, through the creation of a stronger partnership between education and sport organisations. whether it is in terms of the objectives, the list of activities that will help achieve the objectives mentioned, or the proposed financial envelope, which should focus on supporting concrete initiatives that strengthen the links between sport and education, this initiative is undoubtedly worth supporting, and i express my support by voting in favour of it.
in writing. - (pt) i have supported this report and i fully agree with the approach set out by the committee on culture, youth, education, the media and sport, which aims to amend the commission's proposal by eliminating the budget lines for surveys and studies and adding the resources saved (around eur 420 000) to the amounts available for local, national and transnational actions, as well as for initiatives by sports and educational organisations whose task is to disseminate information about this year. as you know, i have always supported these initiatives, ever since 2004 was declared the european year of education through sport - as i said during the first debates in parliament. i repeat that this is a major boost to a better use of the educational values of sport to improve school curricula and, above all, parallel learning, through the creation of increased partnership between educational and sports organisations. in this context, i believe that it is not enough to strengthen the links between sport and education, thus increasing the visibility of the european year of education through sport, by organising and financing, for example, school-level athletics competitions in each participating country. school sport must be the top priority in sport policies and at the same time occupy a prominent place in school and educational policies.
in writing. - (pt) the conclusions of the copenhagen summit show that the european council decided to postpone the solution of serious problems, in a real leap forward, after having decided to enlarge to ten new countries, on the basis of discrimination, not only against them, but also against the current member states with the weakest economy and the most acute social problems, as in the case of portugal. the council has done the same with regard to cyprus, creating greater doubt about future events in that country. nevertheless, in the summit conclusions, the reference to the specific situation of portuguese agriculture is so vague that it does not guarantee anything at all. the consequences could be very negative if portuguese interests are not properly protected in the forthcoming negotiations, even after the political decision on accession. it is unacceptable not to advocate a thorough revision of the cap and the financial perspective, as with the joint resolution which we reject. inequalities and divergences risk becoming more pronounced and moving further and further away from the objective of economic and social cohesion. we also reject support for the closer relationship between the european union and nato, through the global agreement that has been reached.
in writing. - (pt) i voted in favour of the joint resolution on the copenhagen european council, despite the differences on certain paragraphs, since the historic step of enlargement reached at this european council represents a decisive challenge for peace, democracy and the development of the european nations, and a fully justified tribute to the efforts made by the candidate countries in their process of adaptation to the political and economic criteria of copenhagen and to the acquis communautaire. however, among other differences, i must mention my disagreement with the second part of paragraph 5 of the resolution, which stresses the need to reform the common agricultural policy before 2006 on the basis of the commission's proposals. the proposal is contrary to the decision of the 15 member states not to amend the cap before 2006 and expresses its a priori support for substantive options which have not yet been the subject of a necessary parliamentary debate. in the case of portugal, this debate will certainly take account of the specific nature of portuguese agriculture and the abnormally low income of portuguese farmers, which is the result of our accession to the communities in 1986 alone, whereas the other member states have been benefiting from it since 1962.
in writing. - (pt) it is very positive that it is once again possible, in what is most important at european level, to draw up a commitment reflecting the general consensus of the political groups. i fully agree with the basic text which we have presented with others and which follows the useful substance of the debates in plenary today and on 21 november, as well as the previous resolution on the subject. i would like to reiterate our two main lines: the commission and the council must draw the immediate consequences of this very serious disaster, in terms of new stringent measures for the future, the anticipation of the full applicability of other measures already applied and a strict control of the entire legal and technical framework. it is also important that the commission draw up and present to us a detailed and rigorous inventory of the entire prestige crisis, including the way in which it was actually managed. we all have something to gain from learning, point by point, this terrible lesson. that's why this lesson must be technically rigorous, scientifically accurate and politically neutral and unemotional. the national partisan quibbling about this indispensable assessment, like those on the left of this house who break the spontaneous consensus of 21 november and seek to lead the european parliament towards a political 'maneuver' by the spanish parliament, can only harm the seriousness and objectivity - of all parties, i stress - and thus mortgage our future to all of us again, neglecting the main thing: our coasts, coastal populations, the interest of european citizens, a greater future capacity for prevention and response. the important thing is the sea, not aznar!
